Finally, the closing statement on fixed alternatives holds by the entrywise envelope
n^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{\top}\Delta\boldsymbol{G}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}\leq W\lVert\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}=O_{p}(n)
(so
L_{M}=O_{p}(\sqrt{n})
) together with the Gram contraction
\sup_{\gamma}n^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}\leq\lVert\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}\rVert^{2}=O_{p}(n)
(so
\overline{M}_{n}=O_{p}(\sqrt{n})
); parts (b) and (c) carry no residuals.

The following corollary packages the Lipschitz-modulus equicontinuity of the transported statistic process for standalone citation.

Corollary 3
(Lipschitz-modulus equicontinuity)

The process
S_{n}(\eta):=\lVert\widetilde{A}_{n}(\eta)\widetilde{M}_{n}(\gamma)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}^{2}
,
\eta=(\gamma,\lambda)\in\Upsilon
, satisfies, for all
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in\Upsilon
,

|S_{n}(\eta_{1})-S_{n}(\eta_{2})|\leq L_{n}\lVert\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\rVert,\qquad L_{n}:=2\lambda_{L}^{-1}\overline{M}_{n}\bigl(\lambda_{L}^{-2}L_{C}\overline{M}_{n}+\lambda_{L}^{-1}L_{M}\bigr)+2\lambda_{L}^{-3}\overline{M}_{n}^{\,2}=O_{p}(1),

where
\overline{M}_{n}:=\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\lVert\widetilde{M}_{n}(\gamma)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}=O_{p}(1)
and
L_{M},L_{C}
are as in parts (a) and (b) of Lemma
10
; consequently
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}S_{n}(\eta)\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\overline{M}_{n}^{\,2}=O_{p}(1)
, and the process is asymptotically stochastically equicontinuous: for every
\varepsilon>0
,

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\sup_{\lVert\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\rVert\leq\delta}|S_{n}(\eta_{1})-S_{n}(\eta_{2})|>\varepsilon\Bigr)=0.

Proof.
Write
\widetilde{A}_{j}:=\widetilde{A}_{n}(\eta_{j})
and
\widetilde{M}_{j}:=\widetilde{M}_{n}(\gamma_{j})
for
\eta_{j}=(\gamma_{j},\lambda_{j})
,
j=1,2
. The three gap-free bounds are as follows.
(R)
\lVert\widetilde{A}_{1}-\widetilde{A}_{2}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}(L_{C}|\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}|+|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|)
and
\lVert\widetilde{A}_{n}(\eta)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}
(Lemma
10
(c)).
(E)
\lVert\widetilde{M}_{1}-\widetilde{M}_{2}\rVert\leq L_{M}|\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}|
(Lemma
10
(a)).
(B)
\overline{M}_{n}=O_{p}(1)
(Lemma
10
(a)).
The reverse triangle inequality on the norm and the telescope give

|S_{n}(\eta_{1})-S_{n}(\eta_{2})|\leq 2\lambda_{L}^{-1}\overline{M}_{n}\,\lVert\widetilde{A}_{1}\widetilde{M}_{1}-\widetilde{A}_{2}\widetilde{M}_{2}\rVert,

and
\lVert\widetilde{A}_{1}\widetilde{M}_{1}-\widetilde{A}_{2}\widetilde{M}_{2}\rVert\leq\lVert\widetilde{A}_{1}-\widetilde{A}_{2}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\widetilde{M}_{1}\rVert+\lVert\widetilde{A}_{2}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\widetilde{M}_{1}-\widetilde{M}_{2}\rVert\leq(\lambda_{L}^{-2}L_{C}\overline{M}_{n}+\lambda_{L}^{-1}L_{M})|\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}|+\lambda_{L}^{-2}\overline{M}_{n}|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|
, whence the displayed Lipschitz bound with
L_{n}
as defined; all ingredients are
O_{p}(1)
and
\lambda_{L}
is deterministic, so
L_{n}=O_{p}(1)
. The uniform bound
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}S_{n}(\eta)\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\overline{M}_{n}^{\,2}
combines
\lVert\widetilde{A}_{n}(\eta)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}
with the definition of
\overline{M}_{n}
. Covering argument: on
\{L_{n}\leq M\}
,
S_{n}
is
M
-Lipschitz on the compact set
\Upsilon
, so with mesh
\delta=\varepsilon/(2M)
,
\sup_{\lVert\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\rVert\leq\delta}|S_{n}(\eta_{1})-S_{n}(\eta_{2})|\leq\varepsilon/2
; choosing
M
with
\mathbb{P}(L_{n}>M)\leq\alpha
(possible as
L_{n}=O_{p}(1)
) gives
\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(\sup_{\lVert\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\rVert\leq\delta}|S_{n}(\eta_{1})-S_{n}(\eta_{2})|>\varepsilon)\leq\alpha
; letting
\alpha\downarrow 0
(with
\delta\downarrow 0
) yields the displayed asymptotic stochastic equicontinuity.

Lemma 11
(Conditional Lipschitz modulus of the multiplied embedding)

Let

\widetilde{M}_{n}^{*}(\gamma):=J_{\gamma}\,n^{-1/2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bigl(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\odot\boldsymbol{V})\bigr)_{j}\,k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}

be the multiplied-then-projected embedding transported to the common space, where
J_{\gamma}
is the isometry of Lemma
10
and
V_{1},\dots,V_{n}
are i.i.d. multipliers with
\mathbb{E}[V]=0
,
\mathbb{E}[V^{2}]=1
, and
|V|\leq\bar{V}
a.s. (e.g. the Mammen multipliers). Under Assumptions
2
(v),
3
(i), and
4
(iii), the score conditions of Assumptions
5
(i)–(ii) (the first through the uniform score bound
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\lVert g_{\theta}(x)\rVert\leq\bar{G}
of Assumption
5
(i)), and, in the sieve regime, Assumption
6
(iii); under the local alternative, Assumption
7
(iii) together with the standing convention
\mathbb{E}[R^{2}]<\infty
of Section
4.1
is additionally invoked. All statements are conditional on the data:

(i)

there exists
L_{M}^{*}=O_{p}(1)
with

\lVert\widetilde{M}_{n}^{*}(\gamma_{1})-\widetilde{M}_{n}^{*}(\gamma_{2})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}\leq L_{M}^{*}|\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}|
for all
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]
, the bound holding conditionally on the data in
\mathbb{P}^{*}
-probability and hence jointly over the data and the multipliers;

(ii)

\overline{M}_{n}^{*}:=\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\lVert\widetilde{M}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}=O_{p}(1)
.

Proof.
Abbreviate
\boldsymbol{a}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\odot\boldsymbol{V})
and write
\mathbb{E}^{*}
and
\mathbb{P}^{*}
for the conditional expectation and conditional probability given the data
\mathcal{D}_{n}:=(\mathbf{X},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0},\hat{\theta})
, of which the multipliers are independent and which fixes
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
, through
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
and
M:=(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}})^{-1}
, and
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
; the inverse is understood on the invertibility events of Fact
2
, as there. For a pair
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})
with
\gamma_{1}\neq\gamma_{2}
, write
T:=|\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}|
.

Multiplier covariance.
By the independence of the multipliers,
\mathbb{E}^{*}[V_{l}V_{m}]=\delta_{lm}
, so the
(j,m)
-entry of the conditional second-moment matrix of
\boldsymbol{a}
is

\bigl(\mathbb{E}^{*}[\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}]\bigr)_{jm}=\sum_{l,l^{\prime}=1}^{n}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{jl}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ml^{\prime}}\,\varepsilon_{0,l}\varepsilon_{0,l^{\prime}}\,\mathbb{E}^{*}[V_{l}V_{l^{\prime}}]=\sum_{l=1}^{n}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{jl}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ml}\,\varepsilon_{0,l}^{2},

that is,
\mathbb{E}^{*}[\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}]=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{D}_{0}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
with
\boldsymbol{D}_{0}:=\operatorname{diag}(\varepsilon_{0,1}^{2},\dots,\varepsilon_{0,n}^{2})
.

Gram reduction.
The Gram–matrix reduction of Lemma
10
(a) is coefficient-free, the feature geometry
\Delta\boldsymbol{G}_{jk}=\langle f(X_{j}),f(X_{k})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}
being derived there for arbitrary coefficient vectors, so

\lVert\widetilde{M}_{n}^{*}(\gamma_{1})-\widetilde{M}_{n}^{*}(\gamma_{2})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}^{2}=T^{2}\,n^{-1}\,\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}\boldsymbol{a},\qquad\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}:=T^{-2}\Delta\boldsymbol{G},

where
\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}
is symmetric positive semidefinite and free of the weights
\boldsymbol{a}
. By Fact
1
(ii), applied pathwise in the realized multipliers at the weights
\boldsymbol{a}
, the pair supremum of the quadratic form is bounded by the one-dimensional supremum of the derivative-feature process,

\sup_{\gamma_{1}\neq\gamma_{2}}n^{-1}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\boldsymbol{a}\;\leq\;\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma),\qquad D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma):=\Bigl\lVert n^{-1/2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{j}\,\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{u}_{\gamma}(X_{j})\Bigr\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}^{2},

(23)

so the pair square never enters as an index set. The remainder of the proof bounds
\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma)
conditionally on the data, mirroring the noise chain of Lemma
10
(a) with a data-random modulus constant.

Leverage control.
All coefficient matrices below are data-measurable and reach the multipliers only through
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
; a single entrywise envelope covers them, and no positive definiteness is required. Write
\boldsymbol{H}:=\hat{\mathbb{G}}M\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}
for the hat matrix, so that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}=\boldsymbol{I}-\boldsymbol{H}
;
\boldsymbol{H}
is symmetric because
M
is, and idempotent because
M(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}})=\boldsymbol{I}
. The claim is

\bigl\lvert(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})_{lm}\bigr\rvert\;\leq\;W^{(e)}\bigl(1+2\hbar_{n}^{1/2}+\hbar_{n}\bigr)\quad\text{for all }l,m,\qquad\hbar_{n}:=n\lVert M\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\sup_{l\leq n}\lVert\hat{g}_{l}\rVert^{2},

(24)

for every symmetric data-measurable
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
with entrywise envelope
|A_{lm}|\leq W^{(e)}
. Expanding
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}=(\boldsymbol{I}-\boldsymbol{H})A(\boldsymbol{I}-\boldsymbol{H})
,

(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})_{lm}=A_{lm}-(\boldsymbol{H}A)_{lm}-(\boldsymbol{A}H)_{lm}+(\boldsymbol{H}AH)_{lm},

the first term is at most
W^{(e)}
in absolute value by the envelope. By symmetry of
A
the two middle terms are transposes,
(\boldsymbol{A}H)_{lm}=(\boldsymbol{H}A)_{ml}
, and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality with the idempotence gives

\bigl\lvert(\boldsymbol{H}A)_{lm}\bigr\rvert=\bigl\lvert\langle\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{e}_{l},A\boldsymbol{e}_{m}\rangle\bigr\rvert\leq\lVert\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{e}_{l}\rVert\,\lVert A\boldsymbol{e}_{m}\rVert=H_{ll}^{1/2}\,\lVert A\boldsymbol{e}_{m}\rVert\leq W^{(e)}(nH_{ll})^{1/2},

since
\lVert\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{e}_{l}\rVert^{2}=\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{H}^{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{l}=H_{ll}
and
\lVert A\boldsymbol{e}_{m}\rVert^{2}=\sum_{j}A_{jm}^{2}\leq nW^{(e)2}
, the column sharing the envelope by symmetry. The corner term follows from the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and the operator norm,

\bigl\lvert(\boldsymbol{H}AH)_{lm}\bigr\rvert=\bigl\lvert\langle\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{e}_{l},A\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{e}_{m}\rangle\bigr\rvert\leq\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\,H_{ll}^{1/2}H_{mm}^{1/2}\leq nW^{(e)}(H_{ll}H_{mm})^{1/2},

where
\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\bigl(\sum_{j,k}A_{jk}^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}\leq nW^{(e)}
, again without definiteness of
A
. The leverages are uniformly small:
H_{ll}=\hat{g}_{l}^{\top}M\hat{g}_{l}\leq\lVert M\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\hat{g}_{l}\rVert^{2}
by Rayleigh–Ritz, so
nH_{ll}\leq\hbar_{n}
for every
l
, and collecting the four terms by the triangle inequality proves (
24
).

The leverage scale is
O_{p}(1)
in both regimes. The score part is bounded first: in the
X
-measurable regime
\hat{g}_{l}=g_{\hat{\theta}}(X_{l})
, so
\sup_{l}\lVert\hat{g}_{l}\rVert\leq\bar{G}
deterministically by the uniform score bound of Assumption
5
(i); in the sieve regime
\hat{g}_{l}=\hat{g}_{\hat{\theta}}(X_{l})
and the sup-norm clause of Assumption
6
(iii) gives
\sup_{l}\lVert\hat{g}_{l}\rVert\leq\sup_{x}\lVert g_{\theta_{0}}(x)\rVert+\lVert\hat{g}_{\hat{\theta}}-g_{\theta_{0}}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\bar{G}+O_{p}(n^{-1/4})
, the population score sharing the bound at
\theta_{0}
. For the inverse, write
g_{i}:=g_{\theta_{0}}(X_{i})
and telescope the Gram entries,

\hat{g}_{i}\hat{g}_{i}^{\top}-g_{i}g_{i}^{\top}=(\hat{g}_{i}-g_{i})\hat{g}_{i}^{\top}+g_{i}(\hat{g}_{i}-g_{i})^{\top},

so that, by the triangle inequality for the Frobenius norm, which dominates the operator norm, and the rank-one identity
\lVert xy^{\top}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}=\lVert x\rVert\,\lVert y\rVert
,

\Bigl\lVert\frac{\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}}}{n}-\Gamma_{\theta_{0}}\Bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lVert\hat{g}_{i}-g_{i}\rVert\bigl(\lVert\hat{g}_{i}\rVert+\lVert g_{i}\rVert\bigr)+\Bigl\lVert n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}g_{i}^{\top}-\Gamma_{\theta_{0}}\Bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}.

The first term is
o_{p}(1)
in both regimes: in the
X
-measurable one the mean-value inequality in
\theta
and Assumption
4
(iii) give
\lVert\hat{g}_{i}-g_{i}\rVert\leq\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert\sup_{\theta}\lVert\nabla g_{\theta}(X_{i})\rVert
, and
n^{-1}\sum_{i}\sup_{\theta}\lVert\nabla g_{\theta}(X_{i})\rVert=O_{p}(1)
by the law of large numbers under the second-derivative clause of Assumption
5
(i) and Assumption
3
(i); in the sieve one
\sup_{i}\lVert\hat{g}_{i}-g_{i}\rVert\leq\lVert\hat{g}_{\hat{\theta}}-g_{\theta_{0}}\rVert_{\infty}=O_{p}(n^{-1/4})
; and
\lVert\hat{g}_{i}\rVert+\lVert g_{i}\rVert\leq 2\bar{G}+O_{p}(n^{-1/4})
throughout. The second term is
o_{p}(1)
by the law of large numbers under Assumptions
5
(i) and
3
(i), the entries of
g_{i}g_{i}^{\top}
being integrable by the first clause of Assumption
5
(i). Since
\Gamma_{\theta_{0}}
is nonsingular (Assumption
5
(ii)), Weyl’s inequality bounds
\lambda_{\min}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}}/n)
away from zero with probability tending to one, whence
\lVert M\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=\lVert n^{-1}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}}/n)^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{p}(n^{-1})
, and therefore
\hbar_{n}=n\lVert M\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\sup_{l}\lVert\hat{g}_{l}\rVert^{2}=O_{p}(1)
in both regimes.

Base point.
Introduce the derivative-feature Gram

G^{(d)}_{\gamma,jk}:=\bigl\langle\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{u}_{\gamma}(X_{j}),\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{u}_{\gamma}(X_{k})\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}},

so that
D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma)=n^{-1}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}G^{(d)}_{\gamma}\boldsymbol{a}
by the Gram representation of Fact
1
(ii), and abbreviate
q_{n}:=n^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{0,l}^{2}
. At the left endpoint, by the covariance step and the cyclicity of the trace,

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigl[D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma_{L})\bigr]=n^{-1}\operatorname{tr}\bigl(G^{(d)}_{\gamma_{L}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{D}_{0}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\bigr)=n^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{0,l}^{2}\bigl(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}G^{(d)}_{\gamma_{L}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\bigr)_{ll}\leq L_{d}^{2}\bigl(1+2\hbar_{n}^{1/2}+\hbar_{n}\bigr)q_{n},

the first equality by the Gram representation and the covariance step, the second moving both projectors onto the Gram by the cyclicity of the trace and reading off the diagonal of
\boldsymbol{D}_{0}
, and the inequality applying (
24
) at
A=G^{(d)}_{\gamma_{L}}
, which is symmetric, data-measurable, and of entrywise envelope
W^{(e)}=L_{d}^{2}
by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality against the derivative bound of Fact
1
(i).

Conditional modulus.
For the increment, put
\Delta^{(d)}:=G^{(d)}_{\gamma_{1}}-G^{(d)}_{\gamma_{2}}
and
B^{(d)}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\Delta^{(d)}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
, and split the difference at the diagonal of the multiplier quadratic form,

D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma_{1})-D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma_{2})=n^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\odot\boldsymbol{V})^{\top}B^{(d)}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\odot\boldsymbol{V})=\Xi_{d}+\Xi_{o},

with
\Xi_{d}:=n^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{0,l}^{2}B^{(d)}_{ll}V_{l}^{2}
and
\Xi_{o}:=n^{-1}\sum_{l\neq m}\varepsilon_{0,l}\varepsilon_{0,m}B^{(d)}_{lm}V_{l}V_{m}
, the expansion substituting
\boldsymbol{a}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\odot\boldsymbol{V})
, which transfers both projections onto the Gram by the symmetry of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
, and collecting the diagonal and off-diagonal summands. The increment of the derivative Gram is Lipschitz: adding and subtracting
\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{2}}(X_{l})
in the first factor of each inner product, then applying the triangle inequality and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\bigl|\Delta^{(d)}_{lm}\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\lVert\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{1}}(X_{l})-\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{2}}(X_{l})\bigr\rVert\,\bigl\lVert\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{1}}(X_{m})\bigr\rVert+\bigl\lVert\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{2}}(X_{l})\bigr\rVert\,\bigl\lVert\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{1}}(X_{m})-\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{2}}(X_{m})\bigr\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 2L_{d}L_{dd}\,\lvert\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\rvert,

uniformly in
l,m
, the last line by Fact
1
(i); since
\Delta^{(d)}
is symmetric and data-measurable, (
24
) at the envelope
2L_{d}L_{dd}\lvert\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\rvert
gives

\bigl|B^{(d)}_{lm}\bigr|\leq 2L_{d}L_{dd}\bigl(1+2\hbar_{n}^{1/2}+\hbar_{n}\bigr)\lvert\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\rvert.

By the independence of the multipliers,
\mathbb{E}^{*}[V_{l}^{2}V_{m}^{2}]=\delta_{lm}\mathbb{E}[V^{4}]+(1-\delta_{lm})
with
\mathbb{E}[V^{4}]\leq\bar{V}^{4}
, and the two parts of the split have conditional second moments

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigl[\Xi_{d}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle=n^{-2}\sum_{l,m}\varepsilon_{0,l}^{2}\varepsilon_{0,m}^{2}B^{(d)}_{ll}B^{(d)}_{mm}\,\mathbb{E}^{*}[V_{l}^{2}V_{m}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\bar{V}^{4}n^{-2}\sum_{l}\varepsilon_{0,l}^{4}\bigl(B^{(d)}_{ll}\bigr)^{2}+n^{-2}\Bigl(\sum_{l}\varepsilon_{0,l}^{2}\bigl|B^{(d)}_{ll}\bigr|\Bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(\bar{V}^{4}+1\bigr)\bigl(2L_{d}L_{dd}\bigr)^{2}\bigl(1+2\hbar_{n}^{1/2}+\hbar_{n}\bigr)^{2}\lvert\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\rvert^{2}q_{n}^{2},

the second step discarding the nonpositive diagonal subtraction inside the square of the off-diagonal pairings, and the last inserting the entrywise bound together with the inequality
n^{-2}\sum_{l}x_{l}^{4}\leq\bigl(n^{-1}\sum_{l}x_{l}^{2}\bigr)^{2}
(a consequence of
\bigl(\sum_{l}y_{l}\bigr)^{2}-\sum_{l}y_{l}^{2}=2\sum_{l<m}y_{l}y_{m}\geq 0
at
y_{l}=x_{l}^{2}
) for the first summand and the definition of
q_{n}
for the second; and

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigl[\Xi_{o}^{2}\bigr]=2n^{-2}\sum_{l\neq m}\varepsilon_{0,l}^{2}\varepsilon_{0,m}^{2}\bigl(B^{(d)}_{lm}\bigr)^{2}\leq 2\bigl(2L_{d}L_{dd}\bigr)^{2}\bigl(1+2\hbar_{n}^{1/2}+\hbar_{n}\bigr)^{2}\lvert\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\rvert^{2}q_{n}^{2},

the identity keeping only the two orderings
l^{\prime}=l,m^{\prime}=m
and
l^{\prime}=m,m^{\prime}=l
of the pairings
\mathbb{E}^{*}[V_{l}V_{m}V_{l^{\prime}}V_{m^{\prime}}
, the only ones in which every multiplier appears twice, each equal to one, and the inequality inserting the entrywise bound and
\sum_{l\neq m}x_{l}^{2}x_{m}^{2}\leq\bigl(\sum_{l}x_{l}^{2}\bigr)^{2}
. Combining the two displays through
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
,

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigl[\bigl(D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma_{1})-D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma_{2})\bigr)^{2}\bigr]\;\leq\;C_{D}^{*}\,\lvert\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\rvert^{2},\qquad C_{D}^{*}:=\bigl(2\bar{V}^{4}+6\bigr)\bigl(2L_{d}L_{dd}\bigr)^{2}\bigl(1+2\hbar_{n}^{1/2}+\hbar_{n}\bigr)^{2}q_{n}^{2},

a deterministic function of the data. Only second moments of
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
are consumed, the multiplier fourth moment entering solely through the diagonal pairing; and
q_{n}=O_{p}(1)
: under
H_{0}
the law of large numbers applies directly (Assumptions
2
(v) and
3
(i)), while under the local alternative
\varepsilon_{0,l}=R(X_{l})/\sqrt{m+n}+v_{l}
and
2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
give

q_{n}\;\leq\;\frac{2}{m+n}\,n^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{n}R^{2}(X_{l})+2\,n^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{n}v_{l}^{2}\;=\;O_{p}(1),

the two averages obeying the law of large numbers under the standing convention
\mathbb{E}[R^{2}]<\infty
of Section
4.1
and Assumption
7
(iii). Hence
C_{D}^{*}=O_{p}(1)
, being a continuous function of the
O_{p}(1)
quantities
\hbar_{n}
and
q_{n}^{2}
with deterministic constants.

Conditional chaining.
The paths
\gamma\mapsto D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma)
are continuous at the realized weights, by Fact
1
(i) and the finiteness of the sum. Let
\mathcal{G}_{J}:=\{\gamma_{L}+kh_{J}:0\leq k\leq 2^{J}\}
with
h_{J}:=(\gamma_{U}-\gamma_{L})2^{-J}
be the dyadic grids; the dyadic expansion of
\gamma-\gamma_{L}
in binary digits, each partial sum and its successor being adjacent points of
\mathcal{G}_{J}
, telescopes the increment into level maxima,

\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl|D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma)-D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma_{L})\bigr|\;\leq\;\sum_{J\geq 0}\ \max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{J}\\
\lvert\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\rvert=h_{J}\end{subarray}}\bigl|D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma)-D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma^{\prime})\bigr|,

the limit term vanishing by path continuity. Each level contains
2^{J}
adjacent pairs, so the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality for maxima within a level, followed by the conditional expectation of the modulus display, gives

\mathbb{E}^{*}\Bigl[\max_{\lvert\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\rvert=h_{J}}\bigl|D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma)-D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma^{\prime})\bigr|\Bigr]\;\leq\;\bigl(2^{J}\bigr)^{1/2}\bigl(C_{D}^{*}h_{J}^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}\;=\;C_{D}^{*1/2}(\gamma_{U}-\gamma_{L})\,2^{-J/2},

and the geometric series at ratio
2^{-1/2}
sums to
(1-2^{-1/2})^{-1}
, whence, combining with the base-point bound,

\mathbb{E}^{*}\Bigl[\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma)\Bigr]\;\leq\;L_{d}^{2}\bigl(1+2\hbar_{n}^{1/2}+\hbar_{n}\bigr)q_{n}+K_{D}^{*}\;=:\;B_{n},

with
K_{D}^{*}:=C_{D}^{*1/2}(\gamma_{U}-\gamma_{L})\bigl(1-2^{-1/2}\bigr)^{-1}
, so that
B_{n}
is a data-measurable
O_{p}(1)
bound. Markov’s inequality applied conditionally gives
\mathbb{P}^{*}\bigl(\sup_{\gamma}D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma)>M\bigr)\leq B_{n}/M
for every
M>0
, so

\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\sup_{\gamma}D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma)>M\Bigr)=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathbb{P}^{*}\Bigl(\sup_{\gamma}D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma)>M\Bigr)\Bigr]\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl[\min\{1,B_{n}/M\}\bigr]\leq\varepsilon+\mathbb{P}(B_{n}>\varepsilon M)

for every
\varepsilon,M>0
; given
\eta>0
, choosing
\varepsilon=\eta/2
and
M
so large that
\limsup_{n}\mathbb{P}(B_{n}>\varepsilon M)\leq\eta/2
, possible since
B_{n}=O_{p}(1)
, yields
\limsup_{n}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\sup_{\gamma}D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma)>M\bigr)\leq\eta
. Thus
\sup_{\gamma}D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma)=O_{p^{*}}(1)
conditionally on the data and
O_{p}(1)
jointly over the data and the multipliers, and, by (
23
),

L_{M}^{*2}:=\sup_{\gamma_{1}\neq\gamma_{2}}n^{-1}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}\boldsymbol{a}\;\leq\;\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}D_{\boldsymbol{a}}(\gamma),

so
\lVert\widetilde{M}_{n}^{*}(\gamma_{1})-\widetilde{M}_{n}^{*}(\gamma_{2})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}=T\bigl(n^{-1}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}\boldsymbol{a}\bigr)^{1/2}\leq L_{M}^{*}\lvert\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\rvert
with
L_{M}^{*}=O_{p}(1)
, conditionally on the data in
\mathbb{P}^{*}
-probability and hence jointly, which proves (i).

Boundedness.
The leverage control (
24
), at
A=\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}
of envelope one, bounds the pointwise second moment. By the isometry of
J_{\gamma}
,

\bigl\lVert\widetilde{M}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)\bigr\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}^{2}=\Bigl\lVert n^{-1/2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{j}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)\Bigr\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2}=n^{-1}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}\boldsymbol{a},

the second equality expanding the squared RKHS norm through the Gram matrix, so that, by the covariance step and the cyclicity of the trace,

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigl\lVert\widetilde{M}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)\bigr\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}^{2}=n^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{0,l}^{2}\,(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})_{ll}.

The Gram matrix is symmetric, data-measurable, and of envelope one,
|(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma})_{jk}|=|k_{\gamma}(X_{j},X_{k})|\leq 1
with diagonal
(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma})_{ll}=k_{\gamma}(X_{l},X_{l})=1
; (
24
) at
W^{(e)}=1
yields

(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})_{ll}\leq 1+2\hbar_{n}^{1/2}+\hbar_{n}=1+O_{p}(1)

uniformly in
l
and
\gamma
, whence

\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigl\lVert\widetilde{M}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)\bigr\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}^{2}\leq\bigl(1+O_{p}(1)\bigr)\,q_{n}=O_{p}(1)

by the law of large numbers, as in the conditional-modulus step. Markov’s inequality applied conditionally gives
\lVert\widetilde{M}_{n}^{*}(\gamma_{*})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}=O_{p^{*}}(1)
, hence jointly
O_{p}(1)
by the upgrade of the conditional-chaining step, at any fixed base point
\gamma_{*}\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]
, and the base-point triangle with the Lipschitz bound of part (i),

\overline{M}_{n}^{*}\leq\lVert\widetilde{M}_{n}^{*}(\gamma_{*})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}+L_{M}^{*}\,(\gamma_{U}-\gamma_{L})=O_{p}(1)

at any fixed base point
\gamma_{*}
, proves (ii).

B.3.2
Proof of Theorem
5
(Uniform Weak Convergence of the Statistic Process)

The theorem has two parts: the null clause, under which
S_{n}^{\perp}\rightsquigarrow S_{\infty}
in
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
with
S_{\infty}(\eta)=\sum_{i\geq 1}\mu_{i}(\gamma)(\mu_{i}(\gamma)+\lambda)^{-2}(Z_{i}^{\perp})^{2}
; and the local-alternative clause, under which, at the total-sample rate,
S_{n}^{\perp}\rightsquigarrow S_{\infty}(\cdot;h_{c})
with
h_{c}:=\sqrt{1-c}\,h
and the noncentral limit of Corollary
2
attenuated by the testing share (see the local-alternative paragraph below). The proof proceeds through the following paragraphs: pointwise convergence; the matrix representation and path regularity; gradient moments; boundedness; asymptotic equicontinuity; tightness, finite-dimensional convergence, and the limit; and the local alternative. Throughout,
S_{n}^{\perp}
is computed on the testing sample of size
n
, and all statements are uniform in
\eta\in\Upsilon
unless otherwise noted.

Notation.
Let
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\hat{\varepsilon}}
denote the orthogonalized residual vector on the testing sample (Section
3
) and write
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
for the true residual vector. Let
\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}
be the
n\times n
Gram matrix,
(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma})_{jl}=k_{\gamma}(X_{j},X_{l})
, and let
\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}:\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
be the restriction operator,
(\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}f)_{j}=f(X_{j})
, so that
\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}=\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}
and
C_{n,\gamma}=n^{-1}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}
, the last identity being the operator form of the definition
C_{n,\gamma}=n^{-1}\sum_{j}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)\otimes k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)
. Let
C_{\gamma}=\mathbb{E}[k_{\gamma}(X,\cdot)\otimes k_{\gamma}(X,\cdot)]
denote the population second-moment operator on
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}
. The empirical covariance admits the spectral decomposition

C_{n,\gamma}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)\,\hat{e}_{i,n}^{(\gamma)}\otimes\hat{e}_{i,n}^{(\gamma)},\qquad\langle\hat{e}_{i,n}^{(\gamma)},\hat{e}_{j,n}^{(\gamma)}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=\delta_{ij},

where
\{\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)\}_{i=1}^{n}
are the eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n
(Proposition
7
) and
\{\hat{e}_{i,n}^{(\gamma)}\}
the Nyström eigenfunctions (these objects are introduced in the notation paragraph of Appendix
B.3.1
). Define the scaled mean embedding

M_{n}(\gamma):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}\,k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)=n^{-1/2}\,\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},

and, for the oracle residuals,
M_{n}^{(0)}(\gamma):=n^{-1/2}\sum_{j}\varepsilon_{0,j}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)
. The statistic admits the RKHS representation

S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)=\big\|(C_{n,\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}M_{n}(\gamma)\big\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{(\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)+\lambda)^{2}}\,\big\langle M_{n}(\gamma),\hat{e}_{i,n}^{(\gamma)}\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2}.

We now prove the theorem.

Proof.

Pointwise convergence.
At each fixed
\eta\in\Upsilon
the residual vector
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}
and the Gram matrix
\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}
are those of a sample of size
n
from the fixed kernel
k_{\gamma}
, so the pointwise limit is already known from the corollaries. Under
H_{0}
,
S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)\xrightarrow{d}S_{\infty}(\eta)=\sum_{i\geq 1}\mu_{i}(\gamma)(\mu_{i}(\gamma)+\lambda)^{-2}(Z_{i}^{\perp})^{2}
by Corollary
1
; under the local alternative at the total-sample rate the limit is the projected law with the attenuated signal
h_{c}:=\sqrt{1-c}\,h
(see the local-alternative paragraph below). The corollaries are stated for exactly the projected residuals, so they already account for the projection. What the theorem requires beyond these pointwise limits is
uniformity in
\eta
:
S_{n}^{\perp}\rightsquigarrow S_{\infty}
in
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
, which needs the gradient-moment and equicontinuity argument below. For this we must know the orders of the two remainder sources that distinguish
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}
from the ideal residuals
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
:

\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}+\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}+\boldsymbol{\delta},\qquad\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\hat{\varepsilon}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\ \ (\text{estimation}),\qquad\boldsymbol{\delta}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\ \ (\text{projection});

for any vector
\boldsymbol{v}
, write
M_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{v})}(\gamma):=n^{-1/2}\sum_{j}v_{j}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)
for the corresponding scaled mean embedding. These two remainders behave very differently at the embedding level. The
estimation
remainder is negligible. By Lemma
3
,
\lVert R_{w}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2}=o_{p}(1)
for
R_{w}:=\sqrt{n}\,\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n+\lambda\boldsymbol{I})^{-1}\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}
, the standing exponent being
\varrho>1/4
. Passing from
R_{w}
to the embedding is immediate at the quadratic-form level:
\lVert M_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}})}(\gamma)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2}=\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}=o_{p}(1)
uniformly in
\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]
, which is the quadratic-form clause of Fact
2
at sample size
n
. Estimating
\theta_{0}
by
\hat{\theta}
leaves no trace in the limit. The
projection
remainder, in contrast, is
not
negligible: although
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
componentwise, the scaled embedding of the difference is of order one. Indeed, with
\hat{\beta}:=(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}})^{-1}\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
(so that
\boldsymbol{\delta}=-\hat{\mathbb{G}}\hat{\beta}
;
\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}/\sqrt{n}=O_{p}(1)
by the CLT for the fixed-score matrix, extended to
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
by the convention of Remark
2
, and
(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}}/n)^{-1}=O_{p}(1)
under Assumption
5
, hence
\lVert\hat{\beta}\rVert=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
),

\lVert M_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\delta})}(\gamma)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2}=\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)\boldsymbol{\delta}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}H(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)H\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\leq\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}H\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}=O_{p}(1),

uniformly in
\gamma
: the identity is
\boldsymbol{\delta}=-H\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
with the hat projector
H:=\hat{\mathbb{G}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}})^{-1}\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}
, of rank
d_{\theta}
on the invertibility events of Fact
2
, so that
\hat{\mathbb{G}}\hat{\beta}=H\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
, and the inequality is Loewner,
H(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)H\preceq H
(congruence preserving the order,
\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n\preceq\boldsymbol{I}
by Step A, idempotence of
H
); the terminal quantity is the single flagged object of Lemma
10
(a), there
O_{p}(1)
under the convention of Remark
2
. Consequently
S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)
and the “raw” statistic
S_{n}(\eta)
built on
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
differ by
O_{p}(1)
at the process level, not
o_{p}(1)
: the projection reshapes the limit law into the
Z^{\perp}
-law of Corollary
1
rather than vanishing asymptotically.

The shift embedding.
The local alternative of Section
4.1
shifts the residual function by
\boldsymbol{R}/\sqrt{m+n}
; the following record underlies the second envelope of the uniform-split paragraph below and is consumed in the local-alternative paragraph below. With
M_{n}^{(b)}(\gamma):=n^{-1/2}\sum_{j}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R})_{j}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)
: pathwise,
\|M_{n}^{(b)}(\gamma)\|^{2}=(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R})\leq\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R}\|^{2}\leq\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}=O_{p}(n)
by
\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\operatorname{tr}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)=1
and the LLN under
\mathbb{E}[R^{2}]<\infty
; hence
\|M_{n}^{(b)}(\gamma)\|^{2}/(m+n)=O_{p}(n/(m+n))=O_{p}(1)
,
\gamma
-free, the form in which the uniform-split paragraph consumes it.

Step A: the matrix representation and path regularity.

This step derives the native quadratic form of the statistic and the deterministic regularity of its sample paths. Fix
\eta=(\gamma,\lambda)\in\Upsilon
and abbreviate
R_{\lambda}:=(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n+\lambda\boldsymbol{I})^{-1}
. The derivation enters through the push-through identity

\bigl(\lambda\boldsymbol{I}+n^{-1}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}\bigr)^{-1}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}=\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}\bigl(\lambda\boldsymbol{I}+\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n\bigr)^{-1},

which is verified by multiplying both sides on the left by
(\lambda\boldsymbol{I}+n^{-1}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma})
and using
\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}=\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}
. Since
M_{n}(\gamma)=n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}
, the push-through identity applied to
(C_{n,\gamma}+\lambda\boldsymbol{I})^{-1}M_{n}(\gamma)=n^{-1/2}\bigl(\lambda\boldsymbol{I}+n^{-1}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}\bigr)^{-1}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}
gives

\bigl(C_{n,\gamma}+\lambda\boldsymbol{I}\bigr)^{-1}M_{n}(\gamma)=n^{-1/2}\,\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}\bigl(\lambda\boldsymbol{I}+\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n\bigr)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp},

and squaring in
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}
,

\displaystyle S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{\top}\bigl(\lambda\boldsymbol{I}+\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n\bigr)^{-1}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}\bigl(\lambda\boldsymbol{I}+\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n\bigr)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{\top}\bigl(\lambda\boldsymbol{I}+\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n\bigr)^{-1}\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}\bigl(\lambda\boldsymbol{I}+\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n\bigr)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{\top}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}+\lambda\boldsymbol{I}\Bigr)^{-2}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp},

the second line substituting
\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}=\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}
and the third extracting the factor
\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}=n\,(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)
against the prefactor
n^{-1}
. This resolves the normalization: with

\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta):=\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}+\lambda\boldsymbol{I}\Bigr)^{-1}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}+\lambda\boldsymbol{I}\Bigr)^{-1}=\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}+\lambda\boldsymbol{I}\Bigr)^{-2},

(25)

the two forms agreeing because the resolvents are functions of
\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n
and commute with it, the representation

S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{\top}\,\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}

holds with prefactor exactly
1
. The matrix
\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)
is symmetric, being a function of the symmetric matrix
\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n
, and the spectral mapping theorem gives it the eigenvalue–eigenvector pairs
\bigl(f_{\lambda}(\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)),\boldsymbol{u}_{i,n}(\gamma)\bigr)
for
f_{\lambda}(t):=t\,(t+\lambda)^{-2}
, so

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\max_{i\leq n}\frac{\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)}{(\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)+\lambda)^{2}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2},

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\leq n}\frac{\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)}{(\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)+\lambda)^{2}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\sum_{i\leq n}\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)=\lambda_{L}^{-2},

using
(\nu+\lambda)^{2}\geq\lambda^{2}
,
\nu_{i,n}\leq 1
(the largest eigenvalue of a positive semidefinite matrix is at most its trace), and
\sum_{i\leq n}\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)=\operatorname{tr}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)=n^{-1}\sum_{j}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},X_{j})=1
for the Gaussian kernel.

For the path regularity, the entries of
\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}
are real-analytic in
\gamma
with

\bigl|\partial_{\gamma}k_{\gamma}(x,y)\bigr|=\|x-y\|^{2}e^{-\gamma\|x-y\|^{2}}\leq(e\,\gamma_{L})^{-1},\qquad x,y\in\mathcal{X},

the maximum of
s\mapsto se^{-\gamma s}
over
s\geq 0
being attained at
s=\gamma^{-1}
with value
(e\gamma)^{-1}
, and
1/\gamma\leq 1/\gamma_{L}
. For a symmetric matrix
A
,
\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|A\|_{\infty}=\max_{j}\sum_{l}|A_{jl}|
, so the entrywise envelope gives, by row averaging,

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\partial_{\gamma}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)\Bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j}\sum_{l}\frac{1}{n}\bigl|\partial_{\gamma}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},X_{l})\bigr|\leq(e\,\gamma_{L})^{-1},

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\partial_{\gamma}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)\Bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot(e\,\gamma_{L}\,n)^{-1}=(e\,\gamma_{L})^{-1},

deterministic, design-free bounds. The product rule through the three factors of (
25
) runs on two spectral inputs:
\|R_{\lambda}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}
, and the Frobenius–operator pairing

\Bigl\|\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)R_{\lambda}\Bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\Bigl(\sum_{i\leq n}\Bigl(\frac{\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)}{\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)+\lambda}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}\Bigl(\sum_{i\leq n}\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)=\lambda_{L}^{-1},

the first step using
\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)+\lambda\geq\lambda\geq\lambda_{L}
, so that
\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)/(\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)+\lambda)\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)
, the second
\sum_{i\leq n}\nu_{i,n}^{2}\leq\bigl(\sum_{i\leq n}\nu_{i,n}\bigr)^{2}=1
, and the last the trace identity
\sum_{i\leq n}\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)=\operatorname{tr}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)=1
established above; in particular
\bigl\|\bigl(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n\bigr)R_{\lambda}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}
. Each term of the product rule is then bounded by pairing one factor in Frobenius norm against the other two in operator norm; the factor
R_{\lambda}
enters only through its operator norm, never through
\|R_{\lambda}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=O(\sqrt{n})
:

\displaystyle\partial_{\gamma}\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)

\displaystyle=\bigl(\partial_{\gamma}R_{\lambda}\bigr)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)R_{\lambda}+R_{\lambda}\bigl(\partial_{\gamma}\tfrac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\bigr)R_{\lambda}+R_{\lambda}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)\bigl(\partial_{\gamma}R_{\lambda}\bigr),

\displaystyle\bigl\|\partial_{\gamma}R_{\lambda}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}(e\,\gamma_{L})^{-1},\qquad\bigl\|\partial_{\gamma}R_{\lambda}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}(e\,\gamma_{L})^{-1},

\displaystyle\bigl\|(\partial_{\gamma}R_{\lambda})\bigl(\tfrac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\bigr)R_{\lambda}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|\partial_{\gamma}R_{\lambda}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}\,\Bigl\|\Bigl(\tfrac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)R_{\lambda}\Bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-3}(e\,\gamma_{L})^{-1},

\displaystyle\bigl\|R_{\lambda}\bigl(\partial_{\gamma}\tfrac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\bigr)R_{\lambda}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|\partial_{\gamma}\tfrac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}\,\|R_{\lambda}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}(e\,\gamma_{L})^{-1},

\displaystyle\bigl\|\partial_{\gamma}\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda_{L}^{-3}(e\,\gamma_{L})^{-1}+\lambda_{L}^{-2}(e\,\gamma_{L})^{-1},

\displaystyle\bigl\|\partial_{\gamma}\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(2\lambda_{L}^{-3}+\lambda_{L}^{-2}\bigr)(e\,\gamma_{L})^{-1},

the third line covering the last product term by the transpose-invariance of the Frobenius norm (all three factors are symmetric), both resolvent bounds following from
\partial_{\gamma}R_{\lambda}=-R_{\lambda}(\partial_{\gamma}\tfrac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n})R_{\lambda}
by submultiplicativity, and the operator-norm total following by the same submultiplicativity through
\|\partial_{\gamma}R_{\lambda}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}(e\,\gamma_{L})^{-1}
,
\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
, and
\|R_{\lambda}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}
. Likewise, with
\partial_{\lambda}R_{\lambda}=-R_{\lambda}^{2}
, the same pairing gives
\|\partial_{\lambda}\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 2\|R_{\lambda}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\bigl\|\bigl(\tfrac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\bigr)R_{\lambda}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\lambda_{L}^{-3}
:

\bigl\|\partial_{\lambda}\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 2\lambda_{L}^{-3},\qquad\bigl\|\partial_{\lambda}\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\lambda_{L}^{-3}.

Writing
C_{\mathsf{W}}:=\bigl(2\lambda_{L}^{-3}+\lambda_{L}^{-2}\bigr)(e\,\gamma_{L})^{-1}+2\lambda_{L}^{-3}
, all four derivative norms are bounded by
C_{\mathsf{W}}
, uniformly in
(n,\eta)
and the design. Finally, the paths are continuously differentiable in
\eta
on
\Upsilon
. The entries of
\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n
are continuously differentiable in
\gamma
, with
\partial_{\gamma}k_{\gamma}(x,y)=-\|x-y\|^{2}k_{\gamma}(x,y)
continuous in
(\gamma,x,y)
and
\bigl|\partial_{\gamma}^{2}k_{\gamma}(x,y)\bigr|=\|x-y\|^{4}e^{-\gamma\|x-y\|^{2}}\leq 4(e^{2}\gamma_{L}^{2})^{-1}
(the maximum of
s\mapsto s^{2}e^{-\gamma s}
over
s\geq 0
being attained at
s=2/\gamma
), so the mean-value theorem and row averaging give

\Bigl\|\partial_{\gamma}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)-\partial_{\gamma}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}{n}\Bigr)\Bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 4(e^{2}\gamma_{L}^{2})^{-1}\,\bigl|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\bigr|,

deterministic and design-free; the resolvent increments obey, by the resolvent identity together with
\bigl\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n-\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma^{\prime}}/n\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(e\gamma_{L})^{-1}\bigl|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\bigr|
(the same device at the first derivative),

\bigl\|R_{\lambda}-R_{\lambda^{\prime}}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\Bigl[(e\,\gamma_{L})^{-1}\bigl|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\bigr|+\bigl|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}\bigr|\Bigr],\qquad\eta=(\gamma,\lambda),\ \eta^{\prime}=(\gamma^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}),

so
\eta\mapsto R_{\lambda}
is operator-norm continuous on
\Upsilon
. Each term of the product-rule displays for
\partial_{\gamma}\mathsf{W}_{n}
and
\partial_{\lambda}\mathsf{W}_{n}
is a product of factors drawn from
R_{\lambda}
,
\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n
, and
\partial_{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)
, all continuous in
\eta
in operator norm by the two increment bounds just recorded and uniformly bounded by this step; hence
\eta\mapsto\partial_{\eta}\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)
is operator-norm continuous and
\eta\mapsto\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)
is
C^{1}
on
\Upsilon
. Since the representation
S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)=\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)\boldsymbol{x}
at
\boldsymbol{x}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}
is
\boldsymbol{x}
-generic,
\eta\mapsto S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)
is
C^{1}
for every realization of
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}
, and the sample paths are continuous on the compact set
\Upsilon
.

Step B: gradient moments.

This step establishes the fourth moments of the gradient process. Write

\partial_{\eta}S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{\top}B_{n}(\eta)\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp},\qquad B_{n}(\eta):=\partial_{\eta}\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta),

a conditionally deterministic symmetric matrix given
\mathbf{X}
, with
\|B_{n}(\eta)\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C_{\mathsf{W}}
and
\|B_{n}(\eta)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{\mathsf{W}}
for every
(n,\eta)
by Step A. Throughout this step, matrices carrying the estimated objects
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
,
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
are treated as conditionally deterministic given
\mathbf{X}
, the convention of Remark
2
; conditional independence refers to the innovations
\varepsilon_{0,1},\dots,\varepsilon_{0,n}
, which given
\mathbf{X}
are independent and centered with
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,j}^{2}\mid X_{j}]=\sigma^{2}(X_{j})\in[\sigma_{\min}^{2},\sigma_{\max}^{2}]
and
\bar{M}_{2p}:=\sup_{j}\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,j}^{2p}\mid X_{j}]<\infty
for the exponent
p\in(2,4]
of Assumption
7
(v) (Assumption
7
(iii), (v)). One input is used, the order-
p
moment of a centered quadratic form: Lemma
9
, applied conditionally on
\mathbf{X}
to these innovations and to any symmetric
\mathbf{X}
-measurable
A
, gives (
21
) at
\mathcal{G}=\sigma(\mathbf{X})
, that is,

\begin{gathered}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}A\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}-\operatorname{tr}(A\,D)\bigr|^{p}\,\Big|\,\mathbf{X}\Bigr]\leq C_{p}\bigl(\sigma_{\max},\bar{M}_{2p}\bigr)\,\|A\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{p},\\
D:=\operatorname{diag}\bigl(\sigma^{2}(X_{1}),\dots,\sigma^{2}(X_{n})\bigr),\qquad\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}.\end{gathered}

The uniform split of the statistic.
In both regimes the statistic splits exactly into a null-type field, two shift terms, and a uniformly negligible remainder; this paragraph sets up the split and records the envelopes consumed by the later steps. By Fact
2
and the definition of the shift,

\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}+\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{sh}}+\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}},\qquad\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{sh}}:=\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R}}{\sqrt{m+n}},

with
\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{sh}}:=\boldsymbol{0}
under
H_{0}
;
\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{sh}}
is
\mathbf{X}
-measurable in the
X
-measurable regime and conditionally deterministic under the convention of Remark
2
in the sieve regime. Under
H_{1,n}
the symbol
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
in this split denotes the centered noise,
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\theta_{0})-\boldsymbol{R}/\sqrt{m+n}
pointwise; the two readings of the symbol coincide under
H_{0}
, and all stochastic statements below (Steps B–E) use the centered reading, under which the entries of
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
are conditionally independent and centered given
\mathbf{X}
. Here
\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}:=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}-\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{sh}}
is the pure estimation remainder: under
H_{0}
it is the
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
of the pointwise-convergence paragraph, and under
H_{1,n}
it is
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}[\hat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\theta_{0})]
with
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\theta_{0})
the true residual at
\theta_{0}
: the shift enters
\hat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
and
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\theta_{0})
identically through the observed responses and cancels, so the quantity is the same estimation effect as under
H_{0}
and Lemma
3
’s bound applies verbatim. For the shift component,
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R}=\boldsymbol{\Pi}R(X_{j})+o_{p}(1)
componentwise: writing
d_{j}:=(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R})_{j}-(\boldsymbol{\Pi}R)(X_{j})
, Assumption
6
(iii) gives
n^{-1}\sum_{j}d_{j}^{2}=o_{p}(1)
, and the embedding of the difference is negligible uniformly in
\gamma
: indeed
\boldsymbol{d}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}\boldsymbol{d}\leq\bigl(\sum_{j}|d_{j}|\bigr)^{2}\leq n\sum_{j}d_{j}^{2}
by
|k_{\gamma}|\leq 1
and Cauchy–Schwarz, so

\Bigl\|\sqrt{\tfrac{n}{m+n}}\;\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j}d_{j}\,k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)\Bigr\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2}=\frac{1}{n(m+n)}\,\boldsymbol{d}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}\,\boldsymbol{d}\leq\frac{1}{m+n}\sum_{j}d_{j}^{2}=o_{p}(1),

uniformly in
\gamma
. Define the projected-noise and shift embeddings

M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0})_{j}\,k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot),\qquad M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma):=\sqrt{\frac{n}{m+n}}\;\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R})(X_{j})\,k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot),

so that
M_{n}^{(h)}
is the embedding of
\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{sh}}
and
M_{n}^{(h)}\equiv\boldsymbol{0}
under
H_{0}
, and the exact pathwise remainder

r_{n}(\gamma):=M_{n}(\gamma)-M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma)-M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)=M_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}})}(\gamma),\qquad M_{n}^{(d)}(\gamma):=\sqrt{\frac{n}{m+n}}\;\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j}d_{j}\,k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot),

where
M_{n}^{(d)}
is the embedding of the hat–true shift discrepancy
\boldsymbol{d}/\sqrt{m+n}
, negligible by the
d
-display above and consumed in the local-alternative paragraph below; the equality
r_{n}=M_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}})}
is exact by linearity,
M_{n}^{(h)}
being the embedding of
\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{sh}}
and
(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R})(X_{j})=(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R})_{j}
. Four envelopes are read off. First, the definitional split
M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}=M_{n}-M_{n}^{(h)}-r_{n}
and the triangle inequality with
\sup(f+g)\leq\sup f+\sup g
give

\displaystyle\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl\|M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma)\bigr\|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl\|M_{n}(\gamma)\bigr\|+\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl\|M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)\bigr\|+\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl\|r_{n}(\gamma)\bigr\|

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1)+O_{p}(1)+o_{p}(1)=O_{p}(1),

the first term by the uniform boundedness conclusion of Lemma
10
(a), which is regime-free: its proof bounds the base point by Markov’s inequality in both regimes, the local-alternative shift being carried by the
\mathbf{X}
-measurable envelope of the regime-coverage paragraph, and its projected-score step is licensed by the convention of Remark
2
; the second by envelope two below (absent under
H_{0}
); the third by envelope three below. Second,

\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl\|M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)\bigr\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2}=\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{sh}}^{\top}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{sh}}\leq\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{sh}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{f}_{\mathrm{sh}}=\frac{\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R}}{m+n}=O_{p}(1),

by
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\preceq\boldsymbol{I}
and the pathwise bound
\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R}\leq\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}=O_{p}(n)
, the LLN at the testing sample under the standing convention
\mathbb{E}[R^{2}]<\infty
of Section
4.1
(the shift-embedding record),
\gamma
-free. Third,

\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl\|r_{n}(\gamma)\bigr\|=\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl\|M_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}})}(\gamma)\bigr\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=o_{p}(1),

the first envelope being the
\eta
-uniform estimation-remainder bound of the pointwise-convergence paragraph and the second the
d
-display above. Fourth, writing
\mathsf{A}_{n}:=(\boldsymbol{C}_{n,\gamma}+\lambda\boldsymbol{I})^{-1}
for the resolvent,
\bigl\|\mathsf{A}_{n}M_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{x})}(\gamma)\bigr\|\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}\bigl\|M_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{x})}(\gamma)\bigr\|
for every
\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and every
\gamma
: by the push-through identity of Step A applied to
\mathsf{A}_{n}M_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{x})}(\gamma)=n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}R_{\lambda}\boldsymbol{x}
with
R_{\lambda}:=(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n+\lambda\boldsymbol{I})^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathsf{A}_{n}M_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{x})}(\gamma)\bigr\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}R_{\lambda}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)R_{\lambda}\,\boldsymbol{x}=\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)\,\boldsymbol{x}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\,\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{L}^{-2}\bigl\|M_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{x})}(\gamma)\bigr\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2},

the first identity squaring the norm formula
\|n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}\|^{2}=\boldsymbol{y}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)\boldsymbol{y}
at
\boldsymbol{y}=R_{\lambda}\boldsymbol{x}
, the middle identity being the definition (
25
) of
\mathsf{W}_{n}
, the inequality the spectral comparison
t(t+\lambda)^{-2}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}t
, valid since
(t+\lambda)^{2}\geq\lambda^{2}\geq\lambda_{L}^{2}
, and the last identity the embedding-norm identity of the pointwise-convergence paragraph. The exact split of the statistic reads

\begin{split}S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)&=\Bigl\|\mathsf{A}_{n}\bigl(M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma)+M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)+r_{n}(\gamma)\bigr)\Bigr\|^{2}\\
&=\underbrace{\bigl\|\mathsf{A}_{n}M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma)\bigr\|^{2}}_{=:\,A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)}+\underbrace{2\bigl\langle\mathsf{A}_{n}M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma),\,\mathsf{A}_{n}M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}}_{=:\,2B_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)}\\
&\quad+\underbrace{\bigl\|\mathsf{A}_{n}M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)\bigr\|^{2}}_{=:\,C_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)}+\underbrace{2\bigl\langle\mathsf{A}_{n}\bigl(M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma)+M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)\bigr),\,\mathsf{A}_{n}r_{n}(\gamma)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}+\bigl\|\mathsf{A}_{n}r_{n}(\gamma)\bigr\|^{2}}_{=:\,\rho_{n}(\eta)},\end{split}

exact in both regimes: the first line substitutes
M_{n}=M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}+M_{n}^{(h)}+r_{n}
and the remaining lines expand the squared norm of the three-term sum by polarization. The remainder is uniformly negligible: with
\overline{M}_{\perp 0}:=\sup_{\gamma}\|M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma)\|
,
\overline{M}_{h}:=\sup_{\gamma}\|M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)\|
, and
\overline{r}_{n}:=\sup_{\gamma}\|r_{n}(\gamma)\|
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|\rho_{n}(\eta)\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|\bigl\langle\mathsf{A}_{n}\bigl(M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma)+M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)\bigr),\,\mathsf{A}_{n}r_{n}(\gamma)\bigr\rangle\bigr|+\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl\|\mathsf{A}_{n}r_{n}(\gamma)\bigr\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl\|\mathsf{A}_{n}\bigl(M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma)+M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)\bigr)\bigr\|\,\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl\|\mathsf{A}_{n}r_{n}(\gamma)\bigr\|+\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl\|\mathsf{A}_{n}r_{n}(\gamma)\bigr\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda_{L}^{-2}\,\sup_{\gamma}\bigl\|M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma)+M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)\bigr\|\,\sup_{\gamma}\bigl\|r_{n}(\gamma)\bigr\|+\lambda_{L}^{-2}\sup_{\gamma}\bigl\|r_{n}(\gamma)\bigr\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda_{L}^{-2}\bigl(\overline{M}_{\perp 0}+\overline{M}_{h}\bigr)\overline{r}_{n}+\lambda_{L}^{-2}\,\overline{r}_{n}^{\,2},

by the triangle inequality in the first row, Cauchy–Schwarz in the second, envelope four in the third, and the triangle inequality in the fourth; reading envelopes one to three in the last row gives
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}|\rho_{n}(\eta)|=o_{p}(1)\,O_{p}(1)=o_{p}(1)
. Term
A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
is the noise statistic: Step B takes its gradient moments and Step D its equicontinuity, in both regimes; the shift terms and the
H_{1,n}
process-level conclusion are closed in the local-alternative paragraph below;
\rho_{n}
is the uniform remainder just bounded.

Gradient moments of the null-type field (Step B, continued).
Now condition on
\mathbf{X}
and split by the residual decomposition of Fact
2
,
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}+\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}
with
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}+\boldsymbol{\delta}
,
\boldsymbol{\delta}:=-\hat{\mathbb{G}}\hat{\beta}=-H\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
,
\hat{\beta}
as in the pointwise-convergence paragraph, and
H:=\hat{\mathbb{G}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}})^{-1}\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}
the hat projector of rank
d_{\theta}
, with Frobenius norm
\sqrt{d_{\theta}}
and operator norm
\|H\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
:

\partial_{\eta}A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)=\underbrace{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}B_{n}(\eta)\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}}_{\text{oracle term}}+\underbrace{2\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}B_{n}(\eta)\,\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{\text{cross term}}+\underbrace{\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}B_{n}(\eta)\,\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{\text{projection term}},

the expansion of
\nabla_{\eta}A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)=(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}+\boldsymbol{\delta})^{\top}B_{n}(\eta)(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}+\boldsymbol{\delta})
, which holds by Step A’s quadratic-form representation applied to the embedding of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}+\boldsymbol{\delta}
, the representation being
\boldsymbol{x}
-generic and the vector
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}+\boldsymbol{\delta}
being
\eta
-free; and
\nabla_{\eta}S_{n}^{c}(\eta)=\nabla_{\eta}A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)-\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}(\eta)\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n})
with
S_{n}^{c}:=A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}-a_{n}
.
The
oracle term
is centered by construction,
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}B_{n}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}D)
, and (
21
) with
A=B_{n}
gives

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}B_{n}(\eta)\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}-\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}(\eta)D)\bigr|^{p}\,\Big|\,\mathbf{X}\Bigr]\leq C_{p}\,C_{\mathsf{W}}^{p}.

The
cross term
. Its
\boldsymbol{\delta}
-part is
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}B_{n}\boldsymbol{\delta}=-\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}B_{n}H\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}=-\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}A_{H}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
with
A_{H}:=\operatorname{sym}(B_{n}H)
; only the symmetric part enters a quadratic form, and
A_{H}=\operatorname{sym}\bigl(B_{n}\hat{\mathbb{G}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}})^{-1}\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\bigr)
under the identity
H=\hat{\mathbb{G}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}})^{-1}\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}
. The Frobenius norm satisfies
\|A_{H}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|B_{n}H\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|B_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|H\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\|B_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sqrt{d_{\theta}}
; (
21
) with
A=A_{H}
therefore gives
\mathbb{E}[|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}A_{H}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}-\operatorname{tr}(A_{H}D)|^{p}\mid\mathbf{X}]\leq C_{p}\,d_{\theta}^{p/2}\,C_{\mathsf{W}}^{p}
. The
projection term
is a quadratic form in
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
with conditionally deterministic symmetric matrix:
\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}B_{n}(\eta)\boldsymbol{\delta}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}HB_{n}(\eta)H\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
with
\|HB_{n}(\eta)H\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|B_{n}(\eta)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\|H\|_{\mathrm{F}}=C_{\mathsf{W}}\sqrt{d_{\theta}}
by
\|H\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
. Centering at the conditional mean
\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}(\eta)HDH)=\operatorname{tr}(HB_{n}(\eta)HD)
, whose modulus is bounded tracially,
|\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}HDH)|\leq\|B_{n}(\eta)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\operatorname{tr}(HDH)\leq C_{\mathsf{W}}\sigma_{\max}^{2}d_{\theta}
since
HDH\preceq\sigma_{\max}^{2}H
and
\operatorname{tr}(H)=d_{\theta}
, (
21
) with
A=HB_{n}(\eta)H
gives

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}B_{n}(\eta)\boldsymbol{\delta}-\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}(\eta)HDH)\bigr|^{p}\,\Big|\,\mathbf{X}\Bigr]\leq C_{p}\,C_{\mathsf{W}}^{p}d_{\theta}^{p/2}.

Collecting the pieces defines the centered field and the drift,

S_{n}^{c}(\eta):=A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)-a_{n}(\eta),\qquad a_{n}(\eta):=\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)\,\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}\bigr),

with
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
of Step B. The role of the field
a_{n}
is confined to hypothesis (ii) of Lemma
8
, recorded after the assembly.
Writing
\nabla_{\eta}S_{n}^{c}(\eta)=(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}+\boldsymbol{\delta})^{\top}B_{n}(\eta)(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}+\boldsymbol{\delta})-\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}(\eta)\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n})
, the identity of the assembly above, and decomposing
\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}(\eta)\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n})=\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}(\eta)D)-\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}(\eta)(D-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}))
, the gradient splits into three groups,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\eta}S_{n}^{c}(\eta)

\displaystyle=\bigl(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}B_{n}(\eta)\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}-\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}(\eta)D)\bigr)+2\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}B_{n}(\eta)\boldsymbol{\delta}

\displaystyle+\Bigl(\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}B_{n}(\eta)\boldsymbol{\delta}+\operatorname{tr}\bigl(B_{n}(\eta)(D-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n})\bigr)\Bigr),

Group 1 is (
21
) with
A=B_{n}(\eta)
, the oracle-term display: at most
C_{p}\,C_{\mathsf{W}}^{p}
, conditionally. Group 2 is the
A_{H}
-treatment:
2\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}B_{n}\boldsymbol{\delta}=-2\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}A_{H}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
, centered at
-2\operatorname{tr}(A_{H}D)
, (
21
) with
A=A_{H}
giving
C_{p}\,d_{\theta}^{p/2}\,C_{\mathsf{W}}^{p}
; the centering constant is absorbed deterministically,
\bigl|\operatorname{tr}(A_{H}D)\bigr|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl|\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}HD)\bigr|+\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl|\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}DH)\bigr|
with
\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}DH)=\operatorname{tr}(HB_{n}D)
, and

\bigl|\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}HD)\bigr|\leq\bigl\|B_{n}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\bigl\|HD\bigr\|_{(1)}\leq\sqrt{d_{\theta}}\,\bigl\|B_{n}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\bigl\|HD\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\,d_{\theta}\,\bigl\|B_{n}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}},

by operator–nuclear duality, the rank bound
\|M\|_{(1)}\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{rank}(M)}\,\|M\|_{\mathrm{F}}
with
\operatorname{rank}(HD)\leq\operatorname{rank}(H)=d_{\theta}
, and
\|HD\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|H\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|D\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sqrt{d_{\theta}}
, the modulus of
\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}DH)
admitting the same bounds since
\|DH\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\|HD\|_{\mathrm{F}}
. Group 3 combines the projection-term bound
\sup_{\eta}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}B_{n}(\eta)\boldsymbol{\delta}-\operatorname{tr}(B_{n}(\eta)HDH)\bigr|^{p}\,\big|\,\mathbf{X}\bigr]\leq C_{p}\,C_{\mathsf{W}}^{p}d_{\theta}^{p/2}
with the bias envelope
\bigl|\operatorname{tr}(A(D-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}))\bigr|\leq 3\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sqrt{d_{\theta}}\,\|A\|_{\mathrm{F}}
: since
D-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}=HD+DH-HDH
with the hat projector
H
of the split, Cauchy–Schwarz in the Hilbert–Schmidt inner product gives
\bigl|\operatorname{tr}(A(D-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}))\bigr|\leq\|A\|_{\mathrm{F}}\bigl(\|HD\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|DH\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|HDH\|_{\mathrm{F}}\bigr)\leq 3\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sqrt{d_{\theta}}\,\|A\|_{\mathrm{F}}
, each Frobenius norm being at most
\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sqrt{d_{\theta}}
: the first two by
\|HD\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|H\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|D\|_{\mathrm{op}}
and symmetry, the third by the intermediate bound
\|HDH\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|HD\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|H\|_{\mathrm{op}}
, through
\|H\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\sqrt{d_{\theta}}
,
\|H\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
, and
\|D\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
; the pathwise piece is therefore at most
3\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sqrt{d_{\theta}}\,C_{\mathsf{W}}
.Consequently,

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\|\nabla_{\eta}S_{n}^{c}(\eta)\bigr\|^{p}\,\Big|\,\mathbf{X}\Bigr]\leq N_{p},\qquad\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigl\|\nabla_{\eta}S_{n}^{c}(\eta)\bigr\|^{p}\leq N_{p},

with
N_{p}
deterministic, depending only on
(p,\gamma_{L},\lambda_{L},\sigma_{\max},\bar{M}_{2p},d_{\theta})
, the conditional bound combining the three groups through
(a+b+c)^{p}\leq 3^{p-1}(a^{p}+b^{p}+c^{p})
and, within group 3,
(x+y)^{p}\leq 2^{p-1}(|x|^{p}+y^{p})
for the deterministic bias piece, a fixed
p
-constant; the passage from the two coordinate bounds to the gradient norm uses
\lVert\nabla_{\eta}S_{n}^{c}\rVert^{p}=\bigl(|\partial_{\gamma}S_{n}^{c}|^{2}+|\partial_{\lambda}S_{n}^{c}|^{2}\bigr)^{p/2}\leq 2^{p/2-1}\bigl(|\partial_{\gamma}S_{n}^{c}|^{p}+|\partial_{\lambda}S_{n}^{c}|^{p}\bigr)
by
(a+b)^{q}\leq 2^{q-1}(a^{q}+b^{q})
at
q=p/2
, the factor
2^{p/2-1}
being absorbed into
N_{p}
; and, identically with
\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)
in place of
B_{n}(\eta)
at the level of the statistic, using
\|\mathsf{W}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{p}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2p}
(spectrally,
\sum_{i}\bigl(\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)/(\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)+\lambda)^{2}\bigr)^{2}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-4}\sum_{i}\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)^{2}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-4}
),
\|\mathsf{W}_{n}H\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\mathsf{W}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sqrt{d_{\theta}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\sqrt{d_{\theta}}
, and the tracial centering
\bigl|\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W}_{n}HDH)\bigr|\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\sigma_{\max}^{2}d_{\theta}
,

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigl|S_{n}^{c}(\eta)\bigr|^{p}\leq N_{p}^{(0)}.

For the drift’s gradient, since
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}
and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}
do not depend on
\eta
, differentiating
a_{n}(\eta)=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n})
gives
\partial_{\eta_{l}}a_{n}(\eta)=\operatorname{tr}\bigl((\partial_{\eta_{l}}\mathsf{W}_{n})(\eta)\,\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}\bigr)
for
l\in\{\gamma,\lambda\}
; the two coordinates are bounded separately. In the
\lambda
-coordinate,
\partial_{\lambda}\mathsf{W}_{n}=-2\bigl(\tfrac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\bigr)R_{\lambda}^{3}
by the second form of (
25
) and
\partial_{\lambda}R_{\lambda}=-R_{\lambda}^{2}
, a symmetric function of
\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n
with nonpositive spectrum
\{-2\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)(\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)+\lambda)^{-3}\}_{i\leq n}
(spectral mapping theorem), so its nuclear norm is

\bigl\|\partial_{\lambda}\mathsf{W}_{n}\bigr\|_{(1)}=2\sum_{i\leq n}\frac{\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)}{(\nu_{i,n}(\gamma)+\lambda)^{3}}\leq 2\lambda_{L}^{-1}\,\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}_{n}\leq 2\lambda_{L}^{-3},

termwise by
(\nu+\lambda)^{-1}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}
and
\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}_{n}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}
(Step A); the trace–nuclear duality
|\operatorname{tr}(NS)|\leq\|N\|_{(1)}\|S\|_{\mathrm{op}}
with
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\|D\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
then gives
|\partial_{\lambda}a_{n}(\eta)|\leq 2\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-3}
, uniformly in
(n,\eta)
. In the
\gamma
-coordinate, abbreviate
\mathsf{T}_{\gamma}:=\partial_{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)
for the tangent matrix; the product rule through the three factors of
\mathsf{W}_{n}=R_{\lambda}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)R_{\lambda}
, with the resolvent derivative
\partial_{\gamma}R_{\lambda}=-R_{\lambda}\mathsf{T}_{\gamma}R_{\lambda}
of the resolvent identity, reads

\partial_{\gamma}\mathsf{W}_{n}=-R_{\lambda}\mathsf{T}_{\gamma}R_{\lambda}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)R_{\lambda}+R_{\lambda}\mathsf{T}_{\gamma}R_{\lambda}-R_{\lambda}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)R_{\lambda}\mathsf{T}_{\gamma}R_{\lambda},

and cyclicity of the trace isolates the tangent matrix against a bracket,
\partial_{\gamma}a_{n}(\eta)=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{T}_{\gamma}\mathcal{B}_{\gamma})
with

\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}:=R_{\lambda}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}R_{\lambda}-R_{\lambda}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)R_{\lambda}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}R_{\lambda}-R_{\lambda}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}R_{\lambda}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}}{n}\Bigr)R_{\lambda}.

Since
\mathsf{T}_{\gamma}
is symmetric, only the symmetric part of the bracket contributes; with
\mathcal{S}_{\gamma}:=\operatorname{sym}(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma})=R_{\lambda}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}R_{\lambda}-2\operatorname{sym}\bigl(\mathsf{W}_{n}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}R_{\lambda}\bigr)
, using
R_{\lambda}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}/n)R_{\lambda}=\mathsf{W}_{n}
and the symmetry of its three factors,

\bigl\|\mathcal{S}_{\gamma}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\bigl\|R_{\lambda}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}+2\bigl\|\mathsf{W}_{n}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigl\|R_{\lambda}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\bigl(\lambda_{L}^{-2}+2\lambda_{L}^{-3}\bigr),

by
\|\operatorname{sym}(M)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|M\|_{\mathrm{op}}
, submultiplicativity,
\|R_{\lambda}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}
,
\|\mathsf{W}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}
(Step A), and
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
. It remains to pair
\mathsf{T}_{\gamma}
against
\mathcal{S}_{\gamma}
. Transporting to the common space, write
u_{\gamma}(x):=J_{\gamma}k_{\gamma}(x,\cdot)\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}
for the transported features of Fact
1
, so that
\langle u_{\gamma}(X_{j}),u_{\gamma}(X_{k})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}=k_{\gamma}(X_{j},X_{k})
and
\|u_{\gamma}(x)\|^{2}=k_{\gamma}(x,x)=1
; differentiating the Gram entries,

(\mathsf{T}_{\gamma})_{jk}=\frac{1}{n}\,\partial_{\gamma}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},X_{k})=\frac{2}{n}\,\bigl\langle u_{\gamma}(X_{j}),\,\partial_{\gamma}u_{\gamma}(X_{k})\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}},

the product rule for the inner product giving the two half-derivatives, which coincide in the present pairing: only the symmetric part of the coefficient matrix enters against the symmetric
\mathcal{S}_{\gamma}
, and relabeling the summation indices
(j,k)\leftrightarrow(k,j)
exchanges the two halves. Therefore, by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality in
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}^{n}
applied to the tuples
\bigl(\sum_{j}(\mathcal{S}_{\gamma})_{jk}u_{\gamma}(X_{j})\bigr)_{k\leq n}
and
\bigl(\partial_{\gamma}u_{\gamma}(X_{k})\bigr)_{k\leq n}
,

\bigl|\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{T}_{\gamma}\mathcal{S}_{\gamma})\bigr|\leq\frac{2}{n}\,\Bigl(\sum_{k}\Bigl\|\sum_{j}(\mathcal{S}_{\gamma})_{jk}\,u_{\gamma}(X_{j})\Bigr\|^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\sum_{k}\bigl\|\partial_{\gamma}u_{\gamma}(X_{k})\bigr\|^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}.

The second factor is at most
\sqrt{n}L_{d}
by Fact
1
(i). For the first, expanding the squared norms through the Gram representation gives
\sum_{k}\bigl\|\sum_{j}(\mathcal{S}_{\gamma})_{jk}u_{\gamma}(X_{j})\bigr\|^{2}=\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\mathcal{S}_{\gamma}\mathcal{S}_{\gamma}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}\bigr)\leq\|\mathcal{S}_{\gamma}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\operatorname{tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}=n\|\mathcal{S}_{\gamma}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}
, by
\operatorname{tr}(PQ)\leq\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}\operatorname{tr}(Q)
for positive semidefinite
Q
and
\operatorname{tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma}=n
; hence

\bigl|\partial_{\gamma}a_{n}(\eta)\bigr|\leq 2L_{d}\,\sigma_{\max}^{2}\bigl(\lambda_{L}^{-2}+2\lambda_{L}^{-3}\bigr),\qquad\text{uniformly in }(n,\eta),

with
L_{d}
the deterministic constant of Fact
1
(i), depending only on
(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U},d)
.

Step C: boundedness at one point.

Fix
\eta_{0}\in\Upsilon
. The drift is bounded at
\eta_{0}
: under
H_{0}
,

\displaystyle a_{n}(\eta_{0})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta_{0})\,\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}\bigr)\leq\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta_{0})\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2},

using
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}\preceq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\preceq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\boldsymbol{I}
(Step B) and
\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}_{n}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}
(Step A), while
\sup_{\eta}\mathbb{E}|S_{n}^{c}(\eta)|^{p}\leq N_{p}^{(0)}
by Step B and
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}|\rho_{n}(\eta)|=o_{p}(1)
by the uniform-split paragraph, so
S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{0})=S_{n}^{c}(\eta_{0})+a_{n}(\eta_{0})+\rho_{n}(\eta_{0})=O_{p}(1)+O_{p}(1)+o_{p}(1)=O_{p}(1)
; under
H_{1,n}
the exact split adds the shift contribution
C_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)=\bigl(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R}/\sqrt{m+n}\bigr)^{\top}\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)\bigl(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R}/\sqrt{m+n}\bigr)
, i.e.

\Bigl(\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R}}{\sqrt{m+n}}\Bigr)^{\!\top}\!\mathsf{W}_{n}(\eta)\Bigl(\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R}}{\sqrt{m+n}}\Bigr)\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\,\frac{\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R}}{m+n}=O_{p}\bigl(n/(m+n)\bigr)=O_{p}(1),

by
\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{R}\leq\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}
and the LLN under
\mathbb{E}[R^{2}]<\infty
, while

\bigl|B_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)\bigr|\leq\bigl\|\mathsf{A}_{n}M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma)\bigr\|\,\bigl\|\mathsf{A}_{n}M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)\bigr\|\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl\|M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}\bigr\|\,\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl\|M_{n}^{(h)}\bigr\|=O_{p}(1)

by Cauchy–Schwarz and envelope four, the envelopes of the uniform-split paragraph, so
S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{0})=S_{n}^{c}(\eta_{0})+a_{n}(\eta_{0})+2B_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta_{0})+C_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta_{0})+\rho_{n}(\eta_{0})=O_{p}(1)
in both regimes.

Step D: asymptotic equicontinuity and tightness.

By Step A the field
A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
is
C^{1}
in
\eta
on
\Upsilon
, Step A’s quadratic-form representation being
\boldsymbol{x}
-generic, and so is
S_{n}^{c}=A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}-a_{n}
. Lemma
8
applies to
S_{m}:=A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
with the decomposition
S_{m}=S_{m}^{c}+a_{m}+e_{m}
,
S_{m}^{c}:=S_{n}^{c}
,
a_{m}:=a_{n}
,
e_{m}:=\boldsymbol{0}
: hypothesis (i) is Step B’s
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigl\|\nabla_{\eta}S_{n}^{c}(\eta)\bigr\|^{p}\leq N_{p}
with
N_{p}
deterministic and regime-free, the three groups consuming only the conditional law of
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
given
\mathbf{X}
, identical in both regimes, and the centering being built from
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{n}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
, which is shift-free; hypothesis (ii) holds with
\Lambda:=2\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-3}+2L_{d}\sigma_{\max}^{2}\bigl(\lambda_{L}^{-2}+2\lambda_{L}^{-3}\bigr)
and
r_{m}:=0
, since by the mean-value form on the convex rectangle
\Upsilon
and the drift-gradient bounds displayed in Step B,
\bigl|a_{n}(\eta)-a_{n}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr|\leq\sup_{\xi\in\Upsilon}\bigl\|\nabla_{\eta}a_{n}(\xi)\bigr\|\,\bigl\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\bigr\|\leq\Lambda\,\bigl\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\bigr\|
, both coordinate bounds being deterministic and
(n,\eta)
-uniform; hypothesis (iii) is vacuous with
e_{m}:=\boldsymbol{0}
. The pointwise bound is
\bigl|A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta_{0})\bigr|\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\bigl(\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl\|M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}\bigr\|\bigr)^{2}=O_{p}(1)
by envelopes four and one of the uniform-split paragraph. Its conclusions are asymptotic equicontinuity of
(A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}})
,

\lim_{\delta\downarrow 0}\ \limsup_{n\to\infty}\ \mathbb{P}\Bigl(\sup_{\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\|\leq\delta}\bigl|A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)-A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr|>\varepsilon\Bigr)=0\qquad\text{for every }\varepsilon>0,

and
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)\bigr|=O_{p}(1)
. Under
H_{0}
,
S_{n}^{\perp}=A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}+\rho_{n}
with
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|\rho_{n}\bigr|=o_{p}(1)
, so the equicontinuity display and
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)\bigr|=O_{p}(1)
follow for
S_{n}^{\perp}
; under
H_{1,n}
the process-level assembly is completed in the local-alternative paragraph below.

Step E: tightness, finite-dimensional convergence, and the limit.

By Step A the sample paths of
S_{n}^{\perp}
are a.s. continuous on the compact set
\Upsilon
; with boundedness and asymptotic equicontinuity (Step D), Theorem 1.5.4 of
van der Vaart and Wellner [1996]
, in its
C(\Upsilon)
form for processes with continuous paths, yields asymptotic tightness of
(S_{n}^{\perp})
in
C(\Upsilon)\subset\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
, and every subsequential limit has a.s. continuous sample paths; this is the source of the theorem’s continuity claim for
S_{\infty}
.

For the finite-dimensional marginals, fix
\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{J}
and consider the random elements
U_{j}:=\varepsilon_{0,j}\bigl(k_{\gamma_{1}}(X_{j},\cdot),\dots,k_{\gamma_{J}}(X_{j},\cdot)\bigr)
in the separable Hilbert space
\bigoplus_{l=1}^{J}\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{l}}
; under
H_{0}
,
\mathbb{E}[U_{j}]=0
and
\mathbb{E}\lVert U_{j}\rVert^{2}=\sum_{l}\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0}^{2}k_{\gamma_{l}}(X,X)]=J\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0}^{2}]<\infty
, so the Hilbert-space CLT (Theorem
13
) gives
(M_{n}^{(0)}(\gamma_{1}),\dots,M_{n}^{(0)}(\gamma_{J}))\xrightarrow{d}(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{J})
, a Gaussian element of the product space.
By the pointwise-convergence paragraph and the proof of Corollary
1
,
M_{n}(\gamma_{l})=M_{n}^{(0)}(\gamma_{l})+M_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\delta})}(\gamma_{l})+o_{p}(1)
with
M_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\delta})}(\gamma_{l})=-n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma_{l}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}}\,\hat{\beta}=-\bigl(n^{-1}\boldsymbol{R}_{n,\gamma_{l}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}}\bigr)\sqrt{n}\,\hat{\beta}\xrightarrow{d}-A_{\gamma_{l}}^{\top}\Gamma_{\theta_{0}}^{-1}W
(jointly in
l
: the same
\hat{\beta}
enters for every
l
, and
(M_{n}^{(0)},\sqrt{n}\,\hat{\beta})\xrightarrow{d}(\xi,\Gamma_{\theta_{0}}^{-1}W)
jointly), so the projected embedding converges to the projected Gaussian element,

(M_{n}(\gamma_{1}),\dots,M_{n}(\gamma_{J}))\xrightarrow{d}(G_{\perp,\gamma_{1}},\dots,G_{\perp,\gamma_{J}}),\qquad G_{\perp,\gamma_{l}}:=\xi_{l}-A_{\gamma_{l}}^{\top}\Gamma_{\theta_{0}}^{-1}W.

By Lemma
26
,
C_{n,\gamma_{l}}\xrightarrow{p}C_{\gamma_{l}}
for each fixed
l
(no uniformity is needed at fixed
\gamma_{l}
), and the map
(x_{1},\dots,x_{J})\mapsto\bigl(\lVert(C_{\gamma_{1}}+\lambda_{1}I)^{-1}x_{1}\rVert^{2},\allowbreak\dots,\lVert(C_{\gamma_{J}}+\lambda_{J}I)^{-1}x_{J}\rVert^{2}\bigr)
is continuous; Slutsky’s theorem yields the joint convergence of
(S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{1}),\dots,S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{J}))
to the corresponding vector of quadratic forms in
(G_{\perp,\gamma_{1}},\dots,G_{\perp,\gamma_{J}})
.

It remains to identify the law of each coordinate with the spectral form of Corollary
1
. Fix
\eta
and write
w_{i}(\gamma):=\mu_{i}(\gamma)(\mu_{i}(\gamma)+\lambda)^{-2}
and
Z_{i}^{\perp}(\gamma):=\langle G_{\perp,\gamma},e_{i}(\gamma)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}/\sqrt{\mu_{i}(\gamma)}
in the eigenbasis of
\boldsymbol{L}_{k_{\gamma}}
, so that
\lVert(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}G_{\perp,\gamma}\rVert^{2}=\sum_{i\geq 1}w_{i}(\gamma)(Z_{i}^{\perp}(\gamma))^{2}
, with
(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}
acting by multiplication by
(\mu_{i}(\gamma)+\lambda)^{-1}
and
\langle G_{\perp,\gamma},e_{i}(\gamma)\rangle^{2}=\mu_{i}(\gamma)(Z_{i}^{\perp}(\gamma))^{2}
. The tail of the series is controlled pointwise in
\gamma
: the directional variances satisfy
\tau_{i}^{\perp}(\gamma):=\mathbb{E}[(Z_{i}^{\perp}(\gamma))^{2}]=\Sigma^{\perp}_{ii}(\gamma)\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
(Corollary
1
; the projection onto
\operatorname{span}(g_{\theta_{0}})
contracts the
L^{2}
-norm), hence, for every fixed
\gamma
,

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sum_{i>K}w_{i}(\gamma)\,\bigl(Z_{i}^{\perp}(\gamma)\bigr)^{2}\Bigr]=\sum_{i>K}w_{i}(\gamma)\,\tau_{i}^{\perp}(\gamma)\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\,\lambda_{L}^{-2}\sum_{i>K}\mu_{i}(\gamma)\;\longrightarrow\;0\qquad(K\to\infty),

the last convergence being the term test for the convergent series
\sum_{i\geq 1}\mu_{i}(\gamma)=1
