at the fixed
\gamma
; no uniformity in
\gamma
is claimed or needed at this step. The spectral form of the limit at each fixed
\eta
is therefore that of Corollary
1
. Theorem 1.5.4 of
van der Vaart and Wellner [1996]
now gives
S_{n}^{\perp}\rightsquigarrow S_{\infty}
in
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
.

The local alternative.
Under the local alternative at the total-sample rate (Section
4.1
), the residual function is
\varepsilon_{0}+R/\sqrt{m+n}
, and the exact split of the uniform-split paragraph already separates the statistic into the null-type field
A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
, the shift terms
2B_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
and
C_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
, and the uniformly negligible remainder
\rho_{n}
. Term
C_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
is deterministic conditional on
\mathbf{X}
:
M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)
, defined in the uniform-split paragraph together with the
d
-display that licenses the replacement
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}R\to\Pi R
at the embedding level, is
\sqrt{n/(m+n)}
times a sample mean of the functions
x\mapsto(\Pi R)(x)k_{\gamma}(x,\cdot)
up to that
o_{p}(1)
replacement. The uniform law of large numbers over
\gamma
is organized on the common space: transporting by the isometry
J_{\gamma}
(which preserves norms), the objects

J_{\gamma}M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)=\sqrt{n/(m+n)}\;n^{-1}\sum_{j}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}R)(X_{j})\,J_{\gamma}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)

all live in
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}
, and the class is Lipschitz in
\gamma
with the
O_{p}(1)
envelope

n^{-1}\sum_{j}|\Pi R(X_{j})|
, by the feature moduli recorded below (the feature-distance bound of the proof of Lemma
10
(a), via the closed form of Lemma
5
). At each fixed
\gamma
,
n^{-1}\sum_{j}(\boldsymbol{\Pi}R)(X_{j})u_{\gamma}(X_{j})\xrightarrow{p}m_{h}(\gamma)
in
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}
by the Hilbert-space LLN, the summands being i.i.d. with
\mathbb{E}\|(\boldsymbol{\Pi}R)(X)u_{\gamma}(X)\|^{2}\leq\bar{k}\,\mathbb{E}[(\boldsymbol{\Pi}R)^{2}]<\infty
. Covering
[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]
by finitely many cells of width
t
, the Lipschitz bound and the triangle inequality across the mesh give, for every fixed
t>0
,
\sup_{\gamma}\|M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)-\sqrt{1-c}\,m_{h}(\gamma)\|\leq\max_{l}\|\,\cdot\,\|_{\gamma_{l}}+O_{p}(1)\,t
; letting
n\to\infty
at fixed
t
and then
t\downarrow 0
gives the uniform convergence in probability. Hence
M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)\xrightarrow{p}\sqrt{1-c}\,m_{h}(\gamma)
,
m_{h}(\gamma):=\mathbb{E}[(\Pi R)(X)k_{\gamma}(X,\cdot)]
,
c:=\lim m/(m+n)
, uniformly in
\gamma
. The resolvent converges uniformly as well: moreover,
\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\lVert C_{n,\gamma}-C_{\gamma}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(1)
by Lemma
10
(b), whose inputs are design-only. The population bound is the same in expectation, and it is here that the feature moduli enter: with
u_{\gamma}(x):=J_{\gamma}k_{\gamma}(x,\cdot)
,
\sup_{x}\|u_{\gamma}(x)\|\leq\bar{k}^{1/2}
and
\|u_{\gamma}(x)-u_{\gamma^{\prime}}(x)\|\leq L_{k}^{\prime}|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}|
(the feature-distance bound of the proof of Lemma
10
(a), via the closed form of Lemma
5
), the rank-one telescoping of part (b) of that lemma gives
\|C_{\gamma}-C_{\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|C_{\gamma}-C_{\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq 2\bar{k}^{1/2}L_{k}^{\prime}\,|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}|
, in expectation. Since
(\boldsymbol{C}_{n,\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}-(\boldsymbol{C}_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}=(\boldsymbol{C}_{n,\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{C}_{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{C}_{n,\gamma})(\boldsymbol{C}_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}
, both resolvent factors being of operator norm at most
\lambda_{L}^{-1}
,
\bigl\|(\boldsymbol{C}_{n,\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}-(\boldsymbol{C}_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\lVert C_{n,\gamma}-C_{\gamma}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
by the resolvent identity,
C_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)\xrightarrow{p}\lVert(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}\sqrt{1-c}\,m_{h}(\gamma)\rVert^{2}
uniformly in
\eta
, a continuous deterministic limit.

Both shift terms are asymptotically equicontinuous, which closes the process level under
H_{1,n}
. For
2B_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
: transporting by the isometries
J_{\gamma}
(which preserve norms and inner products), write
\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}_{n}(\eta):=J_{\gamma}\mathsf{A}_{n}J_{\gamma}^{\top}
,
\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma):=J_{\gamma}M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma)
,
\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma):=J_{\gamma}M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)
, and define the two factor maps on the common space
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}
,

\widetilde{F}_{1,n}(\eta):=\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}_{n}(\eta)\,\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma),\qquad\widetilde{F}_{2,n}(\eta):=\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}_{n}(\eta)\,\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma),

so that
B_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)=\bigl\langle\widetilde{F}_{1,n}(\eta),\,\widetilde{F}_{2,n}(\eta)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}
. Each factor is bounded in
\sup_{\eta}
,
\bigl\|\widetilde{F}_{1,n}(\eta)\bigr\|\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl\|M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}\bigr\|=O_{p}(1)
and
\bigl\|\widetilde{F}_{2,n}(\eta)\bigr\|\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl\|M_{n}^{(h)}\bigr\|=O_{p}(1)
by envelope four and envelopes one and two of the uniform-split paragraph. Each factor is asymptotically equicontinuous with an
O_{p}(1)
modulus,
n
-uniform: writing
\Delta\widetilde{F}_{i,n}:=\widetilde{F}_{i,n}(\eta)-\widetilde{F}_{i,n}(\eta^{\prime})
and inserting and subtracting
\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}_{n}(\eta^{\prime})\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(i)}(\gamma)
,

\bigl\|\Delta\widetilde{F}_{i,n}\bigr\|\leq\bigl\|\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}_{n}(\eta)-\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}_{n}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl\|\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(i)}\bigr\|+\lambda_{L}^{-1}\bigl\|\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(i)}(\gamma)-\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(i)}(\gamma^{\prime})\bigr\|,

the resolvent increment being at most
\lambda_{L}^{-2}\bigl(L_{C}\,\bigl|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\bigr|+\bigl|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}\bigr|\bigr)
by Lemma
10
(c) and the transported resolvent having operator norm at most
\lambda_{L}^{-1}
by Step A. For
i=2
the embedding increment satisfies

\bigl\|\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma)-\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma^{\prime})\bigr\|\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{n}{m+n}}\;L_{k}^{\prime}\,n^{-1}\sum_{j}\bigl|(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}R)(X_{j})\bigr|\,\bigl|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\bigr|,

by the feature Lipschitz bound, with

n^{-1}\sum_{j}\bigl|(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}R)(X_{j})\bigr|\leq\Bigl(n^{-1}\sum_{j}d_{j}^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}+n^{-1}\sum_{j}\bigl|(\boldsymbol{\Pi}R)(X_{j})\bigr|=o_{p}(1)+O_{p}(1)=O_{p}(1),

the first term by Cauchy–Schwarz and the
d
-display of the uniform-split paragraph, the second by the LLN under
\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\boldsymbol{\Pi}R)^{2}\bigr]\leq\mathbb{E}[R^{2}]<\infty
. For
i=1
the embedding increment is at most
O_{p}(1)\bigl|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\bigr|+o_{p}(1)
in both regimes: in either regime the exact split
\widetilde{M}_{n}=\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(\perp 0)}+\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(h)}+\widetilde{r}_{n}
holds (
\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(h)}\equiv\boldsymbol{0}
under
H_{0}
), so the triangle inequality adds to the
O_{p}(1)
Lipschitz modulus of
\widetilde{M}_{n}
provided by Lemma
10
(a) the modulus of
\widetilde{M}_{n}^{(h)}
displayed above and the uniform envelope
\sup_{\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]}\bigl\|\widetilde{r}_{n}\bigr\|=o_{p}(1)
(envelope three). The product identity gives, on
\bigl\{\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\|\leq\delta\bigr\}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\|\leq\delta}\bigl|B_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)-B_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\|\leq\delta}\Bigl(\bigl\|\widetilde{F}_{1,n}(\eta)\bigr\|\,\bigl\|\Delta\widetilde{F}_{2,n}\bigr\|+\bigl\|\Delta\widetilde{F}_{1,n}\bigr\|\,\bigl\|\widetilde{F}_{2,n}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr\|\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\delta\,O_{p}(1)+o_{p}(1),

with the
O_{p}(1)

n
-uniform, by adding and subtracting
\bigl\langle\widetilde{F}_{1,n}(\eta),\widetilde{F}_{2,n}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr\rangle
, Cauchy–Schwarz, and the boundedness and modulus estimates above; the
o_{p}(1)
term vanishes in
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
for every fixed
\delta
, so
2B_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
is asymptotically equicontinuous. For
C_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
: the uniform convergence to the continuous deterministic limit just established gives, by the triangle inequality and the modulus of continuity of the limit,

\sup_{\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\|\leq\delta}\bigl|C_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)-C_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr|\leq 2\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|C_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}(\eta)-C^{\mathrm{sh},\infty}(\eta)\bigr|+\omega_{C^{\mathrm{sh},\infty}}(\delta),

with
C^{\mathrm{sh},\infty}(\eta):=\bigl\|(\boldsymbol{C}_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}\sqrt{1-c}\,m_{h}(\gamma)\bigr\|^{2}
and
\omega_{C^{\mathrm{sh},\infty}}(\delta):=\sup_{\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\|\leq\delta}\bigl|C^{\mathrm{sh},\infty}(\eta)-C^{\mathrm{sh},\infty}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr|\downarrow 0
as
\delta\downarrow 0
by continuity; so
C_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
is asymptotically equicontinuous. Assembling the exact split, writing
\Delta T:=T(\eta)-T(\eta^{\prime})
and taking the outer suprema over
\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\|\leq\delta
, for
\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\|\leq\delta
,

\bigl|S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)-S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr|\leq\sup_{\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\|\leq\delta}\bigl|\Delta A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}\bigr|+2\sup_{\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\|\leq\delta}\bigl|\Delta B_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}\bigr|+\sup_{\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\|\leq\delta}\bigl|\Delta C_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}\bigr|+2\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|\rho_{n}\bigr|,

by the triangle inequality, the four pieces being licensed by Step D, whose conclusions hold for
A_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
in both regimes, by the
B_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
and
C_{n}^{\mathrm{sh}}
blocks above, and by the uniform-split paragraph; hence
S_{n}^{\perp}
is asymptotically equicontinuous under
H_{1,n}
, with
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)\bigr|=O_{p}(1)
the pointwise bound of Step C.

The finite-dimensional convergence follows from the same product-space argument with the Gaussian limit shifted; the multi-
\gamma
joint CLT is the stacked Lindeberg CLT in

\bigl(\bigoplus_{l}\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{l}}\bigr)\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{\theta}}
: every linear combination of the coordinates is a univariate sum of independent summands whose Lindeberg ratios vanish: the summands are bounded by
\bar{k}\,\max_{j\leq n}\lvert\varepsilon_{0,j}\rvert
times unit-sphere coefficients in the Hilbert coordinates, and by
\bar{G}\max_{j\leq n}\lvert\varepsilon_{0,j}\rvert
times unit-sphere coefficients in the
\mathbb{R}^{d_{\theta}}
coordinates, the score factors
\hat{g}_{\hat{\theta}}(X_{j})
entering the latter being conditionally deterministic by Remark
2
and uniformly bounded by the score bound of Assumption
5
(i) in the
X
-measurable regime and by
\bar{G}+O_{p}(n^{-1/4})
through the sup-norm clause of Assumption
6
(iii) in the sieve regime; and
\max_{j\leq n}\lvert\varepsilon_{0,j}\rvert=o_{p}(n^{1/2})
under
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0}^{2}]<\infty
(Assumption
7
(iii)) by Chebyshev’s inequality applied to the truncated variables,
\mathbb{P}(\max_{j\leq n}\lvert\varepsilon_{0,j}\rvert>\varepsilon n^{1/2})\leq n\,\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\mathbf{1}(\lvert\varepsilon_{0}\rvert>\varepsilon n^{1/2})]/(\varepsilon^{2}n)=\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\mathbf{1}(\lvert\varepsilon_{0}\rvert>\varepsilon n^{1/2})]/\varepsilon^{2}\to 0
by dominated convergence, which is the classical Lindeberg condition for independent summands with finite second moments, and the Cramér–Wold device lifts the univariate conclusions.
Precisely,
(M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma_{1}),\dots,M_{n}^{(\perp 0)}(\gamma_{J}))\xrightarrow{d}(G_{\perp,\gamma_{1}},\dots,G_{\perp,\gamma_{J}})
(proof of Corollary
1
) and
M_{n}^{(h)}(\gamma_{l})\xrightarrow{p}\sqrt{1-c}\,m_{h}(\gamma_{l})
.
Hence
S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})\xrightarrow{d}\lVert(C_{\gamma_{l}}+\lambda_{l}I)^{-1}(G_{\perp,\gamma_{l}}+\sqrt{1-c}\,m_{h}(\gamma_{l}))\rVert^{2}=\sum_{i\geq 1}\mu_{i}(\gamma_{l})(\mu_{i}(\gamma_{l})+\lambda_{l})^{-2}(Z_{i,l}^{\perp}+\sqrt{1-c}\,h_{i}(\gamma_{l}))^{2}
with
h_{i}(\gamma):=\mathbb{E}[(\Pi R)(X)\phi_{i}(X;\gamma)]
, matching
S_{\infty}(\eta_{l};h_{c})
of Theorem
5
(
h_{c}:=\sqrt{1-c}\,h
). Asymptotic equicontinuity of
S_{n}^{\perp}
under
H_{1,n}
(the assembly above), the pointwise bound of Step C, and the finite-dimensional convergence of the preceding paragraph give, by Theorem 1.5.4 of
van der Vaart and Wellner [1996]
,
S_{n}^{\perp}\rightsquigarrow S_{\infty}(\cdot;h_{c})
in
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
; the sample paths of the limit are continuous by the tightness in
C(\Upsilon)
established in Step D and the assembly above.

B.3.3
Sample-Path Continuity of the Limit Fields

The limit fields of Theorems
5
and
6
admit modifications with Hölder-
\beta
sample paths for every
\beta<1/2
; the derivation below is self-contained and is cited by the convergence proofs.

Continuity of the limit fields via functional calculus.

The sample-path regularity of the limiting Gaussian fields follows from the functional calculus of
the analytic operator family
\gamma\mapsto\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}
, and no eigenpair regularity enters.

White-noise coupling and the operator representation.

Couple the projected limit of Corollary
1
with an isonormal Gaussian noise
\zeta
on

L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
, i.e. a Gaussian white noise with

\mathbb{E}[\langle\zeta,f\rangle\langle\zeta,g\rangle]=\langle f,g\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})}
:
under this coupling the directional coordinates
\langle G_{\perp,\gamma},e_{i}(\gamma)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}/\sqrt{\mu_{i}(\gamma)}
and the score limit
W
are the evaluations of the single noise

\zeta
on the features
\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\phi_{i}(\cdot;\gamma)
and

\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}g_{\theta_{0}}
, so
G_{\perp,\gamma}\overset{d}{=}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\zeta
at process level and

Z_{i}^{\perp}(\gamma)=\langle\zeta,\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\phi_{i}(\cdot;\gamma)\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})},

with
\operatorname{Cov}(Z_{i}^{\perp}(\gamma),Z_{j}^{\perp}(\gamma))=\mathbb{E}[\sigma^{2}(X)(\boldsymbol{\Pi}\phi_{i})(X)(\boldsymbol{\Pi}\phi_{j})(X)]=\Sigma^{\perp}_{ij}(\gamma)
,
the covariance of the projected limit (
\boldsymbol{\Pi}\phi_{i}=\phi_{i}-\kappa_{i}g_{\theta_{0}}
by
Corollary
1
). At differing bandwidths the same coupling gives the cross-
\gamma
covariance

\begin{gathered}\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(Z_{i}^{\perp}(\gamma),Z_{j}^{\perp}(\gamma^{\prime})\bigr)=\bigl\langle\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\phi_{i}(\cdot;\gamma),\ \boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\phi_{j}(\cdot;\gamma^{\prime})\bigr\rangle_{L^{2}}\\
=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sigma^{2}(X)\,\bigl(\boldsymbol{\Pi}\phi_{i}\bigr)(X;\gamma)\,\bigl(\boldsymbol{\Pi}\phi_{j}\bigr)(X;\gamma^{\prime})\bigr],\end{gathered}

reducing to
\Sigma_{ij}^{\perp}(\gamma)
at equal parameters. Substituting into the spectral form,

\displaystyle S_{\infty}(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\geq 1}w_{i}(\gamma)\bigl(Z_{i}^{\perp}(\gamma)\bigr)^{2}=\langle\zeta,\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma}\zeta\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{W}(\gamma)\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma},\qquad\boldsymbol{W}(\gamma):=\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}\bigl(\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}+\lambda\boldsymbol{I}\bigr)^{-2},

where
w_{i}(\gamma):=\mu_{i}(\gamma)/(\mu_{i}(\gamma)+\lambda)^{2}
. The operator
\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma}
is
nonnegative, self-adjoint, and trace class with
\lVert\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma}\rVert_{1}=\operatorname{tr}\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma}=\sum_{i\geq 1}w_{i}(\gamma)\,\tau_{i}^{\perp}(\gamma)\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}

uniformly in
\gamma
, since
\mu_{i}/(\mu_{i}+\lambda)^{2}\leq\mu_{i}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
and

\operatorname{tr}\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}=\sum_{i}\mu_{i}(\gamma)=\mathbb{E}[k_{\gamma}(X,X)]=1
; in particular

\mathbb{E}\,S_{\infty}(\gamma)=\operatorname{tr}\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
and the
quadratic form is finite almost surely.

The increment bound: divided differences and the trace norm.

The parameter increments are controlled by the Birman–Solomyak form of the spectral calculus. For self-adjoint
S,T
with spectra in
[0,\bar{k}]
and
f(t):=t/(t+\lambda)^{2}
, the increment admits the double-operator-integral representation

f(S)-f(T)=T_{f^{[1]}}(S-T),\qquad f^{[1]}(s,t):=\frac{f(s)-f(t)}{s-t},

and on the Hilbert–Schmidt and trace classes the double operator integral is a contraction up to its symbol’s supremum,
\lVert T_{f^{[1]}}(X)\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\sup_{s\neq t}\lvert f^{[1]}(s,t)\rvert\,\lVert X\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}
and likewise for the trace norm (Birman–Solomyak); the operator-level statement with the same constant fails for noncommuting self-adjoints and is not used. Here
\sup_{s\neq t}\lvert f^{[1]}(s,t)\rvert\leq\sup_{[0,\bar{k}]}\lvert f^{\prime}\rvert\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}
by the mean value theorem. With
S=\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}
,
T=\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
, hence, using
\lVert\boldsymbol{\Pi}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
,

\lVert\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sigma_{\max}
, and the kernel-level Hilbert–Schmidt bound of
Lemma
10
(b),

\lVert\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lVert\boldsymbol{W}(\gamma)-\boldsymbol{W}(\gamma^{\prime})\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq C\,|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}|,

and likewise, writing
\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}=\boldsymbol{P}_{\gamma}^{*}\boldsymbol{P}_{\gamma}
with the transported-feature operator
(\boldsymbol{P}_{\gamma}h)(x)=\langle h,\boldsymbol{u}_{\gamma}(x,\cdot)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}
(so that
\boldsymbol{P}_{\gamma}^{*}\boldsymbol{P}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
has kernel
\boldsymbol{K}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}=c_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}k_{\gamma_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}}
, Lemma
5
), the trace-class difference satisfies
\lVert\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\rVert_{1}\leq 2\lVert\boldsymbol{P}_{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{P}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}=2\sqrt{2\,(1-c_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}})}\leq C|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}|
.
The last inequality is the closed form of Lemma
5
:
c_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}=\bigl(4\gamma\gamma^{\prime}/({\gamma+\gamma^{\prime}})^{2}\bigr)^{d/4}
with
4\gamma\gamma^{\prime}/({\gamma+\gamma^{\prime}})^{2}\in[c_{\min}^{4/d},1]
by AM–GM, and
1-4\gamma\gamma^{\prime}/({\gamma+\gamma^{\prime}})^{2}=({\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}})^{2}/({\gamma+\gamma^{\prime}})^{2}\leq(2\gamma_{L})^{-2}|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}|^{2}
, so
1-c_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\leq(d/4)\max\{1,c_{\min}^{1-4/d}\}(2\gamma_{L})^{-2}|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}|^{2}
by the mean value theorem applied to
t\mapsto t^{d/4}
on
[c_{\min}^{4/d},1]
.
Cyclicity then gives
|\operatorname{tr}(\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma^{\prime}})|\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\rVert_{1}\leq C|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}|
.
The
\lambda
-direction is identical, with the derivative of the rational weights bounded by a constant proportional to
\lambda_{L}^{-3}
, so both bounds hold uniformly over
\Upsilon
with
\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert
in place of
|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}|
.

Fourth moments and Kolmogorov–Chentsov.

The Wick identity (Isserlis’ theorem) applied to the difference of the quadratic forms gives the exact increment

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(S_{\infty}(\gamma)-S_{\infty}(\gamma^{\prime})\bigr)^{2}\bigr]=2\,\lVert\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}+\bigl(\operatorname{tr}(\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma^{\prime}})\bigr)^{2}\leq C\,\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert^{2},

so the canonical metric of the field is Lipschitz in the parameter. The centered increment is a second-order chaos; hypercontractivity of second-order chaoses gives
\mathbb{E}[(\Delta S-\mathbb{E}\Delta S)^{4}]\leq C\,(\mathbb{E}[(\Delta S-\mathbb{E}\Delta S)^{2}])^{2}\leq C\,\lVert\Delta\eta\rVert^{4}
for a universal constant, and adding back the deterministic mean increment via the trace bound and Jensen’s inequality yields
\mathbb{E}[(\Delta S)^{4}]\leq C\,\lVert\Delta\eta\rVert^{4}
. The moment criterion of Kolmogorov–Chentsov with
p=4>d=\dim\Upsilon=2
and exponent
4
then yields a modification of
S_{\infty}
whose sample paths are Hölder-
\beta
for every
\beta<1/2
.

The remaining fields: drifts and the shifted fields.

The same scheme governs the remaining limit fields. The projected chaos kernels are
K_{\eta}:=\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\Pi}P_{\eta}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}
with
P_{\eta}:=\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}+\lambda\boldsymbol{I})^{-1}
, the isometry
\mathbb{E}[(\mathcal{C}(\eta)-\mathcal{C}(\eta^{\prime}))^{2}]=2\lVert K_{\eta}-K_{\eta^{\prime}}\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}
holds, and the resolvent identity gives the
K
-free modulus
\lVert P_{\eta}-P_{\eta^{\prime}}\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\lambda_{U}\lambda_{L}^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}+\bigl(\lambda_{U}\lambda_{L}^{-2}+\lambda_{L}^{-1}\bigr)|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|\leq C\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert
. The drift terms
\nu(\eta)
,
\rho(\eta)
,
\Delta(\eta)
,
V(\eta)
, and
A_{h}(\eta)
are Hilbert–Schmidt inner products or
L^{2}
-norms of
P_{\eta}
and of the resolvent against fixed functions and kernels, hence Lipschitz by the same modulus. The shifted fields of the local alternative split as
S_{\infty}(\gamma;h_{c})=\langle\zeta,\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma}\zeta\rangle+2\langle\zeta,\boldsymbol{b}_{\gamma}\rangle+m_{\gamma}
with
\boldsymbol{b}_{\gamma}:=\sqrt{1-c}\,\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{W}(\gamma)\boldsymbol{\Pi}R
and
m_{\gamma}:=(1-c)\langle\boldsymbol{\Pi}R,\boldsymbol{W}(\gamma)\boldsymbol{\Pi}R\rangle_{L^{2}}
, both Lipschitz in
\gamma
by the divided difference. The conclusion therefore covers
\mathbb{H}
and
\mathbb{H}_{h}^{a}
; the level statement of Theorem
6
(b) and the demoted-fluctuation remark (Remark
11
) of its proof cover the fixed-alternative regime, no limit process being asserted there.

B.3.4
Auxiliary lemmas for the cross-validation criterion process

The seven lemmas below are ordered by their first use in the proof of Theorem
6
: the leave-one-out prediction linearization enters at the outset, the criterion decomposition built on it identifies the objects the theory consumes, the replacement cost of the feasible residuals transfers the feasible criterion to its infeasible counterpart, the conditional variance formulas control the centered pieces, and the bilinear representation with the chaos identification deliver the limit. Throughout the lemmas of this subsubsection,
\varepsilon_{\perp}^{0}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
denotes the infeasible (oracle) orthogonalized residual vector of the selection sample, in the manuscript’s standing notation, with components
\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}
; the feasible vector of (
3
) is
\varepsilon_{\perp}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
, and the transfer between the two criteria is performed once, by Lemma
15
. Proof-level citations that run forward (the decomposition’s proof invokes the variance bound (
29
) of Lemma
14
) are unproblematic, since the cited statements are proved independently of the referring proofs. The operator and kernel tools shared with Theorem
5
are collected in the preceding subsubsection.

Lemma 12
(Leave-one-out prediction linearization)

For
i=1,\dots,m
, let

C_{\gamma}^{(-i)}:=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{j\neq i}k_{\gamma}(X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot)\otimes k_{\gamma}(X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot),\qquad\hat{m}^{(-i)}:=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{j\neq i}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,k_{\gamma}(X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot)

be the leave-one-out covariance operator and mean embedding, both built on the orthogonalized residuals
\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}
, and let

\hat{w}^{(-i)}_{\eta}:=(C^{(-i)}_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}\hat{m}^{(-i)}

be the leave-one-out predictor; write
g_{\eta}
for the smoothing kernel of (
4
). Then, uniformly in
i=1,\dots,m
and
\eta\in\Upsilon
,

\hat{w}^{(-i)}_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}})=L_{i}(\eta)+r_{i},\qquad L_{i}(\eta):=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{j\neq i}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}}),

where
r_{i}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly under
H_{0}
and under local alternatives with signal
O(m^{-1/2})
, and
r_{i}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
uniformly under a fixed alternative; the implied constants are of order
\lambda_{L}^{-2}
, entering through the resolvent identity.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, bounds stated pointwise in
\eta
are upgraded to their

\sup_{\eta}
form by a Lipschitz-plus-
\varepsilon
-net argument over the compact

\Upsilon=[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]\times[\lambda_{L},\lambda_{U}]
, exactly as in
Lemma
10
(b) and Theorem
5
;
we record pointwise rates and note the Lipschitz input when it is not obvious.
We first realize
\hat{w}^{(-i)}_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}})
as a smoothed average plus a
perturbation term. Put

\Delta_{j}:=k_{\gamma}(X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot)\otimes k_{\gamma}(X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot)-C_{\gamma},\qquad\mathbb{E}\Delta_{j}=0,

so that, since
C_{\gamma}
is the population second-moment operator of the standing notation, which is what makes the
\Delta_{j}
centered,
C_{\gamma}^{(-i)}=C_{\gamma}+E_{i}
with

E_{i}:=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{j\neq i}\Delta_{j},

and

\hat{m}^{(-i)}:=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{j\neq i}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}k_{\gamma}(X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot)\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}.

On
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}
the resolvent identity gives

(C_{\gamma}^{(-i)}+\lambda I)^{-1}=(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}-(C_{\gamma}^{(-i)}+\lambda I)^{-1}E_{i}(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}.

Write

\mathsf{R}_{i}:=-(C_{\gamma}^{(-i)}+\lambda I)^{-1}E_{i}(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1},

so that
\hat{w}^{(-i)}_{\eta}:=(C_{\gamma}^{(-i)}+\lambda I)^{-1}\hat{m}^{(-i)}
satisfies

\hat{w}^{(-i)}_{\eta}=(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}\hat{m}^{(-i)}+\mathsf{R}_{i}\hat{m}^{(-i)}.

Evaluation at
X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}}
is the
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}
inner
product with the representer: for any
f\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}
,

f(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}})=\langle k_{\gamma}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot),f\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}

by the reproducing property. Expanding the leading term by linearity and self-adjointness of

(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}
gives

\displaystyle\bigl((C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}\hat{m}^{(-i)}\bigr)(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}})

\displaystyle=\Big\langle k_{\gamma}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot),(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}\hat{m}^{(-i)}\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{j\neq i}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\Big\langle k_{\gamma}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot),(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}k_{\gamma}(X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot)\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}.

By definition (
4
) the inner product is the population
smoothing kernel

g_{\eta}(x,y):=\big\langle k_{\gamma}(x,\cdot),(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}k_{\gamma}(y,\cdot)\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}},

the kernel of
(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}C_{\gamma}
on

L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
, whence

\bigl((C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}\hat{m}^{(-i)}\bigr)(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}})=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{j\neq i}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}}).

Putting
r_{i}:=(\mathsf{R}_{i}\hat{m}^{(-i)})(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}})=\langle k_{\gamma}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot),\mathsf{R}_{i}\hat{m}^{(-i)}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}

gives the claimed algebraic linearization

\hat{w}^{(-i)}_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}})=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{j\neq i}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}})+r_{i},

whose leading term is exactly
L_{i}(\eta)
of the statement; hence
\hat{w}^{(-i)}_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}})=L_{i}(\eta)+r_{i}
as an identity.

It remains to bound
r_{i}
. Since
r_{i}=\langle k_{\gamma}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot),\mathsf{R}_{i}\hat{m}^{(-i)}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}
and
\mathsf{R}_{i}=-(C_{\gamma}^{(-i)}+\lambda I)^{-1}E_{i}(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}
, the rate of
r_{i}
is governed by two factors: the covariance perturbation
E_{i}
and the size of the leave-one-out embedding
\hat{m}^{(-i)}
.

Covariance perturbation.

For
u\otimes v
,
\lVert u\otimes v\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}=\lVert u\rVert\lVert v\rVert
;
by the reproducing property

\lVert k_{\gamma}(X,\cdot)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2}=k_{\gamma}(X,X)
,
hence

\lVert k_{\gamma}(X,\cdot)\otimes k_{\gamma}(X,\cdot)\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}=k_{\gamma}(X,X)\leq\bar{k}

pointwise by Assumption
2
(i), and by Jensen

\lVert C_{\gamma}\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\mathbb{E}k_{\gamma}(X,X)\leq\bar{k}
.
Consequently

\lVert\Delta_{j}\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq 2\bar{k}\quad\text{hence}\quad\lVert\Delta_{j}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\bar{k}\ \ \text{a.s.,}

with deterministic envelope
2\bar{k}
uniform in
\gamma
.
Hilbert–Schmidt orthogonality of centered independent summands gives, for
each fixed
i
,

\mathbb{E}\lVert E_{i}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\lVert E_{i}\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}=\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{j\neq i}\mathbb{E}\lVert\Delta_{j}\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}\leq\frac{4\bar{k}^{2}}{m-1},

so
\lVert E_{i}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
pointwise.
Uniformity in
i
needs no union bound: with

\bar{\Delta}:=m^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Delta_{j}
,

\lVert\bar{\Delta}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
by the same computation,
and

E_{i}=\frac{m}{m-1}\,\bar{\Delta}-\frac{1}{m-1}\,\Delta_{i},\qquad\lVert E_{i}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{m}{m-1}\lVert\bar{\Delta}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\frac{2\bar{k}}{m-1}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})

uniformly in
i
. For uniformity in
\eta=(\gamma,\lambda)
only the

\gamma
-dependence matters. For the Gaussian kernel,

\partial_{\gamma}k_{\gamma}(x,y)=-\lVert x-y\rVert^{2}k_{\gamma}(x,y)
satisfies

|\partial_{\gamma}k_{\gamma}(x,y)|\leq\sup_{t\geq 0}t\,e^{-\gamma_{L}t}=1/(e\gamma_{L})

for all
\gamma\geq\gamma_{L}
, the substitution
s=\gamma_{L}t
reducing the supremum to

\gamma_{L}^{-1}\sup_{s\geq 0}s\,e^{-s}
, attained at
s=1
; the envelope is recorded for use in the
proof of Lemma
13
below. The
\gamma
-Lipschitz bound for the summands follows from the cross-kernel calculus of Lemma
5
. With the transported unit features

\tilde{u}_{\gamma}(x):=J_{\gamma}k_{\gamma}(x,\cdot)
, which have unit norm in the common
space
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}
by the unit normalization
k_{\gamma}(x,x)=1
of the Gaussian
kernel, the closed form (
17
) gives at
x=y
the inner
product
\langle\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{1}}(x),\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{2}}(x)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}=c_{12}
with

c_{12}=\Bigl(1-\frac{(\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2})^{2}}{(\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2})^{2}}\Bigr)^{d/4}.

For unit vectors
u,v
in a Hilbert space,

\lVert u\otimes u-v\otimes v\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}=\operatorname{tr}\bigl[(u\otimes u-v\otimes v)^{2}\bigr]=2-2\langle u,v\rangle^{2}

by
(u\otimes u)(v\otimes v)=\langle u,v\rangle\,u\otimes v
and unit idempotence,
so at the anchor
X_{j}
,

\bigl\lVert\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{1}}(X_{j})\otimes\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{1}}(X_{j})-\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{2}}(X_{j})\otimes\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{2}}(X_{j})\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}=\bigl(2(1-c_{12}^{2})\bigr)^{1/2}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\max\{\sqrt{2},\sqrt{d}\}\,\gamma_{L}^{-1}\,\lvert\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\rvert,

by
1-c_{12}^{2}=1-(1-s^{2})^{d/2}
with

s:=\lvert\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\rvert/(\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2})
, the elementary inequality

1-(1-u)^{\alpha}\leq\max(1,\alpha)\,u
for
u\in[0,1]

(concavity at
\alpha\leq 1
, Bernoulli at
\alpha\geq 1
) at
u=s^{2}
,
\alpha=d/2
, and

s\leq\lvert\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\rvert/(2\gamma_{L})
. The population term is bounded on
the common space by Jensen’s inequality for the Bochner integral: transported,

C_{\gamma}
is the Bochner integral

\mathbb{E}[\tilde{u}_{\gamma}(X)\otimes\tilde{u}_{\gamma}(X)]
, so

\displaystyle\bigl\lVert J_{\gamma_{1}}C_{\gamma_{1}}J_{\gamma_{1}}^{-1}-J_{\gamma_{2}}C_{\gamma_{2}}J_{\gamma_{2}}^{-1}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl\lVert\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{1}}(X)\otimes\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{1}}(X)-\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{2}}(X)\otimes\tilde{u}_{\gamma_{2}}(X)\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\max\{\sqrt{2},\sqrt{d}\}\,\gamma_{L}^{-1}\,\lvert\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\rvert.

The triangle inequality, with both summands of
\Delta_{j}
transported to the
common space by the isometry
J_{\gamma}
, therefore gives

\lVert\Delta_{j}(\gamma_{1})-\Delta_{j}(\gamma_{2})\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\max\{\sqrt{2},\sqrt{d}\}\,\gamma_{L}^{-1}\,\lvert\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\rvert

with a deterministic constant, while each
\Delta_{j}
retains the a.s. envelope

\lVert\Delta_{j}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\bar{k}
recorded above, so

E_{i}=(m-1)^{-1}\sum_{j\neq i}\Delta_{j}
is Lipschitz in
\gamma
, uniformly in
i
,
with the same a.s. envelope. By the standing net argument,

\sup_{i}\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\lVert E_{i}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2}).

Mean embedding.

Write
\hat{m}:=m^{-1}\sum_{j}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)
and

\hat{m}^{(-i)}=\frac{m}{m-1}\,\hat{m}-\frac{1}{m-1}\,\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}k_{\gamma}(X_{i},\cdot),

so that

\lVert\hat{m}^{(-i)}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}\leq\frac{m}{m-1}\lVert\hat{m}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}+\frac{\bar{k}^{1/2}\max_{j}|\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}|}{m-1}.

Conditioning on
\mathbf{X}
, under the convention of Remark
2
,
write
\boldsymbol{g}_{m}:=\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\mid\mathbf{X}]
for the
\mathbf{X}
-measurable
conditional mean of the true residual and
\boldsymbol{v}:=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}-\boldsymbol{g}_{m}

for the conditionally centered noise, and decompose

\hat{m}=\mathbb{E}[\hat{m}\mid\mathbf{X}]+\bar{m},\qquad\mathbb{E}[\hat{m}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{g}_{m})_{j}\,k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot),\qquad\bar{m}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{v})_{j}\,k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot).

The centered part has conditional covariance

\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{v})(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{v})^{\top}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
with

D:=\operatorname{diag}(\sigma^{2}(X_{1}),\dots,\sigma^{2}(X_{m}))
, the noise being
conditionally independent and centered; expanding in the reproducing kernel and
writing
c_{jk}:=(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})_{jk}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\lVert\bar{m}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{j,k}c_{jk}\,k_{\gamma}(X_{j},X_{k})\leq\frac{\bar{k}}{m^{2}}\sum_{j}c_{jj}+\frac{\bar{k}}{m^{2}}\sum_{j\neq k}|c_{jk}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bar{k}\,\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{m}+\bar{k}\sup_{j\neq k}|c_{jk}|=O_{p}(m^{-1}).

Here the diagonal is bounded by the Loewner chain

D\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\boldsymbol{I}
(Assumption
7
(iii)), hence

\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
by congruence and
idempotence, so that

c_{jj}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{jj}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
; the off-diagonal
entries obey
c_{jk}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly for
j\neq k
, the

c_{jk}
-analysis of Section
4.2
, which is
\eta
-free and
uses only
|k_{\gamma}|\leq\bar{k}
; and

\sum_{j\neq k}|c_{jk}|\leq m(m-1)\sup_{j\neq k}|c_{jk}|
. The conditional second
moment
\mathbb{E}[\lVert\bar{m}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]
is thus
\mathbf{X}
-measurable
and
O_{p}(m^{-1})
, and the conditional Markov inequality applied through the
bounded transform
\min(1,\cdot)
,

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\lVert\bar{m}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}>\varepsilon\bigr)\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\mathbb{E}[\lVert\bar{m}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigr)\Bigr],

together with the boundedness of
\min(1,\cdot)
, which turns the convergence in
probability of the conditional second moment into convergence of its expectation,
gives
\lVert\bar{m}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
. The conditional mean is

\mathbf{X}
-measurable, and by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality

\sum_{j}|a_{j}|\leq m^{1/2}\lVert a\rVert
and

\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
(orthogonal projector),

\lVert\mathbb{E}[\hat{m}\mid\mathbf{X}]\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}\leq\frac{\bar{k}^{1/2}}{m}\sum_{j}\bigl|(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{g}_{m})_{j}\bigr|\leq\bar{k}^{1/2}m^{-1/2}\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{g}_{m}\rVert\leq\bar{k}^{1/2}m^{-1/2}\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{m}\rVert,

which is
0
under
H_{0}
,
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
under a local alternative with
signal
O(m^{-1/2})
, and
O_{p}(1)
under a fixed alternative, since

\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{m}\rVert
is
0
,
O_{p}(1)
, and
O_{p}(m^{1/2})
in the three
regimes by the law of large
numbers, the embedding being then of the order of the population object

m_{\Pi f}:=\mathbb{E}[(\Pi f)(X)k_{\gamma}(X,\cdot)]
. Hence

\lVert\hat{m}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
in the null and local regimes,
and
O_{p}(1)
under a fixed alternative.
For the leftover term, the trivial bound

\max_{j}|\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}|\leq\bigl(\sum_{j}(\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0})^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}=O_{p}(m^{1/2})

(no moments beyond those already assumed) puts the second term in the display for
\lVert\hat{m}^{(-i)}\rVert
at
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
, of the same order as the leading term and therefore absorbed by the triangle inequality, and

\lVert\hat{m}^{(-i)}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})\ \text{uniformly in $i$}

in the null and local regimes, and
O_{p}(1)
under a fixed alternative;

\eta
-uniformity holds by the standing net argument (the pointwise bound uses only
\lVert k_{\gamma}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\bar{k}
and the
\eta
-free
c_{jk}
analysis).

Resolvent and evaluation at
X_{i}
.

\lVert(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}
,

\lVert(C_{\gamma}^{(-i)}+\lambda I)^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}
,
so for
\mathsf{R}_{i}
defined above

\lVert\mathsf{R}_{i}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\lVert E_{i}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})

uniformly in
i,\eta
, and

\lVert\mathsf{R}_{i}\hat{m}^{(-i)}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=O_{p}(m^{-1})\ \text{(null/local)},\quad O_{p}(m^{-1/2})\ \text{(fixed)},

uniformly in
i,\eta
. The remainder at
X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}}
is a reproducing evaluation:
for
f=\mathsf{R}_{i}\hat{m}^{(-i)}
,

|r_{i}|=|f(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}})|=|\langle k_{\gamma}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot),f\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}|\leq\lVert k_{\gamma}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},\cdot)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}\leq\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert\mathsf{R}_{i}\hat{m}^{(-i)}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}},

hence

|r_{i}|\leq\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert\mathsf{R}_{i}\hat{m}^{(-i)}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=\begin{cases}O_{p}(m^{-1}),&\text{null and local alternatives},\\[2.0pt]
O_{p}(m^{-1/2}),&\text{fixed alternative},\end{cases}

uniformly in
i,\eta
, with constants depending only on

(\bar{k},\lambda_{L},\sigma_{\max})
and the constants of the
c_{jk}
-orders of
Section
4.2
. In particular the leading
g_{\eta}
-average

L_{i}
is
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
uniformly, while
r_{i}
is an order smaller in
the null and local regimes. This proves the lemma.

Lemma 13
(Leave-one-out criterion decomposition)

Adopt the setting and notation of Lemma
12
, with the linearized predictor
L_{i}
and its remainder
r_{i}
as defined there. Under
H_{0}
and local alternatives the remainders
r_{i}
contribute
o_{p}(m^{-1})
, and expanding the square of the leave-one-out error gives, uniformly in
\eta
,

\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)=\underbrace{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}}_{(\mathrm{I})}\;-\;\underbrace{2\,U_{m}(\eta)}_{(\mathrm{II})}\;+\;\underbrace{Q_{m}(\eta)}_{(\mathrm{III})}\;+\;\underbrace{R_{m}(\eta)}_{(\mathrm{IV})}\;+\;o_{p}(m^{-1}),

with the off-diagonal products of the cross term

U_{m}(\eta):=\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}}),

and the diagonal and off-diagonal contributions of the squared prediction,

Q_{m}(\eta):=\frac{1}{m(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}(\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0})^{2}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}})^{2},

and

R_{m}(\eta):=\frac{2}{m(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j<k\\
j,k\neq i\end{subarray}}g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}})\,g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{k}^{\mathrm{sel}})\,\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,k}^{0};

in
R_{m}
each unordered pair
\{j,k\}
is counted once, the factor
2
coming from the two orders of the double sum. Under a fixed alternative the same decomposition holds at the weaker rate; the proof of Theorem
6
(b) applies its algebra once to the full orthogonalized residual and closes the remainder at
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
uniformly in
\eta
, through the fixed-alternative clause of Lemma
12
.

Proof.
The leave-one-out CV criterion is

\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)=m^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}-\hat{w}^{(-i)}_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}}))^{2}
,
i.e. with
L_{i}
and
r_{i}
as in Lemma
12
,

\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}-L_{i}-r_{i})^{2}.

Expanding the square algebraically,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}-L_{i}-r_{i})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}-\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}L_{i}+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}L_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}-L_{i})r_{i}+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}r_{i}^{2}.

The three
\eta
-dependent sums identify
U_{m}
together with the diagonal and off-diagonal parts
of the
L_{i}^{2}
average that define
Q_{m}
and
R_{m}
. Indeed,

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}L_{i}=\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}})=:U_{m}(\eta),

by definition of
L_{i}
. Moreover

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}L_{i}^{2}=\frac{1}{m(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j\neq i}\sum_{k\neq i}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,k}^{0}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}})g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{k}^{\mathrm{sel}}).

Split the double sum over
j,k\neq i
into the diagonal
j=k
and the
off-diagonal
j\neq k
:

\sum_{j\neq i}\sum_{k\neq i}\!\!=\!\!\sum_{j\neq i}\!(\,j=k\,)\;+\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\neq i,k\neq i\\
j\neq k\end{subarray}},

so that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}L_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{m(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}(\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0})^{2}g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}})^{2}}_{=:Q_{m}(\eta)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{m(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\neq i,k\neq i\\
j\neq k\end{subarray}}g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}})g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{k}^{\mathrm{sel}})\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,k}^{0}}_{\text{ordered }j\neq k}.

In the last ordered sum each unordered pair
\{j,k\}
with
j<k
appears
twice (
g_{ij}g_{ik}=g_{ik}g_{ij}
), whence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\neq i,k\neq i\\
j\neq k\end{subarray}}g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}})g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{k}^{\mathrm{sel}})\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,k}^{0}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2}{m(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j<k\\
j,k\neq i\end{subarray}}g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}})g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{k}^{\mathrm{sel}})\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,k}^{0}=:R_{m}(\eta),

which is exactly the definition of
R_{m}(\eta)
in the statement.
Collecting,

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}-\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}L_{i}+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}L_{i}^{2}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}-2U_{m}(\eta)+Q_{m}(\eta)+R_{m}(\eta)

as an identity, and therefore

\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}-2U_{m}(\eta)+Q_{m}(\eta)+R_{m}(\eta)-\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}-L_{i})r_{i}+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}r_{i}^{2}.

The last two
r_{i}
-terms are the only remainder.

CV remainder:
r_{i}^{2}
and the cross term.

Moreover,
m^{-1}\sum_{i}r_{i}^{2}=O_{p}(m^{-2})=o_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly in

\eta
in the null/local regimes (it is
O_{p}(m^{-1})
under a fixed alternative).
It remains to show the cross term is
o_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly in
\eta

in the null/local regimes. Write the algebraic identity for the remainder
from the proof of Lemma
12
:

r_{i}=\langle k_{\gamma}(X_{i},\cdot),\mathsf{R}_{i}\hat{m}^{(-i)}\rangle=(m-1)^{-1}\sum_{j\neq i}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}h_{ij}
with

h_{ij}:=\big\langle k_{\gamma}(X_{i},\cdot),\mathsf{R}_{i}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)\big\rangle,\qquad|h_{ij}|\leq\bar{k}\,\lVert\mathsf{R}_{i}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})

uniformly in
i,j,\eta
. Both
(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}-L_{i})
and
r_{i}
are
\mathbf{X}
-measurable
linear forms in
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}
; write them

u_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}
and

v_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}
with the design-measurable
coefficient rows

u_{i,l}:=\delta_{il}-\frac{1}{m-1}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{l})\,\mathbf{1}\{l\neq i\},\qquad v_{i,l}:=\frac{1}{m-1}\,h_{il}\,\mathbf{1}\{l\neq i\},

where
\delta_{il}
is the Kronecker delta; equivalently

u_{i}=e_{i}-(m-1)^{-1}(g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{l}))_{l\neq i}
with
e_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}

the
i
-th canonical basis vector, to be distinguished from the population
eigenfunctions
e_{i}=\sqrt{\mu_{i}}\phi_{i}
of
Section
2.1
, which do not appear here. Write the

L_{i}
-factor as
s_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}
with

s_{i,l}:=(m-1)^{-1}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{l})\mathbf{1}\{l\neq i\}
. Two structural
facts drive the orders below:
v_{i,i}=0
, since
r_{i}
carries no coefficient
on the excluded observation, and the envelopes

\sum_{l}|u_{i,l}|\leq 1+\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}=O(1),\qquad\sum_{l}|s_{i,l}|\leq\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}=O(1),\qquad\sum_{l}|v_{i,l}|=O_{p}(m^{-1/2}),

together with
|v_{i,l}|=O_{p}(m^{-3/2})
and the square sums

\sum_{l\neq i}g_{il}^{2}\leq(m-1)\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}=O(m)
and

\sum_{l\neq i}h_{il}^{2}\leq(m-1)\bar{k}^{2}\lVert\mathsf{R}_{i}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}=O_{p}(1)
,
all uniformly in
i,\eta
.

Since
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{0}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
with
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
symmetric and
\mathbf{X}
-measurable, and the innovations are conditionally independent and centred with
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\boldsymbol{0}
and
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}\mid\mathbf{X}]=D
, each conditional expectation reduces in closed form to the second-order pairing

\mathbb{E}\bigl[(u^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{0})(v^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{0})\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=u^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\,D\,\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\,v=\sum_{l}u_{l}v_{l}\,c_{ll}+\sum_{l\neq l^{\prime}}u_{l}v_{l^{\prime}}\,c_{ll^{\prime}},

the expansion recorded in Section
4.2
giving
c_{ll}:=(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})_{ll}=\sigma^{2}(X_{l})+O_{p}(m^{-1})
on the diagonal and
c_{ll^{\prime}}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
for
l\neq l^{\prime}
: variance pairings
\sigma^{2}(X_{l})
on matched indices and covariance pairings
c_{ll^{\prime}}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
otherwise. Under a local alternative at the total-sample rate (Section
4.1
),
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}=R/\sqrt{m+n}+\boldsymbol{v}
, the moment matrix gains
RR^{\top}/(m+n)
, and summing over
i
with the prefactor gives

\frac{2}{m(m+n)}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}R)^{\top}\Bigl(\sum_{i}u_{i}v_{i}^{\top}\Bigr)\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}R,

with
\bigl\lVert\sum_{i}u_{i}v_{i}^{\top}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\,m^{-1/2}
: the
e_{i}
-anchored part
(m-1)^{-1}H
,
H:=(h_{ij})_{i,j}
, has row sums
O_{p}(m^{1/2})
by
|h_{ij}|=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
, and the remainder
(m-1)^{-2}\sum_{i}(g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{l}))_{l\neq i}(h_{il^{\prime}})_{l^{\prime}\neq i}^{\top}
has Hilbert–Schmidt norm at most
(m-1)^{-2}\lVert G\rVert_{F}\lVert H\rVert_{F}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
, from
|g_{\eta}|\leq\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}
(giving
\lVert G\rVert_{F}=O(m)
) and
\lVert H\rVert_{F}^{2}=O_{p}(m)
; while
\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}R\rVert_{2}=O_{p}(m^{1/2})
. The contribution is therefore
O_{p}\bigl(m^{-1/2}/(m+n)\bigr)\leq O_{p}(m^{-3/2})
, below the pairing orders; the stated orders are unchanged.

The conditional mean.
Instantiating the pairing identity at the row pairs
(u_{i},v_{i})
and
(s_{i},v_{i})
:

The
\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}r_{i}
pairing.
The diagonal sum vanishes exactly,
u_{i,i}v_{i,i}c_{ii}=v_{i,i}c_{ii}=0
by
v_{i,i}=0
, so only the off-diagonal pairings survive:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}r_{i}\mid\mathbf{X}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\neq i}v_{i,j}c_{ij}=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{j\neq i}h_{ij}c_{ij},

\displaystyle\bigl|\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}r_{i}\mid\mathbf{X}]\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\neq i}|h_{ij}|\,\sup_{j\neq i}|c_{ij}|=O_{p}(m^{-3/2}),

uniformly in
i
: the pairing is a pure off-diagonal covariance, nonzero only through the projection-induced correlations
c_{ij}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
.

The
L_{i}r_{i}
pairing.
Both parts of the identity contribute:

\mathbb{E}[L_{i}r_{i}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{l\neq i}g_{il}h_{il}c_{ll}+\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l\neq l^{\prime}\\
l,l^{\prime}\neq i\end{subarray}}g_{il}h_{il^{\prime}}c_{ll^{\prime}}.

The diagonal part is bounded by Cauchy–Schwarz, and the off-diagonal part by the product of the row sums:

\Bigl|\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{l\neq i}g_{il}h_{il}c_{ll}\Bigr|\leq\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{(m-1)^{2}}\Bigl(\sum_{l\neq i}g_{il}^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\sum_{l\neq i}h_{il}^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}=O_{p}(m^{-3/2}),

(the
O_{p}(m^{-1})
correction of
c_{ll}
contributing the smaller order
O_{p}(m^{-5/2})
), and

\Bigl|\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l\neq l^{\prime}\\
l,l^{\prime}\neq i\end{subarray}}g_{il}h_{il^{\prime}}c_{ll^{\prime}}\Bigr|\leq\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\,\sup_{l\neq l^{\prime}}|c_{ll^{\prime}}|\,\Bigl(\sum_{l}|g_{il}|\Bigr)\Bigl(\sum_{l^{\prime}}|h_{il^{\prime}}|\Bigr)=O_{p}(m^{-3/2}),

using
\sum_{l}|g_{il}|\leq(m-1)\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}=O(m)
and
\sum_{l^{\prime}}|h_{il^{\prime}}|=O_{p}(m^{1/2})
. All per-
i
bounds are uniform in
i
, so summing over
i
contributes the factor
m
, and the prefactor
2/m
cancels it:

\Bigl|\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}r_{i}\mid\mathbf{X}]\Bigr|+\Bigl|\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[L_{i}r_{i}\mid\mathbf{X}]\Bigr|=O_{p}(m^{-3/2}).

The fluctuation.
Conditional on
\mathbf{X}
, write
w:=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
for the innovations and
\hat{u}_{i}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}u_{i}
,
\hat{v}_{i}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}v_{i}
for the projected coefficient rows, so that
\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}-L_{i}=\hat{u}_{i}^{\top}w
and
r_{i}=\hat{v}_{i}^{\top}w
; the cross term is the quadratic form

\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}-L_{i})r_{i}=w^{\top}B\,w,\qquad B:=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bigl(\hat{u}_{i}\hat{v}_{i}^{\top}+\hat{v}_{i}\hat{u}_{i}^{\top}\bigr).

The key structural fact is the entrywise bound, uniform in
j,j^{\prime}
,

B_{jj^{\prime}}=O_{p}(m^{-5/2}),

obtained by tracking the anchor of the
u
-sum,
u_{j,j}=1
being its only
O(1)
entry:

\sum_{i}u_{i,j}v_{i,j^{\prime}}=v_{j,j^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j\\
i\neq j^{\prime}\end{subarray}}g_{ij}h_{ij^{\prime}}=O_{p}(m^{-3/2})+O_{p}(m^{-3/2}),

with
v_{j,j^{\prime}}=(m-1)^{-1}h_{jj^{\prime}}\,\mathbf{1}\{j^{\prime}\neq j\}
and
\bigl|\sum_{i\neq j,\,i\neq j^{\prime}}g_{ij}h_{ij^{\prime}}\bigr|\leq\bigl(\sum_{i}|g_{ij}|\bigr)\sup_{i}|h_{ij^{\prime}}|=O_{p}(m^{1/2})
, the sign being immaterial to the magnitude; the symmetric partner is identical. The projection corrections enter at the same order: the entries of
\hat{\mathbb{G}}M\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}
, with
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
the sieve-estimated score design matrix and
M:=(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}})^{-1}
so that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}=\boldsymbol{I}-\hat{\mathbb{G}}M\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}
(Section
4.2
), are
O_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly, with row sums
\sum_{l}|(\hat{\mathbb{G}}M\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top})_{jl}|=O_{p}(1)
(the off-diagonal order recorded in Section
4.2
together with
\sum_{a}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ja}^{2}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{jj}\leq 1
), so
|\hat{u}_{i,j}-u_{i,j}|=O_{p}(m^{-1})
contributes

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}|\hat{u}_{i,j}-u_{i,j}|\,|v_{i,j^{\prime}}|=O_{p}(m^{-5/2}),

while the
v
-corrections aggregate through the anchored inner sums just displayed,
\sum_{i}u_{i,j}v_{i,l}=O_{p}(m^{-3/2})
uniformly in
(j,l)
:

\Bigl|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}u_{i,j}(\hat{v}_{i,j^{\prime}}-v_{i,j^{\prime}})\Bigr|=\frac{1}{m}\Bigl|\sum_{l}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}M\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top})_{j^{\prime}l}\,\sum_{i}u_{i,j}v_{i,l}\Bigr|=O_{p}(m^{-5/2}).

Consequently
\lVert B\rVert_{F}^{2}=O_{p}(m^{-3})
with
\mathbb{E}\lVert B\rVert_{F}^{2}=O(m^{-3})
, the constants entering through
\mathbb{E}\lVert\mathsf{R}_{i}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-4}\,\mathbb{E}\lVert E_{i}\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}=O(m^{-1})
as in the proof of Lemma
12
, and (
29
) of Lemma
14
, applied to the conditionally independent centered innovations
w
(for which
d_{j}=\sigma^{2}(X_{j})\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
and
\sup_{j}\mathbb{E}[w_{j}^{2p}\mid X_{j}]\leq\bar{M}_{2p}
by Assumption
7
(iii),(v)), gives

\operatorname{Var}\Bigl(\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}-L_{i})r_{i}\mid\mathbf{X}\Bigr)\leq\bigl(2\sigma_{\max}^{4}+\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p}\bigr)\lVert B\rVert_{F}^{2}=O_{p}(m^{-3}).

This is the degenerate second-order structure: the first-order Hoeffding projection of the kernel
(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}-L_{i})r_{i}
vanishes because both factors are conditionally centered linear forms with
r_{i}
carrying no coefficient on
w_{i}
, so the fluctuation is governed by the second-order pairing term
\lVert B\rVert_{F}^{2}
rather than by a first-order term of larger order. Chebyshev’s inequality with the unconditional moment bound
\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(\,\cdot\mid\mathbf{X})]=O(m^{-3})
gives fluctuation
O_{p}(m^{-3/2})
; the same holds for the centered part under a local alternative, while its non-centered mean is already covered above.
Thus the cross term is
O_{p}(m^{-3/2})=o_{p}(m^{-1})
pointwise in
\eta
.
Both the conditional-mean bounds and the

\operatorname{Var}(\,\cdot\mid\mathbf{X})=O_{p}(m^{-3})
bound are Lipschitz in

\eta
via
\partial_{\gamma}k_{\gamma}
(the envelope recorded in the proof of
Lemma
12
) and the resolvent Lipschitz
Lemma
10
(c) for
h_{ij}(\eta)
and
g_{\eta}

(
\lVert g_{\eta_{1}}-g_{\eta_{2}}\rVert_{\infty}\leq L_{g}\lVert\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\rVert
),
with envelope
2\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}+2\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-2}L_{C}\operatorname{diam}(\Upsilon)
,

L_{C}
as in Lemma
10
(b);
hence the standing net argument upgrades the bound to

\sup_{\eta}O_{p}(m^{-3/2})=o_{p}(m^{-1}).

Consequently

\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}-2U_{m}(\eta)+Q_{m}(\eta)+R_{m}(\eta)+o_{p}(m^{-1})

uniformly in
\eta
, which is (I)–(IV) of the statement with
o_{p}(m^{-1})
remainder
in the null and local regimes. Under a fixed alternative the same
algebra holds with
r_{i}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
, giving only an

O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
remainder.

Lemma 14
(Conditional variance of bilinear and quadratic forms)

Let
(x_{l},y_{l})_{l\leq m}
be conditionally independent pairs given
\mathbf{X}
with
\mathbb{E}[x_{l}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\mathbb{E}[y_{l}\mid\mathbf{X}]=0
,
\mathbb{E}[x_{l}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]=s_{l}^{2}
,
\mathbb{E}[y_{l}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]=t_{l}^{2}
,
\mathbb{E}[x_{l}y_{l}\mid\mathbf{X}]=n_{l}
, and let
B
be design-measurable. Then

\mathbb{E}\bigl[x^{\top}By\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=\sum_{l=1}^{m}B_{ll}\,n_{l},

(26)

and

\operatorname{Var}\bigl(x^{\top}By\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)=\sum_{l\neq k}B_{lk}^{2}s_{l}^{2}t_{k}^{2}+\sum_{l\neq k}B_{lk}B_{kl}\,n_{l}n_{k}+\sum_{l=1}^{m}B_{ll}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[(x_{l}y_{l}-n_{l})^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr],

(27)

the four-equal family being the variance of
x_{l}y_{l}
around its conditional mean
n_{l}
, not a difference of factorized moments. The sibling of (
27
) for quadratic forms is classical: for conditionally independent centered
\zeta_{k}
with
\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]=d_{k}
and symmetric
A
,

\operatorname{Var}\bigl(\zeta^{\top}A\zeta\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)=2\bigl\lVert D_{d}^{1/2}AD_{d}^{1/2}\bigr\rVert_{F}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}A_{kk}^{2}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k}^{4}\mid\mathbf{X}]-3d_{k}^{2}\bigr),

(28)

D_{d}:=\operatorname{diag}(d_{1},\dots,d_{m})
. Moreover, if

\max_{k}d_{k}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
and

\sup_{k}\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k}^{2p}\mid\mathbf{X}]\leq\bar{M}_{2p}
for some
p\in(2,4]
,
then

\operatorname{Var}\bigl(\zeta^{\top}A\zeta\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)\leq\bigl(2\sigma_{\max}^{4}+\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p}\bigr)\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(29)

for every symmetric
\mathbf{X}
-measurable
A
.

Proof.
Write
\mu_{j}:=\mathbb{E}[x_{j}^{2}y_{j}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]
. Throughout the proof, conditional independence across observations factorizes every conditional expectation over the multiset of observation indices appearing in the monomial, and an index appearing exactly once contributes the factor
\mathbb{E}[x_{j}\mid\mathbf{X}]=0
or
\mathbb{E}[y_{j}\mid\mathbf{X}]=0
, so the monomial vanishes.

For (
26
), expand over the entries of
B
and apply the factorization with
\mathbb{E}[x_{l}y_{l}\mid\mathbf{X}]=n_{l}
:

\mathbb{E}\bigl[x^{\top}By\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{k=1}^{m}B_{lk}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[x_{l}y_{k}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=\sum_{l=1}^{m}B_{ll}\,n_{l},

the off-diagonal terms vanishing because for
l\neq k
the variables
x_{l}
and
y_{k}
are conditionally independent with zero conditional means.

For (
27
), expand the square over four indices,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[(x^{\top}By)^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{p=1}^{m}\sum_{q=1}^{m}B_{lk}B_{pq}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[x_{l}y_{k}x_{p}y_{q}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr],

and classify the quadruples
(l,k,p,q)
by the multiplicities of the observation indices in the monomial
x_{l}y_{k}x_{p}y_{q}
. A quadruple in which some index appears exactly once vanishes by the factorization rule above. The surviving quadruples either place all four slots at one observation or place two slots at each of two observations
j\neq j^{\prime}
, and contribute as follows. The all-equal quadruple
(j,j,j,j)
contributes
B_{jj}^{2}\mu_{j}
. The split quadruples
(j,j^{\prime},j,j^{\prime})
and
(j^{\prime},j,j^{\prime},j)
, which place both
x
-slots at one observation and both
y
-slots at the other, contribute
B_{jj^{\prime}}^{2}s_{j}^{2}t_{j^{\prime}}^{2}
and
B_{j^{\prime}j}^{2}s_{j^{\prime}}^{2}t_{j}^{2}
, the two orientations of the split double sum. The crossed quadruples
(j,j^{\prime},j^{\prime},j)
and
(j^{\prime},j,j,j^{\prime})
contribute
B_{jj^{\prime}}B_{j^{\prime}j}n_{j}n_{j^{\prime}}
each, since
x_{j}
shares its index with
y_{j}
and
x_{j^{\prime}}
with
y_{j^{\prime}}
. The mixed quadruples
(j,j,j^{\prime},j^{\prime})
and
(j^{\prime},j^{\prime},j,j)
, which pair the slots within each of the two factors
x^{\top}By
separately, contribute
B_{jj}B_{j^{\prime}j^{\prime}}n_{j}n_{j^{\prime}}
each. Each of the crossed and mixed families contributes twice per unordered pair
\{j,j^{\prime}\}
with a summand invariant under
j\leftrightarrow j^{\prime}
, so its total
2\sum_{j<j^{\prime}}
equals the ordered double sum
\sum_{l\neq k}
. Collecting,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[(x^{\top}By)^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=\sum_{j}B_{jj}^{2}\mu_{j}+\sum_{l\neq k}B_{lk}^{2}s_{l}^{2}t_{k}^{2}+\sum_{l\neq k}B_{lk}B_{kl}\,n_{l}n_{k}+\sum_{l\neq k}B_{ll}B_{kk}\,n_{l}n_{k}.

Squaring (
26
),

\Bigl(\sum_{l}B_{ll}n_{l}\Bigr)^{2}=\sum_{j}B_{jj}^{2}n_{j}^{2}+\sum_{l\neq k}B_{ll}B_{kk}\,n_{l}n_{k},

so the mixed family cancels against the square of the mean, and on the diagonal, by
\mathbb{E}[x_{j}y_{j}\mid\mathbf{X}]=n_{j}
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[(x_{j}y_{j}-n_{j})^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=\mu_{j}-2n_{j}\,\mathbb{E}[x_{j}y_{j}\mid\mathbf{X}]+n_{j}^{2}=\mu_{j}-n_{j}^{2},

which turns the all-equal family into
\sum_{j}B_{jj}^{2}\mathbb{E}[(x_{j}y_{j}-n_{j})^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]
and gives (
27
).

For (
28
), specialize (
27
) to
x=y=\boldsymbol{\zeta}
and

B=A
: the pairs are conditionally independent and centered given
\mathbf{X}
with

s_{l}^{2}=t_{l}^{2}=n_{l}=d_{l}
, the second family coincides with the first by
the symmetry of
A
,
A_{lk}A_{kl}=A_{lk}^{2}
, and the four-equal entry is

\mathbb{E}[(\zeta_{l}^{2}-d_{l})^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\mu_{l}-d_{l}^{2}
with

\mu_{l}=\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{l}^{4}\mid\mathbf{X}]
as above, so

\operatorname{Var}\bigl(\zeta^{\top}A\zeta\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)=2\sum_{l\neq k}A_{lk}^{2}d_{l}d_{k}+\sum_{l}A_{ll}^{2}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{l}^{4}\mid\mathbf{X}]-d_{l}^{2}\bigr).

Adding and subtracting
2\sum_{l}A_{ll}^{2}d_{l}^{2}
and using

\lVert D_{d}^{1/2}AD_{d}^{1/2}\rVert_{F}^{2}=\sum_{l,k}A_{lk}^{2}d_{l}d_{k}

gives

\operatorname{Var}\bigl(\zeta^{\top}A\zeta\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)=2\bigl\lVert D_{d}^{1/2}AD_{d}^{1/2}\bigr\rVert_{F}^{2}+\sum_{l}A_{ll}^{2}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{l}^{4}\mid\mathbf{X}]-3d_{l}^{2}\bigr),

which is (
28
). For (
29
), the first family of
(
28
) is bounded entrywise,

\lVert D_{d}^{1/2}AD_{d}^{1/2}\rVert_{F}^{2}=\sum_{l,k}A_{lk}^{2}d_{l}d_{k}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{4}\lVert A\rVert_{F}^{2}
by

\max_{k}d_{k}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
, and the diagonal family by Lyapunov norm
monotonicity at
q=4\leq 2p
,

\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k}^{4}\mid\mathbf{X}]\leq\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k}^{2p}\mid\mathbf{X}]\bigr)^{2/p}\leq\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p}
, so that, dropping the nonpositive
-3d_{k}^{2}
and using

\sum_{k}A_{kk}^{2}\leq\lVert A\rVert_{F}^{2}
,

\sum_{k}A_{kk}^{2}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k}^{4}\mid\mathbf{X}]-3d_{k}^{2}\bigr)\leq\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p}\sum_{k}A_{kk}^{2}\leq\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p}\lVert A\rVert_{F}^{2};

summing the two families gives (
29
).

Lemma 15
(Replacement cost of the feasible residuals)

Let Assumptions
1
–
6
hold, with Assumption
4
(iii) and the rate condition of Assumption
6
(iii) holding at the selection sample of size
m
, and let Assumptions
7
(iii) and
7
(v) hold. Suppose the null premise that the true residual is conditionally centered,
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\boldsymbol{0}
, as holds under
H_{0}
, where
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
is the noise
\boldsymbol{v}
; and the joint moment bound
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\,\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\lVert\nabla_{\theta}^{2}\varepsilon(S;\theta)\rVert^{2}]<\infty
, automatic in the conditional-mean case by Assumptions
5
(iii) and
7
(iii). Let
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{0}_{\perp}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\perp}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
denote the infeasible and feasible orthogonalized residuals on the selection sample. Then, for

\mathrm{CV}^{\mathrm{dep}}_{m}(\eta;v):=-2U_{m}(\eta;v)+Q_{m}(\eta;v)+R_{m}(\eta;v)
,
the
\eta
-dependent part of the leave-one-out criterion evaluated at a residual vector
v
, namely the statistics
U_{m}
,
Q_{m}
,
R_{m}
of Lemma
13
with the coefficient vector
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{0}
replaced by
v
,

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|m\,\mathrm{CV}^{\mathrm{dep}}_{m}(\eta;\hat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\perp})-m\,\mathrm{CV}^{\mathrm{dep}}_{m}(\eta;\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{0}_{\perp})\bigr|=O_{p}\bigl(m^{-\varrho}+m^{1/2-2\varrho}+m^{1-4\varrho}\bigr)=o_{p}(1),

the three rates being the idiosyncratic-linear, smooth-second-order, and quadratic contributions. When the score is
X
-measurable the idiosyncratic increment of the proof is absent and the remainder is
O_{p}(m^{1/2-2\varrho}+m^{-2\varrho}+m^{1-4\varrho})
; rates faster than
o_{p}(1)
require additional structure that removes the smooth conditional mean of the second-order term, as in the location model where
\boldsymbol{r}^{(2)}\equiv\boldsymbol{0}
.

Proof.
All bounds are uniform in
\eta\in\Upsilon
: only the envelopes
\lVert g_{\eta}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}
and
\lVert g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}
enter, obtained from
g_{\eta}(x,x)\leq\lambda^{-1}k_{\gamma}(x,x)=\lambda^{-1}
and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality
|g_{\eta}(x,y)|\leq\sqrt{g_{\eta}(x,x)g_{\eta}(y,y)}
, together with
|(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(x,y)|\leq\mathbb{E}_{Z}[g_{\eta}(x,Z)g_{\eta}(Z,y)]
and
|r_{\eta}|\leq 2\lambda_{L}^{-2}
; consequently every matrix
\Psi
with
\lVert\Psi\rVert_{\max}\leq\bar{\psi}
obeys
\lVert\Psi v\rVert\leq\lVert\Psi\rVert_{F}\lVert v\rVert\leq m\bar{\psi}\,\lVert v\rVert
together with the entrywise bound
\lVert\Psi v\rVert_{\infty}\leq\bar{\psi}\lVert v\rVert_{1}
. Conditional moments given
\mathbf{X}
treat
\hat{\theta}
, the fitted sieve, and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
as conditionally deterministic, under the convention of Remark
2
, valid here since
\hat{\theta}
is a smooth functional of sample averages; the
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
-dependence of these objects contributes only the higher-order corrections already recorded in Section
4.2
and in the proof of Lemma
17
.

Structural facts.

Let
T:=\boldsymbol{I}-P_{Z}
, idempotent and symmetric with
|T_{jk}|\leq\mathbf{1}_{j=k}+Cm^{-1}
(bounded sieve basis and
\lambda_{\min}(Z^{\top}Z/m)\geq c
, Assumption
6
(ii)), and recall the recorded off-diagonal order
\max_{a\neq b}|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ab}|=O_{p}(m^{-1})
of Section
4.2
. Three facts are used throughout. First, the row sums

\max_{l}\sum_{i}\bigl|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{il}\bigr|=O_{p}(1),\qquad\max_{j}\sum_{i}|T_{ij}|=O(1),

by idempotence (
\sum_{i}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{il}^{2}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ll}\leq 1
,
\sum_{i}T_{ij}^{2}=T_{jj}\leq 1
) together with the off-diagonal orders across
m
terms. Second, diagonal dominance: any matrix
M
with
M_{jj}=O_{p}(1)
and
M_{jk}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
for
j\neq k
obeys
\bigl|\sum_{i,j}\psi_{ij}M_{ij}\bigr|=O_{p}(\bar{\psi}m)
for every
\Psi
with
\lVert\Psi\rVert_{\max}\leq\bar{\psi}
, by
\bigl|\sum_{i,j}\psi_{ij}M_{ij}\bigr|\leq\bar{\psi}\sum_{j}|M_{jj}|+\bar{\psi}\sum_{i\neq j}|M_{ij}|
. Third, with
V_{j}:=\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0,j}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0,j}^{\top}\mid X_{j}]
and
\boldsymbol{v}_{j}:=\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,j}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0,j}\mid X_{j}]
, the envelopes
\mathbb{E}\lVert V_{j}\rVert<\infty
,
\mathbb{E}\lVert V_{j}\rVert^{2}<\infty
and
\mathbb{E}\lVert\boldsymbol{v}_{j}\rVert<\infty
follow from Assumptions
5
(iii) and
7
(iii) by Jensen and Cauchy–Schwarz against the fourth-moment envelope of the raw score, so by Markov’s inequality

\tfrac{1}{m}\sum_{l}\lVert V_{l}\rVert=O_{p}(1),\qquad\tfrac{1}{m}\sum_{l}\lVert V_{l}\rVert^{2}=O_{p}(1),\qquad\tfrac{1}{m}\sum_{l}\lVert\boldsymbol{v}_{l}\rVert=O_{p}(1).

Consequently, for
B:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\Psi T
,

\displaystyle|B_{ll}|

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\psi}\Bigl(\sum_{i}|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{li}|\Bigr)\Bigl(\sum_{j}|T_{jl}|\Bigr)\leq\bar{\psi}\,O_{p}(1),\qquad\sum_{l}B_{lk}^{2}=\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\Psi Te_{k}\rVert^{2}\leq\lVert\Psi Te_{k}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq m\bar{\psi}^{2}\,\Bigl(\max_{j}\sum_{i}|T_{ij}|\Bigr)^{2}=O(m\bar{\psi}^{2})

uniformly in
k
, the last bound entrywise; this column-sum refinement replaces the Frobenius bound
\lVert B\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq m^{2}\bar{\psi}^{2}
, which would inflate every order below by a factor
m
. Fourth, the conditional second moments of the infeasible orthogonalized residuals: under the null premise
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}=\boldsymbol{v}
and the convention of Remark
2
,

c_{ij}:=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\varepsilon^{0}_{\perp,i}\varepsilon^{0}_{\perp,j}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})_{ij},\qquad D:=\operatorname{diag}\bigl(\sigma^{2}(X_{1}),\dots,\sigma^{2}(X_{m})\bigr),

and
D\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\boldsymbol{I}
in Loewner order (Assumption
7
(iii))
implies by congruence and idempotence

\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
, whence the diagonal

c_{jj}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{jj}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2},

while for
i\neq j
, using
|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ii}|\leq 1
(idempotence) and

|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ab}|\leq\max_{a\neq b}|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ab}|
off the diagonal,

|c_{ij}|\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\Bigl(|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ii}|\,|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ij}|+\max_{a\neq b}|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ab}|\sum_{a\neq i}|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{aj}|\Bigr)\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\max_{a\neq b}|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ab}|\Bigl(1+\sum_{a}|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{aj}|\Bigr)=O_{p}(m^{-1}),

every product carrying an off-diagonal entry (the factor
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ij}
at

a=i
, the factor
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ia}
otherwise), the row sums being
O_{p}(1)
by
the first fact.

The increment and its split.

Write
\boldsymbol{\delta}:=\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}
and expand the feasible residuals at the selection sample exactly as in the proof of Lemma
3
,
\hat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}+\mathbb{G}\boldsymbol{\delta}+\boldsymbol{r}^{(2)}
with
\mathbb{G}
the raw-score matrix (rows
\nabla_{\theta}\varepsilon(s_{j};\hat{\theta})^{\top}
) and
\boldsymbol{r}^{(2)}=O_{p}(m^{-2\varrho})
element-wise. The identities
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\hat{\mathbb{G}}=\boldsymbol{0}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\boldsymbol{I}-P_{Z})=(\boldsymbol{I}-P_{Z})
of that proof give, for the increment
d:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\perp}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{0}_{\perp}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\mathbb{G}\boldsymbol{\delta}+\boldsymbol{r}^{(2)})
,

d=(\boldsymbol{I}-P_{Z})\,\mathbb{G}\,\boldsymbol{\delta}+\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{r}^{(2)}=a+b+c,

with

a:=(\boldsymbol{I}-P_{Z})\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}\boldsymbol{\delta},\qquad b:=(\boldsymbol{I}-P_{Z})\boldsymbol{g}\boldsymbol{\delta},\qquad c:=(\boldsymbol{I}-P_{Z})\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}\boldsymbol{\delta}\boldsymbol{\delta}+\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{r}^{(2)},

where
\mathbb{G}=\boldsymbol{g}+\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}+\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}\boldsymbol{\delta}
is the decomposition into conditional score, conditionally centered idiosyncratic score, and path-averaged Hessian rows used in that proof, and
(\boldsymbol{I}-P_{Z})\hat{\mathbb{G}}=\boldsymbol{0}
. Write
c=u+\tilde{c}
with
u:=\mathbb{E}[c\mid\mathbf{X}]
.

Orders and conditional moments.

The observations are i.i.d. (Assumption
3
(i)). With
d_{k}:=\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}V_{k}\boldsymbol{\delta}
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[aa^{\top}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=T\operatorname{diag}\bigl(d_{1},\dots,d_{m}\bigr)T,

whose diagonal entries are
O_{p}(\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2})
and whose off-diagonal entries are
O_{p}(\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2}m^{-1})
uniformly: the diagonal is
T_{jj}d_{j}+\sum_{k\neq j}T_{jk}^{2}d_{k}
with
\sum_{k\neq j}T_{jk}^{2}d_{k}\leq(C/m)^{2}\sum_{k}d_{k}=O_{p}(m^{-1}\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2})
by the off-diagonal bound
|T_{jk}|\leq Cm^{-1}
of Assumption
6
(ii) and the first Markov average, and the off-diagonal entry
\sum_{k}T_{jk}T_{j^{\prime}k}d_{k}
splits into the two fixed-index terms
T_{jj^{\prime}}d_{j}+T_{j^{\prime}j}d_{j^{\prime}}=O_{p}(m^{-1}\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2})
and an
O(m^{-2})\sum_{k}d_{k}
remainder, against
\frac{1}{m}\sum_{k}d_{k}=O_{p}(\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2})
(the first Markov average). Moreover

\lVert a\rVert^{2}=\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}^{\top}T\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}\boldsymbol{\delta}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\bigl(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0,j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta}\bigr)^{2}=O_{p}\bigl(m\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2}\bigr),

the conditional expectation of the middle sum being
\sum_{j}d_{j}=O_{p}(m\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2})
, so
\lVert a\rVert=O_{p}(m^{1/2-\varrho})
; and
a_{j}=\sum_{k}T_{jk}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0,k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta}
is a linear form in conditionally independent centered variables with
\sum_{k}T_{jk}^{2}=T_{jj}\leq 1
. For
b
:
T\boldsymbol{g}=T(\boldsymbol{g}-\hat{\boldsymbol{g}})
since
P_{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}=\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}
, so
\lVert b\rVert\leq\lVert\boldsymbol{g}-\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}\rVert_{F}\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert=o_{p}(m^{1/2-3\varrho})
by the sieve rate of Assumption
6
(iii) at the selection sample (the sieve-error step of the proof of Lemma
3
). For the second-order component,
\mathbb{E}[c_{j}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]=O_{p}(m^{-4\varrho})
uniformly in
j
(Assumptions
5
(iii) and
4
(iii), Cauchy–Schwarz, and the
O(1)
row sums of
T
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
), whence, by Jensen,

\lVert u\rVert^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}u_{j}^{2}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\bigl[c_{j}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=O_{p}\bigl(m^{1-4\varrho}\bigr),

and

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\tilde{c}_{j}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=O_{p}(m^{-4\varrho}),\qquad\mathbb{E}\bigl[\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{j^{\prime}}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=O_{p}(m^{-1-4\varrho})\quad(j\neq j^{\prime}),

the off-diagonal order following from the same-index survival of the conditionally independent per-observation factors
\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}_{k}\boldsymbol{\delta}
and
r_{k}^{(2)}
together with the off-diagonal entries of
T
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
, exactly as in the enumeration of Lemma
14
.

The three differences.

With
K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}:=(g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})\mathbf{1}_{i\neq j})
,
g_{ij}:=g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
, and
s_{j}:=\sum_{i\neq j}g_{ij}^{2}\leq(m-1)\lambda_{L}^{-2}
,

\Delta^{U}_{m}(\eta):=mU_{m}(\eta;\hat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\perp})-mU_{m}(\eta;\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{0}_{\perp})=\frac{2}{m-1}\,w^{\top}K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}d+\frac{1}{m-1}\,d^{\top}K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}d,

\Delta^{Q}_{m}(\eta)=\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}w_{j}d_{j}s_{j}+\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}d_{j}^{2}g_{ij}^{2},

and, since
v_{j}v_{k}\mapsto(w_{j}+d_{j})(w_{k}+d_{k})-w_{j}w_{k}=w_{j}d_{k}+w_{k}d_{j}+d_{j}d_{k}
contributes both orientations of its linear part under the once-counted symmetric pair sum,

\Delta^{R}_{m}(\eta)=\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j<k\\
j,k\neq i\end{subarray}}g_{ij}g_{ik}\bigl(w_{j}d_{k}+w_{k}d_{j}+d_{j}d_{k}\bigr).

Splitting
g_{ij}g_{ik}=(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{j},X_{k})+r_{\eta}(X_{i};X_{j},X_{k})
and summing the population piece over the
m-2
anchors, the linear part of
\Delta^{R}_{m}
becomes

\Delta^{R}_{m,\mathrm{lin}}(\eta)=\frac{2(m-2)}{(m-1)^{2}}\,w^{\top}K^{\circ,\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}d+\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\,w^{\top}\Psi^{r}_{\eta}d,

where
K^{\circ,\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}:=((g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{j},X_{k})\mathbf{1}_{j\neq k})
and
(\Psi^{r}_{\eta})_{jk}:=\mathbf{1}_{j\neq k}\sum_{i\neq j,k}r_{\eta}(X_{i};X_{j},X_{k})
is a design-measurable symmetric matrix with
\lVert\Psi^{r}_{\eta}\rVert_{\max}\leq 2m\lambda_{L}^{-2}
.

Linear forms against the increment.

Let
\Psi
be any design-measurable symmetric matrix with
\lVert\Psi\rVert_{\max}\leq\bar{\psi}
and put
B:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\Psi T
.

(i) The idiosyncratic channel.
By (
26
) with
(x,y)=(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0},\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}\boldsymbol{\delta})
, the pairs being conditionally independent and centered given
\mathbf{X}
with
n_{l}=\boldsymbol{v}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta}
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[w^{\top}\Psi a\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=\sum_{l=1}^{m}B_{ll}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta}=O_{p}\bigl(\bar{\psi}\,m\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert\bigr),

the order by the diagonal bound on
B
and the Markov average of the
\boldsymbol{v}_{l}
. No centering improves the rate: the criterion difference contains
w^{\top}\Psi a
itself, whose conditional mean is of the same order as the fluctuation below. The variance is (
27
) with
s_{l}^{2}=\sigma^{2}(X_{l})
(Assumption
7
(iii)),
t_{k}^{2}=d_{k}
, and
n_{l}=\boldsymbol{v}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta}
. The product family is

\sum_{l\neq k}B_{lk}^{2}\sigma^{2}(X_{l})d_{k}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\bigl(\sum_{k}d_{k}\bigr)\max_{k}\sum_{l}B_{lk}^{2}=O_{p}\bigl(\bar{\psi}^{2}m^{2}\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2}\bigr)

by the column-sum refinement; the crossed family is

\sum_{l\neq k}B_{lk}B_{kl}\boldsymbol{v}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta}\leq\bigl(\max_{l,k}|B_{lk}|\bigr)^{2}\bigl(\sum_{l}|\boldsymbol{v}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta}|\bigr)^{2}=O_{p}\bigl(\bar{\psi}^{2}m^{2}\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2}\bigr)
,
the entries of
B
being
\bar{\psi}\,O_{p}(1)
uniformly by the row sums; and the four-equal family is

\sum_{l}B_{ll}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\varepsilon_{0,l}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0,l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta}-\boldsymbol{v}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta}\bigr)^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]\leq\bar{\psi}^{2}O_{p}(1)\sum_{l}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,l}^{4}\mid\mathbf{X}]\bigr)^{1/2}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0,l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta})^{4}\mid\mathbf{X}]\bigr)^{1/2}=O_{p}\bigl(\bar{\psi}^{2}m\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2}\bigr)

by Cauchy–Schwarz with
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,l}^{4}\mid\mathbf{X}]\leq\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p}
(the Lyapunov interpolation of the
(2p)
-clause of Assumption
7
(v) at
q=4
, the null premise giving
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}=\boldsymbol{v}
) and
\mathbb{E}[(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0,l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta})^{4}\mid\mathbf{X}]\leq C\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{4}
(Assumption
5
(iii)). Collecting,

\operatorname{Var}\bigl(w^{\top}\Psi a\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)=O_{p}\bigl(\bar{\psi}^{2}m^{2}\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2}\bigr),

whence

\bigl|w^{\top}\Psi a\bigr|=O_{p}\bigl(\bar{\psi}\,m^{1-\varrho}\bigr).

(ii) The smooth and second-order deterministic channels.
For design-measurable
e
,
\mathbb{E}[w\mid\mathbf{X}]=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\,\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\boldsymbol{0}
by the null premise, so
\mathbb{E}[w^{\top}\Psi e\mid\mathbf{X}]=0
and

\operatorname{Var}\bigl(w^{\top}\Psi e\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)=e^{\top}\Psi^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\Psi e\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lVert\Psi e\rVert^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}m^{2}\bar{\psi}^{2}\lVert e\rVert^{2},

with
D:=\operatorname{diag}(\sigma^{2}(X_{1}),\dots,\sigma^{2}(X_{m}))
, whence
|w^{\top}\Psi b|=o_{p}(\bar{\psi}m^{3/2-3\varrho})
and
|w^{\top}\Psi u|=O_{p}(\bar{\psi}m^{3/2-2\varrho})
, the bound being uniform over the smooth and conditionally centered components of
e
, so the smooth component of
u
enters at full strength.

(iii) The centered second-order channel.
Write the centered second-order increment as a sum of conditionally centered per-observation factors,
\tilde{c}_{j}=\sum_{l}\tilde{F}^{(j)}_{l}
, and let
\gamma^{(j)}_{l}:=\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,l}\tilde{F}^{(j)}_{l}\mid X_{l}]
. Conditional independence across observations gives the exact mean

\mathbb{E}\bigl[w_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=\sum_{l}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{il}\,\gamma^{(j)}_{l}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ij}\,\nu_{j}+\rho_{ij},

with
\nu_{j}:=\gamma^{(j)}_{j}=\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,j}\tilde{c}_{j}\mid X_{j}]
and
\rho_{ij}:=\sum_{l\neq j}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{il}\gamma^{(j)}_{l}
: only the same-index factor survives in
\nu_{j}
, and
|\nu_{j}|=O_{p}(m^{-2\varrho})
by Cauchy–Schwarz and the element-wise orders under the stated moment bound. For
l\neq j
the factor
\tilde{F}^{(j)}_{l}
carries the off-diagonal weights
T_{jl}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{jl}
, both
O_{p}(m^{-1})
, so the same Cauchy–Schwarz argument under the joint moment bound gives
|\gamma^{(j)}_{l}|=O_{p}(m^{-1-2\varrho})
and
|\rho_{ij}|=O_{p}(m^{-1-2\varrho})
. The mean is therefore

\mathbb{E}\bigl[w^{\top}\Psi\tilde{c}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=\sum_{i,j}\Psi_{ij}\,\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ij}\nu_{j}+O_{p}(\bar{\psi}m^{1-2\varrho})=O_{p}(\bar{\psi}m^{1-2\varrho}),

the first term by the row sums of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
, the cross-index contribution by

\sum_{j}\sum_{l\neq j}|\gamma^{(j)}_{l}|\,\Bigl(\sum_{i}|\Psi_{ij}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{il}|\Bigr)\leq\bar{\psi}\,O_{p}(1)\sum_{j,l}|\gamma^{(j)}_{l}|=O_{p}(m^{1-2\varrho}),

the
m(m-1)
cross-index pairs each
