O_{p}(m^{-1-2\varrho})
. Write
\mu_{ij}:=\mathbb{E}[w_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\mid\mathbf{X}]
. The centered second moment pairs as in the idiosyncratic case, and centering at the true conditional means makes the crossed family the exact product of the crossed means,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[(w_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\mu_{ij})(w_{i^{\prime}}\tilde{c}_{j^{\prime}}-\mu_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}})\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=c_{ii^{\prime}}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{j^{\prime}}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]+\mu_{ij^{\prime}}\,\mu_{i^{\prime}j}+\kappa^{\prime}_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}},

with
\kappa^{\prime}_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}
collecting the four-equal
\varepsilon
–factor configurations together with the same-observation and diagonal corrections left out of the two named families,
|\kappa^{\prime}_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}|\leq Cm^{-4\varrho}\sum_{l}|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{il}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{i^{\prime}l}|\,\bigl(|T_{jl}|+|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{jl}|\bigr)\bigl(|T_{j^{\prime}l}|+|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{j^{\prime}l}|\bigr)
(element-wise orders, Cauchy–Schwarz under the stated moment bound on
\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\lVert\nabla_{\theta}^{2}\varepsilon\rVert^{2}
). The first family contributes
O_{p}(\bar{\psi}^{2}m^{2-4\varrho})
: its four
(i,i^{\prime})\times(j,j^{\prime})
configurations pair the recorded diagonal and off-diagonal orders of
c_{ii^{\prime}}
and
\mathbb{E}[\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{j^{\prime}}\mid\mathbf{X}]
, the four products
O_{p}(m^{-4\varrho})
,
O_{p}(m^{-1-4\varrho})
,
O_{p}(m^{-1-4\varrho})
,
O_{p}(m^{-2-4\varrho})
against
m^{2}
,
m^{3}
,
m^{3}
,
m^{4}
index-quadruples respectively. The second family contributes
O_{p}(\bar{\psi}^{2}m^{2-4\varrho})
: the
i,i^{\prime}
-sum is
\bar{\psi}^{2}O_{p}(1)
per index pair by the row sums of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
, against
|\mu_{ij^{\prime}}\mu_{i^{\prime}j}|\leq Cm^{-4\varrho}
over the
m^{2}
pairs, since
|\mu_{ij}|\leq|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ij}|\,|\nu_{j}|+|\rho_{ij}|=O_{p}(m^{-2\varrho})
uniformly. The cumulant contributes
O_{p}(\bar{\psi}^{2}m^{1-4\varrho})
: its four-equal configurations force the per-observation factors of
\tilde{c}_{j}
and
\tilde{c}_{j^{\prime}}
onto the common index
l
, so summing first over
j,j^{\prime}
costs
\bar{\psi}^{2}(\max_{j}\sum_{i}|T_{ij}|)^{2}
per
l
, and the remaining
\sum_{l}(\sum_{i}|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{il}|)^{2}=O_{p}(m)
. Hence

\bigl|w^{\top}\Psi\tilde{c}\bigr|=O_{p}\bigl(\bar{\psi}m^{1-2\varrho}\bigr).

Quadratic parts.

The quadratic forms are controlled by two deterministic operator envelopes, uniform in
\eta
:
\lVert K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(m-1)\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}
by the row sums and the entrywise envelope
\lVert g_{\eta}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}
, and
\lVert W^{\prime}_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2(m-1)^{2}\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
for
W^{\prime}_{\eta}:=(K^{\mathrm{od},\top}_{\eta}K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta})-\operatorname{diag}(s_{\eta})
, the matrix of the
(Q,R)
-kernel, since the operator norm of
K^{\mathrm{od},\top}_{\eta}K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}
is
\lVert K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}
and
\max_{j}s_{\eta,j}\leq(m-1)\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
. For components without
a
, the bounds are then pathwise, the operator norms acting on the Euclidean norms:

\tfrac{1}{m-1}\bigl|e^{\top}K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}e^{\prime}\bigr|\leq\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}\lVert e\rVert\lVert e^{\prime}\rVert,\qquad\tfrac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\bigl|e^{\top}W^{\prime}_{\eta}e^{\prime}\bigr|\leq 4\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}\lVert e\rVert\lVert e^{\prime}\rVert,

so the
(b,b)
-pieces are
o_{p}(m^{1-6\varrho})
, and every piece involving
u
or
\tilde{c}
is
O_{p}(m^{1-4\varrho})
in
\Delta^{U}_{m}
and
O_{p}(m^{1-4\varrho})
in
\Delta^{R}_{m}
. For the
(a,a)
-pieces, the conditional mean is

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\tfrac{1}{m-1}a^{\top}K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}a\Bigm|\mathbf{X}\Bigr]=\tfrac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}g_{ij}\,\mathbb{E}[a_{i}a_{j}\mid\mathbf{X}]=O_{p}(\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2}),

by diagonal dominance of
\mathbb{E}[aa^{\top}\mid\mathbf{X}]
(with
\Psi=K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}
), and the fluctuation is, by (
28
) with
\zeta_{k}:=\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0,k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta}
and
A:=TK^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}T
,

\operatorname{Var}\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{m-1}a^{\top}K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}a\Bigm|\mathbf{X}\Bigr)=\tfrac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\Bigl(2\sum_{k,l}A_{kl}^{2}d_{k}d_{l}+\sum_{k}A_{kk}^{2}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k}^{4}\mid\mathbf{X}]-3d_{k}^{2}\bigr)\Bigr)=O_{p}\bigl(\lambda_{L}^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{4}\bigr),

since
|A_{kl}|\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}C
by the row sums of
T
,
\sum_{k}d_{k}=O_{p}(m\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2})
, and
\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k}^{4}\mid\mathbf{X}]\leq\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{4}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0,k}\rVert^{4}\mid X_{k}]\leq C\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{4}
(Assumption
5
(iii)); hence the
(a,a)
-piece of
\Delta^{U}_{m}
is
O_{p}(\lambda_{L}^{-1}\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2})
, and the
(a,a)
-pieces of
\Delta^{Q}_{m}
and
\Delta^{R}_{m}
are the same computation with the weight matrices
\operatorname{diag}(s)
and
W^{\prime}
,
W^{\prime}_{jk}:=\sum_{i\neq j,k}g_{ij}g_{ik}
,
\lVert W^{\prime}\rVert_{\max}\leq m\lambda_{L}^{-2}
, giving
O_{p}(\lambda_{L}^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2})
after their prefactors. For the mixed pieces
(a,b)
and
(a,u)
, the conditional mean vanishes, and expanding
a^{\top}\Psi e=(T\Psi e)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}\boldsymbol{\delta}
in the conditionally independent centered variables
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0,k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\delta}
,

\operatorname{Var}\bigl(a^{\top}\Psi e\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)=\sum_{k=1}^{m}(T\Psi e)_{k}^{2}\,\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}V_{k}\boldsymbol{\delta}\leq\lVert\Psi e\rVert\,\lVert\Psi e\rVert_{\infty}\,O_{p}\bigl(\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert^{2}m^{1/2}\bigr),

the inequality Cauchy–Schwarz in
\bigl((T\Psi e)_{k}^{2}\bigr)_{k}
against
\bigl(\sum_{k}\lVert V_{k}\rVert^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}=O_{p}(m^{1/2})
, with
\lVert Tz\rVert\leq\lVert z\rVert
and
\lVert Tz\rVert_{\infty}\leq(1+C)\lVert z\rVert_{\infty}
; hence
|a^{\top}\Psi e|=O_{p}(\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert\bar{\psi}m\lVert e\rVert)
, giving
O_{p}(\bar{\psi}m^{1/2-3\varrho})
in
\Delta^{U}_{m}
and
O_{p}(\bar{\psi}^{2}m^{1/2-3\varrho})
in
\Delta^{R}_{m}
, both
o_{p}(m^{-\varrho})
, after the prefactors; for
(a,\tilde{c})
, Cauchy–Schwarz gives mean
O_{p}(\bar{\psi}m^{-3\varrho})
(same-index survival of
\mathbb{E}[a_{j}\tilde{c}_{j}]
, of size
O_{p}(\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert m^{-2\varrho})
by the element-wise orders) and fluctuation, by the same variance expansion, with
\lVert\tilde{c}\rVert=O_{p}(m^{1/2-2\varrho})
by the displayed second moments and the same averaging as for
\lVert u\rVert
, so that
\lVert K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}\tilde{c}\rVert\leq m\lambda_{L}^{-1}\lVert\tilde{c}\rVert=O_{p}(\lambda_{L}^{-1}m^{3/2-2\varrho})
and
\lVert K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}\tilde{c}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}\lVert\tilde{c}\rVert_{1}=O_{p}(\lambda_{L}^{-1}m^{1-2\varrho})
,

\tfrac{2}{m-1}\bigl|a^{\top}K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}\tilde{c}\bigr|=\tfrac{2}{m-1}\,O_{p}\bigl(\lVert\boldsymbol{\delta}\rVert\lambda_{L}^{-1}m^{3/2-2\varrho}\bigr)=O_{p}\bigl(\lambda_{L}^{-1}m^{1/2-3\varrho}\bigr)=o_{p}\bigl(m^{-\varrho}\bigr).

Assembly.

The linear channels collect through the four instantiations
\Psi=K^{\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}
,
K^{\circ,\mathrm{od}}_{\eta}
,
\Psi^{r}_{\eta}
,
\operatorname{diag}(s)
with prefactors
\Gamma_{1}:=\tfrac{2}{m-1}
,
\Gamma_{2}:=\tfrac{2(m-2)}{(m-1)^{2}}
,
\Gamma_{3}:=\Gamma_{4}:=\tfrac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}
and
\bar{\psi}
-values
\lambda_{L}^{-1}
,
\lambda_{L}^{-2}
,
2m\lambda_{L}^{-2}
,
(m-1)\lambda_{L}^{-2}
; each product
\Gamma_{\Psi}\bar{\psi}_{\Psi}
is
O(m^{-1})
up to
\lambda_{L}
-constants, so

\displaystyle a\text{-linear:}

\displaystyle\sum_{\Psi}\Gamma_{\Psi}\bar{\psi}_{\Psi}\,O_{p}(m^{1-\varrho})

\displaystyle=O_{p}(m^{-\varrho}),

\displaystyle u\text{-linear:}

\displaystyle\sum_{\Psi}\Gamma_{\Psi}\bar{\psi}_{\Psi}\,O_{p}(m^{3/2-2\varrho})

\displaystyle=O_{p}(m^{1/2-2\varrho}),

\displaystyle\tilde{c}\text{- and }b\text{-linear:}

\displaystyle\sum_{\Psi}\Gamma_{\Psi}\bar{\psi}_{\Psi}\,\bigl(O_{p}(m^{1-2\varrho})+o_{p}(m^{3/2-3\varrho})\bigr)

\displaystyle=O_{p}(m^{-2\varrho})+o_{p}(m^{1/2-3\varrho}),

with
O_{p}(m^{-3\varrho})
added by the same-index means of the
(a,\tilde{c})
-pairing. The quadratic parts collect as
O_{p}(m^{-2\varrho})
from the
(a,a)
-pieces and
O_{p}(m^{1-4\varrho})
from the
(e,e^{\prime})
-pieces with
e,e^{\prime}\in\{u,\tilde{c}\}
, the
(b,b)
-pieces being
o_{p}(m^{1-6\varrho})
, and the mixed
(a,e)
- and
(a,\tilde{c})
-pieces are
o_{p}(m^{-\varrho})
. Since
\varrho>1/4
every exponent displayed is negative, and the three statement rates correspond one-to-one to the idiosyncratic-linear, smooth-second-order, and quadratic channels; hence

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|\Delta^{U}_{m}(\eta)+\Delta^{Q}_{m}(\eta)+\Delta^{R}_{m}(\eta)\bigr|=O_{p}\bigl(m^{-\varrho}+m^{1/2-2\varrho}+m^{1-4\varrho}\bigr)=o_{p}(1),

which is the displayed conclusion, the left-hand side being the difference of the two
m\,\mathrm{CV}^{\mathrm{dep}}_{m}
processes by the linearity of the linearization displays in the residual vector.

When the score is
X
-measurable,
\nabla_{\theta}\varepsilon(s;\theta)=g_{\theta}(X)
implies
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}=\boldsymbol{0}
and hence
a=\boldsymbol{0}
, leaving the remainder recorded in the statement.

Lemma 16
(Bilinear representation of the criterion’s quadratic statistics)

Let Assumption
1
hold and let
\varepsilon_{\perp,1},\dots,\varepsilon_{\perp,m}
be scalar residual coefficients on a sample of size
m
. The identities in (i) and (ii) are algebraic and hold for arbitrary scalar coefficients. Define the scaled mean embedding
M_{m}(\gamma):=m^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}k_{\gamma}(X_{i},\cdot)\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}
, its transport
\widetilde{M}_{m}(\gamma):=J_{\gamma}M_{m}(\gamma)
to the common space
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}
, and the
population
operators

A^{0}_{\eta}:=(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1},\qquad B^{0}_{\eta}:=(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}C_{\gamma}(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1},

on
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}
. Their reproducing kernels are
g_{\eta}
and
(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})
on
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
, where
(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(x,y):=\mathbb{E}_{Z}[g_{\eta}(x,Z)g_{\eta}(Z,y)]
: by the Mercer expansion of Assumption
1
,

\langle k_{\gamma}(X_{i},\cdot),A^{0}_{\eta}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})

and likewise with
B^{0}_{\eta}
and
(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{i},X_{j})
. Then:

(i)

Exact identity.
For every
\eta\in\Upsilon
,

mU_{m}(\eta)=\frac{m}{m-1}\bigl\langle M_{m}(\gamma),A^{0}_{\eta}M_{m}(\gamma)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}-\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{i}).

(ii)

Order-two reduction of
R_{m}
.
With
h_{\eta}^{(R)}(z_{1},z_{2}):=\varepsilon_{\perp,1}\varepsilon_{\perp,2}\,(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(x_{1},x_{2})
,

\displaystyle mR_{m}(\eta)

\displaystyle=\frac{m(m-2)}{m-1}\,U_{m}(h_{\eta}^{(R)})+\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)

\displaystyle:=\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j<k\\
j,k\neq i\end{subarray}}r_{\eta}(X_{i};X_{j},X_{k})\,\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,k}^{0},

where
r_{\eta}(x;y,z):=g_{\eta}(x,y)g_{\eta}(x,z)-(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(y,z)
satisfies
\mathbb{E}_{X}[r_{\eta}(X;y,z)]=0
, and

mU_{m}(h_{\eta}^{(R)})=\frac{m}{m-1}\bigl\langle M_{m}(\gamma),B^{0}_{\eta}M_{m}(\gamma)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}-\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{i},X_{i});

The uniform probabilistic bound for
\mathcal{R}_{m}
is stated in Lemma
17
below. The calligraphic
\mathcal{R}_{m}
denotes the anchor-centered remainder of this reduction and is to be distinguished from the criterion component
R_{m}
of Section
4.2
.

(iii)

Deterministic moduli and boundedness.

(a)
For all
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in\Upsilon
,

\begin{gathered}\sup_{x,y}\bigl|g_{\eta}(x,y)-g_{\eta^{\prime}}(x,y)\bigr|\leq C_{g}\,\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert,\qquad\sup_{x,y,z}\bigl|r_{\eta}(x;y,z)-r_{\eta^{\prime}}(x;y,z)\bigr|\leq C^{\prime\prime}\,\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert,\\
\sup_{x,y}\bigl|(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(x,y)-(g_{\eta^{\prime}}\circ g_{\eta^{\prime}})(x,y)\bigr|\leq C^{\prime}_{g}\,\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert,\end{gathered}

with
C^{\prime\prime}:=2\bar{k}\,\lambda_{L}^{-1}C_{g}+C^{\prime}_{g}
and all three constants deterministic, depending only on
(\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U},\lambda_{L},\lambda_{U},d,\bar{k})
. Moreover, the three kernels are entrywise continuously differentiable in
\eta
on
\Upsilon
, and, for
l\in\{\gamma,\lambda\}
,

\sup_{\eta,x,y}\bigl|\partial_{\eta_{l}}g_{\eta}(x,y)\bigr|\leq C_{g},\qquad\sup_{\eta,x,y}\bigl|\partial_{\eta_{l}}(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(x,y)\bigr|\leq C^{\prime}_{g},\qquad\sup_{\eta,x,y,z}\bigl|\partial_{\eta_{l}}r_{\eta}(x;y,z)\bigr|\leq C^{\prime\prime},

the derivative envelopes being bounded by the moduli above.
(b)
If, in addition, Assumptions
2
–
6
and
7
(iii), (v) hold, and under the local alternative the Pitman calibration of Lemma
10
, then
\sup_{\gamma}\lVert\widetilde{M}_{m}(\gamma)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}=O_{p}(1)
.

Proof.

(i)
The claim is algebra alone: the coefficients
\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}
enter as indeterminates, and no distributional assumption is used. Since
M_{m}(\gamma)=m^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\,k_{\gamma}(X_{i},\cdot)
, bilinearity of the inner product and the reproducing-kernel identification of the statement give

\displaystyle\bigl\langle M_{m}(\gamma),A^{0}_{\eta}M_{m}(\gamma)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,\bigl\langle k_{\gamma}(X_{i},\cdot),A^{0}_{\eta}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j}).

The index set splits into the strict off-diagonal and the diagonal,

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i,j}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i\neq j}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{i}),

and the symmetry
g_{\eta}(x,y)=g_{\eta}(y,x)
pairs the summand of each ordered pair
(i,j)
,
i\neq j
, with the equal summand of
(j,i)
, so

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i\neq j}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})=\frac{2}{m}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq m}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j}).

With the recorded normalization
U_{m}(\eta)=\frac{2}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i<j}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
, the off-diagonal term is

\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i<j}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})=\frac{m-1}{m}\cdot\frac{2}{m-1}\sum_{i<j}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})=\frac{m-1}{m}\,mU_{m}(\eta),

the first equality inserting
\tfrac{m-1}{m-1}
, the second the normalization. Collecting the two pieces,

\bigl\langle M_{m}(\gamma),A^{0}_{\eta}M_{m}(\gamma)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=\frac{m-1}{m}\,mU_{m}(\eta)+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{i}),

multiplying by
\tfrac{m}{m-1}
and simplifying the scalar coefficients,

\frac{m}{m-1}\bigl\langle M_{m}(\gamma),A^{0}_{\eta}M_{m}(\gamma)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=mU_{m}(\eta)+\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{i}),

and subtracting the diagonal term yields

mU_{m}(\eta)=\frac{m}{m-1}\bigl\langle M_{m}(\gamma),A^{0}_{\eta}M_{m}(\gamma)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}-\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{i}),

which is (i).

(ii)
The proof is algebra alone: four exact identities, none involving the law of the residuals. The uniform probabilistic bound for the remainder
\mathcal{R}_{m}
is established in the proof of Lemma
17
below.

The anchor-centered split.
The split
g_{\eta}(x,y)g_{\eta}(x,z)=(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(y,z)+r_{\eta}(x;y,z)
restates the definition of
r_{\eta}
in the statement;
r_{\eta}
is symmetric in
(y,z)
, and
\mathbb{E}_{X}[r_{\eta}(X;y,z)]=\mathbb{E}_{X}[g_{\eta}(X,y)g_{\eta}(X,z)]-(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(y,z)=0
by the definition of the composed kernel.

The reduction, by anchor counting.
Substituting the split into
mR_{m}(\eta)
and collecting the two pieces of each summand,

\displaystyle mR_{m}(\eta)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j<k\\
j,k\neq i\end{subarray}}(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{j},X_{k})\,\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,k}^{0}}_{\text{the population piece: independent of the anchor }i}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j<k\\
j,k\neq i\end{subarray}}r_{\eta}(X_{i};X_{j},X_{k})\,\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,k}^{0}}_{\text{the anchored remainder}}.

By the definition in the statement, the second double sum is
\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)
. The population piece does not depend on the anchor
i
, and each unordered pair
\{j,k\}
admits exactly
m-2
anchors:

\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j<k\\
j,k\neq i\end{subarray}}(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{j},X_{k})\,\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,k}^{0}=\frac{2(m-2)}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{j<k}(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{j},X_{k})\,\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,k}^{0}.

The ordered double sum in
U_{m}(h_{\eta}^{(R)})
counts each unordered pair twice:

\sum_{j<k}(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{j},X_{k})\,\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,k}^{0}=\frac{m(m-1)}{2}\,U_{m}\bigl(h_{\eta}^{(R)}\bigr),

and the population piece equals

\frac{2(m-2)}{(m-1)^{2}}\cdot\frac{m(m-1)}{2}\,U_{m}\bigl(h_{\eta}^{(R)}\bigr)=\frac{m(m-2)}{m-1}\,U_{m}\bigl(h_{\eta}^{(R)}\bigr),

which is the reduction displayed in the statement.

The composed kernel.

A^{0}_{\eta}:=(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}
is self-adjoint with
\lVert A^{0}_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}
, and
B^{0}_{\eta}:=A^{0}_{\eta}C_{\gamma}A^{0}_{\eta}
has the composed kernel as its reproducing kernel:

\displaystyle\bigl\langle k_{\gamma}(x,\cdot),B^{0}_{\eta}k_{\gamma}(y,\cdot)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\bigl\langle A^{0}_{\eta}k_{\gamma}(x,\cdot),\,C_{\gamma}\,A^{0}_{\eta}k_{\gamma}(y,\cdot)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z}\bigl[(A^{0}_{\eta}k_{\gamma}(x,\cdot))(Z)\,(A^{0}_{\eta}k_{\gamma}(y,\cdot))(Z)\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z}\bigl[g_{\eta}(x,Z)\,g_{\eta}(Z,y)\bigr]=(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(x,y),

the first equality by self-adjointness of
A^{0}_{\eta}
, the second by the covariance property
\langle f,C_{\gamma}g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=\mathbb{E}[f(X)g(X)]
for
f,g\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}
, and the third by the reproducing property
(A^{0}_{\eta}k_{\gamma}(y,\cdot))(Z)=\langle k_{\gamma}(Z,\cdot),A^{0}_{\eta}k_{\gamma}(y,\cdot)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=g_{\eta}(Z,y)
.

The bilinear representation.
With the scaled embedding

M_{m}(\gamma):=m^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\,k_{\gamma}(X_{i},\cdot),

the split of the double sum into
i\neq j
and
i=j
gives

\displaystyle\frac{m}{m-1}\bigl\langle M_{m},B^{0}_{\eta}M_{m}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i,j}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\,(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{i},X_{j})

\displaystyle=mU_{m}\bigl(h_{\eta}^{(R)}\bigr)+\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{i},X_{i}),

and solving for
mU_{m}
gives the identity displayed in the statement. This is the algebra of (i) with
A^{0}_{\eta}
replaced by
B^{0}_{\eta}
and
g_{\eta}
by its composition; like (i), it holds for arbitrary scalar coefficients.

(iii)
Transport to the common space, with
\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}:=J_{\gamma}A^{0}_{\eta}J_{\gamma}^{-1}
and
\widetilde{B}^{0}_{\eta}:=J_{\gamma}B^{0}_{\eta}J_{\gamma}^{-1}
:
g_{\eta}(x,y)=\bigl\langle\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(x),\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(y)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}
with
\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(x):=J_{\gamma}k_{\gamma}(x,\cdot)
and
\sup_{x}\lVert\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(x)\rVert\leq\bar{k}^{1/2}
; the feature distance
\bigl\lVert\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(x)-\widetilde{k}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(x)\bigr\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}\leq L_{d}\,\lvert\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\rvert
follows from Fact
1
(i) by the mean value theorem. Adding and subtracting
\bigl\langle\widetilde{k}_{\gamma^{\prime}}(x),\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(y)\bigr\rangle
and telescoping over the three factors,

\bigl|g_{\eta}(x,y)-g_{\eta^{\prime}}(x,y)\bigr|\leq 2\lambda_{L}^{-1}\bar{k}^{1/2}\,L_{d}\,|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}|+\bar{k}\,\bigl\lVert\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}-\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{op}},

the two feature-increment terms being contracted by
\lVert\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}
and each bounded by
\bar{k}^{1/2}L_{d}\lvert\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\rvert
; the operator increment obeys, by the resolvent identity,

\lVert\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}-\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta^{\prime}}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\bigl(\lVert\widetilde{C}_{\gamma}-\widetilde{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\lvert\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}\rvert\bigr)\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\bigl(2\bar{k}^{1/2}L_{d}\,\lvert\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\rvert+\lvert\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}\rvert\bigr)
,
the last step combining Fact
1
(iii),
\lVert\widetilde{C}_{\gamma}-\widetilde{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq 2\,\bar{k}^{1/2}L_{d}\,\lvert\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\rvert
, with
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}
. This is the first modulus. The second follows by the same telescope with
\widetilde{B}^{0}
in place of
\widetilde{A}^{0}
, using the product increment: on the common space
\widetilde{B}^{0}_{\eta}=\bigl(\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}\bigr)^{2}\widetilde{C}_{\gamma}
, and the split

\widetilde{B}^{0}_{\eta}-\widetilde{B}^{0}_{\eta^{\prime}}=\bigl[\bigl(\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}\bigr)^{2}-\bigl(\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr)^{2}\bigr]\widetilde{C}_{\gamma}+\bigl(\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr)^{2}\bigl(\widetilde{C}_{\gamma}-\widetilde{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\bigr)

gives

\bigl\lVert\widetilde{B}^{0}_{\eta}-\widetilde{B}^{0}_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}\bigl\lVert\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}-\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\lambda_{L}^{-2}\bigl\lVert\widetilde{C}_{\gamma}-\widetilde{C}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{op}},

using
\bigl\lVert\bigl(\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}\bigr)^{2}-\bigl(\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr)^{2}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\lambda_{L}^{-1}\lVert\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}-\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta^{\prime}}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
and
\lVert\widetilde{C}_{\gamma}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\operatorname{tr}\widetilde{C}_{\gamma}=\mathbb{E}[k_{\gamma}(X,X)]=1=\bar{k}
for the unit-normalized Gaussian kernel.

The anchor modulus.
Adding and subtracting
g_{\eta^{\prime}}(x,y)\,g_{\eta}(x,z)
in the first product of the definition of
r_{\eta}
gives the split, with
\delta g_{\eta}:=g_{\eta}-g_{\eta^{\prime}}
and
\delta(g\circ g)_{\eta}:=(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})-(g_{\eta^{\prime}}\circ g_{\eta^{\prime}})
,

r_{\eta}(x;y,z)-r_{\eta^{\prime}}(x;y,z)=\delta g_{\eta}(x,y)\,g_{\eta}(x,z)+g_{\eta^{\prime}}(x,y)\,\delta g_{\eta}(x,z)-\delta(g\circ g)_{\eta}(y,z),

so, by the sup-norm bound
|g_{\eta}|\leq\bar{k}\,\lambda_{L}^{-1}
and the two moduli above,

\bigl|r_{\eta}(x;y,z)-r_{\eta^{\prime}}(x;y,z)\bigr|\leq 2\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}C_{g}\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert+C^{\prime}_{g}\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert=C^{\prime\prime}\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert,

the third modulus of (iii)(a), which proves that clause.

Differentiability.
On the common space,
\partial_{\lambda}\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}=-(\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta})^{2}
and
\partial_{\gamma}\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}=-\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}\bigl(\partial_{\gamma}\widetilde{C}_{\gamma}\bigr)\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}
by the derivative-of-the-inverse identity applied to
\widetilde{C}_{\gamma}+\lambda I
,
\gamma\mapsto\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(x)
is
C^{1}
with
\sup_{\gamma,x}\bigl\lVert\partial_{\gamma}\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(x)\bigr\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}\leq L_{d}
by Fact
1
(i), and
\gamma\mapsto\widetilde{C}_{\gamma}
is
C^{1}
in Hilbert–Schmidt norm with
\bigl\lVert\partial_{\gamma}\widetilde{C}_{\gamma}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq 2\,\bar{k}^{1/2}L_{d}
by Fact
1
(iii); the product rule on the transported pairing
g_{\eta}(x,y)=\bigl\langle\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(x),\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(y)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}
and dominated convergence over
\mathbb{P}_{Z}
for the composition give entrywise continuous derivatives of
g_{\eta}
,
(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})
, and
r_{\eta}
. Since
\Upsilon
is a convex rectangle, the mean value theorem along segments applied to the moduli of the statement yields the three derivative envelopes.

(iii)(b): the boundedness clause.
The proof of Lemma
10
(a) is residual- and sample-size-agnostic: it consumes only the Lipschitz and moment inputs recorded there, at a generic sample size. At the selection sample the Pitman shift is
f_{m}=\delta_{m}h
with
\sqrt{m}\,\delta_{m}\to a<\infty
, so the calibration
N\lVert g\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=O(1)
of that proof holds with
N=m
, and every display of the proof transfers at sample size
m
, giving
\sup_{\gamma}\lVert\widetilde{M}_{m}(\gamma)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}=O_{p}(1)
.

Lemma 17
(Uniform bound for the anchor-centered remainder)

Let Assumption
1
hold and let
\mathcal{R}_{m}
be the anchor-centered remainder of Lemma
16
(ii). The bound is stated for the projected residuals
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{0}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
of the null regime: their innovations are conditionally centered,
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\boldsymbol{0}
, conditionally independent given
\mathbf{X}
with
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,a}^{2}\mid X_{a}]=\sigma^{2}(X_{a})
and
\bar{M}_{2p}:=\sup_{a}\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,a}^{2p}\mid X_{a}]<\infty
for the exponent
p\in(2,4]
of Assumption
7
(iii), (v);
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
-sandwiched coefficient matrices are taken as conditionally deterministic under the convention of Remark
2
. Then

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)\bigr|=O_{p}\bigl(m^{-1/2}\bigr)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)\bigm|\mathbf{X}\bigr]\bigr|=O_{p}\bigl(m^{-1/2}\bigr).

Proof.

Design of the bound.
The target is stronger than a pointwise rate: Chebyshev’s inequality applied to the second-moment bound below gives
\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
at each fixed
\eta
, whereas the criterion analysis subtracts the remainder uniformly over the whole tuning set, and a pointwise rate says nothing about the supremum. The proof is organized around three observations. First, the summands of
\mathcal{R}_{m}
and of its
\eta
-gradients are bounded and Lipschitz in
\eta
with deterministic, design-free envelopes; every later moment computation consumes these envelopes, and the path-regularity hypothesis of the Sobolev inequality is literally a deterministic gradient bound. Second, the rate is a normalization fact: the coefficient matrix of the matrix form below has Frobenius norm
O(m^{-1/2})
in expectation, and this single estimate funds every later bound. Third, the projection
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
makes the entries of
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{0}
conditionally dependent given
\mathbf{X}
, so computations for independent products are unavailable; the workhorse is an order-
p
moment bound for the sandwiched quadratic form, obtained by decoupling and conditional Rosenthal’s inequality at the exponent
p\in(2,4]
of Assumption
7
(v), and the anchor-multiset count isolates the combinatorics of the coefficient increments so that the derivative family reuses it verbatim. No stochastic covering or chaining enters: the only two-point computation is the anchor-multiset count for the coefficient matrices, and the uniformity over
\Upsilon
is assembled from within-cell oscillations on a mesh fixed independently of
m
; the uniform gradient moment is converted into those oscillations by the Sobolev maximal inequality of Lemma
6
at the exponent
p\in(2,4]
,
p>d=2
, the mesh levels are governed by the pointwise variances, and the suprema over cells are assembled by Hölder’s inequality. Each paragraph below records the later paragraphs that consume it.

The matrix form of
\mathcal{R}_{m}
.
Let
A_{\eta}:=(A_{\eta,jk})
be the symmetric matrix with entries
A_{\eta,jk}:=\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j,k}r_{\eta}(X_{i};X_{j},X_{k})
for
j\neq k
and zero diagonal. Symmetry of
r_{\eta}
in its last two slots makes the ordered double sum twice the unordered one, so

\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{\top}A_{\eta}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{0}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}B_{\eta}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0},\qquad B_{\eta}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}},

the first identity reproducing the definition of
\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)
and the second inserting
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\perp}^{0}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
and using the idempotence and symmetry of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
. The algebra of the reduction of Lemma
16
(ii) is complete: the third-order sum
R_{m}
is a second-order U-statistic scaled by
m(m-2)/(m-1)
, itself a quadratic form of the mean embedding against a deterministic operator, plus the anchor-centered remainder
\mathcal{R}_{m}
in matrix form.

The probabilistic input is the null-regime innovation structure: conditional on
\mathbf{X}
, the innovations are independent and centered with
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,a}^{2}\mid X_{a}]=\sigma^{2}(X_{a})
, and the fourth-moment level consumed below is carried by the Lyapunov interpolation of the
(2p)
-clause of Assumption
7
(v) at
q=4
,
\bar{M}_{4}:=\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p}\geq\sup_{a}\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,a}^{4}\mid X_{a}]
, a deterministic constant depending only on
(p,\bar{M}_{2p})
(Assumption
7
(iii), (v)).

Deterministic and moment envelopes.
From
g_{\eta}(x,x)\leq\lambda^{-1}k_{\gamma}(x,x)\leq\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}
and Cauchy–Schwarz,

\lVert g_{\eta}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1},\qquad\lVert g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2},\qquad\lvert r_{\eta}\rvert\leq 2\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2},\qquad\mathbb{E}[r_{\eta}^{2}]\leq 4\bar{k}^{4}\lambda_{L}^{-4},

and, by the anchor-modulus constant of part (iii)(a) of Lemma
16
,
\lvert r_{\eta}(x;y,z)-r_{\eta^{\prime}}(x;y,z)\rvert\leq C^{\prime\prime}\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert
on
\mathcal{X}^{3}
,
C^{\prime\prime}:=2\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}C_{g}+C^{\prime}_{g}
. For the centered innovation squares, at the exponent
p
of Assumption
7
(v),

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl|\varepsilon_{0,a}^{2}-\sigma^{2}(X_{a})\bigr|^{p}\mid X_{a}\bigr]\leq 2^{p-1}\bigl(\bar{M}_{2p}+\sigma_{\max}^{2p}\bigr),

by
|a-b|^{p}\leq 2^{p-1}(a^{p}+b^{p})
and Jensen’s inequality.

The size of the coefficient matrices.
Since
r_{\eta}
is
E_{X}
-centered in its anchor argument and the
m-2
anchors are independent,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(m-1)^{4}}\sum_{j\neq k}\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\neq j,k}\mathbb{E}[r_{\eta}(X_{i};X_{j},X_{k})r_{\eta}(X_{i^{\prime}};X_{j},X_{k})]

\displaystyle=\frac{m(m-1)(m-2)}{(m-1)^{4}}\,\mathbb{E}[r_{\eta}^{2}]\leq\frac{m(m-1)(m-2)}{(m-1)^{4}}\,4\bar{k}^{4}\lambda_{L}^{-4}=O(m^{-1}),

the middle equality retaining only the diagonal
i=i^{\prime}
of the anchor double sum. Markov’s inequality gives
\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
, and the contraction
\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
gives
\lVert B_{\eta}\rVert_{F}\leq\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}
.

Means and variances.
Let
D:=\operatorname{diag}\bigl(\sigma^{2}(X_{1}),\dots,\sigma^{2}(X_{m})\bigr)
and
C:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
. Under the null premise
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\boldsymbol{0}
,

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}]=\sum_{a}(B_{\eta})_{aa}\sigma^{2}(X_{a})=\operatorname{tr}(B_{\eta}D)=\operatorname{tr}(A_{\eta}C^{\mathrm{od}}),

by cyclicity of the trace, the zero diagonal of
A_{\eta}
annihilating the diagonal of
C
; Cauchy–Schwarz gives
|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{R}_{m}\mid\mathbf{X}]|\leq\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}\lVert C^{\mathrm{od}}\rVert_{F}
, with

\lVert C^{\mathrm{od}}\rVert_{F}^{2}=\sum_{j\neq k}c_{jk}^{2}\leq 3\bigl(2\sigma_{\max}^{4}d_{\theta}+\sigma_{\max}^{4}d_{\theta}\bigr)=9\sigma_{\max}^{4}d_{\theta}

deterministically, through the expansion recorded in Section
4.2
of the main text: with
H:=\hat{\mathbb{G}}M\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}
the rank-
d_{\theta}
hat projection of that expansion,
c_{jk}=-\sigma^{2}(X_{j})H_{jk}-\sigma^{2}(X_{k})H_{jk}+(HDH)_{jk}
,
\sum_{j\neq k}H_{jk}^{2}\leq\operatorname{tr}(H^{2})=\operatorname{tr}(H)=d_{\theta}
, and
\lVert HDH\rVert_{F}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lVert H\rVert_{F}=\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sqrt{d_{\theta}}
(
\lVert D\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
,
\lVert H\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
,
H
idempotent of rank
d_{\theta}
). Hence
|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{R}_{m}\mid\mathbf{X}]|=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
. For the variance, Lemma
14
applied to the conditionally independent, centered innovations
\varepsilon_{0,a}
gives

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}\bigl(\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)

\displaystyle=2\bigl\lVert D^{1/2}B_{\eta}D^{1/2}\bigr\rVert_{F}^{2}+\sum_{a=1}^{m}(B_{\eta})_{aa}^{2}\,\kappa_{4}(a)\leq\bigl(2\sigma_{\max}^{4}+2\bar{M}_{4}\bigr)\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{2},

with
\kappa_{4}(a):=\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,a}^{4}\mid X_{a}]-3\sigma^{4}(X_{a})
, where
|\kappa_{4}(a)|\leq 2\bar{M}_{4}
:
\kappa_{4}(a)\leq\bar{M}_{4}
by the interpolated fourth moment of the matrix-form paragraph,
\kappa_{4}(a)\geq-2\sigma^{4}(X_{a})\geq-2\sigma_{\max}^{4}
by Jensen’s inequality
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,a}^{4}\mid X_{a}]\geq\sigma^{4}(X_{a})
, and
\sigma_{\max}^{4}\leq\bar{M}_{4}
by the same Jensen bound; the remaining steps use
\lVert D^{1/2}B_{\eta}D^{1/2}\rVert_{F}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lVert B_{\eta}\rVert_{F}
,
\sum_{a}(B_{\eta})_{aa}^{2}\leq\lVert B_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{2}
, and the contraction of the size paragraph. Collecting mean and variance,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]\leq\bigl(2\sigma_{\max}^{4}+2\bar{M}_{4}+\lVert C^{\mathrm{od}}\rVert_{F}^{2}\bigr)\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{2}=O_{p}(m^{-1}).

Order-
p
moments of projected-residual quadratic forms.
This paragraph isolates the order-
p
moment bound for a generic coefficient matrix, at the exponent
p\in(2,4]
of Assumption
7
(v). Let
A
be a design-measurable symmetric
m\times m
matrix with zero diagonal and set
\widetilde{A}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
, which is again symmetric and, being
\hat{\theta}
-dependent through
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
, conditionally deterministic under the convention of Remark
2
. Lemma
9
, applied conditionally on
\mathcal{G}=\sigma(\mathbf{X})
to the conditionally independent, centered innovations and to the coefficient matrix
\widetilde{A}
, gives

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}\widetilde{A}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}-\operatorname{tr}(\widetilde{A}\,D)\bigr|^{p}\mid\mathbf{X}\Bigr]\leq C_{p}(\sigma_{\max},\bar{M}_{2p})\,\lVert\widetilde{A}\rVert_{F}^{p},

the bound (
21
) at the selection sample; the contraction
\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\rVert_{F}\leq\lVert A\rVert_{F}
of the idempotent projection converts it to the coefficient matrix
A
.
The conditional mean is absorbed by the zero diagonal of
A
: with
C:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
of the means-and-variances paragraph and
C_{0}:=9\sigma_{\max}^{4}d_{\theta}
,

\bigl|\operatorname{tr}(\widetilde{A}\,D)\bigr|=\bigl|\operatorname{tr}(A\,C)\bigr|=\bigl|\operatorname{tr}(A\,C^{\mathrm{od}})\bigr|\leq\lVert A\rVert_{F}\,\lVert C^{\mathrm{od}}\rVert_{F}\leq C_{0}^{1/2}\lVert A\rVert_{F},

the first equality by cyclicity of the trace, the second discarding the diagonal of
C
against the zero diagonal of
A
, the inequality Cauchy–Schwarz with the deterministic envelope
\lVert C^{\mathrm{od}}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq C_{0}
of the means-and-variances paragraph. Assembling the centered and mean parts by
(x+y)^{p}\leq 2^{p-1}(x^{p}+y^{p})
and the tower property,

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\bigr|^{p}\;\Big|\;\mathbf{X}\Bigr]\leq C_{p}(\sigma_{\max},\bar{M}_{2p},C_{0})\,\lVert A\rVert_{F}^{p},

the constant depending only on
(p,\sigma_{\max},\bar{M}_{2p},d_{\theta})
; this plain form is the one cited below. Alongside it, the anchor-multiset count for coefficient increments is recorded, whose pattern the process-level paragraph reuses for the derivative family.

The anchor-multiset count.

Writing
\Delta A_{jk}=\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j,k}\delta r_{\eta}(X_{i};X_{j},X_{k})
with
\delta r_{\eta}:=r_{\eta}-r_{\eta^{\prime}}
, which is
\mathbb{E}_{X}
-centered and bounded by
C^{\prime\prime}\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert
on
\mathcal{X}^{3}
, and abbreviating
\delta r_{i}:=\delta r_{\eta}(X_{i};X_{j},X_{k})
at fixed
(j,k)
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\Delta A_{jk}^{2}\,\Delta A_{j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}^{2}\bigr]=\frac{1}{(m-1)^{8}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2}\in A(j,k)\\
i_{3},i_{4}\in A(j^{\prime},k^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\delta r_{i_{1}}\,\delta r_{i_{2}}\,\delta r_{i_{3}}\,\delta r_{i_{4}}\bigr],\qquad A(j,k):=\{1,\dots,m\}\setminus\{j,k\}.

The factors
\delta r_{i}
are conditionally independent across distinct anchors outside the conditioning set
\{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}\}
and
\mathbb{E}_{X}
-centered, so a four-factor product vanishes unless every anchor index outside
\{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}\}
appears at least twice among
\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}\}
; anchors falling inside
\{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}\}
are conditioning-measurable and bounded by
C^{\prime\prime}\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert
, and the configurations containing them add only
O(m)
bounded summands per entry pair, absorbed in the constant below. The surviving multisets are the all-equal one,
i_{1}=i_{2}=i_{3}=i_{4}
, which requires
i\in A(j,k)\cap A(j^{\prime},k^{\prime})
, about
m
choices, each summand at most
C^{\prime\prime 4}\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert^{4}
, and the two-and-two ones, dominantly
i_{1}=i_{2}\in A(j,k)
with
i_{3}=i_{4}\in A(j^{\prime},k^{\prime})
, about
m^{2}
choices, each contributing
\mathbb{E}[\delta r^{2}]^{2}\leq C^{\prime\prime 4}\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert^{4}
, the cross-paired two-and-two configurations having about
m
choices; hence

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\Delta A_{jk}^{2}\,\Delta A_{j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}^{2}\bigr]\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime 4}\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert^{4}\,C\,(m^{2}+m)}{(m-1)^{8}}\leq C\,\frac{\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert^{4}}{m^{6}},

and summing over the
m^{2}(m-1)^{2}
index pairs,

\mathbb{E}\lVert\Delta A\rVert_{F}^{4}\leq C\,\frac{\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert^{4}}{m^{2}}.

With
r_{\eta}
itself in place of
\delta r_{\eta}
, likewise
\mathbb{E}_{X}
-centered and bounded by
K_{r}:=2\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
, the same count gives
\mathbb{E}\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{4}\leq C\,K_{r}^{4}/m^{2}
, consumed by the conditional-mean paragraph below.

The process level: gradient moments, fixed mesh, Sobolev–Morrey.

Normalize
S_{m}(\eta):=\sqrt{m}\,\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)
. No stochastic covering or chaining enters: the only two-point computation is the anchor-multiset count for the coefficient matrices, and the uniformity over
\Upsilon
is assembled from within-cell oscillations on a mesh fixed independently of
m
.

Path regularity and gradient envelopes.

By the derivative-envelope clause of Lemma
16
(iii)(a), the kernels
g_{\eta}
,
(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})
, and
r_{\eta}
are entrywise
C^{1}
in
\eta
on
\Upsilon
with
\sup_{\eta,x,y}\lvert\partial_{\eta_{l}}g_{\eta}\rvert\leq C_{g}
,
\sup_{\eta,x,y}\lvert\partial_{\eta_{l}}(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})\rvert\leq C^{\prime}_{g}
, and
\sup_{\eta,x,y,z}\lvert\partial_{\eta_{l}}r_{\eta}\rvert\leq C^{\prime\prime}
for
l\in\{\gamma,\lambda\}
, all three bounds deterministic and design-free; centering is inherited,
\mathbb{E}_{X}[\partial_{\eta_{l}}r_{\eta}(X;y,z)]=\partial_{\eta_{l}}\,\mathbb{E}_{X}[r_{\eta}(X;y,z)]=0
, the interchange by dominated convergence with dominator
C^{\prime\prime}
. Consequently the matrix field
A_{\eta}
of the matrix-form paragraph is entrywise
C^{1}
with

\partial_{\eta_{l}}A_{\eta,jk}=\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j,k}\partial_{\eta_{l}}r_{\eta}(X_{i};X_{j},X_{k})\quad(j\neq k),\qquad\partial_{\eta_{l}}A_{\eta,jj}=0,

so
S_{m}
has
C^{1}
paths and
\partial_{\eta_{l}}S_{m}(\eta)=\sqrt{m}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\partial_{\eta_{l}}A_{\eta})\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
.

Gradient moments.

The derivative family
\partial_{\eta_{l}}r_{\eta}
is
\mathbb{E}_{X}
-centered and bounded by
C^{\prime\prime}
, the constant of the envelope paragraph. The anchor-multiset count above, applied verbatim with
\partial_{\eta_{l}}r_{\eta}
in place of
\delta r_{\eta}
, gives

\mathbb{E}\bigl\lVert\partial_{\eta_{l}}A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{4}\leq C\,\frac{C^{\prime\prime 4}}{m^{2}},

and the size count of the size paragraph, applied with the same substitution, gives

\mathbb{E}\bigl\lVert\partial_{\eta_{l}}A_{\eta}\bigr\rVert_{F}^{2}=\frac{m(m-1)(m-2)}{(m-1)^{4}}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\partial_{\eta_{l}}r_{\eta})^{2}\bigr]\leq\frac{2\,C^{\prime\prime 2}}{m},

using
\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{\eta_{l}}r_{\eta})^{2}]\leq C^{\prime\prime 2}
,
m(m-2)\leq(m-1)^{2}
, and
(m-1)^{-1}\leq 2\,m^{-1}
for
m\geq 2
. With
Z:=\lVert\partial_{\eta_{l}}A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{2}
and
q:=p/2\in(1,2]
, Lyapunov’s interpolation of moments,
\mathbb{E}Z^{q}\leq\bigl(\mathbb{E}Z\bigr)^{2-q}\bigl(\mathbb{E}Z^{2}\bigr)^{q-1}
for
1\leq q\leq 2
, gives

\mathbb{E}\bigl\lVert\partial_{\eta_{l}}A_{\eta}\bigr\rVert_{F}^{p}=\mathbb{E}\bigl[Z^{q}\bigr]\leq\Bigl(\frac{2\,C^{\prime\prime 2}}{m}\Bigr)^{2-q}\Bigl(C\,\frac{C^{\prime\prime 4}}{m^{2}}\Bigr)^{q-1}\leq C_{p}\,\frac{C^{\prime\prime p}}{m^{p/2}},

the exponents of
C^{\prime\prime}
and
m
combining to
2(2-q)+4(q-1)=2q=p
and
-(2-q)-2(q-1)=-q=-p/2
. The plain form of the order-
p
paragraph, applied with
A=\partial_{\eta_{l}}A_{\eta}
and the tower property, gives

\mathbb{E}\bigl|\partial_{\eta_{l}}S_{m}(\eta)\bigr|^{p}=m^{p/2}\,\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\partial_{\eta_{l}}A_{\eta})\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}\bigr|^{p}\Bigr]\leq C_{p}\,m^{p/2}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl\lVert\partial_{\eta_{l}}A_{\eta}\bigr\rVert_{F}^{p}\leq C_{p}\,C^{\prime\prime p}.

Since
\lVert\nabla_{\eta}S_{m}\rVert^{p}=\bigl(|\partial_{\gamma}S_{m}|^{2}+|\partial_{\lambda}S_{m}|^{2}\bigr)^{p/2}\leq 2^{p/2-1}\bigl(|\partial_{\gamma}S_{m}|^{p}+|\partial_{\lambda}S_{m}|^{p}\bigr)
by
(a+b)^{q}\leq 2^{q-1}(a^{q}+b^{q})
at
q=p/2
, the Sobolev input of Lemma
6
holds with
p\in(2,4]
,
p>d=2
:

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigl\lVert\nabla_{\eta}S_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rVert^{p}\leq N_{p},\qquad N_{p}:=2^{p/2}\,C_{p}\,C^{\prime\prime p},

a constant independent of
m
.

The fixed mesh and the assembly.

Partition
\Upsilon=[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]\times[\lambda_{L},\lambda_{U}]
by a grid of mesh width
\delta>0
, fixed once and independently of
m
: at most
J:=\bigl\lceil(\gamma_{U}-\gamma_{L})/\delta\bigr\rceil\bigl\lceil(\lambda_{U}-\lambda_{L})/\delta\bigr\rceil
cells
Q
, each contained in a square of side
\delta
contained in
\Upsilon
, and at most
(J+1)^{2}
grid points, all
m
-free. The grid values are controlled unconditionally, because
\lVert C^{\mathrm{od}}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq C_{0}
deterministically turns the means-and-variances bound into

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[S_{m}(\eta)^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle=m\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)^{2}\bigr]\leq m\bigl(2\sigma_{\max}^{4}+2\bar{M}_{4}+C_{0}\bigr)\,\mathbb{E}\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq m\bigl(2\sigma_{\max}^{4}+2\bar{M}_{4}+C_{0}\bigr)\frac{4\bar{k}^{4}\lambda_{L}^{-4}}{m-1}\leq\bigl(2\sigma_{\max}^{4}+2\bar{M}_{4}+C_{0}\bigr)\,8\bar{k}^{4}\lambda_{L}^{-4}=:c_{*},

using the size count,
\mathbb{E}\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{m(m-1)(m-2)}{(m-1)^{4}}\,4\bar{k}^{4}\lambda_{L}^{-4}\leq\frac{4\bar{k}^{4}\lambda_{L}^{-4}}{m-1}
, the last step by
m(m-2)\leq(m-1)^{2}
, and
\frac{m}{m-1}\leq 2
for
m\geq 2
. Markov’s inequality and the union bound over the fixed grid give
\max_{\mathrm{grid}}|S_{m}|=O_{p}(1)
. On each cell
Q
, Lemma
6
with field
S_{m}
, dimension
d=2
, exponent
p\in(2,4]
,
p>d
, and
N_{p}
yields

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sup_{t,s\in Q}\bigl|S_{m}(t)-S_{m}(s)\bigr|^{p}\Bigr]\leq C_{2,p}\,N_{p}\,\delta^{p},\qquad\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\sup_{t,s\in Q}\bigl|S_{m}(t)-S_{m}(s)\bigr|\geq u\Bigr)\leq C_{2,p}\,N_{p}\,\delta^{p}\,u^{-p},

both bounds uniform in
(m,Q)
. The assembly over the fixed family of cells,

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|S_{m}(\eta)\bigr|\leq\max_{\mathrm{grid}}\bigl|S_{m}\bigr|+\sum_{Q}\sup_{t,s\in Q}\bigl|S_{m}(t)-S_{m}(s)\bigr|,

is a sum of
J+1
terms, each
O_{p}(1)
uniformly in
m
by the displays above and Markov’s inequality, the cell sum being governed through Hölder’s inequality
(x_{1}+\dots+x_{J})^{p}\leq J^{p-1}\sum_{Q}x_{Q}^{p}
; hence
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}|S_{m}(\eta)|=O_{p}(1)
, that is,

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)\bigr|=O_{p}\bigl(m^{-1/2}\bigr),

the first conclusion of the lemma; it remains to record the uniform bound on the conditional mean.

The uniform conditional mean.

Let
\mu_{m}(\eta):=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)\bigm|\mathbf{X}\bigr]
. By the means-and-variances paragraph,
\mu_{m}(\eta)=\operatorname{tr}\bigl(A_{\eta}C^{\mathrm{od}}\bigr)
with
\lVert C^{\mathrm{od}}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq C_{0}
deterministic, so Cauchy–Schwarz for the Frobenius inner product gives the pointwise bounds

\bigl|\mu_{m}(\eta)\bigr|\leq C_{0}^{1/2}\,\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F},\qquad\bigl|\partial_{\eta_{l}}\mu_{m}(\eta)\bigr|=\bigl|\operatorname{tr}\bigl((\partial_{\eta_{l}}A_{\eta})\,C^{\mathrm{od}}\bigr)\bigr|\leq C_{0}^{1/2}\,\bigl\lVert\partial_{\eta_{l}}A_{\eta}\bigr\rVert_{F},

the differentiation passing under the trace because
C^{\mathrm{od}}
is
\eta
-free and
A_{\eta}
is entrywise
C^{1}
by the path-regularity paragraph. The moment chain of the coefficient field combines the size paragraph, the multiset count of the preceding paragraph with
r_{\eta}
in place of
\delta r_{\eta}
, and Lyapunov’s interpolation of moments at
q=p/2
,
\mathbb{E}Z^{q}\leq(\mathbb{E}Z)^{2-q}(\mathbb{E}Z^{2})^{q-1}
for
1\leq q\leq 2
applied to
Z:=\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{4\bar{k}^{4}\lambda_{L}^{-4}}{m-1},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{4}\leq C\,\frac{K_{r}^{4}}{m^{2}},

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta}\mathbb{E}\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\frac{4\bar{k}^{4}\lambda_{L}^{-4}}{m-1}\Bigr)^{2-q}\Bigl(C\,\frac{K_{r}^{4}}{m^{2}}\Bigr)^{q-1}\leq C_{p}\,m^{-p/2},

with
K_{r}:=2\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
, the exponents of
m
combining as
-(2-q)-2(q-1)=-q=-p/2
and the constants
2^{2-q}\bigl(4\bar{k}^{4}\lambda_{L}^{-4}\bigr)^{2-q}\bigl(CK_{r}^{4}\bigr)^{q-1}
into
C_{p}
. Since
\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\mu_{m}\rVert^{2}=|\partial_{\gamma}\mu_{m}|^{2}+|\partial_{\lambda}\mu_{m}|^{2}
, the norm monotonicity
(a^{2}+b^{2})^{q}\leq 2^{q-1}(a^{q}+b^{q})
at
q=p/2
, the pointwise gradient bound, and the gradient-moments paragraph,
\sup_{\eta}\mathbb{E}\lVert\partial_{\eta_{l}}A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{p}\leq C_{p}C^{\prime\prime p}m^{-p/2}
, give the Sobolev input

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigl\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\mu_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rVert^{p}\leq 2^{p/2}\,C_{0}^{p/2}\,C_{p}\,C^{\prime\prime p}\,m^{-p/2}=:N^{\prime}_{p}.

The assembly mirrors the fixed-mesh paragraph on the same mesh: Markov’s inequality with the pointwise bound at the
(J+1)^{2}
grid points and
\sup_{\eta}\mathbb{E}\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{p}\leq C_{p}m^{-p/2}
gives
\max_{\mathrm{grid}}|\mu_{m}|=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
; on each cell
Q
, Lemma
6
with field
\mu_{m}
, dimension
d=2
, exponent
p\in(2,4]
,
p>d
, and
N^{\prime}_{p}
gives
\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sup_{t,s\in Q}\bigl|\mu_{m}(t)-\mu_{m}(s)\bigr|^{p}\bigr]\leq C_{2,p}\,N^{\prime}_{p}\,\delta^{p}
; and Hölder’s inequality over the fixed family of
J
cells,
(x_{1}+\dots+x_{J})^{p}\leq J^{p-1}\sum_{Q}x_{Q}^{p}
, assembles

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl|\mu_{m}(\eta)\bigr|=O_{p}\bigl(m^{-1/2}\bigr),

the second conclusion of the lemma. This completes the proof.

Under the local alternative,
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}=\boldsymbol{v}+\boldsymbol{f}_{m}
with the signal at the local scale
\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{f}_{m}\rVert^{2}=O_{p}(1)
, the signal part of
\mathcal{R}_{m}
contributes
\boldsymbol{f}_{m}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{f}_{m}
and
2\boldsymbol{f}_{m}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{v}
, bounded by
\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{f}_{m}\rVert^{2}
and, in conditional second moment, by
4\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{f}_{m}\rVert^{2}
: with
c:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{f}_{m}
,
\operatorname{Var}\bigl(2\boldsymbol{f}_{m}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{v}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)=4\,c^{\top}Dc\leq 4\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lVert c\rVert^{2}\leq 4\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{f}_{m}\rVert^{2}
, the conditional covariance of
\boldsymbol{v}
being
D
, with
\lVert D\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
and
\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lVert A_{\eta}\rVert_{F}
. Both bounds are event-free: no truncation event enters, the deterministic constants of the null-regime displays being inflated by the design-measurable factor
\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{f}_{m}\rVert^{2}=O_{p}(1)
. The uniform conclusion of the lemma is not extended to this regime; the gradient-moment input of the process level is noise-only by construction.

Lemma 18
(Chaos identification for the criterion limits)

Let
J\geq 1
, let
\eta_{1}=(\gamma_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,\eta_{J}=(\gamma_{J},\lambda_{J})\in\Upsilon
, and let
G_{\perp,\gamma_{1}},\dots,G_{\perp,\gamma_{J}}
be the projected Gaussian elements of Corollary
1
; for
\eta=(\gamma,\lambda)\in\Upsilon
, write

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\perp}(\eta)

\displaystyle:=\bigl\langle G_{\perp,\gamma},A^{0}_{\eta}G_{\perp,\gamma}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}-\mathbb{E}\bigl\langle G_{\perp,\gamma},A^{0}_{\eta}G_{\perp,\gamma}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\perp}(\eta)

\displaystyle:=\bigl\langle G_{\perp,\gamma},B^{0}_{\eta}G_{\perp,\gamma}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}-\mathbb{E}\bigl\langle G_{\perp,\gamma},B^{0}_{\eta}G_{\perp,\gamma}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}},

with
A^{0}_{\eta},B^{0}_{\eta}
as in Lemma
16
. Then
\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{\perp}(\eta_{l}),\mathcal{D}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})\bigr)_{l\leq J}
is the image of a single isonormal process under the quadratic forms with kernels
(K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp}_{\eta},K^{D,\perp}_{\eta})
(jointly in
l
), a centered second-order Gaussian chaos with, for all
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in\Upsilon
, covariance entries

\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{\perp}(\eta),\mathcal{C}^{\perp}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr)=2\bigl\langle K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp}_{\eta},K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp}_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr\rangle_{\mathrm{HS}}
,

\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{\perp}(\eta),\mathcal{D}^{\perp}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr)=2\bigl\langle K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp}_{\eta},K^{D,\perp}_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr\rangle_{\mathrm{HS}}
,

\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(\mathcal{D}^{\perp}(\eta),\mathcal{D}^{\perp}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr)=2\bigl\langle K^{D,\perp}_{\eta},K^{D,\perp}_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr\rangle_{\mathrm{HS}}
,
with
K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp}_{\eta}(x,x^{\prime})=\sigma(x)(\Pi g_{\eta}\Pi)(x,x^{\prime})\sigma(x^{\prime})
and

K^{D,\perp}_{\eta}(x,x^{\prime})=\sigma(x)\bigl(\Pi(g\circ g)_{\eta}\Pi\bigr)(x,x^{\prime})\sigma(x^{\prime})
, exactly as in Theorem
6
.

Proof.
The proof proceeds in three paragraphs: it identifies the two centered pairings
\mathcal{C}^{\perp},\mathcal{D}^{\perp}
as quadratic forms of a single isonormal process through the operator families of Lemma
16
, identifies the integral kernels of those quadratic forms, and reads off all covariances from the Wick identity through the Hilbert–Schmidt inner products of the kernels.

The isonormal coupling.

Work in the white-noise coupling of Appendix
B.3.3
: the isonormal process
\zeta
there is a centered Gaussian family
\{\zeta(f):f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})\}
with
\mathbb{E}[\zeta(f)\,\zeta(g)]=\langle f,g\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})}
for all
f,g
, and the limit objects of the present lemma are defined in it as evaluations of the single noise
\zeta
: the directional coordinates
Z^{\perp}_{i}(\gamma)=\bigl\langle\zeta,\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\phi_{i}(\cdot;\gamma)\bigr\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})}
of the projected Gaussian elements, jointly over all eigen-indices
i\geq 1
and all bandwidths
\gamma\in[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]
, and the score limit
W=\bigl\langle\zeta,\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}g_{\theta_{0}}\bigr\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})}
; the cross-
\gamma
covariances are the
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
inner products of the feature family
\{\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\phi_{i}(\cdot;\gamma)\}_{i,\gamma}\cup\{\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}g_{\theta_{0}}\}
, as recorded there, reducing to
\Sigma^{\perp}_{ij}(\gamma)
at equal parameters. The argument is limit-object-only: no finite-sample central limit theorem enters, the joint Gaussianity of any finite subcollection being inherited from the linearity of the evaluations of
\zeta
. On the selection sample the same coupling underlies the convergence arguments: the embedding paragraph of part (a) of the proof of Theorem
6
records the joint convergence of the scaled embeddings with the score limit, so the statistics converging to the present pairings are driven, in the limit, by that single coupling.

Functions of a single isonormal process.

In the eigenbasis of
C_{\gamma}
,

\bigl\langle G_{\perp,\gamma},A^{0}_{\eta}G_{\perp,\gamma}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=\sum_{i\geq 1}\frac{G_{i}^{2}}{\mu_{i}(\gamma)+\lambda}=\sum_{i\geq 1}\kappa_{i}(\eta)\,\bigl(Z^{\perp}_{i}(\gamma)\bigr)^{2},\qquad\kappa_{i}(\eta):=\frac{\mu_{i}(\gamma)}{\mu_{i}(\gamma)+\lambda},

with
G_{i}:=\langle G_{\perp,\gamma},e_{i}(\gamma)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=\mu_{i}(\gamma)^{1/2}Z^{\perp}_{i}(\gamma)
. Substituting the coupling and writing
\psi_{i}:=\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\phi_{i}(\cdot;\gamma)
,

\sum_{i\geq 1}\kappa_{i}(\eta)\,\langle\zeta,\psi_{i}\rangle^{2}=\bigl\langle\zeta,\boldsymbol{K}^{(C)}_{\eta}\zeta\bigr\rangle_{L^{2}},\qquad\boldsymbol{K}^{(C)}_{\eta}:=\sum_{i\geq 1}\kappa_{i}(\eta)\,\psi_{i}\otimes\psi_{i}=\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\Pi}P_{\eta}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma},

the last identity being the spectral expansion
P_{\eta}=\sum_{i}\kappa_{i}(\eta)\,\phi_{i}\otimes\phi_{i}
of the smoothing operator
P_{\eta}
of Appendix
B.3.3
,
P_{\eta}:=\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}+\lambda\boldsymbol{I})^{-1}
, whose kernel is
g_{\eta}
. Identically,

\bigl\langle G_{\perp,\gamma},B^{0}_{\eta}G_{\perp,\gamma}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=\langle\zeta,\boldsymbol{K}^{(D)}_{\eta}\zeta\rangle
with

\boldsymbol{K}^{(D)}_{\eta}:=\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\Pi}(P_{\eta})^{2}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}
, the kernel of
(P_{\eta})^{2}
being
\mathbb{E}_{Z}[g_{\eta}(x,Z)g_{\eta}(Z,y)]=(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(x,y)
by operator composition. This proves the first claim: both centered pairings are quadratic forms of the single isonormal process
\zeta
, jointly in
l
through the coupling.

Integral kernels of the quadratic forms.

Kernels of Hilbert–Schmidt operators.
A bounded operator
T
on
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
is Hilbert–Schmidt iff it admits an integral kernel
k_{T}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{P}_{X})
with
(Tu)(x)=\int k_{T}(x,y)\,u(y)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{X}(y)
; the map
T\mapsto k_{T}
is an isometry from the Hilbert–Schmidt class,
\lVert T\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}=\operatorname{tr}(T^{*}T)
, onto a closed subspace of
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{P}_{X})
, and
\operatorname{tr}(T_{1}T_{2})=\langle k_{T_{1}},k_{T_{2}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{P}_{X})}
for Hilbert–Schmidt
T_{1},T_{2}
. Both operators qualify: the partial sums of the spectral form above increase in the Loewner order,
\kappa_{i}(\eta)\geq 0
, so the trace passes to the limit by monotone convergence for the trace on positive semidefinite operators,

\operatorname{tr}\boldsymbol{K}^{(C)}_{\eta}=\sum_{i\geq 1}\kappa_{i}(\eta)\,\lVert\psi_{i}(\gamma)\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sum_{i\geq 1}\kappa_{i}(\eta)\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-1}
,
using
\lVert\psi_{i}(\gamma)\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=\Sigma^{\perp}_{ii}(\gamma)
(the covariance of the directional coordinates, Appendix
B.3.3
),
\Sigma^{\perp}_{ii}(\gamma)\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
(Corollary
1
),
\kappa_{i}(\eta)\leq\mu_{i}(\gamma)\lambda_{L}^{-1}
, and the trace identity
\sum_{i}\mu_{i}(\gamma)=\operatorname{tr}\boldsymbol{L}_{\gamma}=1
; identically
\operatorname{tr}\boldsymbol{K}^{(D)}_{\eta}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-1}
through
\kappa_{i}(\eta)^{2}\leq\kappa_{i}(\eta)
, so both operators are trace class, hence Hilbert–Schmidt. The spectral forms above record the integral kernel
g_{\eta}
of
P_{\eta}
; the composition rule for integral kernels,
(ST)(x,y)=\int S(x,z)\,T(z,y)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{X}(z)
, gives the composed kernel
(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(x,y)=\mathbb{E}_{Z}[g_{\eta}(x,Z)\,g_{\eta}(Z,y)]
of
(P_{\eta})^{2}
; the
\boldsymbol{\Pi}
-sandwich composes the kernel with the projection in each argument,
(\Pi k\,\Pi)(x,x^{\prime})
denoting the kernel of
\boldsymbol{\Pi}T\boldsymbol{\Pi}
for an operator
T
with kernel
k
; and the multiplication sandwich
\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}\,\cdot\,\boldsymbol{M}_{\sigma}
multiplies it by the factor
\sigma(x)\sigma(x^{\prime})
. Hence the quadratic-form kernels have the integral kernels

K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp}_{\eta}(x,x^{\prime})=\sigma(x)\,(\Pi g_{\eta}\Pi)(x,x^{\prime})\,\sigma(x^{\prime}),\qquad K^{D,\perp}_{\eta}(x,x^{\prime})=\sigma(x)\,\bigl(\Pi(g\circ g)_{\eta}\Pi\bigr)(x,x^{\prime})\,\sigma(x^{\prime}).

(30)

This proves the kernel identification.

The covariances.

All three covariance families follow from one computation. The two operators share the single feature family
\{\psi_{i}(\gamma)\}_{i}
: with the spectral weights
\kappa^{(1)}_{i}(\eta):=\kappa_{i}(\eta)
of
P_{\eta}
and
\kappa^{(2)}_{i}(\eta):=\kappa_{i}(\eta)^{2}
of
P_{\eta}^{2}
,

\boldsymbol{K}^{(C)}_{\eta}=\sum_{i\geq 1}\kappa^{(1)}_{i}(\eta)\,\psi_{i}(\gamma)\otimes\psi_{i}(\gamma)

and

\boldsymbol{K}^{(D)}_{\eta}=\sum_{i\geq 1}\kappa^{(2)}_{i}(\eta)\,\psi_{i}(\gamma)\otimes\psi_{i}(\gamma)
.
Wick’s identity for quadratic forms of an isonormal process,

\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(\langle\zeta,K\zeta\rangle,\langle\zeta,K^{\prime}\zeta\rangle\bigr)=2\operatorname{tr}(KK^{\prime})
, reduces every covariance between two of the pairings to the single parametrized identity

2\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\boldsymbol{K}_{\eta}\boldsymbol{K}_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr)=2\sum_{i,j\geq 1}\kappa^{(p)}_{i}(\eta)\,\kappa^{(q)}_{j}(\eta^{\prime})\,\bigl\langle\psi_{i}(\gamma),\psi_{j}(\gamma^{\prime})\bigr\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})}^{2},\qquad(p,q)\in\{1,2\}^{2},

the Gram matrix
\bigl(\langle\psi_{i}(\gamma),\psi_{j}(\gamma^{\prime})\rangle\bigr)_{i,j}
being free of
\lambda
, so that
\lambda
enters only through the scalar spectral weights. By the trace–kernel isometry of the integral-kernels paragraph,
\operatorname{tr}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\eta}\boldsymbol{K}_{\eta^{\prime}})
is the
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{P}_{X})
inner product of the integral kernels (
30
), so the three instances
(p,q)=(1,1)
,
(1,2)
,
(2,2)
are exactly the three covariance families of the statement,

\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{\perp}(\eta),\mathcal{C}^{\perp}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr)=2\bigl\langle K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp}_{\eta},K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp}_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr\rangle_{\mathrm{HS}}
,

\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{\perp}(\eta),\mathcal{D}^{\perp}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr)=2\bigl\langle K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp}_{\eta},K^{D,\perp}_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr\rangle_{\mathrm{HS}}
,

\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(\mathcal{D}^{\perp}(\eta),\mathcal{D}^{\perp}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr)=2\bigl\langle K^{D,\perp}_{\eta},K^{D,\perp}_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr\rangle_{\mathrm{HS}}
,
cross-covariances between distinct
\gamma_{l}
covered by the same computation, the coupling being simultaneous in
\gamma
. This completes the proof.

B.3.5
Proof of Theorem
6
: Uniform Limit Theory of the CV-Criterion Process

Proof.
Assume Assumption
7
(iii)
0<\sigma_{\min}^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}(X)\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
a.s., and (v) there exist
p\in(2,4]
and
\bar{M}_{2p}<\infty
with
\sup_{x}\mathbb{E}[v^{2p}\mid X=x]\leq\bar{M}_{2p}
, and for part (b)
\|\Pi f\|_{\infty}<\infty
;
\varrho>1/4
is Assumption
4
(iii). Throughout the proof of part (a) the conditional
(2p)
-moment clause
\sup_{a}\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0,a}^{2p}\mid X_{a}]\leq\bar{M}_{2p}<\infty
for the exponent
p\in(2,4]
of Assumption
7
(v) is in force, as required by the projected-residual hypothesis of Lemma
17
and by the order-
p
moment bounds below. We prove (a)–(c) through the bilinear representation of Lemma
16
: the centered quadratic statistics are quadratic forms of the single scaled mean embedding against deterministic population operators, the finite-dimensional limits follow from the embedding CLT by continuous mapping with the chaos specification identified by Lemma
18
, and stochastic equicontinuity follows from uniform gradient moments through the shifted-grid containment and equicontinuity lemmas (Lemmas
7
and
8
); no process-level entropy criterion enters.

Linearization.

In the null and local regimes Lemma
13
gives, uniformly in
\eta\in\Upsilon
,

\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta;\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{0}_{\perp})=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}-2U_{m}(\eta)+Q_{m}(\eta)+R_{m}(\eta)+o_{p}(m^{-1}),

with

\displaystyle U_{m}(\eta)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j}),

(31)

\displaystyle Q_{m}(\eta)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{m(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}(\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0})^{2}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})^{2},

(32)

\displaystyle R_{m}(\eta)

\displaystyle:=\frac{2}{m(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j<k\\
j,k\neq i\end{subarray}}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{k})\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,k}^{0},

(33)

g_{\eta}(x,y)=\langle k_{\gamma}(x,\cdot),(C_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}k_{\gamma}(y,\cdot)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}
,
and
F_{m}(\eta):=m[\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)-m^{-1}\sum_{i}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0})^{2}]=-2mU_{m}(\eta)+mQ_{m}(\eta)+mR_{m}(\eta)+o_{p}(1)
.
We call
F_{m}
the centered CV criterion process, the criterion being evaluated at the infeasible projected residuals in the notation of Lemma
15
in parts (a) and (c) below, the transfer to the feasible residuals being recorded in the Feasibility paragraph of part (a); part (b) works with the feasible criterion directly, through the pathwise leave-one-out bound available there.

Proof of (a): null limit (the local
O(m^{-1/2})
case is part (c)).

Work with
F_{m}
above. Let
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon(\theta_{0})
be the infeasible residual at the true
\theta_{0}
, with
\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}=O_{p}(m^{-\varrho})
for some
\varrho>1/4
, and set
\varepsilon_{\perp}^{0}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\varepsilon_{0}
. By Lemma
15
the estimation-effect remainder is
o_{p}(1)
uniformly in
\eta
, so the limit theory is developed on
\varepsilon_{\perp}^{0}
and transferred back to the feasible residuals
\varepsilon_{\perp}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\hat{\varepsilon}
at the end of the proof. The projection is not a perturbation to be removed: the embedding of
\varepsilon_{\perp}^{0}
converges to the projected Gaussian element
G_{\perp,\gamma}
of Corollary
1
, so the rank-
d_{\theta}
projection is carried by the limit itself.

Finite-dimensional convergence: the embedding.

Fix
\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{J}\in\Upsilon
and let
M_{m}(\gamma):=m^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}w_{i}\,k_{\gamma}(X_{i},\cdot)
be the scaled mean embedding built on the projected residual vector
w=\varepsilon_{\perp}^{0}
, with transport
\widetilde{M}_{m}(\gamma)
to the common space. The joint convergence of the embeddings is that of Corollary
1
, transported to the selection sample: the Hilbert–space CLT and the projection correction are sample-size-agnostic, so

\bigl(\widetilde{M}_{m}(\gamma_{1}),\dots,\widetilde{M}_{m}(\gamma_{J})\bigr)\xrightarrow{\;d\;}\bigl(G_{\perp,\gamma_{1}},\dots,G_{\perp,\gamma_{J}}\bigr)\quad\text{in }\textstyle\bigoplus_{l\leq J}\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{l}}

jointly with the score limit. The transfer to size
m
is verbatim: the matrix averages behind Assumption
5
(ii) are laws of large numbers in the sample size, Assumption
5
(iii) supplies the moment conditions of the joint central limit theorem, and the sieve rate of Assumption
6
(iii) holds at size
m
, since
\hat{\theta}
, the sieve, and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
are formed on each subsample (Section
4.1
). The embedding CLT is the only central-limit input of part (a); the remaining inputs are laws of large numbers, continuous mapping, and moment bounds.

Finite-dimensional convergence: the drifts.

The deterministic part of
F_{m}
is assembled from the conditional means of
mU_{m}
,
mQ_{m}
,
mR_{m}
given
\mathbf{X}
. With
c_{ij}:=\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{0}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{0}\mid\mathbf{X}]
,

\nu_{m}(\eta):=\mathbb{E}[mU_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}]=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}c_{ij}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j}),

\rho_{m}(\eta):=\mathbb{E}[mR_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}]=\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j<k,\,j,k\neq i}c_{jk}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{k}),

\mathbb{E}[mQ_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}]=\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}c_{jj}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}.

The route to the limits is explicit in the conditional second moments of
w=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\varepsilon_{0}
. Under
H_{0}
the innovations are conditionally independent and centered with
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0}\varepsilon_{0}^{\top}\mid\mathbf{X}]=D:=\operatorname{diag}(\sigma^{2}(X_{1}),\dots,\sigma^{2}(X_{m}))
, and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}=\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\mathbb{G}}M\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}
with
M:=(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}})^{-1}
and
\hat{g}_{i}:=\hat{g}_{\hat{\theta}}(X_{i})
, so expanding
(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})_{ij}
gives, the sandwich being conditionally deterministic under the convention of Remark
2
,

c_{ij}=\delta_{ij}\,\sigma^{2}(X_{i})-\sigma^{2}(X_{i})\,\hat{g}_{i}^{\top}M\hat{g}_{j}-\sigma^{2}(X_{j})\,\hat{g}_{i}^{\top}M\hat{g}_{j}+\hat{g}_{i}^{\top}M\bigl(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}D\hat{\mathbb{G}}\bigr)M\hat{g}_{j}.

For
i\neq j
each of the three terms is an
m
-normalized product of the design factors with the middle factor
mM
:
mM\xrightarrow{p}\Gamma_{\theta_{0}}^{-1}
by Assumption
5
(ii) at the selection sample, and
m^{-1}\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}D\hat{\mathbb{G}}\xrightarrow{p}\mathbb{E}[\sigma^{2}(X)g_{\theta_{0}}(X)g_{\theta_{0}}(X)^{\top}]
by the law of large numbers. The fitted factors are replaced by their population counterparts at
o_{p}(1)
cost: splitting
\hat{g}_{i}-g_{\theta_{0}}(X_{i})=\bigl(g_{\hat{\theta}}(X_{i})-g_{\theta_{0}}(X_{i})\bigr)+\bigl(\hat{g}_{i}-g_{\hat{\theta}}(X_{i})\bigr)
, the
\hat{\theta}
-drift is
O_{p}(m^{-\varrho})
element-wise by the moment bounds of Assumption
5
(i) and the Lipschitz-in-
\theta
structure of
g_{\theta}
on the compact
\Theta
, and the sieve-fit deviation is
O_{p}(m^{-1/4})
by the sup-norm side condition of Assumption
6
(iii), the
\hat{\theta}
-drift being absorbed since
\varrho>1/4
, so that
\sup_{i}\lVert\hat{g}_{i}\rVert\leq\bar{G}+O_{p}(m^{-1/4})
in both regimes, the bounded-score clause of Assumption
5
(i) giving
\sup_{i}\lVert g_{\theta_{0}}(X_{i})\rVert\leq\bar{G}
and the sup-norm side condition of Assumption
6
(iii) covering the fitted factors. The uniformity in
(i,j)
is licensed by these bounds together with the Loewner order:
\hat{g}_{i}^{\top}M\hat{g}_{j}
is bounded in modulus by
(\bar{G}+O_{p}(m^{-1/4}))^{2}\lVert M\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
, and
M(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}D\hat{\mathbb{G}})M\preceq\sigma_{\max}^{2}M
by congruence from
D\preceq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{m}
, so that
\hat{g}_{i}^{\top}M(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}D\hat{\mathbb{G}})M\hat{g}_{j}
is bounded in modulus by
\sigma_{\max}^{2}(\bar{G}+O_{p}(m^{-1/4}))^{2}\lVert M\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
, with
\lVert M\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
by Assumption
5
(ii). Hence
c_{ij}=m^{-1}c^{*}(X_{i},X_{j})+o_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly in
(i,j)
, with
c^{*}
as recorded in Section
4.2
, while the diagonal keeps its leading value,
c_{jj}=\sigma^{2}(X_{j})+O_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly in
j
: at
i=j
the two correction terms of the expansion are bounded by the two bounds just recorded, uniformly in
j
.

The three drift averages are therefore
m
-normalized averages of the recorded factors,

\nu_{m}(\eta)=\frac{m}{m-1}\cdot\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}c^{*}(X_{i},X_{j})\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})+o_{p}(1),

\rho_{m}(\eta)=\frac{m^{2}}{(m-1)^{2}}\cdot\frac{2}{m^{3}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j<k,\,j,k\neq i}c^{*}(X_{j},X_{k})\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{k})+o_{p}(1),

\mathbb{E}[mQ_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}]=\frac{m^{2}}{(m-1)^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j}\sigma^{2}(X_{j})\Bigl(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i\neq j}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}\Bigr)+O_{p}(m^{-1}).

The averages converge by the
U
- and
V
-statistic-type laws of large numbers, uniformly in
\eta
through the Lipschitz modulus of
g_{\eta}
(Lemma
16
(iii)(a)): the off-diagonal averages have the limits
\nu(\eta)
and
\rho(\eta)
, the second using the symmetry
\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}[g_{\eta}(X_{1},X_{2})g_{\eta}(X_{1},X_{3})]=(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{2},X_{3})
; in the diagonal average, the inner sum
\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i\neq j}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}\xrightarrow{p}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\bigl[g_{\eta}(X_{j},Z)^{2}\bigr]
uniformly in
(j,\eta)
(bounded summands, Chebyshev with a union bound over
j
, Lipschitz modulus in
\eta
), which assembles the limit
V(\eta)
. Collecting the three limits,

\sup_{\eta}\bigl|\nu_{m}(\eta)-\nu(\eta)\bigr|\xrightarrow{p}0,\qquad\sup_{\eta}\bigl|\rho_{m}(\eta)-\rho(\eta)\bigr|\xrightarrow{p}0,\qquad\sup_{\eta}\bigl|\mathbb{E}[mQ_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}]-V(\eta)\bigr|\xrightarrow{p}0,

where

\nu(\eta)=\mathbb{E}\bigl[c^{*}(X_{1},X_{2})g_{\eta}(X_{1},X_{2})\bigr],\qquad\rho(\eta)=\mathbb{E}\bigl[c^{*}(X_{1},X_{2})(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{1},X_{2})\bigr],

V(\eta)=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sigma^{2}(X_{2})g_{\eta}(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}\bigr],

the first two being the recorded projection corrections of Section
4.2
. Hence the deterministic part of
F_{m}
converges uniformly to
-2\nu+V+\rho=V+\Delta
with
\Delta:=\rho-2\nu
.

Finite-dimensional convergence: the centered statistics.

Write
\bar{U}_{m}(\eta):=mU_{m}(\eta)-\nu_{m}(\eta)
and
\bar{R}_{m}(\eta):=mR_{m}(\eta)-\rho_{m}(\eta)
.

The
U
-coordinate.
By Lemma
16
(i),
mU_{m}(\eta)=Q^{U}_{m}(\eta)-D_{m}(\eta)
with

Q^{U}_{m}(\eta):=\frac{m}{m-1}\bigl\langle\widetilde{M}_{m}(\gamma),\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}\widetilde{M}_{m}(\gamma)\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}},\qquad D_{m}(\eta):=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i}w_{i}^{2}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{i}),

so by linearity of the conditional expectation
\bar{U}_{m}(\eta)=\bigl[Q^{U}_{m}(\eta)-\mathbb{E}\bigl(Q^{U}_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)\bigr]-\bigl[D_{m}(\eta)-\mathbb{E}\bigl(D_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)\bigr]
. The map
x\mapsto\bigl(\langle x_{\gamma_{l}},\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta_{l}}x_{\gamma_{l}}\rangle\bigr)_{l\leq J}
on
\bigoplus_{l\leq J}\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{l}}
is continuous and the operators
\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta_{l}}
are deterministic, so the continuous mapping theorem applied to the joint convergence of the embeddings gives the joint convergence of the bilinear forms to
\bigl(\langle G_{\perp,\gamma_{l}},A^{0}_{\eta_{l}}G_{\perp,\gamma_{l}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{l}}}\bigr)_{l\leq J}
.

The conditional mean of
Q^{U}_{m}(\eta)
reduces by the trace, the sandwich being conditionally deterministic under the convention of Remark
2
. Since
\widehat{\Sigma}_{m}(\gamma):=\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{M}_{m}(\gamma)\widetilde{M}_{m}(\gamma)^{*}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i,j}c_{ij}\,\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(X_{i})\otimes\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(X_{j})
and
\langle\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(X_{i}),\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}\widetilde{k}_{\gamma}(X_{j})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{0}}}=g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
(the reproducing-kernel identification in the statement of Lemma
16
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(Q^{U}_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{m}{m-1}\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}\widehat{\Sigma}_{m}(\gamma)\bigr)=\frac{m}{m-1}\cdot\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i,j}c_{ij}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})

\displaystyle=\nu_{m}(\eta)+\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i}c_{ii}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{i}),

the last step by
\sum_{i\neq j}c_{ij}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})=(m-1)\nu_{m}(\eta)
. By the Drifts paragraph the diagonal average converges by the law of large numbers,
c_{ii}=\sigma^{2}(X_{i})+O_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly in
i
, so
\mathbb{E}\bigl(Q^{U}_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)\xrightarrow{p}a(\eta):=\nu(\eta)+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sigma^{2}(X)g_{\eta}(X,X)\bigr]
uniformly in
\eta
; while the conditional mean
\mathbb{E}[D_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}]=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i}c_{ii}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{i})
converges to
d(\eta):=\mathbb{E}[\sigma^{2}(X)g_{\eta}(X,X)]
by the law of large numbers and
c_{ii}=\sigma^{2}(X_{i})+O_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly in
i
, uniformly in
\eta
through the Lipschitz modulus of
g_{\eta}
; the fluctuation
D_{m}(\eta)-\mathbb{E}[D_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}]
is the centered quadratic form at
A^{(D)}_{\eta}:=\frac{1}{m-1}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\operatorname{diag}\bigl(g_{\eta}(X_{1},X_{1}),\dots,g_{\eta}(X_{m},X_{m})\bigr)\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
, symmetric and conditionally deterministic under Remark
2
, with
\lVert A^{(D)}_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{m}}{m-1}\,\bar{k}\,\lambda_{L}^{-1}\leq 2\,\bar{k}\,\lambda_{L}^{-1}\,m^{-1/2}
for
m\geq 2
, so (
29
) gives the variance
O(m^{-1})
uniformly in
\eta
and the fluctuation is
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
; so the second bracket of
\bar{U}_{m}(\eta)
vanishes in the limit. Slutsky’s theorem gives

\bar{U}_{m}(\eta_{l})\;\Rightarrow\;\bigl\langle G_{\perp,\gamma_{l}},A^{0}_{\eta_{l}}G_{\perp,\gamma_{l}}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{l}}}-a(\eta_{l}),\qquad l\leq J\ \text{jointly}.

Since
\mathbb{E}[\bar{U}_{m}(\eta)]=0
for every
m
while the second moments are uniformly bounded, the shifted limit has mean zero,
a(\eta)=\mathbb{E}\langle G_{\perp,\gamma},A^{0}_{\eta}G_{\perp,\gamma}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}
:
\bar{U}_{m}(\eta)
is the centered quadratic form at the
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
-sandwich of the design-measurable
A^{(g)}_{\eta,ij}:=\frac{1}{m-1}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})\mathbf{1}_{i\neq j}
, symmetric, zero-diagonal, and conditionally deterministic under Remark
2
through the sandwich, so (
29
) gives
\sup_{m\geq 2,\eta}\mathbb{E}[\bar{U}_{m}(\eta)^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]\leq(2\sigma_{\max}^{4}+\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p})\,2\,\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
through
\lVert A^{(g)}_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{m}{m-1}\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
and
\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}
; the same display at the composed kernel, whose coefficient matrix has entries
\frac{m-2}{(m-1)^{2}}(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{i},X_{j})\mathbf{1}_{i\neq j}
and Frobenius norm at most
\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
, bounds the centered bilinear part of the
R
-coordinate below; and the limit is the centered chaos
\mathcal{C}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})
of Lemma
18
.

The
R
-coordinate.
The same device applies with
\widetilde{B}^{0}_{\eta}
in place of
\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}
: by Lemma
16
(ii),

mR_{m}(\eta)=\frac{m(m-2)}{m-1}\,U_{m}(h_{\eta}^{(R)})+\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta),

where
mU_{m}(h_{\eta}^{(R)})
is the bilinear form of
\widetilde{B}^{0}_{\eta}
with the composed-kernel diagonal correction, and
\sup_{\eta}|\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)|=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
. The conditional mean of the bilinear form reduces by the same trace computation, the reproducing kernel of
\widetilde{B}^{0}_{\eta}
being the composed
(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{i},X_{j})
, and converges to
b(\eta):=\rho(\eta)+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sigma^{2}(X)(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X,X)\bigr]
uniformly in
\eta
, the deterministic bookkeeping
\rho_{m}\to\rho
cancelling against the off-diagonal part of the trace; the same mean-zero argument identifies
b(\eta)=\mathbb{E}\langle G_{\perp,\gamma},B^{0}_{\eta}G_{\perp,\gamma}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}
, and
\mathcal{R}_{m}
is
o_{p}(1)
pointwise. Hence
\bar{R}_{m}(\eta_{l})\Rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})
jointly.

The
Q
-coordinate.
The fluctuation of
mQ_{m}
vanishes at the limit. Regrouping by the squared residual,

\bar{Q}_{m}(\eta):=mQ_{m}(\eta)-\mathbb{E}\bigl[mQ_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}B_{\eta}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}-\operatorname{tr}(B_{\eta}D),\qquad B_{\eta}:=\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\operatorname{diag}(s_{\eta})\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}},

with
s_{\eta,j}:=\sum_{i\neq j}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}
, the second identity following from
w=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\varepsilon_{0}
together with the conditional independence and centering of the innovations. Lemma
14
, applied to
\zeta=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
and
A=B_{\eta}
(symmetric and conditionally deterministic under Remark
2
), gives by the variance bound (
29
), uniformly in
\eta
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bar{Q}_{m}(\eta)^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]\leq\bigl(2\sigma_{\max}^{4}+\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p}\bigr)\lVert B_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\bigl(2\sigma_{\max}^{4}+\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p}\bigr)\frac{m\bigl((m-1)\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}\bigr)^{2}}{(m-1)^{4}},

the second inequality by
\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\operatorname{diag}(s_{\eta})\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\lVert\operatorname{diag}(s_{\eta})\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}
(idempotence of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
,
\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
) and the envelope
s_{\eta,j}\leq(m-1)\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
from
|g_{\eta}|\leq\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}
. Hence
\mathbb{E}[\bar{Q}_{m}(\eta)^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]=O(m^{-1})
uniformly in
\eta
, and
\bar{Q}_{m}(\eta_{l})=o_{p}(1)
pointwise by Chebyshev’s inequality, so the
Q
-coordinate of the fidi limit is deterministic. Assembling the three coordinates,

\bigl(mU_{m}(\eta_{l}),mQ_{m}(\eta_{l}),mR_{m}(\eta_{l})\bigr)_{l\leq J}\xrightarrow{d}\bigl(\nu(\eta_{l})+\mathcal{C}^{\perp}(\eta_{l}),\;V(\eta_{l}),\;\rho(\eta_{l})+\mathcal{D}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})\bigr)_{l\leq J},

the chaoses carrying the kernels
K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp},K^{D,\perp}
of Lemma
18
.

Stochastic equicontinuity.
