Equicontinuity and boundedness follow from uniform gradient moments through the shifted-grid equicontinuity lemma (Lemma
8
), whose geometric input is the containment of short pairs by shifted grids (Lemma
7
); the uniform remainder bound of Lemma
17
and deterministic drift moduli supply its additive-term hypotheses. Alongside the centered statistics
\bar{U}_{m}
and
\bar{Q}_{m}
of the finite-dimensional paragraph, write

mR^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}(\eta):=\frac{m(m-2)}{m-1}\,U_{m}\bigl(h^{(R)}_{\eta}\bigr),\qquad\bar{R}^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}(\eta):=mR^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}(\eta)-\mathbb{E}\bigl[mR^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}(\eta)\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr],

so that
mR_{m}=mR^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}+\mathcal{R}_{m}
by Lemma
16
(ii).

Step A: kernel-derivative envelopes.
By the derivative-envelope clause of Lemma
16
(iii)(a), the kernels
g_{\eta}
,
(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})
, and
r_{\eta}
are entrywise
C^{1}
on
\Upsilon
with, for
l\in\{\gamma,\lambda\}
,

\sup_{\eta,\,x,y}\bigl|\partial_{\eta_{l}}g_{\eta}(x,y)\bigr|\leq C_{g}=:G_{1},\qquad\sup_{\eta,\,x,y}\bigl|\partial_{\eta_{l}}(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(x,y)\bigr|\leq C^{\prime}_{g}=:G_{2},

and
|\partial_{\eta_{l}}(g_{\eta}^{2})(x,y)|\leq 2\bar{k}\,\lambda_{L}^{-1}G_{1}
by the product rule and
|g_{\eta}|\leq\bar{k}\,\lambda_{L}^{-1}
; the constants are deterministic and
\mathbf{X}
-free. The same term-by-term differentiation applied to the finite sums defining the centered statistics shows that
\bar{U}_{m}
,
\bar{R}^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}
, and
\bar{Q}_{m}
have
C^{1}
paths on
\Upsilon
, with gradients the formal term-by-term derivatives; entrywise boundedness uniform in
(\eta,x,y)
is all that Step B uses, and no second derivative enters.

Step B: gradient moments, conditional on
\mathbf{X}
.

Throughout the step,
w=\varepsilon^{0}_{\perp}
,
D=\operatorname{diag}\bigl(\sigma^{2}(X_{1}),\dots,\sigma^{2}(X_{m})\bigr)
, and the entries of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
are
c_{ij}=\mathbb{E}[w_{i}w_{j}\mid\mathbf{X}]
.
The
U
-piece.
The definition of
U_{m}
gives
mU_{m}(\eta)=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}w_{i}w_{j}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
, the off-diagonal form of Lemma
16
(i), and term-by-term differentiation of the finite sums gives
\nabla_{\eta}\nu_{m}(\eta)=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}c_{ij}\,\partial_{\eta}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
; hence

\nabla_{\eta}\bar{U}_{m}(\eta)=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}\bigl(w_{i}w_{j}-c_{ij}\bigr)\,\partial_{\eta}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A^{(U)}_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}-\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A^{(U)}_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\bigr),

where
A^{(U)}_{\eta,ij}:=\frac{1}{m-1}\,\partial_{\eta}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})\,\mathbf{1}_{i\neq j}
is symmetric, zero-diagonal and design-measurable, the identity holding componentwise in
(\gamma,\lambda)
, the second identity inserting
w=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
and computing the conditional mean as
\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\bigr)=\operatorname{tr}\bigl(A\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\bigr)=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}c_{ij}\,\partial_{\eta}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
by cyclicity of the trace. By Step A,

\bigl\lVert A^{(U)}_{\eta}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\bigl(\partial_{\eta}g_{\eta}\bigr)^{2}(X_{i},X_{j})\leq\frac{m(m-1)\,G_{1}^{2}}{(m-1)^{2}}\leq 2\,G_{1}^{2}\qquad\text{for }m\geq 2,

deterministically. The order-
p
moment bound (
21
) (Lemma
9
), instantiated at the coefficient matrix
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A^{(U)}_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
, conditionally deterministic under Remark
2
, gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\lvert\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A^{(U)}_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}

\displaystyle-\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A^{(U)}_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\bigr)\bigr\rvert^{p}\Bigm|\mathbf{X}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\,\bigl\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A^{(U)}_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{p}\leq C_{p}\,\bigl\lVert A^{(U)}_{\eta}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{p}\leq C_{p}\,(2G_{1})^{p}=:C_{U},

the middle step by
\bigl\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}
(
\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
) and the last by
\lVert A^{(U)}_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 2\,G_{1}
from the envelope displayed above, where and below
C_{p}:=C_{p}(\sigma_{\max},\bar{M}_{2p})
denotes the constant of (
21
). Hence
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\lvert\nabla_{\eta}\bar{U}_{m}(\eta)\rvert^{p}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]\leq C_{U}
uniformly in
m\geq 2
, and the bound being
\mathbf{X}
-free it holds unconditionally.
The
R
-piece.
The same computation applies to the bilinear part: by the off-diagonal expansion of
U_{m}
at the composed kernel (clause (i) of Lemma
16
with
(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})
in place of
g_{\eta}
),
mU_{m}\bigl(h^{(R)}_{\eta}\bigr)=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}w_{i}w_{j}\,(g\circ g)_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
, so

\nabla_{\eta}\bar{R}^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}(\eta)=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A^{(R)}_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}-\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A^{(R)}_{\eta}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\bigr),\qquad A^{(R)}_{\eta,ij}:=\frac{m-2}{(m-1)^{2}}\,\partial_{\eta}(g\circ g)_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})\,\mathbf{1}_{i\neq j},

the prefactor being
\frac{m(m-2)}{m-1}\cdot\frac{1}{m(m-1)}=\frac{m-2}{(m-1)^{2}}
, the anchor-count prefactor of the reduction of Lemma
16
(ii) times the
U_{m}
normalization; and
\bigl\lVert A^{(R)}_{\eta}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{(m-2)\sqrt{m(m-1)}}{(m-1)^{2}}\,G_{2}\leq 4\,G_{2}
for
m\geq 2
; the same order-
p
enumeration gives

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\lvert\nabla_{\eta}\bar{R}^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}(\eta)\rvert^{p}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]\leq C_{p}(\sigma_{\max},\bar{M}_{2p})\,(4G_{2})^{p}=:C_{R}.

The
Q
-piece.
The centered diagonal statistic is regrouped by the squared residual, from the matrix form
B_{\eta}=\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\operatorname{diag}(s_{\eta})\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
of the finite-dimensional paragraph and the conditional centering of the innovations,

\bar{Q}_{m}(\eta)=\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}\,\xi_{j},\qquad\xi_{j}:=w_{j}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\bigl[w_{j}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr],

and the coefficients
\xi_{j}
are free of
\eta
, so

\nabla_{\eta}\bar{Q}_{m}(\eta)=\sum_{j=1}^{m}a_{\eta,j}\,\xi_{j},\qquad a_{\eta,j}:=\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\partial_{\eta}\bigl(g_{\eta}^{2}\bigr)(X_{i},X_{j}),

with
\bigl\lvert\partial_{\eta}(g_{\eta}^{2})\bigr\rvert=2\lvert g_{\eta}\rvert\,\lvert\partial_{\eta}g_{\eta}\rvert\leq 2\bar{k}\,\lambda_{L}^{-1}G_{1}
, whence
\lvert a_{\eta,j}\rvert\leq\frac{2\bar{k}\,\lambda_{L}^{-1}G_{1}}{m-1}
and

\sum_{j=1}^{m}a_{\eta,j}^{2}\leq\frac{4\,m\,\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}G_{1}^{2}}{(m-1)^{2}}\leq\frac{16\,\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}G_{1}^{2}}{m}\qquad\text{for }m\geq 2.

Inserting
w=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}
gives the sandwich reduction

\sum_{j}a_{\eta,j}\xi_{j}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\operatorname{diag}(a_{\eta})\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}-\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\operatorname{diag}(a_{\eta})\,\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\bigr),

turning
\sum_{j}a_{\eta,j}\xi_{j}
into the centered quadratic form at the diagonal matrix
\operatorname{diag}(a_{\eta})
, where the centered form requires no zero-diagonal condition, the diagonal entries shifting only the subtracted conditional mean; the same order-
p
enumeration gives

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\lvert\nabla_{\eta}\bar{Q}_{m}(\eta)\rvert^{p}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]\leq C_{p}\Bigl(\sum_{j}a_{\eta,j}^{2}\Bigr)^{p/2}\leq C_{p}\,\frac{\bigl(16\,\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}G_{1}^{2}\bigr)^{p/2}}{m^{p/2}}=:C_{Q}\,m^{-p/2}.

All three gradient bounds hold conditionally on
\mathbf{X}
with
\mathbf{X}
-free constants, hence unconditionally.

Drift modulus.

The drift
\mathrm{dr}_{m}:=-2\nu_{m}+\mathbb{E}[mQ_{m}\mid\mathbf{X}]+\rho_{m}
is the additive term of the decomposition of Step C and enters the equicontinuity argument through hypothesis (ii) of Lemma
8
, so it is controlled by a Lipschitz modulus and uniform convergence rather than by a gradient moment. The drifts are bounded and Lipschitz: the recorded expansion of the Drifts paragraph,
c_{jk}=-\sigma^{2}(X_{j})H_{jk}-\sigma^{2}(X_{k})H_{jk}+(HDH)_{jk}
with the idempotent
H=\hat{\mathbb{G}}M\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}
, gives
|H_{jk}|^{2}\leq H_{jj}H_{kk}\leq 1
(the Gram bound for positive semidefinite matrices,
H_{jj}\leq 1
by idempotence) and
\lvert(HDH)_{jk}\rvert\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}\sqrt{H_{jj}H_{kk}}
(Cauchy–Schwarz with
\lVert H_{j\cdot}\rVert^{2}=(H^{2})_{jj}=H_{jj}
), so
\lvert c_{jk}\rvert\leq 3\sigma^{2}_{\max}
deterministically and hence
\lvert c^{*}\rvert\leq 3\sigma^{2}_{\max}
a.s. through the uniform convergence of the Drifts paragraph; consequently the limit drifts are Lipschitz with
\mathbf{X}
-free constants,

\displaystyle\max\Bigl(\bigl\lvert\nu(\eta)-\nu(\eta^{\prime})\bigr\rvert,\,\bigl\lvert\rho(\eta)-\rho(\eta^{\prime})\bigr\rvert,\,\bigl\lvert V(\eta)-V(\eta^{\prime})\bigr\rvert\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq L_{\mathrm{dr}}\,\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert,

\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{dr}}

\displaystyle:=\bigl(3\sigma^{2}_{\max}C_{g}\bigr)\vee\bigl(3\sigma^{2}_{\max}C^{\prime}_{g}\bigr)

\displaystyle\vee\bigl(2\bar{k}\,\lambda_{L}^{-1}\sigma^{2}_{\max}C_{g}\bigr),

and bounded,
\lVert\nu\rVert_{\infty}\leq 3\sigma^{2}_{\max}\bar{k}\,\lambda_{L}^{-1}
,
\lVert\rho\rVert_{\infty}\leq 3\sigma^{2}_{\max}\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
,
\lVert V\rVert_{\infty}\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
; the three uniform convergences displayed in the Drifts paragraph give
r^{\mathrm{dr}}_{m}:=\sup_{\eta}\bigl(\lvert\nu_{m}-\nu\rvert+\lvert\rho_{m}-\rho\rvert+\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}[mQ_{m}\mid\mathbf{X}]-V\bigr\rvert\bigr)=o_{p}(1)
.

Step C: equicontinuity and tightness.

Fix
\eta_{0}\in\Upsilon
. Write
\bar{F}_{m}:=-2\,\bar{U}_{m}+\bar{Q}_{m}+\bar{R}^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}
and
\Delta S_{m}:=S_{m}(\eta)-S_{m}(\eta^{\prime})
for the increment of a field
S_{m}
at a pair of tuning parameters. Collecting the decompositions
mU_{m}=\bar{U}_{m}+\nu_{m}
,
mQ_{m}=\bar{Q}_{m}+\mathbb{E}[mQ_{m}\mid\mathbf{X}]
and
mR_{m}=\bar{R}^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}+\rho_{m}+\bigl(\mathcal{R}_{m}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{R}_{m}\mid\mathbf{X}]\bigr)
with
\mathrm{dr}_{m}:=-2\nu_{m}+\mathbb{E}[mQ_{m}\mid\mathbf{X}]+\rho_{m}
,

F_{m}=\bar{F}_{m}+\mathrm{dr}_{m}+\bigl(\mathcal{R}_{m}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{R}_{m}\mid\mathbf{X}]\bigr)+o_{p}(1),

the last term being the uniform linearization remainder of Lemma
13
. The criterion and the three centered pieces have
C^{1}
paths on
\Upsilon
:
\mathrm{CV}_{m}
is a rational function of the leave-one-out Gram resolvents
\bigl(\boldsymbol{K}^{(-i)}_{\gamma}+\lambda(m-1)\boldsymbol{I}\bigr)^{-1}
with the real-analytic kernel entries
e^{-\gamma\lVert X_{i}-X_{j}\rVert^{2}}
, and
\bar{U}_{m}
,
\bar{R}^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}
and
\bar{Q}_{m}
have
C^{1}
paths by Step A.

The key estimate is the gradient moment of the centered part. Since
\lVert a+b+c\rVert^{p}\leq 3^{p-1}\bigl(\lVert a\rVert^{p}+\lVert b\rVert^{p}+\lVert c\rVert^{p}\bigr)
,

\displaystyle\bigl\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\bar{F}_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rVert^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 3^{p-1}\Bigl(2^{p}\,\bigl\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\bar{U}_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rVert^{p}+\bigl\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\bar{R}^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rVert^{p}+\bigl\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\bar{Q}_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rVert^{p}\Bigr),

\displaystyle\bigl\lVert\nabla_{\eta}S_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rVert^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p/2-1}\Bigl(\bigl\lvert\partial_{\gamma}S_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rvert^{p}+\bigl\lvert\partial_{\lambda}S_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rvert^{p}\Bigr),

the first from
\lVert-2\nabla_{\eta}\bar{U}_{m}+\nabla_{\eta}\bar{R}^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}+\nabla_{\eta}\bar{Q}_{m}\rVert\leq 2\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\bar{U}_{m}\rVert+\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\bar{R}^{\mathrm{bil}}_{m}\rVert+\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\bar{Q}_{m}\rVert
and the
\ell^{1}
–
\ell^{p}
bound on three terms, the second from
\lVert\nabla_{\eta}S_{m}\rVert^{2}=\lvert\partial_{\gamma}S_{m}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\partial_{\lambda}S_{m}\rvert^{2}
and the norm monotonicity
(a^{2}+b^{2})^{p/2}\leq 2^{p/2-1}(a^{p}+b^{p})
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and it holds for each of the three centered pieces. Taking expectations and inserting the componentwise bounds of Step B,

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigl\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\bar{F}_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rVert^{p}\leq 3^{p-1}\bigl(2^{3p/2}C_{U}+2^{p/2}C_{R}+2^{p/2}C_{Q}\bigr)\\
\leq 3^{p-1}2^{p/2}\bigl(2^{p}C_{U}+C_{R}+C_{Q}\bigr)=:N_{p},

the second row collecting terms and using
m^{-p/2}\leq 1
for
m\geq 2
.

The additive terms are excluded from the moment route by construction and are priced by the level. The drift
\mathrm{dr}_{m}
is measurable with respect to
\mathbf{X}
, and the Drift modulus paragraph gives

\bigl\lvert\mathrm{dr}_{m}(\eta)-\mathrm{dr}_{m}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr\rvert\leq\Lambda\,\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert+2\,r_{m},\qquad\Lambda:=4L_{\mathrm{dr}},\quad r_{m}:=2\,r^{\mathrm{dr}}_{m}=o_{p}(1):

by the triangle inequality
\lvert\mathrm{dr}_{m}(\eta)-\mathrm{dr}_{m}(\eta^{\prime})\rvert\leq\lvert\mathrm{dr}_{m}(\eta)-\mathrm{dr}(\eta)\rvert+\lvert\mathrm{dr}(\eta)-\mathrm{dr}(\eta^{\prime})\rvert+\lvert\mathrm{dr}(\eta^{\prime})-\mathrm{dr}_{m}(\eta^{\prime})\rvert
, the middle term is at most
(2+1+1)L_{\mathrm{dr}}\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert
by the Lipschitz display of the Drift modulus paragraph applied to
\mathrm{dr}:=-2\nu+\rho+V
, and
\sup_{\eta}\lvert\mathrm{dr}_{m}(\eta)-\mathrm{dr}(\eta)\rvert\leq 2\,r^{\mathrm{dr}}_{m}
, since
\mathrm{dr}_{m}-\mathrm{dr}=-2(\nu_{m}-\nu)+\bigl(\mathbb{E}[mQ_{m}\mid\mathbf{X}]-V\bigr)+(\rho_{m}-\rho)
and
r^{\mathrm{dr}}_{m}
is the supremum of the sum of the three absolute values. The remainder term obeys

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl\lvert F_{m}(\eta)-\bar{F}_{m}(\eta)-\mathrm{dr}_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rvert\leq 2\sup_{\eta}\bigl\lvert\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rvert+2\sup_{\eta}\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{R}_{m}\mid\mathbf{X}]\bigr\rvert+o_{p}(1)=o_{p}(1),

the two suprema being
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
, the first by the uniform remainder bound of Lemma
17
and the second by the uniform conditional-mean bound of Lemma
17
, and the
o_{p}(1)
being the uniform linearization remainder.

Lemma
8
now applies to
S_{m}:=F_{m}
with
S^{c}_{m}:=\bar{F}_{m}
,
a_{m}:=\mathrm{dr}_{m}
and
e_{m}:=F_{m}-\bar{F}_{m}-\mathrm{dr}_{m}
: hypothesis (i) is the gradient-moment display with
N_{p}=3^{p-1}2^{p/2}(2^{p}C_{U}+C_{R}+C_{Q})
, hypothesis (ii) is the drift display with
\Lambda=4L_{\mathrm{dr}}
and
r_{m}=2r^{\mathrm{dr}}_{m}
, hypothesis (iii) is the remainder display, and the pointwise bound
F_{m}(\eta_{0})=O_{p}(1)
is the finite-dimensional paragraph, whose display gives
\bigl(mU_{m}(\eta_{0}),mQ_{m}(\eta_{0}),mR_{m}(\eta_{0})\bigr)=O_{p}(1)
and which combines with
F_{m}=-2mU_{m}+mQ_{m}+mR_{m}+o_{p}(1)
at the uniform linearization remainder. Its conclusions are asymptotic equicontinuity,

\lim_{\delta\downarrow 0}\ \limsup_{m\to\infty}\ \mathbb{P}\Bigl(\sup_{\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert\leq\delta}\bigl\lvert\Delta F_{m}\bigr\rvert>\varepsilon\Bigr)=0\qquad\text{for every }\varepsilon>0,

and
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\lvert F_{m}(\eta)\rvert=O_{p}(1)
. The exponent surplus
p-d=p-2>0
is exactly what drives the first display to zero as
\delta\downarrow 0
, uniformly in
m
: the per-cell bound of Lemma
6
at
p>d=2
is
C_{2,p}N_{p}h^{p}
with
h=2\delta
, and the union runs over the
\lvert\mathcal{G}_{\delta}\rvert\leq C_{\Upsilon}\delta^{-2}
cells of Lemma
7
, so that
\lvert\mathcal{G}_{\delta}\rvert h^{p}\leq 2^{p}\,C_{\Upsilon}\delta^{p-2}
.

Finite-dimensional convergence together with asymptotic equicontinuity and boundedness, both supplied by Lemma
8
in Step C, gives weak convergence in
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)

[
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, Th. 1.5.4]
,

F_{m}\rightsquigarrow\mathbb{H}=-2\mathcal{C}^{\perp}+V+\mathcal{D}^{\perp}+\Delta\quad\text{in }\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon),

(limit-null)

which is part (a) of Theorem
6
: the projection is already accounted for in the limit element
G_{\perp,\gamma}
, the chaoses carrying the kernels
K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp},K^{D,\perp}
by Lemma
18
, and the drift being
\Delta=\rho-2\nu
; the limit
\mathbb{H}
has continuous sample paths by the sample-path continuity of the limit fields established in Appendix
B.3.3
, which covers
\mathbb{H}
explicitly.

Feasibility and bookkeeping.

The limit (
limit-null
) is stated at the infeasible level: Lemma
15
transfers the
\eta
-dependent part,
\sup_{\eta}|m\,\mathrm{CV}^{\mathrm{dep}}_{m}(\eta;\hat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\perp})-m\,\mathrm{CV}^{\mathrm{dep}}_{m}(\eta;\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{0}_{\perp})|=o_{p}(1)
. The level difference between the two centerings,

\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bigl[\hat{\varepsilon}_{\perp,i}^{2}-(\varepsilon^{0}_{\perp,i})^{2}\bigr]=2\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}+\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}\rVert^{2},\qquad\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0},

is free of
\eta
but not covered by Lemma
15
: in the sieve regime of Assumption
4
(iii) it is
O_{p}(m^{1/2-\varrho}\vee m^{1-2\varrho})
, vanishing for
\varrho>1/2
, the linear term being
O_{p}(\lVert\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}\rVert)
by Chebyshev’s inequality against the conditionally centered noise (Remark
2
); when the scores are
\mathbf{X}
-measurable it vanishes at the second-order rate of Fact
2
, since
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\hat{\mathbb{G}}=\boldsymbol{0}
annihilates the linear part of
\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}
exactly. Two bookkeeping points complete part (a). First, the limit (
limit-null
) is computed directly on the projected residuals, the embedding CLT delivering
G_{\perp,\gamma}
for
\varepsilon_{\perp}^{0}
, so the projection fluctuation
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}-\boldsymbol{\Pi}
needs no separate bookkeeping: it is part of the limit and is reflected in the projected kernels
K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp},K^{D,\perp}
and in the drift
\Delta
; there is no
\eta
-constant shift to subtract. Second, the level term
\sum_{i}(\varepsilon^{0}_{\perp,i})^{2}
removed by the centering is constant in
\eta
:
\eta
-free additive shifts leave the argmin location and every argmin-based conclusion unchanged, though in the sieve regime they displace the centered process itself by a nonvanishing amount; the conclusions drawn from (
limit-null
), in particular the selector’s argmin over
\Upsilon
, therefore never depend on the level at which the centering is taken. This proves part (a).

Proof of (b): fixed alternative.

Road map.
The proof proceeds in four moves and records three lemmas. (1)
One linearization:
the fixed-alternative clause of Lemma
12
bounds the leave-one-out remainder by
r_{i}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
, uniformly in
(i,\eta)
, so the algebra of Lemma
13
, applied once to the full orthogonalized residual, represents
\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)
as the level
m^{-1}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{2}
plus the three quadratic statistics with a remainder
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
uniform in
\eta
. (2)
One channel ledger:
the split
\varepsilon_{\perp,i}=s(X_{i})+r(X_{i})+v_{i}
turns each statistic into a sum of pairings of the signal, estimation-error, and noise components; the ledger routes every pairing to a deterministic constant collected into
R_{\infty}(\eta)
, to a centered bounded average collected into the pooled family
\psi_{\eta}
of (
43
), to the drift term
\mathrm{Drift}_{m}(\eta)
, or to an
O_{p}(m^{-1})
remainder, and yields the collection (
44
) of the criterion around its risk. (3)
Two uniform laws of large numbers:
Lemma
19
fixes the deterministic centering, moment, and smoothness constants of the pooled family, and Lemma
20
converts them into
\sup_{\eta}\bigl\lvert m^{-1}\sum_{i}\psi_{\eta}(X_{i},v_{i})\bigr\rvert=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
by a fixed mesh, Chebyshev’s inequality, the Marcinkiewicz–Zygmund inequality, and the Sobolev maximal inequality of Lemma
6
; no central limit argument is consumed at this stage. (4)
Assembly:
the triangle inequality over (
44
), the drift bound (
46
), and the replacement cost gives the level statement (
47
). Lemma
21
, recorded after Lemma
20
, upgrades the same two inputs to the process-level fluctuation law; it is not consumed by the level statement and is stated for use by the proof of part (c) and by the remark at the end of the proof.

Setup.
Write the residual at the true parameter as in the Introduction,

\varepsilon_{0}=f(X)+v
, with
f(X):=\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{0}\mid X]

deterministic,
f\neq 0
,
\|f\|_{L^{2}}<\infty
, and

v:=\varepsilon_{0}-f(X)
,
\mathbb{E}[v\mid X]=0
. The
orthogonalized residual has effective signal
\Pi f
. For part (b)
assume
\|\Pi f\|_{\infty}<\infty
. Throughout part (b) the noise moments are those of Assumption
7
(v), the noise being
v
:
\sup_{x}\mathbb{E}[v^{2p}\mid X=x]\leq\bar{M}_{2p}
; write
\bar{M}_{4}:=\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p}
for the interpolated conditional fourth moment,
\sup_{x}\mathbb{E}[(v^{\rm raw})^{4}\mid X=x]\leq\bar{M}_{4}
, finite and deterministic by the Lyapunov interpolation of the
(2p)
-clause at
q=4
(as in the proof of Lemma
17
), whose Marcinkiewicz–Zygmund linear-form bound passes the bound to the projected component
v_{k}=\sum_{a}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}_{ka}v^{\rm raw}_{a}
at
q=4
; every fourth-moment constant below depends only on
(p,\bar{M}_{2p})
. Write

r:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{f}-\boldsymbol{\Pi}f
for the difference between the empirical and population projections of the signal, with
\boldsymbol{f}:=(f(X_{1}),\dots,f(X_{m}))^{\top}
the raw-signal evaluations and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}=\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\mathbb{G}}M\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}
,
M:=(\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}})^{-1}
, so that

\varepsilon_{\perp,i}=\Pi f(X_{i})+r(X_{i})+v^{\rm raw}_{i}+\mathbf{d}_{i}=\Pi f(X_{i})+r(X_{i})+v_{i}
uniformly in
i
,
where
v^{\rm raw}_{i}
is the raw noise,
v_{i}=[\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm raw}]_{i}
is the noise component of the orthogonalized residual, and
\mathbf{d}:=\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm raw}=-(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm raw}
is the replacement defect;
\lVert r\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
by Section
4.2
. Put
w^{*}_{\eta}=P_{\eta}(\Pi f)
,

e_{\eta}=\Pi f-w^{*}_{\eta}
,
A(\eta)=\|e_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
,

\bar{\sigma}^{2}=\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]
,
R_{\infty}=\bar{\sigma}^{2}+A
. Put
a_{\eta}=e_{\eta}
,
b_{\eta}=P_{\eta}a_{\eta}
, so

a_{\eta}-b_{\eta}=(I-P_{\eta})^{2}\Pi f,\qquad(a_{\eta}-b_{\eta})_{\ell}=\frac{\lambda^{2}}{(\mu_{\ell}+\lambda)^{2}}\,(\Pi f)_{\ell}(\gamma),

(34)

with

(\Pi f)_{\ell}(\gamma)=\langle\Pi f,\phi_{\ell}(\cdot;\gamma)\rangle

and
0\leq\lambda^{2}/(\mu_{\ell}+\lambda)^{2}\leq 1
. Two further population objects enter. The anchor of the composed kernel, in the sense of (
4
), is

\kappa_{\eta}(x):=\mathbb{E}_{Z}\bigl[g_{\eta}(x,Z)\,w^{*}_{\eta}(Z)\bigr]=\bigl(P_{\eta}^{2}\,\Pi f\bigr)(x),\qquad(\kappa_{\eta})_{\ell}=\frac{\mu_{\ell}^{2}}{(\mu_{\ell}+\lambda)^{2}}\,(\Pi f)_{\ell}(\gamma):

the first equality inserts the kernel of
S_{\lambda}
and exchanges the expectations,
\mathbb{E}[w^{*}_{\eta}(Z)\phi_{\ell}(Z)]=\frac{\mu_{\ell}}{\mu_{\ell}+\lambda}(\Pi f)_{\ell}(\gamma)
, the second reads off the multiplier
t_{\ell}^{2}
of
S^{2}_{\lambda}
, with
t_{\ell}:=\frac{\mu_{\ell}(\gamma)}{\mu_{\ell}(\gamma)+\lambda}
. The anchors are uniformly bounded:
\lvert w^{*}_{\eta}(x)\rvert\leq\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert w^{*}_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}\leq(4\lambda_{L})^{-1/2}\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert\Pi f\rVert_{L^{2}}
and
\lVert\kappa_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}\leq\lVert w^{*}_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}
, the filter
P_{\eta}
being an
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}
-contraction and the spectral norm having multiplier
t^{2}_{\ell}/\mu_{\ell}=\mu_{\ell}/(\mu_{\ell}+\lambda)^{2}\leq(4\lambda)^{-1}\leq(4\lambda_{L})^{-1}
, so
\lVert w^{*}_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}\leq(4\lambda_{L})^{-1/2}\lVert\Pi f\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})}
; likewise
\lvert b_{\eta}(x)\rvert\leq\lVert a_{\eta}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lVert g_{\eta}(x,\cdot)\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert\Pi f\rVert_{L^{2}}
.
Replacement of the noise component.
Every pairing of the projected noise
v_{i}
below survives, at
o_{p}(1)
cost after the
\sqrt{m}
normalization and uniformly in
\eta
, the replacement of
v_{i}
by the raw noise
v^{\rm raw}_{i}
:
\lVert\mathbf{d}\rVert^{2}=(\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm raw})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm raw}
has
\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathbf{d}\rVert^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\operatorname{tr}\{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})\}\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}d_{\theta}
, with
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}:=\operatorname{diag}(\sigma^{2}(X_{1}),\dots,\sigma^{2}(X_{m}))
, so
\lVert\mathbf{d}\rVert=O_{p}(1)
; in particular
\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}\mathbf{d}=0
by
\boldsymbol{v}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm raw}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})=\boldsymbol{0}
. The three replacement pairings are bounded sharply. The level square is exact,
m^{-1}\sum_{i}(v_{i}^{2}-v^{\rm raw\,2}_{i})=m^{-1}\bigl(\lVert\boldsymbol{v}\rVert^{2}-\lVert\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm raw}\rVert^{2}\bigr)=-\lVert\mathbf{d}\rVert^{2}/m=O_{p}(m^{-1})
. The signal cross-pairing closes through the exact identity
(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})(\boldsymbol{s}+\boldsymbol{r})=\boldsymbol{0}
, where
\boldsymbol{s}:=(\Pi f(X_{1}),\dots,\Pi f(X_{m}))^{\top}
and
\boldsymbol{s}+\boldsymbol{r}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{f}
, which gives
m^{-1}\boldsymbol{s}^{\top}\mathbf{d}=-m^{-1}(\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{S}}\boldsymbol{s})^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm raw}=m^{-1}(\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{S}}\boldsymbol{r})^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm raw}
; this is conditionally centered with conditional variance
m^{-2}(\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{S}}\boldsymbol{r})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}(\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{S}}\boldsymbol{r})\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}m^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{r}\rVert^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}m^{-2}\,m\lVert\boldsymbol{r}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=O_{p}(m^{-2})
, hence
m^{-1}\boldsymbol{s}^{\top}\mathbf{d}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
by Chebyshev’s inequality, within the unit-scale budget below; the quadratic defect
\lVert\mathbf{d}\rVert^{2}/m=O_{p}(m^{-1})
is absorbed at the same order through the trace bound above, and no second-order term survives the split. The linear
v
-borne pairings close conditionally at conditional mean zero and conditional variance
m^{-2}\boldsymbol{h}_{\eta}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})\boldsymbol{\Sigma}(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})\boldsymbol{h}_{\eta}\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}m^{-2}\lVert(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})\boldsymbol{h}_{\eta}\rVert^{2}=O_{p}(m^{-2})
uniformly in
\eta
: writing
\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{S}}:=\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
and
\boldsymbol{P}_{S}:=\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\boldsymbol{\Pi}
for the empirical and population score-span projections; here
\boldsymbol{P}_{S}\boldsymbol{g}
denotes the design evaluation of the population projection
g\mapsto g-\Pi g
, so that
\boldsymbol{P}_{S}\boldsymbol{h}_{\eta}=\boldsymbol{0}
exactly,
h_{\eta}\in S^{\perp}
making
P_{S}h_{\eta}=0
pointwise, and
\boldsymbol{r}=(\boldsymbol{P}_{S}-\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{S}})\boldsymbol{f}
follows from
\boldsymbol{s}=(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\boldsymbol{P}_{S})\boldsymbol{f}
together with the exact identity
(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})(\boldsymbol{s}+\boldsymbol{r})=\boldsymbol{0}
; the free insertion of
\Pi
displayed below gives
h_{\eta}\in S^{\perp}
, so
\lVert(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})\boldsymbol{h}_{\eta}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}\leq\lVert\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{S}}-\boldsymbol{P}_{S}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\,\lVert h_{\eta}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}
with
\boldsymbol{r}=(\boldsymbol{P}_{S}-\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{S}})\boldsymbol{f}
and
\lVert\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{S}}-\boldsymbol{P}_{S}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
, the projection-perturbation rate induced by
\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbb{G}}/m\xrightarrow{p}\Gamma_{\theta_{0}}
(Assumption
5
(ii)), consistent with the rate of
\lVert\boldsymbol{r}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}
. We may therefore read
v_{i}
as
v^{\rm raw}_{i}
wherever the i.i.d. structure of the summands is invoked, and we drop the distinction from the notation. One further convention. Whenever a statistic
T
satisfies
\mathbb{E}[T^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]\leq W
for an
\mathbf{X}
-measurable
W
with
W=O_{p}(s_{m}^{2})
at a deterministic scale
s_{m}
, then
T=O_{p}(s_{m})
: split on the event
\{W\leq Cs_{m}^{2}\}
, apply conditional Chebyshev’s inequality at the level
Ks_{m}
to get
\mathbb{P}(\lvert T\rvert>Ks_{m}\mid\mathbf{X})\leq C/K^{2}
, let
K\uparrow\infty
and then
C\uparrow\infty
. This variance-event splitting is invoked below as such.

Leave-one-out remainder.

This paragraph closes the linearization remainder at
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
uniformly in
\eta
, which is what allows the proof to run on a single linearization. The coefficient averages of the orthogonalized residual are bounded,

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lvert\varepsilon_{\perp,j}\rvert=O_{p}(1),

by Jensen’s inequality against
\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{2}=O_{p}(1)
, which follows from the split
\varepsilon_{\perp,i}=s(X_{i})+r(X_{i})+v_{i}
of the setup, the bound
\lvert\Pi f\rvert\leq\lVert\Pi f\rVert_{\infty}
, the perturbation rate
\lVert r\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
, and
\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j}v_{j}^{2}=O_{p}(1)
read at the raw noise. The fixed-alternative clause of Lemma
12
, stated there at the oracle residual and extending verbatim to the feasible one, its stochastic inputs being the design concentration of the leave-one-out covariance perturbation and the bounded coefficient averages just displayed, therefore gives

\max_{i\leq m}\,\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\,\lvert r_{i}\rvert=O_{p}(m^{-1/2}).

Applying Lemma
13
once to the full orthogonalized residual, the algebra of its proof, expanding the square and collecting the three
\eta
-dependent sums into
U_{m},Q_{m},R_{m}
and leaving the remainder
-\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}-L_{i})r_{i}+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}r_{i}^{2}
, is an identity at every realized sample, and the two remainder sums are pathwise

\displaystyle\Bigl\lvert\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\varepsilon_{\perp,i}-L_{i})\,r_{i}\Bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\Bigl(\max_{j}\lvert L_{j}\rvert+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j}\lvert\varepsilon_{\perp,j}\rvert\Bigr)\max_{i}\lvert r_{i}\rvert=O_{p}(m^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}r_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i}r_{i}^{2}=O_{p}(m^{-1}),

by the triangle and Cauchy–Schwarz inequalities, with

\lvert L_{j}(\eta)\rvert\leq\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}\,\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}\lvert\varepsilon_{\perp,i}\rvert=O_{p}(1)

uniformly in
(j,\eta)
. Hence, uniformly in
\eta
,

\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{2}-2U_{m}(\eta)+Q_{m}(\eta)+R_{m}(\eta)+O_{p}(m^{-1/2}),

(35)

where
U_{m}(\eta)=\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
and
Q_{m},R_{m}
are as displayed in Section
4.2
. The weaker order carries through unchanged: the assembly below consumes the collection only at the
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
scale.

Anchor decomposition of the signal-side channels.

For a symmetric kernel
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}\in\{g_{\eta},(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})_{\eta}\}
write

U^{(\mathfrak{g})}_{m}(\eta):=\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\Pi f(X_{i})\,\Pi f(X_{j})\,\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
,

\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(x):=\mathbb{E}_{Y}\bigl[\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(x,Y)\Pi f(Y)\bigr]
, and

\theta_{\mathfrak{g}}(\eta):=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\Pi f(X)\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X)\bigr]
. The anchors are exactly the two population objects above:
\vartheta_{g}=w^{*}_{\eta}
, since
\mathbb{E}_{Y}[g_{\eta}(x,Y)\Pi f(Y)]
applies the kernel of
S_{\lambda}
to the coordinate sequence of
\Pi f
; and
\vartheta_{g\circ g}=\kappa_{\eta}
, since

\mathbb{E}_{Y}[(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(x,Y)\Pi f(Y)]=\mathbb{E}_{Z}\bigl[g_{\eta}(x,Z)\,\mathbb{E}_{Y}[g_{\eta}(Z,Y)\Pi f(Y)]\bigr]=\mathbb{E}_{Z}[g_{\eta}(x,Z)w^{*}_{\eta}(Z)]
.
Introduce the centered, doubly degenerate kernel

\hat{h}_{\eta}(x,y):=\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(x,y)\,\Pi f(x)\,\Pi f(y)-\Pi f(x)\,\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(x)-\Pi f(y)\,\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(y)+\theta_{\mathfrak{g}}(\eta),\qquad\mathbb{E}_{X}\bigl[\hat{h}_{\eta}(x,X)\bigr]=0:

the first and second terms of the expectation cancel by the definition of
\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}
, the third and fourth by the definition of
\theta_{\mathfrak{g}}
. The exact decomposition, by counting,

U^{(\mathfrak{g})}_{m}(\eta)-\theta_{\mathfrak{g}}(\eta)=\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Bigl[\Pi f(X_{i})\,\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X_{i})-\theta_{\mathfrak{g}}(\eta)\Bigr]+\underbrace{\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\hat{h}_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})}_{=:\ \mathrm{D}^{(\mathfrak{g})}_{m}(\eta)},

(36)

follows since
\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\Pi f(X_{i})\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X_{i})=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}\Pi f(X_{i})\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X_{i})
, the index
i
contributing
m-1
terms, and likewise with
i
in the second argument, while
\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\theta_{\mathfrak{g}}=\theta_{\mathfrak{g}}
. All summands are bounded:
\lvert\hat{h}_{\eta}\rvert\leq C^{(0)}_{\mathrm{an}}
, where
C^{(0)}_{\mathrm{an}}:=\bar{k}\,\lambda_{L}^{-1}\lVert\Pi f\rVert_{\infty}^{2}+2\,(4\lambda_{L})^{-1/2}\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert\Pi f\rVert_{\infty}\lVert\Pi f\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\Pi f\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}
for
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}=g_{\eta}
, and
C^{(0)}_{\mathrm{an}}
with
\bar{k}\,(4\lambda_{L})^{-1}
in place of
\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}
for the composed kernel: the composed kernel is that of
R_{\lambda}C_{\gamma}R_{\lambda}
, an operator of norm
\sup_{\ell}\mu_{\ell}/(\mu_{\ell}+\lambda)^{2}\leq(4\lambda_{L})^{-1}
, so
\lvert(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(x,y)\rvert\leq\bar{k}\,(4\lambda_{L})^{-1}
against sections of norm
\bar{k}^{1/2}
. The degenerate remainder is
O_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly in
\eta
, in three steps.

First, the pointwise second moment. Since
\mathbb{E}[\hat{h}_{\eta}(x,X_{j})\mid X_{j}]=0
, a product
\mathbb{E}[\hat{h}_{ij}\hat{h}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}]
vanishes whenever one of the four indices is private to its factor, the expectation over that coordinate being taken against a centered anchor; the survivors are the diagonal
i=i^{\prime},j=j^{\prime}
and the transposed
i=j^{\prime},j=i^{\prime}
, whence

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathrm{D}^{(\mathfrak{g})}_{m}(\eta)^{2}\bigr]=\frac{2\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[\hat{h}_{\eta}(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}\bigr]}{m(m-1)}\leq\frac{2\,C^{(0)\,2}_{\mathrm{an}}}{m(m-1)},\qquad\max_{\eta_{l}\in\mathcal{M}}\bigl\lvert\mathrm{D}^{(\mathfrak{g})}_{m}(\eta_{l})\bigr\rvert=O_{p}(m^{-1})

at the points of any fixed mesh
\mathcal{M}
by Chebyshev’s inequality.

Second, the paths and the gradient envelope. Term-by-term differentiation is justified by dominated convergence through deterministic envelopes: Step A of the proof of part (a) gives
\sup_{\eta,x,y}\lvert\partial_{\eta}\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(x,y)\rvert\leq G_{1}\vee G_{2}
entrywise, and the anchor functions, represented as
\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(x)=\mathbb{E}_{Y}[\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(x,Y)\,\Pi f(Y)]
, inherit the envelope through dominated convergence and Cauchy–Schwarz,

\displaystyle\bigl\lvert\nabla_{\eta}\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(x)\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{Y}\bigl[\lvert\nabla_{\eta}\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(x,Y)\rvert\,\lvert\Pi f(Y)\rvert\bigr]\leq(G_{1}\vee G_{2})\,\lVert\Pi f\rVert_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\bigl\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigr\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(G_{1}\vee G_{2})\,\lVert\Pi f\rVert_{L^{2}},

the second bound by quadrature of the first over
x
. The population means differentiate under the same envelopes,
\lvert\nabla_{\eta}\theta_{\mathfrak{g}}(\eta)\rvert\leq\lVert\Pi f\rVert_{L^{2}}\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq(G_{1}\vee G_{2})\,\lVert\Pi f\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}
. Hence
\nabla_{\eta}\hat{h}_{\eta}
exists with
\lvert\nabla_{\eta}\hat{h}_{\eta}(x,y)\rvert\leq C_{\mathrm{an}}
for a deterministic
C_{\mathrm{an}}
, the gradient inherits the double degeneracy by dominated convergence,

\nabla_{\eta}\mathrm{D}^{(\mathfrak{g})}_{m}(\eta)=\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\nabla_{\eta}\hat{h}_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j}),\qquad\mathbb{E}_{X}\bigl[\nabla_{\eta}\hat{h}_{\eta}(x,X)\bigr]=\nabla_{\eta}\,\mathbb{E}_{X}\bigl[\hat{h}_{\eta}(x,X)\bigr]=0,

and
\mathrm{D}^{(\mathfrak{g})}_{m}
has
C^{1}
paths on
\Upsilon
.

Third, the uniform upgrade by the Sobolev route. In the fourfold product
\mathbb{E}\prod_{l=1}^{4}\nabla_{\eta}\hat{h}_{\eta}(X_{i_{l}},X_{j_{l}})
, every design index must occur at least twice among the eight slots, or the factor carrying it privately is centered by the double degeneracy and kills the expectation; the induced partition of the eight slots has all blocks of size at least two, there are at most
\mathrm{B}_{8}=4140
such partition patterns, each carries at most
m^{4}
index assignments, and each assignment contributes at most
C^{4}_{\mathrm{an}}
. The same count with
\hat{h}_{\eta}
in place of
\nabla_{\eta}\hat{h}_{\eta}
gives
\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{D}^{(\mathfrak{g})}_{m}(\eta)^{4}]\leq C\,m^{-4}
. Therefore, with
C_{\mathrm{D}}:=16\,\mathrm{B}_{8}\,C^{4}_{\mathrm{an}}
,

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\lvert\nabla_{\eta}\mathrm{D}^{(\mathfrak{g})}_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rvert^{4}\bigr]\leq\frac{C_{\mathrm{D}}}{m^{4}}\qquad\text{for }m\geq 2,

the constant being
\mathbf{X}
-free and
m
-uniform since
(m-1)^{4}\geq m^{4}/16
. Lemma
6
with
p=4>d=2
on each cell of a fixed mesh gives cell oscillations
\max_{K}\sup_{K}\lvert\Delta\mathrm{D}^{(\mathfrak{g})}_{m}\rvert=O_{p}(\delta\,m^{-1})
, and the mesh level is
O_{p}(m^{-1})
by the pointwise second moment, so

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\Bigl\lvert\mathrm{D}^{(g\circ g)}_{m}(\eta)-2\,\mathrm{D}^{(g)}_{m}(\eta)\Bigr\rvert=O_{p}(m^{-1}).

(37)

The three channels.

Collect (
35
) channel by channel, writing
s
for
\Pi f
. The split
\varepsilon_{\perp,i}=s(X_{i})+r(X_{i})+v_{i}
turns each statistic of (
35
) into a sum of
pairings
, and this subsection routes every pairing to one of four destinations. The level is the square of a trinomial: it contributes the three self-pairings
s^{2}
,
r^{2}
,
v^{2}
and the three cross-pairings
2sr
,
2sv
,
2rv
, each summed over
i
with weight
m^{-1}
. The statistics
-2U_{m}
and
R_{m}
are bilinear, and by the exact reduction of Lemma
16
(ii) stated below,
R_{m}
equals
\frac{m-2}{m-1}\,U^{(g\circ g)}_{m}
up to a remainder; each bilinear summand carries one copy of each of the nine coefficient pairings
a_{i}b_{j}
with
a,b\in\{s,r,v\}
, through the kernel
g_{\eta}
for
U_{m}
and the composed kernel
(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})
for
U^{(g\circ g)}_{m}
:

\underbrace{(s,s)}_{\text{mean, linear}}\quad\underbrace{(s,v),\,(v,s)}_{\text{linear in }v}\quad\underbrace{(s,r),\,(r,s)}_{\text{linear in }r}\quad\underbrace{(v,v),\,(v,r),\,(r,v),\,(r,r)}_{\text{die at }\sqrt{m}}

and
Q_{m}
carries the squared coefficients
\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{2}
and dies by normalization alone. The routing rule, applied to this list and used without further comment below, reads:
(i)
a pairing with a nonzero mean contributes a deterministic constant, collected into
R_{\infty}(\eta)
;
(ii)
a pairing linear in the noise is an average of
m
centered bounded terms of size
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
and enters the pooled family (
43
);
(iii)
a pairing linear in the estimation error has the same size and is collected into the drift term
\mathrm{Drift}_{m}
of (
44
);
(iv)
the remaining pairings are
O_{p}(m^{-1})
and die, since
\sqrt{m}\,O_{p}(m^{-1})=o_{p}(1)
:
(v,v)
is centered once the projected noise is read as raw noise, at replacement cost
O_{p}(m^{-1})
,
(v,r)
and
(r,v)
carry the rate
\lVert r\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
against their normalizations, and
(r,r)
carries
\lVert r\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
against an
m^{-2}
prefactor. The pairing ledger, class by class; for each class, the carriers, the mechanism, the order at the criterion scale, and the destination.
Class
(s,s)
.
Carriers:
U^{(g)}_{m}
and
R_{m}
’s
\theta
-anchored part
\frac{m-2}{m-1}\,U^{(g\circ g)}_{m}
, both handled by the anchor decomposition displayed above (the doubly degenerate kernel
\hat{h}_{\eta}
, remainders

’s own
(s,s)
pairing, the only one that does not die, whose first Hoeffding projection (

) is routed to the pooled family (

) while its two chaos components are
O_{p}(m^{-1})
.
Class

.
Carriers:

, contributing
-\frac{4}{m}\sum_{j}v_{j}\,\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}s(X_{i})\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
through the kernel
g_{\eta}
;

+\frac{2}{m}\sum_{j}v_{j}\,\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}s(X_{i})\,(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{i},X_{j})

,
\kappa_{\eta}(X_{j})
at cost

):

’s

pairing, is

and dies.

.
Carriers: the level,

;

of (

:
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
by Cauchy–Schwarz against
C_{\rm dr}
and the perturbation rate, dead at the budget, no central limit theorem consumed.
Class
(v,v)
.

U_{m}
and

’s

-anchored part combine as

: centered after the raw-noise replacement at cost

and dead at the

. The level’s

by centering;

’s pair is

.

against the

The routing applies to the pairings of
-2U_{m}+Q_{m}+R_{m}
proper; the reduction remainder
\mathcal{R}_{m}
is routed separately in the signal–noise channel, whose
(s,s)
pairing carries a surviving first Hoeffding projection (

). The subsection implements the route in order: the level is sorted in place;

and the noise–noise pairing die; the signal–noise pairings merge with the level’s

term into the single process (

); the signal–signal pairing supplies the constants completing

and the linear brackets of (

-pairings form the drift term collected at the end of the subsection.
The level (one-point classes).
Inserting the split,

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{2}=\bar{\sigma}^{2}+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Bigl[\bigl(v_{i}^{2}-\bar{\sigma}^{2}\bigr)+2\,v_{i}\,s(X_{i})+s(X_{i})^{2}\Bigr]+2\langle s,r\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}+\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{lev}}_{m},

where
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{lev}}_{m}:=\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i}v_{i}r(X_{i})+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}r(X_{i})^{2}
: the first piece has conditional mean zero and conditional variance
\frac{4\sigma^{2}_{\max}}{m^{2}}\sum_{i}r(X_{i})^{2}\leq\frac{4\sigma^{2}_{\max}}{m}\lVert r\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}^{2}=O_{p}(m^{-2})
, and the second is
O_{p}(m^{-1})
by Cauchy–Schwarz, so
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{lev}}_{m}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
;
2\langle s,r\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}
is the level’s contribution to the drift term, collected below; and
\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}s(X_{i})^{2}=\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
by the law of large numbers, the summand being bounded. The
2\,v_{i}s(X_{i})
term of the bracket is the level’s copy of the cross-pairing
(s,v)
; it is deferred to the signal–noise channel, with which it merges in (
42
) below.

The
Q
-channel.
Grouping by the squared index,

Q_{m}(\eta)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{2}\;\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})^{2},\qquad\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}\leq\frac{\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}}{m-1},

so
\sup_{\eta}\lvert Q_{m}(\eta)\rvert\leq\frac{\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}}{m-1}\,\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{2}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
: the
Q
-channel dies at the
\sqrt{m}
scale by normalization alone, uniformly in
\eta
.

Class
(v,v)
(noise–noise pairing) dies by a conditional-moment count after the raw-noise switch.
By the replacement convention of the setup the pairings
-2U^{(v,v)}_{m}+U^{(v,v,g\circ g)}_{m}
are read at the raw noise
\boldsymbol{u}:=\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm raw}
, and the switch costs
O_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly in
\eta
. Let
C
be either kernel matrix,
C_{ij}=g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
or
C_{ij}=(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{i},X_{j})
for
i\neq j
, symmetrized with zero diagonal, at the common envelope
\lvert C_{ij}\rvert\leq\bar{c}:=\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}
, and write
H:=\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}=\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{S}}
, symmetric idempotent of rank
d_{\theta}
. Inserting
v_{i}=[(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-H)\boldsymbol{u}]_{i}
and expanding the product gives
v_{i}v_{j}-u_{i}u_{j}=-u_{i}[Hu]_{j}-[Hu]_{i}u_{j}+[Hu]_{i}[Hu]_{j}
, the two cross terms contributing equally by the symmetry of
C
, so the switch of the pairing
\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}C_{ij}v_{i}v_{j}
is the quadratic form

\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}C_{ij}\bigl(v_{i}v_{j}-u_{i}u_{j}\bigr)=\frac{\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\bigl(HCH-2\,CH\bigr)\boldsymbol{u}}{m(m-1)}.

Its conditional mean is
\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}C_{ij}\,[\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}]_{ij}
, the conditional covariance of
v
being
[\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}]_{ij}
, and the same expansion at the covariance level gives, off the diagonal,
[\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}]_{ij}=-H_{ij}\bigl(\sigma^{2}(X_{i})+\sigma^{2}(X_{j})\bigr)+[HDH]_{ij}
, whence Cauchy–Schwarz against
\sum_{i\neq j}H^{2}_{ij}\leq\operatorname{tr}(H^{2})=d_{\theta}
for the first term, and the row-norm bound
\sum_{i\neq j}\lvert[HDH]_{ij}\rvert\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}\sum_{k}\lVert H_{\cdot k}\rVert^{2}_{1}\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}\,m\,d_{\theta}
for the second, give

\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}C_{ij}\bigl(v_{i}v_{j}-u_{i}u_{j}\bigr)\Bigm|\mathbf{X}\Bigr]\Bigr|\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}_{\max}\bar{c}\,\bigl(2\sqrt{d_{\theta}}+d_{\theta}\bigr)}{m-1}=O(m^{-1}),

deterministically in
\mathbf{X}
and uniformly in
\eta
. The centered remainder of the switch dies at second-moment level. Its cross part is the quadratic form
\frac{2}{m(m-1)}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}CH\boldsymbol{u}
minus its conditional mean, and the conditional-variance clause (
29
) of Lemma
14
, applied to the raw noise at the symmetrized matrix, bounds its conditional variance by

\frac{4\,\bigl(2\sigma^{4}_{\max}+\bar{M}_{4}\bigr)\,\lVert CH\rVert^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}}{m^{2}(m-1)^{2}}\leq\frac{4\,\bigl(2\sigma^{4}_{\max}+\bar{M}_{4}\bigr)\,\bar{c}^{2}}{(m-1)^{2}}=O\bigl(m^{-2}\bigr),\qquad\lVert CH\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\lVert C\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq m\bar{c},

so the cross part is
O_{p}(m^{-1})
by the variance-event splitting of the setup. The quadratic part is bounded pathwise,

\Bigl\lvert\frac{\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}HCH\boldsymbol{u}}{m(m-1)}\Bigr\rvert=\frac{\bigl\lvert(Hu)^{\top}C(Hu)\bigr\rvert}{m(m-1)}\leq\frac{\bar{c}}{m(m-1)}\Bigl(\sum_{i}\lvert[Hu]_{i}\rvert\Bigr)^{2}\leq\frac{\bar{c}\,\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}H\boldsymbol{u}}{m-1},

by the entrywise envelope and Cauchy–Schwarz, and
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}H\boldsymbol{u}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\operatorname{tr}(HD)\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}d_{\theta}
,
H
being an orthogonal projector, so Markov’s inequality gives the quadratic part as
O_{p}(m^{-1})
. Assembling the two kernel matrices with their prefactors
2
and
\frac{m-2}{m-1}\leq 1
, the switch is
O_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly in
\eta
. After the switch, both pairings are quadratic forms
\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}C_{ij}u_{i}u_{j}
in the conditionally i.i.d. raw noise: conditionally centered given
\mathbf{X}
, with conditional second moments
O(1)
at the
mU_{m}
scale by Lemma
14
at the covariance
D
and the fourth-moment bound
\bar{M}_{4}=\bar{M}^{2/p}_{2p}
, both pairings are
O_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly in
\eta
and die at the
\sqrt{m}
scale.

Class
(s,v)+(v,s)
(the signal–noise channel).
Pairings
(s,v)
and
(v,s)
, together with the level cross-pairing
2\,v_{i}s(X_{i})
deferred above, are linear in the noise and merge into a single process. By symmetry of the sum indices and of
g_{\eta}
,

-2\cdot\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\bigl[s(X_{i})v_{j}+s(X_{j})v_{i}\bigr]g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})=-\frac{4}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}v_{j}\,\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}s(X_{i})\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j});

likewise, by the exact reduction of Lemma
16
(ii), valid for arbitrary coefficients,

R_{m}=\frac{m-2}{m-1}\,U^{(g\circ g)}_{m}+\mathcal{R}_{m}/m
with

U^{(g\circ g)}_{m}:=\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{i},X_{j})
,
so the bilinear part contributes
+\frac{2}{m}\sum_{j}v_{j}\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}s(X_{i})(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{i},X_{j})
at the same scale, the prefactor
\frac{m-2}{m-1}
differing from
1
by
O(m^{-1})
. The remainder of the reduction is controlled under the fixed alternative by the
anchor-centeredness
\mathbb{E}_{X}[r_{\eta}(X;y,z)]=0
, with one pairing that does
not die and is treated first. Reindexing the remainder by its pair slots,

\mathcal{R}_{m}(\eta)=\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{j<k}\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk}(\eta)\;\varepsilon_{\perp,j}\varepsilon_{\perp,k},\qquad\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk}(\eta):=\sum_{i\neq j,k}r_{\eta}(X_{i};X_{j},X_{k}),

(38)

(the coefficient matrix of the matrix-form paragraph in the proof of
Lemma
17
has entries
\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk}/(m-1)^{2}
), the
envelope
\lvert r_{\eta}\rvert\leq K_{r}
,
K_{r}:=2\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
,
and the anchor-centeredness give

\mathbb{E}[\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk}\mid X_{j},X_{k}]=0
,

\mathbb{E}[(\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk})^{2}\mid X_{j},X_{k}]\leq(m-2)K_{r}^{2}
, and

\mathbb{E}\lVert\Gamma^{(m)}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq m(m-1)(m-2)K_{r}^{2}
. The pairings
(v,r)
,
(r,v)
,
(r,r)
are
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
and
(v,v)
is
O_{p}(1)
, all at the
\mathcal{R}_{m}
scale and uniformly in
\eta
, each
O_{p}(m^{-1})
at the criterion scale. For
(v,v)
, introduce the symmetric zero-diagonal matrix
A
with entries
A_{jk}:=\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk}/(m-1)^{2}
for
j\neq k
, so that the pairing reads
T_{(v,v)}=\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}A\boldsymbol{v}
. The components of
v
are conditionally centered but not conditionally independent, so the count is run after the sandwich
T_{(v,v)}=\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}A^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}
with
A^{\prime}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}A\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
and
\boldsymbol{u}:=\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm raw}
: the components of
\boldsymbol{u}
are conditionally i.i.d., centered, with conditional covariance
D
, and this is the operational content of applying Lemma
14
at the conditional covariance
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
. Lemma
14
, applied to
\boldsymbol{u}
at
A^{\prime}
through its conditional-variance clause (
29
) (the kurtosis-excess terms bounded through the interpolated fourth moment
\bar{M}_{4}=\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p}
), gives

\operatorname{Var}\bigl(T_{(v,v)}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)\leq\bigl(2\sigma^{4}_{\max}+\bar{M}^{2/p}_{2p}\bigr)\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

using
\lVert D^{1/2}A^{\prime}D^{1/2}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}\lVert A^{\prime}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}
and
\lVert A^{\prime}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}
(the Frobenius contraction of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\otimes\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
). The conditional mean
\mathbb{E}[T_{(v,v)}\mid\mathbf{X}]=\operatorname{tr}(A^{\prime}\,D)=\operatorname{tr}(A\,C_{v})
with
C_{v}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
does not vanish, and

\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}[T_{(v,v)}\mid\mathbf{X}]\bigr\rvert\leq\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\,\lVert C_{v}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}},\qquad\begin{aligned} \lVert C_{v}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}&=\operatorname{tr}\bigl(C_{v}^{2}\bigr)\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}\operatorname{tr}\bigl(C_{v}\bigr)\\
&=\sigma^{2}_{\max}\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\bigr)\leq\sigma^{4}_{\max}\,(m-d_{\theta}):\end{aligned}

the Loewner bound
C_{v}^{2}\leq\lVert C_{v}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}C_{v}
with
\lVert C_{v}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}
, the cyclicity
\operatorname{tr}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})=\operatorname{tr}(D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})
through the idempotence of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
, and
\operatorname{tr}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D)\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}\operatorname{rank}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})
with
\operatorname{rank}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})=m-d_{\theta}
: the rank-
d_{\theta}
object of the problem is the complementary projector
\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}=\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{S}}
, which carries the replacement defect of the setup, whereas
C_{v}
lives on
\operatorname{range}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})
and carries the full noise mass. Hence
\lVert C_{v}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}\sqrt{m}
and the conditional mean is
O_{p}(1)
at the
\mathcal{R}_{m}
scale. It remains to bound
\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}
. Expanding the square over anchor pairs, the summands with
i\neq i^{\prime}
have conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}\bigl[r_{\eta}(X_{i};y,z)\,r_{\eta}(X_{i^{\prime}};y,z)\bigm|y,z\bigr]=\bigl(\mathbb{E}_{X}[r_{\eta}(X;y,z)]\bigr)^{2}=0
at the frozen realized pair
(y,z)=(X_{j},X_{k})
(anchor-centeredness), so only the
i=i^{\prime}
diagonal survives in expectation and

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\bigr]\leq\frac{m(m-1)(m-2)\,K_{r}^{2}}{(m-1)^{4}}=O\bigl(m^{-1}\bigr),\qquad K_{r}:=2\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2},

whence
\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
by Markov’s inequality. Collecting,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[T_{(v,v)}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]\leq\bigl(2\sigma^{4}_{\max}+\bar{M}^{2/p}_{2p}\bigr)\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\,\lVert C_{v}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=O_{p}(1)
,
and the variance-event splitting of the setup gives
T_{(v,v)}=O_{p}(1)
at the
\mathcal{R}_{m}
scale, that is,
O_{p}(m^{-1})
at the criterion scale, uniformly in
\eta
. The pairings
(v,r)
and
(r,v)
are linear in
v
with coefficient vector
\Gamma^{(m)}\boldsymbol{r}
and die by conditional Chebyshev; the pairing
(r,r)
dies by pathwise Cauchy–Schwarz. For the first two, symmetry of
\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk}
in its pair slots (the symmetry of
r_{\eta}(x;y,z)
in
(y,z)
) combines the two cross terms into one ordered sum,

T_{(v,r),(r,v)}=\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{j\neq k}\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk}\,v_{j}\,r_{k}=\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\,\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}\bigl(\Gamma^{(m)}\boldsymbol{r}\bigr):

linear in
v
, with the design-only coefficient vector
\Gamma^{(m)}\boldsymbol{r}
(the diagonal convention being immaterial at the final order). The conditional mean vanishes,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[T_{(v,r),(r,v)}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\,(\Gamma^{(m)}\boldsymbol{r})^{\top}\,\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{v}\mid\mathbf{X}]=0

(noise-centeredness), and the conditional variance is

\operatorname{Var}\bigl(T_{(v,r),(r,v)}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)=\frac{4}{(m-1)^{4}}\,(\Gamma^{(m)}\boldsymbol{r})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\,(\Gamma^{(m)}\boldsymbol{r})\leq\frac{4\,\sigma^{2}_{\max}}{(m-1)^{4}}\,\lVert\Gamma^{(m)}\boldsymbol{r}\rVert^{2}=O_{p}\bigl(m^{-1}\bigr),

the inequality being the Loewner bound
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}\boldsymbol{I}_{m}
. The order of the coefficient vector: Cauchy–Schwarz gives

\lVert\Gamma^{(m)}\boldsymbol{r}\rVert\leq\lVert\Gamma^{(m)}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\,\lVert\boldsymbol{r}\rVert

with
\lVert\boldsymbol{r}\rVert=\sqrt{m}\,\lVert r\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=O_{p}(1)
and

\lVert\Gamma^{(m)}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=O_{p}(m^{3})
by the anchor-centeredness count
(expanding
\sum_{j\neq k}(\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk})^{2}
over anchor pairs
i,i^{\prime}
, the terms
i\neq i^{\prime}
have conditional expectation

\mathbb{E}\bigl[r_{\eta}(X_{i};y,z)\,r_{\eta}(X_{i^{\prime}};y,z)\bigm|y,z\bigr]=\bigl(\mathbb{E}_{X}[r_{\eta}(X;y,z)]\bigr)^{2}=0

at the frozen realized pair
(y,z)=(X_{j},X_{k})
, leaving the
i=i^{\prime}
diagonal

O(m^{3}K_{r}^{2})
), so
\lVert\Gamma^{(m)}\boldsymbol{r}\rVert=O_{p}(m^{3/2})
. The variance-event splitting of the setup then gives
T_{(v,r),(r,v)}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
uniformly in
\eta
, that is,
O_{p}(m^{-3/2})
at the criterion scale. For
(r,r)
no centering is available (
r
is not centered), so the route is pathwise:

\bigl\lvert T_{(r,r)}\bigr\rvert\leq\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\Bigl(\sum_{j<k}\bigl(\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk}\bigr)^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\sum_{j<k}r_{j}^{2}r_{k}^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\,\lVert\Gamma^{(m)}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\,\lVert\boldsymbol{r}\rVert^{2}=O_{p}\bigl(m^{-1/2}\bigr)

uniformly in
\eta
, the second factor using
\lVert\boldsymbol{r}\boldsymbol{r}^{\top}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}=\lVert\boldsymbol{r}\rVert^{2}=m\,\lVert r\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}^{2}=O_{p}(1)
, both factors being
O_{p}(m^{3/2})
and
O_{p}(1)
from the anchor count and the perturbation rate. The pairings
(s,r)
and
(r,s)
are
O_{p}(1)
by pathwise Cauchy–Schwarz: no centering is available (both factors are design-measurable and non-centered), so the route is a two-norm bound,

\lvert T_{(s,r),(r,s)}\rvert\leq\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\Bigl(\sum_{j<k}\bigl(\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk}\bigr)^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\sum_{j<k}\bigl(s_{j}r_{k}+r_{j}s_{k}\bigr)^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}.

The kernel factor:
\bigl(\sum_{j<k}(\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk})^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}\leq\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\,\lVert\Gamma^{(m)}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}
(unordered pairs are half the ordered ones), with the deterministic envelope

\lVert\Gamma^{(m)}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq m(m-1)(m-2)^{2}K_{r}^{2}=O(m^{3}K_{r}^{2})

from
\lvert\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk}\rvert\leq(m-2)K_{r}
,
K_{r}:=2\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}
. The residual factor: by
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
and the symmetry bookkeeping
\sum_{j<k}(s_{j}^{2}r_{k}^{2}+r_{j}^{2}s_{k}^{2})\leq(\sum_{j}s_{j}^{2})(\sum_{k}r_{k}^{2})
,

\Bigl(\sum_{j<k}\bigl(s_{j}r_{k}+r_{j}s_{k}\bigr)^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{2}\,\lVert\boldsymbol{s}\rVert\,\lVert\boldsymbol{r}\rVert=\sqrt{2m}\,\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}\cdot O_{p}(1),

using
\lVert\boldsymbol{s}\rVert\leq m^{1/2}\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}
(the part (b) standing assumption) and
\lVert\boldsymbol{r}\rVert=\sqrt{m}\,\lVert r\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=O_{p}(1)
(the perturbation rate). Assembling,

\lvert T_{(s,r),(r,s)}\rvert\leq\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\cdot O\bigl(m^{3/2}K_{r}\bigr)\cdot O\bigl(m^{1/2}\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}\bigr)\cdot O_{p}(1)=O\bigl(K_{r}\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}\bigr)\cdot O_{p}(1)=O_{p}(1),

the printed
\tfrac{4}{(m-1)^{2}}\lVert\Gamma^{(m)}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\lVert\boldsymbol{s}\rVert\,\lVert\boldsymbol{r}\rVert
bound with the constant stated generously. No rate is available or needed:
O_{p}(1)
at the
\mathcal{R}_{m}
scale is
O_{p}(m^{-1})
at criterion scale, inside the budget.

The pairings
(s,v)
and
(v,s)
are linear in
v
with coefficient vector
\Gamma^{(m)}\boldsymbol{s}
, and die by the noise centering. Symmetry of
\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk}
combines them into one ordered sum,

T_{(s,v),(v,s)}=\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{j\neq k}\Gamma^{(m)}_{jk}\,v_{j}\,s_{k}=\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\,\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}\bigl(\Gamma^{(m)}\boldsymbol{s}\bigr),

linear in
v
with the design-only coefficient vector
\boldsymbol{c}:=\Gamma^{(m)}\boldsymbol{s}
. The conditional mean vanishes,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[T_{(s,v),(v,s)}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr]=\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\,\boldsymbol{c}^{\top}\,\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{v}\mid\mathbf{X}]=0

(noise-centeredness), and the conditional variance is bounded through the same Loewner chain as the
(v,r),(r,v)
pairing,

\operatorname{Var}\bigl(T_{(s,v),(v,s)}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)=\frac{4}{(m-1)^{4}}\,\boldsymbol{c}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}D\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\,\boldsymbol{c}\leq\frac{4\,\sigma^{2}_{\max}}{(m-1)^{4}}\,\lVert\boldsymbol{c}\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{4\,\sigma^{2}_{\max}}{(m-1)^{4}}\,\lVert\Gamma^{(m)}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\,\lVert\boldsymbol{s}\rVert^{2},

with
\lVert\boldsymbol{c}\rVert\leq\lVert\Gamma^{(m)}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\lVert\boldsymbol{s}\rVert
by Cauchy–Schwarz and
\lVert\boldsymbol{s}\rVert^{2}\leq m\,\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}^{2}
; the anchor-centeredness count recorded above gives
\mathbb{E}\lVert\Gamma^{(m)}\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq m(m-1)(m-2)K_{r}^{2}=O(m^{3})
, so

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\operatorname{Var}\bigl(T_{(s,v),(v,s)}\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr)\Bigr]\leq\frac{4\,\sigma^{2}_{\max}\,m\,\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,m(m-1)(m-2)K_{r}^{2}}{(m-1)^{4}}=O(1),

and Markov’s inequality, followed by the variance-event splitting of the setup at the unit scale, gives
T_{(s,v),(v,s)}=O_{p}(1)
uniformly in
\eta
, that is,
O_{p}(m^{-1})
at the criterion scale. All eight pairings are therefore
O_{p}(1)
at the
\mathcal{R}_{m}
scale and
O_{p}(m^{-1})
at the criterion scale, within the budget of the collection (
44
) below.

The pairing
(s,s)
does not die: its centering lives only in the anchor slot of

r_{\eta}
, and its first Hoeffding projection survives. Write

T_{m}(\eta):=\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j<k\\
j,k\neq i\end{subarray}}r_{\eta}(X_{i};X_{j},X_{k})\,s(X_{j})s(X_{k}),\qquad u_{\eta}:=w^{*2}_{\eta},

and recall that
\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta)=\lVert w^{*}_{\eta}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}
.
The conditional mean given one coordinate is read off from the position of the
frozen index, and the position is not fixed by the conditioning: freezing the value

X_{1}=x
pins the value at site
1
and leaves the index combinatorics to place it,
so the terms of
T_{m}
fall into three classes. Index
1
as the anchor (
i=1
,

\binom{m-1}{2}
inner pairs); index
1
in a pair slot (
i\neq 1
with
j=1
or
k=1
,

(m-1)(m-2)
terms, more numerous than the anchor class); index
1
away from all
three slots; the conditional mean is the sum of the three class expectations.

Anchor class.
For a fresh pair
\{j,k\}
the expectation factors by
independence, each factor being
\mathbb{E}[s(X_{j})g_{\eta}(x,X_{j})]=w^{*}_{\eta}(x)
and
\mathbb{E}[s(X_{k})g_{\eta}(x,X_{k})]=w^{*}_{\eta}(x)
, so the
product contributes
w^{*2}_{\eta}(x)=u_{\eta}(x)
; the constant term is disposed of
by inserting the definition of the composed kernel and swapping the expectations,

\mathbb{E}[(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{j},X_{k})s(X_{j})s(X_{k})]=\mathbb{E}_{W}[w^{*2}_{\eta}(W)]=\lVert w^{*}_{\eta}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}
. The class
contributes
\binom{m-1}{2}\bigl[u_{\eta}(x)-\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta)\bigr]
.

Slot class.
For
i\neq 1
with
j=1
, the summand is

r_{\eta}(X_{i};x,X_{k})s(x)s(X_{k})
with fresh anchor and fresh partner, and its
two pieces are individually nonzero: the product piece carries the conditional
expectation
\mathbb{E}[g_{\eta}(X_{i},x)g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{k})s(X_{k})\mid X_{i},x]=\mathbb{E}_{X_{i}}[g_{\eta}(x,X_{i})w^{*}_{\eta}(X_{i})]=\kappa_{\eta}(x)
, by the
first anchor identity inside and the second outside; the constant piece carries

\mathbb{E}[(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(x,X_{k})\,s(X_{k})\mid x]=\kappa_{\eta}(x)
; the bracket
cancels as
\kappa_{\eta}(x)-\kappa_{\eta}(x)=0
, a cancellation funded by the anchor
identities rather than an absence of centering. The class contributes zero.

Away class.
With
1\notin\{i,j,k\}
all three indices are fresh, and the
defining centering
\mathbb{E}[r_{\eta}(X;X_{j},X_{k})\mid X_{j},X_{k}]=0
kills the
class. Hence

\mathbb{E}\bigl[T_{m}(\eta)\bigm|X_{1}=x\bigr]=\frac{m-2}{m-1}\bigl[u_{\eta}(x)-\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta)\bigr],\qquad\text{whence}\qquad\mathbb{E}\bigl[T_{m}(\eta)\bigr]=0,

(39)

the coefficient
\frac{m-2}{m-1}
being
\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\binom{m-1}{2}
, the
fresh-pair count of the anchor class; the mean vanishing because

\mathbb{E}[u_{\eta}(X)]=\lVert w^{*}_{\eta}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}
: there is no
deterministic drift, the raw masses of size
m^{3}
in
T_{m}
cancelling through

\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta)=\lVert w^{*}_{\eta}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}
.

Each summand of
T_{m}
involves at most three design points, so Möbius inversion
into the Hoeffding components is exact, with no remainder term of any order. Fold
the double sum by letting each member of an unordered triple take the anchor role
once: with
h(x;y,z):=r_{\eta}(x;y,z)s(y)s(z)
and the symmetric kernel

\tilde{h}(x,y,z):=h(x;y,z)+h(y;x,z)+h(z;x,y)
,

T_{m}(\eta)=\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{\{a,b,c\}\subset[m]}\tilde{h}(a,b,c)
.
The three projections are

\tilde{g}_{1}(x)=\mathbb{E}[\tilde{h}(x,Y,Z)]=u_{\eta}(x)-\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta),

the anchor computation above with its two slot terms zero;

\tilde{g}_{2}(x,y):=\mathbb{E}_{Z}[\tilde{h}(x,y,Z)]-\tilde{g}_{1}(x)-\tilde{g}_{1}(y)=s(y)\Phi_{\eta}(x,y)+s(x)\Phi_{\eta}(y,x)-\bigl[u_{\eta}(x)+u_{\eta}(y)-2\,\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta)\bigr]=\varphi_{\eta}(x,y),

with
\Phi_{\eta}(x,y):=g_{\eta}(x,y)\,w^{*}_{\eta}(x)-\kappa_{\eta}(y)
the
depth-two reduction of the anchor identities and the slot-slot projection dying by
anchor-centeredness; and
\tilde{g}_{3}:=\tilde{h}-\sum_{\text{pairs}}\tilde{g}_{2}-\sum_{\text{points}}\tilde{g}_{1}
, which is the printed
g_{3,\eta}
, its last
pair sum being
\sum_{\text{pairs}}h(c;a,b)=\tilde{h}(x,y,z)
. The components are
centered by construction,
\mathbb{E}[\tilde{h}]=0
being the mean-zero statement of
(
39
). Summing the pointwise identity over the
\binom{m}{3}

triples, each point lies in
\binom{m-1}{2}
triples and each pair in
m-2
, and
the prefactor
\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}
converts these counts into the coefficients of

\begin{split}T_{m}(\eta)=&\frac{m-2}{m-1}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bigl[u_{\eta}(X_{i})-\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta)\bigr]+\frac{2(m-2)}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\varphi_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})\\
&+\frac{2}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i<j<l}g_{3,\eta}(X_{i},X_{j},X_{l}),\end{split}

(40)

with the centered two-point kernel

\displaystyle\varphi_{\eta}(x,y):={}

\displaystyle-\bigl[u_{\eta}(x)+u_{\eta}(y)-2\,\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta)\bigr]+g_{\eta}(x,y)\bigl[s(y)\,w^{*}_{\eta}(x)+s(x)\,w^{*}_{\eta}(y)\bigr]

\displaystyle-s(y)\,\kappa_{\eta}(y)-s(x)\,\kappa_{\eta}(x),\qquad\mathbb{E}_{X}\bigl[\varphi_{\eta}(x,X)\bigr]=0,

and the three-point kernel, centered in each coordinate separately,

\displaystyle g_{3,\eta}(x,y,z):={}

\displaystyle\bigl[u_{\eta}(x)+u_{\eta}(y)+u_{\eta}(z)-3\,\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta)\bigr]

\displaystyle-\sum_{\{a,b\}\subset\{x,y,z\}}\Bigl[s(b)\bigl(g_{\eta}(a,b)\,w^{*}_{\eta}(a)-\kappa_{\eta}(b)\bigr)+s(a)\bigl(g_{\eta}(a,b)\,w^{*}_{\eta}(b)-\kappa_{\eta}(a)\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\{a,b\}\subset\{x,y,z\}}\Bigl[g_{\eta}(c,a)\,g_{\eta}(c,b)-(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(a,b)\Bigr]s(a)s(b),

where
c
denotes the remaining point of
\{x,y,z\}
, and the last sum runs over the
three unordered pairs
\{a,b\}
with
c
the third point. All three kernels are bounded by deterministic,
\mathbf{X}
-free
envelopes:
\lvert u_{\eta}\rvert\leq U_{w}^{2}
,
\lvert\varphi_{\eta}\rvert\leq M_{\varphi}
,
\lvert g_{3,\eta}\rvert\leq M_{3}
, with

U_{w}:=(4\lambda_{L})^{-1/2}\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}
and

M_{\varphi},M_{3}
depending only on
(\lVert s\rVert_{\infty},\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}},\bar{k},\lambda_{L})
through the anchor envelopes of the setup.
The degenerate sums are controlled by their exact second moments, and the control
is uniform in
\eta
because both ingredients are: the centering identities hold at
every fixed tuning, the anchor identities carrying no
\eta
-dependence, and the
envelopes are
\eta
-free. The centering kills every cross term of the chaos
squares, pairs of pairs sharing an index by conditioning on the shared index,
disjoint pairs by independence, so

\operatorname{Var}\bigl(\sum_{i<j}\varphi_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})\bigr)=\binom{m}{2}\,\mathbb{E}[\varphi_{\eta}^{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]\leq\binom{m}{2}M_{\varphi}^{2}
,
likewise
\operatorname{Var}\bigl(\sum_{i<j<l}g_{3,\eta}\bigr)=\binom{m}{3}\,\mathbb{E}[g_{3,\eta}^{2}]\leq\binom{m}{3}M_{3}^{2}
, and

\sum_{i<j}\varphi_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})=O_{p}(m)
,

\sum_{i<j<l}g_{3,\eta}=O_{p}(m^{3/2})
. Collecting, the linear coefficient of
T_{m}/m
rounds
as
\frac{m-2}{m(m-1)}=\frac{1}{m}-\frac{1}{m(m-1)}
, a deterministic
O(m^{-1})
on the
linear sum because
\lvert u_{\eta}-\theta_{g\circ g}\rvert\leq U_{w}^{2}+\theta_{g\circ g}
bounds the sum pathwise; the pairwise prefactor is

O(m^{-2})
against
O_{p}(m)
, giving
O_{p}(m^{-1})
; the triple prefactor is

O(m^{-3})
against
O_{p}(m^{3/2})
, giving
O_{p}(m^{-3/2})
, absorbed. Hence

\frac{\mathcal{R}^{(s,s)}_{m}(\eta)}{m}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bigl[w^{*2}_{\eta}(X_{i})-\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta)\bigr]+O_{p}(m^{-1})\qquad\text{uniformly in }\eta,

(41)

the coefficient
\frac{m-2}{m-1}
differing from
1
by
O(m^{-1})
. The first
Hoeffding projection of the remainder is a centered average of bounded summands
and is routed to the pooled family below, where it becomes the sixth bracket of
(
43
); it is centered because
\mathbb{E}[w^{*2}_{\eta}(X)]=\lVert w^{*}_{\eta}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta)
, and
Gaussian-borne because it is built from
w^{*}_{\eta}
, the kernel ridge
smoothing of the signal in
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}
, the two higher chaos
components having died at
O_{p}(m^{-1})
and
O_{p}(m^{-3/2})
. At the

\sqrt{m}
scale the entire remainder channel contributes only this
Gaussian-borne linear term. The pairings
(s,v)+(v,s)
of
-2U_{m}+Q_{m}+R_{m}

are linear in the noise, and
Q_{m}
carries none, its coefficients

\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{2}
being squared; the two carriers are those displayed
at the start of the signal–noise channel above,
-\frac{4}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}v_{j}\,\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}s(X_{i})\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
through the kernel

g_{\eta}
and
+\frac{2}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}v_{j}\,\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}s(X_{i})\,(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})(X_{i},X_{j})
through the composed kernel,
and each inner leave-one-out average is an empirical plug-in of a population
anchor in the sense of (
36
): for the generic kernel

\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}\in\{g_{\eta},(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})_{\eta}\}
the anchor is

\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(x):=\mathbb{E}_{Y}[\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(x,Y)\,\Pi f(Y)]
, and the
anchor identities give
\vartheta_{g}=w^{*}_{\eta}
and

\vartheta_{g\circ g}=\kappa_{\eta}
respectively. For each of the two inner
factors of the mixed channel, the empirical fluctuation of the anchor around
this population limit is
L^{2}
-small: with
\hat{\vartheta}_{j}:=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}s(X_{i})\,\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
and
\zeta_{ij}:=s(X_{i})\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})-\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X_{j})
,

\hat{\vartheta}_{j}-\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X_{j})=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}\zeta_{ij},\qquad\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\bigl(\hat{\vartheta}_{j}-\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X_{j})\bigr)^{2}\Bigr]=\frac{\mathbb{E}\bigl[\zeta_{12}^{2}\bigr]}{m-1}\leq\frac{C_{\zeta}}{m-1},

the identity inserting the definition of the
\zeta_{ij}
into the leave-one-out
average, the second moment being computed conditionally on
X_{j}
: the
summands
\zeta_{ij}
,
i\neq j
, are bounded and centered over
i
for each
fixed
j
,
\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{ij}\mid X_{j}]=\mathbb{E}[s(X)\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(X,X_{j})]-\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X_{j})=0
by the defining property of the anchor, and
conditionally independent, being functions of the independent fresh draws

X_{i}
, so that the cross terms
i\neq i^{\prime}
vanish in the inner second moment,

\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{ij}\zeta_{i^{\prime}j}\mid X_{j}]=\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{ij}\mid X_{j}]\,\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{i^{\prime}j}\mid X_{j}]=0
, and only
the diagonal
\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{ij}^{2}]

survives;
C_{\zeta}
depends only on the entrywise envelopes,
\lvert s\rvert\leq\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}
,
\lvert g_{\eta}\rvert\leq\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}
,
\lvert(g_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta})\rvert\leq\bar{k}(4\lambda_{L})^{-1}
, with the anchor bounds of the setup, so the anchors
fluctuate at rate
m^{-1/2}
in mean square, uniformly in
\eta
; the same
computation applies with
g_{\eta}
. Inserting

\hat{\vartheta}_{j}=\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X_{j})+(\hat{\vartheta}_{j}-\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X_{j}))
into the two carriers, each anchor fluctuation
enters as
\frac{c}{m}\sum_{j}v_{j}(\hat{\vartheta}_{j}-\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X_{j}))
with

; the pairing with
v
is
conditionally centered with conditional variance

\frac{c^{2}}{m^{2}}\sum_{j}\sigma^{2}(X_{j})\bigl(\hat{\vartheta}_{j}-\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X_{j})\bigr)^{2}\leq\frac{16\,\sigma^{2}_{\max}}{m^{2}}\sum_{j}\bigl(\hat{\vartheta}_{j}-\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X_{j})\bigr)^{2}=O_{p}(m^{-2})
by the displayed bound, the factor
16=4^{2}
taking the worse
of the two coefficients and the
O_{p}(m^{-2})
following by Markov’s
inequality since
\mathbb{E}[\sum_{j}(\hat{\vartheta}_{j}-\vartheta_{\mathfrak{g}}(X_{j}))^{2}]\leq m\,C_{\zeta}/(m-1)=O(1)
, so conditional
Chebyshev’s inequality turns each anchor fluctuation into an

O_{p}(m^{-1})
term, uniformly in
\eta
; and the diagonal corrections are handled by aligning the leave-one-out inner average with the full-sample one: with
\bar{\vartheta}_{j}:=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}s(X_{i})\,\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
,

\hat{\vartheta}_{j}=\frac{m}{m-1}\,\bar{\vartheta}_{j}-\frac{1}{m-1}\,s(X_{j})\,\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(X_{j},X_{j}),

so that, at the prefactors
c\in\{-4,+2\}
of the two carriers, the residual diagonal term enters as
-\frac{c}{m(m-1)}\sum_{j}v_{j}\,s(X_{j})\,\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(X_{j},X_{j})
, is conditionally centered given
\mathbf{X}
, and has conditional variance

\frac{c^{2}}{m^{2}(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{j}\sigma^{2}(X_{j})\,s(X_{j})^{2}\,\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}(X_{j},X_{j})^{2}\leq\frac{16\,\sigma^{2}_{\max}\,\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}\,\lVert s\rVert^{2}_{\infty}}{m\,(m-1)^{2}}=O(m^{-3}),

hence
O_{p}(m^{-1})
after the average over
j
, uniformly in
\eta
. Collecting,

\Bigl[-2U_{m}+Q_{m}+R_{m}\Bigr]_{\text{mixed}}=\frac{2}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}v_{j}\Bigl[-2\,w^{*}_{\eta}(X_{j})+\kappa_{\eta}(X_{j})\Bigr]+O_{p}(m^{-1})\quad\text{uniformly in }\eta,

the bracket collecting the two coefficients as

-4\,w^{*}_{\eta}+2\,\kappa_{\eta}=2\,(-2\,w^{*}_{\eta}+\kappa_{\eta})
. The
coordinate-wise identity
-2t_{\ell}+t_{\ell}^{2}=(1-t_{\ell})^{2}-1
, with
(
34

of the setup, rewrites the bracket as the

-2\,w^{*}_{\eta}+\kappa_{\eta}=(a_{\eta}-b_{\eta})-s:

both sides have spectral coordinates,
w^{*}_{\eta}
and
\kappa_{\eta}

reading at the multipliers
t_{\ell}
and
t_{\ell}^{2}
against

(\Pi f)_{\ell}(\gamma)
and
a_{\eta}-b_{\eta}
at
(1-t_{\ell})^{2}
by
(
34
), so the left side carries
-2t_{\ell}+t_{\ell}^{2}
against

(\Pi f)_{\ell}(\gamma)
and the right side
(1-t_{\ell})^{2}-1
against the
same, equal by the binomial identity. The signal shift is supplied by the
level, whose displayed split pairs
v
against

at

+\frac{2}{m}\sum_{j}v_{j}\,s(X_{j})

pairing collect into the total noise-borne linear channel

, applied
twice. The insertion of
\Pi
is exactly free: the pooled family reads the

h_{\eta}:=\Pi(a_{\eta}-b_{\eta})
, the component of

a_{\eta}-b_{\eta}
orthogonal to the score span, and the discarded component

lies in the score span by construction,

being the projection onto the span of the

score functions,

identically, because

is the noise component

(the finite-sample

projects off). The vanishing is an algebraic identity at

\eta
. The total noise-borne linear channel of the criterion is therefore the

\Bigl[-2U_{m}+Q_{m}+R_{m}\Bigr]_{\text{mixed}}+\frac{2}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}v_{j}\,s(X_{j})=\frac{2}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}v_{j}\,h_{\eta}(X_{j})+O_{p}(m^{-1}),\qquad h_{\eta}:=\Pi(a_{\eta}-b_{\eta}),

(42)

uniformly in
\eta
, and the process enters the pooled family
(

The signal–signal channel.
Pairing
(s,s)
is the only bilinear pairing with a nonzero mean, and its centered linear part survives beside it. By the anchor decomposition (
36
) applied to the two kernels,

\displaystyle-2\,U^{(g)}_{m}(\eta)+U^{(g\circ g)}_{m}(\eta)

\displaystyle=-2\,\theta_{g}(\eta)+\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Bigl[-4\bigl(\Pi f(X_{i})w^{*}_{\eta}(X_{i})-\theta_{g}(\eta)\bigr)\Bigr.\Bigr.

\displaystyle\Bigl.\Bigl.+2\bigl(\Pi f(X_{i})\kappa_{\eta}(X_{i})-\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta)\bigr)\Bigr]+O_{p}(m^{-1}),

the last estimate being (
37
). The deterministic constants collect with the signal level: since
P_{\eta}
is self-adjoint on

and
\theta_{g}=\langle s,w^{*}_{\eta}\rangle_{L^{2}}
,

,

Assembling the level, whose

), the three channels, and

\displaystyle\psi_{\eta}(x,v):={}

\displaystyle\bigl[v^{2}-\bar{\sigma}^{2}\bigr]+\bigl[s(x)^{2}-\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\bigr]-4\bigl[s(x)\,w^{*}_{\eta}(x)-\theta_{g}(\eta)\bigr]

Drift term.

The square completion is performed around the population fit
w^{*}_{\eta}
, and the surviving
r
-pairings (classes
(s,r)+(r,s)
,
(r,v)+(v,r)
,
(r,r)
) are collected into a single pairing against
r
. The
r
-content of the criterion is design-borne and noise-free:
r
is fixed given the design, so each such pairing measures the estimation error against the signal, against the noise, or against itself, and the term collected from them is the drift of the criterion away from its risk.

The level.
In the level the
r
-pairings are
2\langle s,r\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}
plus
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{lev}}_{m}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
, as displayed at the level above: the
v\cdot r
piece is conditionally centered,
\mathbb{E}[v_{i}\mid\mathbf{X}]=0
with
r
design-fixed, at conditional variance
\frac{4\sigma^{2}_{\max}}{m^{2}}\sum_{i}r(X_{i})^{2}=\frac{4\sigma^{2}_{\max}}{m}\lVert r\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=O_{p}(m^{-2})
, and the
r^{2}
piece equals
\lVert r\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=O_{p}(m^{-1})
by the perturbation rate. The cross term survives as the level’s copy of the pairing
(s,r)
.

In
-2U_{m}
.
The pairings with one
r
-factor are
-\frac{4}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}s(X_{i})\,r(X_{j})\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
, the orderings
(s,r)
and
(r,s)
giving equal double sums by the symmetry of
g_{\eta}
. The pairings with two
v
-factors are centered after the raw-noise switch of the noise–noise channel above, at its
O_{p}(m^{-1})
replacement cost, and die by the second-moment count of Lemma
14
at the raw-noise covariance
D
. The pairings with a
v
- and an
r
-factor die by conditional Cauchy–Schwarz: with
r
fixed given the design and
v
conditionally centered,
\mathbb{E}\bigl\lVert\bigl(\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}v_{i}g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})\bigr)_{j}\bigr\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{P}_{m}}\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}_{\max}\bar{k}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}}{m-1}
, the square over the anchor indices
i\neq i^{\prime}
losing its cross terms to
\mathbb{E}[v\mid X]=0
and its diagonal to the kernel envelope
\lvert g_{\eta}\rvert\leq\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}
, so the second factor is
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
uniformly in
\eta
and the product with the rate
\lVert r\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
is
O_{p}(m^{-1})
. The
r,r
-pairings are
O_{p}(m^{-1})
: with the kernel envelope, they are bounded by
\frac{4\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}}{m(m-1)}\bigl(\sum_{i}\lvert r(X_{i})\rvert\bigr)^{2}
, and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality
\bigl(\sum_{i}\lvert r(X_{i})\rvert\bigr)^{2}\leq m\sum_{i}r(X_{i})^{2}=m^{2}\lVert r\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}
against the
m^{-2}
prefactor leaves
O(\lVert r\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})})=O_{p}(m^{-1})
, the quadratic pairing dying at the square of the perturbation rate. The inner factor of the surviving pairing is the empirical anchor
\hat{\vartheta}^{(g)}_{j}:=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\neq j}s(X_{i})\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})
of the signal–noise channel; the anchor fluctuation is
L^{2}
-small in mean square uniformly in
\eta
as displayed there, and Cauchy–Schwarz against the design-fixed
r
costs
\bigl\lvert\frac{4}{m}\sum_{j}r(X_{j})\bigl(\hat{\vartheta}^{(g)}_{j}-w^{*}_{\eta}(X_{j})\bigr)\bigr\rvert\leq 4\,\lVert r\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}\bigl(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j}\bigl(\hat{\vartheta}^{(g)}_{j}-w^{*}_{\eta}(X_{j})\bigr)^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})\,O_{p}(m^{-1/2})=O_{p}(m^{-1})
, so the pairing equals
-4\langle w^{*}_{\eta},r\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}+O_{p}(m^{-1})
.

In
R_{m}
.
The exact reduction
R_{m}=\frac{m-2}{m-1}\,U^{(g\circ g)}_{m}+\mathcal{R}_{m}/m
runs the same computation with the composed kernel, whose anchor is
\vartheta_{g\circ g}=\kappa_{\eta}
by the anchor identities of the setup, and gives
+2\langle\kappa_{\eta},r\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}+O_{p}(m^{-1})
. Collecting the three surviving pairings and inserting the coordinate identity
s-2w^{*}_{\eta}+\kappa_{\eta}=a_{\eta}-b_{\eta}
of (
34
), the spectral collapse
1-2t_{\ell}+t_{\ell}^{2}=(1-t_{\ell})^{2}
