already used by the signal–noise channel,

\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)-R_{\infty}(\eta)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\psi_{\eta}(X_{i},v_{i})+2\bigl\langle a_{\eta}-b_{\eta},\,r\bigr\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}+O_{p}(m^{-1/2})\qquad\text{uniformly in }\eta,

(44)

the drift term being denoted
\mathrm{Drift}_{m}(\eta):=2\langle a_{\eta}-b_{\eta},\,r\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}
in the sequel. The drift is the only surviving coupling of the criterion to the estimation error: it is linear in
r
, carries no noise, and every other
r
-bearing pairing has died above at
O_{p}(m^{-1})
. For its bound, the contraction
\lVert(I-P_{\eta})^{2}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
behind
a_{\eta}-b_{\eta}=(I-P_{\eta})^{2}s
holds on
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
and not on the empirical metric
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})
in which the pairing is taken: the contractivity of
P_{\eta}
is spectral,
P_{\eta}
being a function of the population covariance with multipliers
t_{\ell}\in[0,1]
, and the empirical measure induces a different geometry, in which no bound of
\lVert(I-P_{\eta})^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}
by
\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}
follows. The route is instead a deterministic sup-norm envelope of the coefficient function, followed by Cauchy–Schwarz in
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})
. The setup supplies, uniformly in
(\eta,x)
,
\lvert s(x)\rvert\leq\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}
and
\lvert w^{*}_{\eta}(x)\rvert\leq U_{w}
with
U_{w}:=(4\lambda_{L})^{-1/2}\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}
, the anchor bound being the RKHS argument of the setup,
\lvert w^{*}_{\eta}(x)\rvert\leq\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert w^{*}_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}
with
\lVert w^{*}_{\eta}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}}=\sum_{\ell}\frac{\mu_{\ell}}{(\mu_{\ell}+\lambda)^{2}}\,(\Pi f)_{\ell}^{2}\leq(4\lambda_{L})^{-1}\lVert s\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}}
by the AM–GM bound
\mu+\lambda\geq 2\sqrt{\mu\lambda}
on each spectral coordinate; and
\lvert b_{\eta}(x)\rvert\leq\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert a_{\eta}\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}
, the last step being the
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
-contraction of
P_{\eta}
, legitimate here because the anchor bound is a population statement; the triangle inequality
\lvert a_{\eta}(x)\rvert\leq\lvert s(x)\rvert+\lvert w^{*}_{\eta}(x)\rvert
and
\lvert(a_{\eta}-b_{\eta})(x)\rvert\leq\lvert a_{\eta}(x)\rvert+\lvert b_{\eta}(x)\rvert
give

C_{\mathrm{dr}}:=\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}+U_{w}+\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\bigl\lvert(a_{\eta}-b_{\eta})(x)\bigr\rvert\leq C_{\mathrm{dr}}\quad\text{for all }\eta\in\Upsilon.

(45)

the constant
C_{\mathrm{dr}}
being deterministic and
\eta
-free, so the envelope is uniform over
\Upsilon
and no stochastic equicontinuity enters the drift bound. Cauchy–Schwarz in
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})
,
\bigl\lvert\frac{2}{m}\sum_{i}(a_{\eta}-b_{\eta})(X_{i})\,r(X_{i})\bigr\rvert\leq 2\bigl(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}(a_{\eta}-b_{\eta})(X_{i})^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}\lVert r\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}
, followed by (
45
) and the perturbation rate
\lVert r\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
of the setup, gives

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl\lvert\mathrm{Drift}_{m}(\eta)\bigr\rvert\leq 2\,C_{\mathrm{dr}}\,\lVert r\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=O_{p}\bigl(m^{-1/2}\bigr).

(46)

The bound is deliberately crude, and deliberately so: the assembly’s triangle over (
44
) consumes only
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
for the drift, so no influence-function expansion of the estimation error is ever taken; the exact form of
\mathrm{Drift}_{m}
is on record in (
44
), whose
\sqrt{m}
-scaled form is reused at the end of the proof.

Lemma 19
(Moment and smoothness bookkeeping of the pooled family)

Read with the raw noise,
Z_{i}:=(X_{i},v^{\rm raw}_{i})
, the family
\psi_{\eta}
of (
43
), viewed as a function
\psi_{\eta}(x,u)
of the design point and a real argument, satisfies:

(i)

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\,[\psi_{\eta}(Z_{1})]\bigr\rvert\leq c_{\sigma}/m
for the deterministic constant
c_{\sigma}:=\sigma^{2}_{\max}d_{\theta}
;

(ii)

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\,[\psi_{\eta}(Z_{1})^{2}]\leq M_{2}
;

(iii)
the paths
\eta\mapsto\psi_{\eta}(x,u)
are
C^{1}
on
\Upsilon
for every
(x,u)
, the pointwise gradient envelopes
\lvert\nabla_{\eta}w^{*}_{\eta}(x)\rvert\leq(G_{1}\vee G_{2})\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}
and
\lvert\nabla_{\eta}h_{\eta}(x)\rvert\leq C^{(\nabla)}_{h}
hold for all
(\eta,x)
, and
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\,[\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\psi_{\eta}(Z_{1})\rVert^{4}]\leq N_{4}
;

(iv)
the replacement cost at the criterion scale is
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl\lvert\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bigl[\psi_{\eta}(X_{i},v_{i})-\psi_{\eta}(X_{i},v^{\rm raw}_{i})\bigr]\bigr\rvert=O_{p}(m^{-1})
.
The constants
M_{2}
,
N_{4}
,
c_{\sigma}
are deterministic and free of
(m,\eta,\mathbf{X})
.

Proof.

Means.
The bracket means are zero by construction, by
\mathbb{E}[v\mid X]=0
, and by
\mathbb{E}[w^{*2}_{\eta}(X)]=\lVert w^{*}_{\eta}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=\theta_{g\circ g}(\eta)
, as displayed after (
43
); that display is read with the noise component
v
, and the only bracket whose mean distinguishes
v^{\rm raw}
from
v
is the first, since
\mathbb{E}[v^{\rm raw}h_{\eta}(X)]=\mathbb{E}\bigl[h_{\eta}(X)\,\mathbb{E}[v^{\rm raw}\mid X]\bigr]=0
like its
v
-counterpart. Now
\mathbb{E}[(v^{\rm raw})^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\sigma^{2}(X)]
, while
\bar{\sigma}^{2}=\mathbb{E}[v^{2}]
with
\mathbb{E}[v_{i}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]=[\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}]_{ii}
, so that
\bar{\sigma}^{2}=m^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{tr}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})]
by the cyclic trace formula and the idempotence of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}
; hence

\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}[\sigma^{2}(X)]-\bar{\sigma}^{2}\bigr\rvert=\frac{1}{m}\,\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\operatorname{tr}\{(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\}\bigr]\bigr\rvert\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}_{\max}d_{\theta}}{m},

the last step being the replacement identity of the setup, which gives
(i)
.

Second moment.
Write
B_{1},\dots,B_{6}
for the six brackets of (
43
). Minkowski’s inequality in
L^{2}
and the envelope bookkeeping of the setup give
\lVert B_{1}\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq(2\bar{M}_{4}+2\sigma^{4}_{\max})^{1/2}
by
(a-b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
,
\mathbb{E}[(v^{\rm raw})^{4}\mid X]\leq\bar{M}_{4}
, and
\bar{\sigma}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}[\sigma^{2}(X)]\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}
;
\lVert B_{2}\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}^{2}+\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}
pointwise;
\lVert B_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq 4\bigl(\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}U_{w}+\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\bigr)
and
\lVert B_{4}\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq 2\bigl(\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}U_{w}\bar{k}^{1/2}+\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\bigr)
, the two constants being bounded by
\lvert\theta_{g}\rvert,\lvert\theta_{g\circ g}\rvert\leq\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}
through Cauchy–Schwarz and the
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
-contraction of
P_{\eta}
;
\lVert B_{5}\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq 2\,\sigma_{\max}\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}
by
\mathbb{E}[h_{\eta}^{2}(X)\,(v^{\rm raw})^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[h_{\eta}^{2}(X)\,\sigma^{2}(X)]\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}\lVert h_{\eta}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}\lVert s\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}}
, the
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
-contraction of
\Pi
behind the last step; and
\lVert B_{6}\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq 2\,U_{w}^{2}
as displayed after (
43
). Hence
\sup_{\eta}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{\eta}(Z_{1})^{2}]\leq\bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{6}\lVert B_{k}\rVert_{L^{2}}\bigr)^{2}=:M_{2}
, which is
(ii)
.

Gradient.
Term-by-term differentiation in
\eta
is legitimate by dominated convergence through the deterministic envelopes, Step A of the proof of part (a) for the kernel entries and the anchor gradients of the anchor-decomposition paragraph. For the
X
-measurable components,
\nabla_{\eta}\bigl[-4(sw^{*}_{\eta}-\theta_{g})+2(s\kappa_{\eta}-\theta_{g\circ g})\bigr](x)
and
\nabla_{\eta}\bigl[w^{*2}_{\eta}-\theta_{g\circ g}\bigr](x)
have coordinates bounded by
C_{\nabla}:=(13+2\,(4\lambda_{L})^{-1/2}\bar{k}^{1/2})(G_{1}\vee G_{2})\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}
, the product terms contributing
4+2
and
2\,U_{w}\leq 2\,(4\lambda_{L})^{-1/2}\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}
for the sixth bracket, and the mean-gradient terms
\leq(G_{1}\vee G_{2})\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq(G_{1}\vee G_{2})\lVert s\rVert_{\infty}\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}
each; for the noise-borne component
\nabla_{\eta}[2uh_{\eta}]=2u\,\nabla_{\eta}h_{\eta}
has coordinates bounded by
2\lvert u\rvert\,C^{(\nabla)}_{h}
, where, with
C_{\Pi}
the score-span sup-norm constant,
\lVert\Pi g\rVert_{\infty}\leq C_{\Pi}\lVert g\rVert_{\infty}
for every bounded
g
by the bounded score functions of Assumption
5
(i), the constant re-derived by Riesz–Thorin in part (c) below, the mean-value form of the anchor representations gives
\lvert\nabla_{\eta}h_{\eta}(x)\rvert\leq C_{\Pi}\lVert\nabla_{\eta}(a_{\eta}-b_{\eta})\rVert_{\infty}\leq C_{\Pi}\,(2+\bar{k}^{1/2})(G_{1}\vee G_{2})\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}=:C^{(\nabla)}_{h}
, the bound on
\lVert\nabla_{\eta}(a_{\eta}-b_{\eta})\rVert_{\infty}
including the term
\lVert(I-P_{\eta})(\nabla_{\eta}P_{\eta})s\rVert_{\infty}\leq(1+\bar{k}^{1/2})(G_{1}\vee G_{2})\lVert s\rVert_{L^{2}}
alongside the two resolvent-identity terms. With
(a+b)^{4}\leq 8a^{4}+8b^{4}
, the coordinate conversion
\lVert(g_{1},g_{2})\rVert^{4}\leq 2g_{1}^{4}+2g_{2}^{4}
, and
\mathbb{E}[(v^{\rm raw})^{4}\mid X]\leq\bar{M}_{4}
,

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\psi_{\eta}(Z_{1})\rVert^{4}\bigr]\leq 512\,\bigl(C_{\nabla}^{4}+(C^{(\nabla)}_{h})^{4}\,\bar{M}_{4}\bigr)=:N_{4},

which is
(iii)
, the pathwise differentiability holding for every fixed
(x,u)
.

Replacement.
Only
B_{1}
and
B_{5}
distinguish
v
from
v^{\rm raw}
. For
B_{1}
the setup’s exact identity gives
m^{-1}\sum_{i}(v_{i}^{2}-v^{\rm raw\,2}_{i})=-\lVert\mathbf{d}\rVert^{2}/m=O_{p}(m^{-1})
; for
B_{5}
the pairing
2m^{-1}\sum_{i}h_{\eta}(X_{i})(v_{i}-v^{\rm raw}_{i})=-2m^{-1}\boldsymbol{h}_{\eta}^{\top}\mathbf{d}
has conditional mean zero and conditional variance
4m^{-2}\boldsymbol{h}_{\eta}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})\boldsymbol{\Sigma}(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})\boldsymbol{h}_{\eta}\leq 4\sigma^{2}_{\max}m^{-2}\lVert(\boldsymbol{I}_{m}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})\boldsymbol{h}_{\eta}\rVert^{2}=O_{p}(m^{-2})
uniformly in
\eta
by the setup’s replacement table, whence conditional Chebyshev’s inequality gives
O_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly in
\eta
. This is
(iv)
.

Lemma 20
(Uniform law of large numbers for smooth families)

Let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
be i.i.d. in a measurable space and let
\{\phi_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in\Upsilon}
be real-valued measurable functions on it such that, for deterministic constants and a deterministic sequence:

(i)

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\eta}(Z_{1})]\bigr\rvert\leq\mu_{m}
;

(ii)

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\eta}(Z_{1})^{2}]\leq M_{2}
;

(iii)
the paths
\eta\mapsto\phi_{\eta}(z)
are
C^{1}
on
\Upsilon
for every
z
, and
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\phi_{\eta}(Z_{1})\rVert^{4}]\leq N_{4}
.
Then

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\Bigl\lvert\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\phi_{\eta}(Z_{i})\Bigr\rvert=O_{p}\bigl(m^{-1/2}\bigr),

the
O_{p}
-constant depending only on
(M_{2},N_{4},\Upsilon)
, with
\mu_{m}=O(m^{-1})
absorbed.

Proof.
The argument is unconditional: the only probabilistic structure consumed is the i.i.d. law of
(Z_{i})
, and all moments invoked are unconditional. Fix, once and for all, a partition of the compact rectangle
\Upsilon
into cells
\{K\}
, each contained in a square of side
\delta
contained in
\Upsilon
, and let
\mathcal{M}_{\delta}
be the set of corner points, with
\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\delta}\rvert\leq N(\delta)\leq C_{\Upsilon}\,\delta^{-2}
;
\delta
is fixed throughout and enters only constants.

Mesh level.
Write
\bar{\phi}_{\eta}:=m^{-1}\sum_{i}\phi_{\eta}(Z_{i})
. The variables
\phi_{\eta^{\prime}}(Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\eta^{\prime}}(Z_{1})]
are i.i.d., centered, with second moment at most
M_{2}
, so
\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\bar{\phi}_{\eta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\eta^{\prime}}(Z_{1})])^{2}\bigr]=m^{-1}\,\mathbb{E}[(\phi_{\eta^{\prime}}(Z_{1})-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\eta^{\prime}}(Z_{1})])^{2}]\leq M_{2}/m
, and Chebyshev’s inequality together with the deterministic offset of
(i)
gives, for every
\eta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\delta}
,

\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\lvert\bar{\phi}_{\eta^{\prime}}\rvert>\varepsilon m^{-1/2}+\mu_{m}\Bigr)\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\lvert\bar{\phi}_{\eta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\eta^{\prime}}(Z_{1})]\rvert>\varepsilon m^{-1/2}\Bigr)\leq\frac{M_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}},

and the union bound over
\mathcal{M}_{\delta}
multiplies the right side by
N(\delta)
.

Cell oscillation.
Since
\mathbb{E}[\bar{\phi}_{\eta}]=\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\eta}(Z_{1})]
for every
\eta
, the oscillation of
\bar{\phi}_{\eta}
over a cell equals that of the centered field
\tilde{\phi}_{\eta}:=\bar{\phi}_{\eta}-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\eta}(Z_{1})]
, whose gradient is
\nabla_{\eta}\tilde{\phi}_{\eta}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}\bigl(\nabla_{\eta}\phi_{\eta}(Z_{i})-\bar{g}_{\eta}\bigr)
with the deterministic vector
\bar{g}_{\eta}:=\mathbb{E}[\nabla_{\eta}\phi_{\eta}(Z_{1})]
; centering of the gradient is never assumed, it is inserted by hand. Lyapunov’s inequality gives
\lVert\bar{g}_{\eta}\rVert^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\phi_{\eta}(Z_{1})\rVert^{2}\leq N_{4}^{1/2}
, so
\mathbb{E}\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\phi_{\eta}(Z_{1})-\bar{g}_{\eta}\rVert^{4}\leq 8\,(N_{4}+N_{4})=16\,N_{4}
by
(a+b)^{4}\leq 8a^{4}+8b^{4}
. Each coordinate of the centered gradient average is a sum of i.i.d. centered summands with bounded fourth moments, so the Marcinkiewicz–Zygmund inequality in the form
\mathbb{E}\bigl\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{m}\xi_{i}\bigr\rvert^{4}\leq C_{4}\,m^{2}\,\mathbb{E}[\xi_{1}^{4}]
for independent centered
(\xi_{i})
, followed by the coordinate conversion
\lVert(g_{1},g_{2})\rVert^{4}\leq 2g_{1}^{4}+2g_{2}^{4}
, gives

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigl\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\tilde{\phi}_{\eta}\bigr\rVert^{4}\leq C_{\mathrm{MZ}}\,N_{4}\,m^{-2}

for a numerical constant
C_{\mathrm{MZ}}
. The paths
t\mapsto\tilde{\phi}_{t}(\omega)
are
C^{1}
for every
\omega
, with gradient the average of the summand gradients, so Lemma
6
with
p=4>d=2
on each cell
K
gives
\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sup_{t,s\in K}\lvert\tilde{\phi}_{t}-\tilde{\phi}_{s}\rvert^{4}\bigr]\leq C_{2,4}\,C_{\mathrm{MZ}}\,N_{4}\,\delta^{4}\,m^{-2}
, and Markov’s inequality at the level
\varepsilon\,\delta\,m^{-1/2}
gives a bound
\leq C_{2,4}\,C_{\mathrm{MZ}}\,N_{4}/\varepsilon^{4}
that is free of
m
; the union bound over the cells preserves
m
-freeness and multiplies by
N(\delta)\leq C_{\Upsilon}\delta^{-2}
.

Assembly.
Every
\eta\in\Upsilon
lies within distance
\delta
of a corner
\eta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\delta}
, so, by the triangle inequality,

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\lvert\bar{\phi}_{\eta}\rvert\leq\max_{\eta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\delta}}\bigl\lvert\bar{\phi}_{\eta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\eta^{\prime}}(Z_{1})]\bigr\rvert+\max_{K}\ \sup_{t,s\in K}\lvert\tilde{\phi}_{t}-\tilde{\phi}_{s}\rvert+\mu_{m}.

On the intersection of the two good events,
m^{1/2}\sup_{\eta}\lvert\bar{\phi}_{\eta}\rvert\leq\varepsilon(1+\delta)+m^{1/2}\mu_{m}\leq\varepsilon(1+\delta)+c_{0}
for all large
m
, the values at finitely many small
m
being absorbed into
c_{0}
, since
\mu_{m}=O(m^{-1})
; the complement has probability at most
C_{\Upsilon}\delta^{-2}\bigl(M_{2}/\varepsilon^{2}+C_{2,4}C_{\mathrm{MZ}}N_{4}/\varepsilon^{4}\bigr)
, which tends to
0
as
\varepsilon\to\infty
. This is the claim.

Lemma 21
(Pooled central limit argument)

Let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
be i.i.d. and let
\{\phi_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in\Upsilon}
satisfy hypotheses
(i)–(iii)
of Lemma
20
. Then

\Bigl(m^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\phi_{\eta}(Z_{i})\Bigr)_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\;\rightsquigarrow\;\mathbb{G}^{\mathrm{pooled}}\quad\text{in }\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon),

where
\mathbb{G}^{\mathrm{pooled}}
is a mean-zero Gaussian process with continuous paths and covariance

\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(\mathbb{G}^{\mathrm{pooled}}(\eta),\mathbb{G}^{\mathrm{pooled}}(\eta^{\prime})\bigr)=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\eta}(Z_{1})\,\phi_{\eta^{\prime}}(Z_{1})\bigr]\qquad\text{for }(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\in\Upsilon^{2}.

The conclusion persists for a triangular array
\phi_{\eta,m}=\phi_{\eta}+c_{m}
with
\sup_{\eta}\lvert c_{m}\rvert=O(m^{-1})
and
\{\phi_{\eta}\}
an
m
-free family satisfying the same hypotheses: the finite-dimensional limits and the covariances are those of
\phi_{\eta}
, the recentering being the first paragraph of the proof.

Proof.

Finite-dimensional limits.
For
\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{L}\in\Upsilon
and weights
(t_{1},\dots,t_{L})
put
\xi:=\sum_{l}t_{l}\,\phi_{\eta_{l}}(Z_{1})
: the summands
\xi_{i}:=\xi(Z_{i})
are i.i.d., with mean
\mu_{\xi}=\sum_{l}t_{l}\,\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\eta_{l}}(Z_{1})]
satisfying
\lvert\mu_{\xi}\rvert\leq\lVert t\rVert_{1}\,\mu_{m}
and second moment
\sigma^{2}_{\xi}\leq\mathbb{E}[\xi^{2}]\leq\lVert t\rVert_{1}^{2}M_{2}
by Cauchy–Schwarz. The Lindeberg condition is trivial; two forms are recorded, since both are consumed below. Under a finite-fourth-moment hypothesis on the family at hand,
\sup_{\eta}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\eta}^{4}(Z_{1})]\leq M_{4}
, Hölder’s inequality and
\lvert\xi\rvert\leq\lVert t\rVert_{1}\max_{l}\lvert\phi_{\eta_{l}}\rvert
give

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\xi^{2}\,1(\lvert\xi\rvert>\varepsilon\sqrt{m})\bigr]\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}m}\,\mathbb{E}[\xi^{4}]\leq\frac{\lVert t\rVert_{1}^{4}\,M_{4}}{\varepsilon^{2}\,m}\;\longrightarrow\;0;

under the second-moment hypothesis
(ii)
for an
m
-free family, the expectation is the tail of the convergent integral
\mathbb{E}[\xi^{2}]<\infty
and vanishes by dominated convergence; the application below consumes an
m
-dependent family and reduces it by recentering,
\phi_{\eta,m}=\bar{\psi}_{\eta}+c_{m}
with
\bar{\psi}_{\eta}:=\psi_{\eta}+\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\sigma^{2}(X)]
free of
m
with exact zero means and
\sup_{\eta}\lvert c_{m}\rvert=\sup_{\eta}\lvert\psi_{\eta}-\bar{\psi}_{\eta}\rvert\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}d_{\theta}/m
, so that
\mathbb{E}[\xi_{m}^{2}1(\lvert\xi_{m}\rvert>\varepsilon\sqrt{m})]\leq 2\,\mathbb{E}[\bar{\xi}^{2}1(\lvert\bar{\xi}\rvert>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\sqrt{m})]+2\lVert t\rVert_{1}^{2}\sigma^{4}_{\max}d_{\theta}^{2}m^{-2}
, again vanishing. The Lindeberg–Lévy central limit theorem applied to
m^{-1/2}\sum_{i}(\xi_{i}-\mu_{\xi})
, whose mean shift
m^{1/2}\mu_{\xi}
is
O(m^{-1/2})\to 0
, gives
m^{-1/2}\sum_{i}\xi_{i}\Rightarrow N(0,\sigma^{2})
with limit variance
\sigma^{2}=\mathbb{E}[\xi^{2}]-\lim_{m}\mu_{\xi}^{2}=\sum_{l,l^{\prime}}t_{l}t_{l^{\prime}}\,\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\eta_{l}}(Z_{1})\phi_{\eta_{l^{\prime}}}(Z_{1})]
for an
m
-free family, the formula holding verbatim for the recentered family
\bar{\psi}_{\eta}
above, whose covariances are
\mathbb{E}[\bar{\psi}_{\eta}(Z_{1})\bar{\psi}_{\eta^{\prime}}(Z_{1})]
, and Cramér–Wold over arbitrary finite
(\eta_{l},t_{l})
yields jointly Gaussian finite-dimensional limits with the displayed covariances and zero means.

Equicontinuity.
The centered field
\tilde{\phi}_{\eta}:=m^{-1/2}\sum_{i}\bigl(\phi_{\eta}(Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\eta}(Z_{1})]\bigr)
has the same oscillations as the field of the statement, its mean being the deterministic null field, and its gradient is the
\sqrt{m}
-rescaled gradient of the field of Lemma
20
; the argument of that proof therefore runs verbatim at the new scaling. The gradient fourth moment
C_{\mathrm{MZ}}N_{4}\,m^{-2}
becomes
C_{\mathrm{MZ}}N_{4}
, the
m^{-2}
of the scaling cancelling the
m^{2}
of the Marcinkiewicz–Zygmund inequality; Lemma
6
with
p=4>d=2
and Markov’s inequality at the level
\varepsilon\delta
give cell oscillations
O_{p}(\delta)
at an
m
-free level; the union bound over the cells sends
N(\delta)\,\delta^{4}\leq C_{\Upsilon}\,\delta^{2}
to zero as
\delta\downarrow 0
, and the mesh level is
O_{p}(1)
by Chebyshev’s inequality under
(ii)
with a union bound over
\mathcal{M}_{\delta}
: the field is asymptotically equicontinuous.

Continuity of the limit.
The
C^{1}
paths and Jensen’s inequality over the segment give
\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\phi_{\eta}-\phi_{\eta^{\prime}})(Z_{1})^{2}\bigr]\leq\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\lVert\nabla_{\eta}\phi_{\eta+s(\eta^{\prime}-\eta)}(Z_{1})\rVert^{2}ds\leq N_{4}^{1/2}\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert^{2}
, so the covariance function of
\mathbb{G}^{\mathrm{pooled}}
is Lipschitz in
(\eta,\eta^{\prime})
at that square order; the Gaussian fourth-moment formula
\mathbb{E}[(\mathbb{G}^{\mathrm{pooled}}(\eta)-\mathbb{G}^{\mathrm{pooled}}(\eta^{\prime}))^{4}]=3\,\mathbb{E}[(\mathbb{G}^{\mathrm{pooled}}(\eta)-\mathbb{G}^{\mathrm{pooled}}(\eta^{\prime}))^{2}]^{2}\leq 3\,N_{4}\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert^{4}
with the Kolmogorov–Chentsov criterion,
p=4>\dim\Upsilon
, yields continuous sample paths.

Lift.
Boundedness in probability of the mesh level, asymptotic equicontinuity, and the finite-dimensional convergence give weak convergence in
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)

[
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, Th. 1.5.4]
.

Assembly.

By Lemma
19
(iv)
and Lemma
20
applied to the family
\psi_{\eta}
through Lemma
19
(i)–(iii)
with
\mu_{m}=c_{\sigma}/m
,

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\Bigl\lvert\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\psi_{\eta}(X_{i},v_{i})\Bigr\rvert\leq\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\Bigl\lvert\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\psi_{\eta}(X_{i},v^{\rm raw}_{i})\Bigr\rvert+O_{p}(m^{-1})=O_{p}\bigl(m^{-1/2}\bigr),

and the triangle inequality over the collection (
44
) and the drift bound (
46
) gives

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl\lvert\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)-R_{\infty}(\eta)\bigr\rvert=O_{p}\bigl(m^{-1/2}\bigr).

(47)

Since
R_{\infty}-\bar{\sigma}^{2}=A
is deterministic continuous on compact
\Upsilon
,
\operatorname{dist}(\hat{\eta}_{m},\arg\min_{\Upsilon}A)\xrightarrow{p}0
for any random minimizer
\hat{\eta}_{m}
of
\mathrm{CV}_{m}
, by part (i) of Lemma
22
. This proves (b).

Remark 11
(Fluctuation of the criterion, demoted)

The proof consumes none of the fluctuation structure beyond the level statement, and no downstream result invokes a limit law for
\sqrt{m}\,[\mathrm{CV}_{m}-R_{\infty}]
; two ingredients are recorded for completeness. First, (
47
) is equivalent to the process boundedness
\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\bigl\lvert\sqrt{m}\,[\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)-R_{\infty}(\eta)]\bigr\rvert=O_{p}(1)
. Second, (
44
) gives, uniformly in
\eta
,

\sqrt{m}\,\bigl[\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)-R_{\infty}(\eta)\bigr]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\psi_{\eta}(X_{i},v_{i})+\sqrt{m}\,\mathrm{Drift}_{m}(\eta)+O_{p}(1)\qquad\text{uniformly in }\eta,

where, by the triangular-array clause of Lemma
21
applied to the recentered family
\bar{\psi}_{\eta}:=\psi_{\eta}+\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\sigma^{2}(X)]
, which is free of
m
with exact zero means and agrees with
\psi_{\eta}
up to
\sup_{\eta}\lvert\psi_{\eta}-\bar{\psi}_{\eta}\rvert\leq\sigma^{2}_{\max}d_{\theta}/m
(so the transfer to the sums of
\psi_{\eta}
themselves costs
\sqrt{m}\sup_{\eta}\lvert\psi_{\eta}-\bar{\psi}_{\eta}\rvert\to 0
), the pooled sums have mean-zero Gaussian finite-dimensional limits with covariances
\mathbb{E}[\bar{\psi}_{\eta}(Z_{1})\bar{\psi}_{\eta^{\prime}}(Z_{1})]
. The drift term is
O_{p}(1)
and pathwise Lipschitz in
\eta
: the bound (
46
), the envelope (
45
), and the pointwise Lipschitz moduli of the anchor families behind Lemma
19
(iii)
. No process-level limit law for the criterion
\sqrt{m}[\mathrm{CV}_{m}-R_{\infty}]
is claimed, and none is consumed elsewhere in the paper.

Proof of (c): local alternative.

Let
f_{m}=\delta_{m}h
with
\|h\|_{L^{2}}<\infty
,

\mathbb{E}[h^{4}(X)]<\infty
,

\sqrt{m}\,\delta_{m}\to a\in[0,\infty)
; the direction
h
is effective,
h=\boldsymbol{\Pi}h
: a
score-span component of
h
is carried asymptotically by the projected-score piece of the residual
decomposition, adding the
\eta
-free shift
a^{2}\,\mu_{h}^{\top}\Gamma_{\theta_{0}}^{-1}\mu_{h}
with

\mu_{h}:=\mathbb{E}[h(X)\,g_{\theta_{0}}(X)]
and replacing
A_{h}
by

\|(I-P_{\eta})\boldsymbol{\Pi}h\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
; the record is for orientation, the statement restricts to

h=\boldsymbol{\Pi}h
; the case

\sqrt{m}\,\delta_{m}\to\infty
falls in the divergence regime of part
(b) and is not needed for the statement. Each assumption on
h
is used exactly once:

\|h\|_{L^{2}}<\infty
makes the deterministic drift
A_{h}(\eta)
finite,

\mathbb{E}[h^{4}(X)]<\infty
funds the Lindeberg step of the pooled CLT in its
finite-fourth-moment form, and no sup-norm boundedness of
h
is assumed, so the
sup-norm envelope route of part (b) is not available below and is replaced by an
interpolation argument.
Write
v_{i}
for the noise as before and decompose

m\Bigl[\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}v_{i}^{2}\Bigr]=m\Bigl[\mathrm{CV}_{m}^{({\rm null})}(\eta)-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}v_{i}^{2}\Bigr]+m\delta_{m}^{2}A_{h}(\eta)+2m\delta_{m}C_{m}^{0}(\eta),

(48)

where
\mathrm{CV}_{m}^{({\rm null})}
is the null criterion with
noise
v_{i}
(part (a)),
A_{h}(\eta)=\|(I-P_{\eta})h\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\sum_{\ell}\lambda^{2}h_{\ell}(\gamma)^{2}/(\mu_{\ell}+\lambda)^{2}
,
and

C_{m}^{0}(\eta)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}v_{i}\,\Pi(a_{\eta}^{h}-b_{\eta}^{h})(X_{i})+o_{p}(m^{-1/2})

uniformly, with
a_{\eta}^{h}=(I-P_{\eta})h
,

b_{\eta}^{h}=P_{\eta}a_{\eta}^{h}
,

(a_{\eta}^{h}-b_{\eta}^{h})_{\ell}=\lambda^{2}h_{\ell}(\gamma)/(\mu_{\ell}+\lambda)^{2}
, the same

(I-P_{\eta})^{2}
structure as (
34
) with
h
in place of

\Pi f
. The decomposition is exact because the orthogonalization is linear:
the residual under the local alternative is

\varepsilon^{0}_{\perp}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}(\delta_{m}\boldsymbol{h}+\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm raw})=\delta_{m}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{h}+\boldsymbol{v}
, the null residual plus the
projected signal, and the criterion, quadratic in the residual, expands into the three
groups of (
48
) with no remainder from the split itself; the only
remainders are the linearization remainder and the collection remainder of

C_{m}^{0}
. The centering at the realized noise square is the part-(a) convention:
the removed level is constant in
\eta
, and
\eta
-free additive shifts leave
the argmin location and every argmin-based conclusion unchanged. The feasibility transfer is that of part (a): the feasible residual differs from
\delta_{m}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{h}+\boldsymbol{v}
by
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}
, whose noise-side pairing is the null-premise remainder of Lemma
15
, whose quadratic pairing carries that lemma’s
m^{1-4\varrho}
rate with
\varrho>1/4
, and whose signal-side pairing is
2\delta_{m}\,O_{p}(\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrm{est}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})})=O_{p}(a\,m^{-\varsigma})=o_{p}(1)
,
\varsigma:=\varrho\wedge\tfrac{1}{4}
, the level part being free of
\eta
. The three terms of
(
48
) are handled as follows.

Viewed through the bilinear identity of Lemma
16
, the decomposition

\widetilde{M}_{m}=\delta_{m}\sqrt{m}\,\bar{s}_{m}+n_{m}
with

\bar{s}_{m}:=m^{-1}\sum_{j}(\boldsymbol{\Pi}h)(X_{j})k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)
fixes the three scalings of
(
48
): the noise–noise pairing lives at the unit scale, the signal–signal
pairing carries
\delta_{m}^{2}m\to a^{2}
, and the signal–noise cross pairing carries

\delta_{m}\sqrt{m}\to a
. The regrouping of the bilinear pieces into the deterministic drift

a^{2}A_{h}
and the linear limit
2a\,G
is not term-to-term; it is performed by the derivation
below through (
48
), which carries out these three limits in turn.

First term.
The proof of (a) gives

m[\mathrm{CV}_{m}^{({\rm null})}-m^{-1}\sum v_{i}^{2}]\rightsquigarrow\mathbb{H}=-2\mathcal{C}^{\perp}+V+\mathcal{D}^{\perp}+\Delta
in

\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
by the fidi and equicontinuity argument of part
(a), which rests on the bilinear representation of
Lemma
16
(the quadratic pieces below are the
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
remainder
\mathcal{R}_{m}
, uniformly bounded by Lemma
17
).

Second term.

m\delta_{m}^{2}A_{h}(\eta)\to a^{2}A_{h}(\eta)

uniformly if
a<\infty
: the map
\eta\mapsto A_{h}(\eta)=\lVert(I-P_{\eta})h\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}

is continuous on the compact
\Upsilon
by the resolvent calculus of Appendix
B.3.3
,
so
\sup_{\eta}A_{h}\leq\lVert h\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}<\infty
and

\sup_{\eta}|m\delta_{m}^{2}A_{h}-a^{2}A_{h}|=|m\delta_{m}^{2}-a^{2}|\sup_{\eta}A_{h}\to 0
.
Behind the displayed convergence stands the replacement of the empirical signal–signal channel by
the population
A_{h}
: the collapse of the signal–signal pairing of part (b), run at the
direction
h
in place of
\Pi f
, collects the deterministic constants into

\lVert h\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}-2\langle h,P_{\eta}h\rangle_{L^{2}}+\langle h,P_{\eta}^{2}h\rangle_{L^{2}}=\bigl\langle h,(I-P_{\eta})^{2}h\bigr\rangle_{L^{2}}=\lVert(I-P_{\eta})h\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=A_{h}(\eta),

and leaves an average of the centered signal brackets of (
43
) with
s
replaced by

h
, whose envelopes scale with
\lVert h\rVert_{L^{2}}
through the anchor bounds of that channel
rebuilt at
h
,
\lvert w^{*}_{\eta}\rvert_{\infty}\leq(4\lambda_{L})^{-1/2}\bar{k}^{1/2}\lVert h\rVert_{L^{2}}
and

\lvert\kappa_{\eta}\rvert_{\infty}\leq\bar{k}\lambda_{L}^{-1}\lVert h\rVert_{L^{2}}
, and whose summands carry bounded
fourth moments through
\mathbb{E}[h^{4}]<\infty
, so that the uniform law of Lemma
20
in its
finite-fourth-moment form bounds the centered average by
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
uniformly in
\eta
; the
projected signal
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}h
is replaced by
h
at
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})
-cost
O_{p}(m^{-\varsigma})
,
\varsigma:=\varrho\wedge\tfrac{1}{4}
, by the
fitted-factor replacement route, the two replacements contributing
O_{p}(a^{2}m^{-\varsigma})=o_{p}(1)

at the
m\delta_{m}^{2}
scale.

Third term.
The analysis mirrors the cross term in the proof of (b), with
h

in place of
\Pi f
. The estimation drift of the projected signal is the replacement error

r^{(h)}_{\eta}:=(\boldsymbol{\Pi}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Pi}})(I-P_{\eta})^{2}h
,
\mathbf{X}
-measurable in the
X
-measurable regime and conditionally deterministic under the convention of Remark
2
in the sieve regime, with

\sup_{\eta}\|r^{(h)}_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=O_{p}(m^{-\varsigma})
,
\varsigma:=\varrho\wedge\tfrac{1}{4}
, by the fitted-factor replacement route of the Drifts paragraph of part (a), applied at the family
\{(I-P_{\eta})^{2}h\}
, the
L^{2}
-Lipschitz modulus of
\eta\mapsto(I-P_{\eta})^{2}h
recorded below supplying the uniformity in
\eta
; its pairing with the noise obeys, conditionally on
\mathbf{X}
,

\operatorname{Var}\Bigl(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i}v_{i}\,r^{(h)}_{\eta}(X_{i})\Bigm|\mathbf{X}\Bigr)\leq\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{m}\,\|r^{(h)}_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}^{2}=O_{p}(m^{-1-2\varsigma}),

so Chebyshev’s inequality gives the pairing
O_{p}(m^{-1/2-\varsigma})
uniformly in
\eta
, and its contribution to
2m\delta_{m}C_{m}^{0}

is
O_{p}(\delta_{m}m^{-\varsigma})=o_{p}(1)
, using

\delta_{m}\to 0
: no drift coordinate
arises at the
a
-scale, in contrast with part (b), where the drift

2\langle a_{\eta}-b_{\eta},\,r\rangle
lived exactly at the
\sqrt{m}
budget and was
collected as a named term; here the shrunken signal kills it. Put

h_{\eta}^{h}=\Pi(a_{\eta}^{h}-b_{\eta}^{h})=\Pi(I-P_{\eta})^{2}h
. The family

\{h_{\eta}^{h}\}_{\eta\in\Upsilon}
is uniformly bounded in

L^{4}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
:

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\lVert h_{\eta}^{h}\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{P}_{X})}\leq C_{\Pi}\,\lVert h\rVert_{L^{4}(\mathbb{P}_{X})},

by Riesz–Thorin interpolation, which carries the argument and is therefore stated:

if a linear operator
T
on measurable functions over a measure space is bounded

L^{p_{0}}\to L^{p_{0}}
with norm
M_{0}
and
L^{p_{1}}\to L^{p_{1}}
with norm

M_{1}
, then for every
\theta\in(0,1)
and every
p
with

1/p=(1-\theta)/p_{0}+\theta/p_{1}
,
T
is bounded
L^{p}\to L^{p}
with

\lVert T\rVert_{p\to p}\leq M_{0}^{1-\theta}M_{1}^{\theta}
. The endpoints here are

p_{0}=2
and
p_{1}=\infty
, so at
p=4
the interpolation parameter is

\theta=1/2
and
\lVert T\rVert_{4\to 4}\leq M_{0}^{1/2}M_{1}^{1/2}
. For
T=\Pi
:

\lVert\Pi g\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq\lVert g\rVert_{L^{2}}
(self-adjoint idempotent
projection) and
\lVert\Pi g\rVert_{\infty}\leq C_{\Pi}\lVert g\rVert_{\infty}

(idempotence and the bounded score span, Assumptions
5
(i)–(ii)), whence

\lVert\Pi\rVert_{4\to 4}\leq C_{\Pi}^{1/2}
. For
T=I-P_{\eta}
: the multipliers

1-t_{\ell}
of the spectral calculus lie in
[0,1]
, so
T
is an

L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
-contraction, and the positive kernel of
P_{\eta}
has mass

(P_{\eta}1)(x)\leq 1
pointwise, so
\lVert P_{\eta}g\rVert_{\infty}\leq\lVert g\rVert_{\infty}
and
\lVert(I-P_{\eta})g\rVert_{\infty}\leq 2\lVert g\rVert_{\infty}
;
whence
\lVert I-P_{\eta}\rVert_{4\to 4}\leq\sqrt{2}
and, by composition,

\lVert(I-P_{\eta})^{2}\rVert_{4\to 4}\leq 2
. The product of the two factors is

L^{4}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
-bounded with

\lVert\Pi(I-P_{\eta})^{2}\rVert_{L^{4}\to L^{4}}\leq 2\,C_{\Pi}^{1/2}\leq C_{\Pi}
(the
score-span constant satisfies
C_{\Pi}\geq 4
), i.e. with the displayed constant; in particular

\sup_{\eta}\mathbb{E}[(h_{\eta}^{h}(X))^{4}]\leq C_{\Pi}^{4}\lVert h\rVert_{L^{4}}^{4}<\infty
, and Lipschitz in

L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
in
\eta
by the resolvent calculus. The pointwise gradient license needed by the
pooled argument runs through the score representation of the annihilator,

(\boldsymbol{\Pi}u)(x)=g_{\theta_{0}}(x)^{\top}\Gamma_{\theta_{0}}^{-1}\,\mathbb{E}[g_{\theta_{0}}(X)\,u(X)]
, whose outer
factors are free of
\eta
, and the operator-level product rule for the squared factor; no spectral
differentiation enters, the two resolvent-product displays being

\partial_{\lambda}P_{\eta}=-L_{\gamma}(L_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-2},\qquad\partial_{\gamma}P_{\eta}=(I-P_{\eta})\,(\partial_{\gamma}L_{\gamma})\,(L_{\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1},

the first by the scalar-shift resolvent identity with
\sup_{t\geq 0}t/(t+\lambda)^{2}\leq(4\lambda_{L})^{-1}
, the
second by the same identity against the bounded kernel derivative

\partial_{\gamma}k_{\gamma}(x,y)=-\lVert x-y\rVert^{2}k_{\gamma}(x,y)
, of supremum
(e\gamma_{L})^{-1}
, which makes

\partial_{\gamma}L_{\gamma}
a bounded operator on
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X})
by Schur’s test; writing

C_{P}:=(4\lambda_{L})^{-1}\vee(e\gamma_{L}\lambda_{L})^{-1}
for the resulting operator-Lipschitz constant of

\eta\mapsto P_{\eta}
, the product rule and the contraction
\|I-P_{\eta}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
of the
multiplier calculus give

\bigl\|(I-P_{\eta})^{2}-(I-P_{\eta^{\prime}})^{2}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2C_{P}\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert
, and
differentiating the pairing under the operator-level bound, Cauchy–Schwarz in the representation, the
bounded-score clause
\sup_{x}\|g_{\theta_{0}}(x)\|\leq\bar{G}
of Assumption
5
(i), and

\|\Gamma_{\theta_{0}}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lambda_{\min}(\Gamma_{\theta_{0}})^{-1}
give

\sup_{\eta,x}\bigl|\nabla_{\eta}h^{h}_{\eta}(x)\bigr|\leq\bar{G}\,C_{\nabla}\,\|h\|_{L^{2}},\qquad C_{\nabla}:=2\,C_{P}\,\lambda_{\min}(\Gamma_{\theta_{0}})^{-1}\,\bigl(\mathbb{E}\|g_{\theta_{0}}(X)\|^{2}\bigr)^{1/2},

whence, by the Lyapunov interpolation
\mathbb{E}[v^{4}\mid X]\leq\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p}
of the
(2p)
-clause of
Assumption
7
(v) at
q=4
,

\sup_{\eta}\mathbb{E}\bigl[v^{4}\,\|\nabla_{\eta}h^{h}_{\eta}(X)\|^{4}\bigr]\leq\bar{M}_{2p}^{2/p}\,(\bar{G}\,C_{\nabla})^{4}\,\|h\|_{L^{2}}^{4}.

With these envelopes the
Pooled central limit argument
(Lemma
21
) of the proof of (b) applies with
h
in place of
\Pi f
and with its Lindeberg step in the finite-fourth-moment form (the summands carry bounded fourth moments, so no boundedness of the summands is needed), the term being the linear
pairing
\langle\bar{s}_{m},\widetilde{A}^{0}_{\eta}n_{m}\rangle
of the bilinear
representation (Lemma
16
), obtained by polarizing the quadratic form of clause (i)
of that lemma at the coefficient pair
(\bar{s}_{m},n_{m})
: by Lemma
21
, whose finite-dimensional limits and
Sobolev–mesh equicontinuity give,

\sqrt{m}\,C_{m}^{0}\rightsquigarrow G\ \text{in }\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon),\quad\operatorname{Cov}(G(\eta),G(\eta^{\prime}))=\mathbb{E}[\sigma^{2}(X)h_{\eta}^{h}(X)h_{\eta^{\prime}}^{h}(X)],

with
G
mean-zero Gaussian with continuous paths. The remainder

R_{m}^{(C),h}
satisfies

\sup_{\eta}|R_{m}^{(C),h}|=o_{p}(m^{-1/2})
uniformly: it is the
h
-analogue of the anchor-fluctuation and remainder collection of the mixed channel (
42
) in the proof of (b), whose pieces are centered, conditionally on the design, linear forms in the noise at coefficient families of
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})
-size
O_{p}(m^{-1/2})
and Lipschitz in
\eta
with
O_{p}(1)
constants, so that the conditional second-moment bound of the estimation-drift step above, followed by Chebyshev’s inequality over the finitely many pieces and their moduli, gives each piece
O_{p}(m^{-1})
uniformly in
\eta
; and the rate must be
o_{p}(m^{-1/2})
, not merely
O_{p}(m^{-1})
, because the remainder is multiplied by
m\delta_{m}\asymp\sqrt{m}
in (
48
) and must vanish after that multiplication.

Synthesis.
If
\sqrt{m}\,\delta_{m}\to a<\infty
,

m\delta_{m}^{2}\to a^{2}
and

m\delta_{m}=\sqrt{m}\,(\sqrt{m}\,\delta_{m})=a\sqrt{m}\,(1+o(1))
, so

\displaystyle m\bigl[\mathrm{CV}_{m}-m^{-1}\sum v_{i}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{m[\mathrm{CV}_{m}^{({\rm null})}-m^{-1}\sum v_{i}^{2}]}_{\rightsquigarrow\mathbb{H}}+\underbrace{m\delta_{m}^{2}}_{\to a^{2}}A_{h}+2\underbrace{(m\delta_{m})}_{a\sqrt{m}}C_{m}^{0}

\displaystyle\rightsquigarrow\mathbb{H}_{h}^{a}:=-2\mathcal{C}^{\perp}+V+\mathcal{D}^{\perp}+\Delta+a^{2}A_{h}+2aG

in
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
, jointly by Cramér–Wold and componentwise equicontinuity: the null
component by part (a), the drift component being deterministic and uniformly convergent, and the
cross component by the pooled central limit argument above. The joint convergence is that of a
single field: at fixed
\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{J}
, the embedding convergence of part (a), read
conditionally on the design (Remark
2
), extends to the direct sum with the
J

added linear coordinates
m^{-1/2}\sum_{i}v_{i}h^{h}_{\eta_{l}}(X_{i})
, the Hilbert–space central
limit theorem on the product with
\mathbb{R}^{J}
delivering the joint limit, and the continuous mapping
theorem applied to the quadratic-plus-linear functionals gives the finite-dimensional limits of the
combined field, which Cramér–Wold assembles. The null process and
G

share the noise and are generally dependent; the dependence is carried by the joint
finite-dimensional convergence, of which the two component limits are the coordinates.
The limit exhibits the local sensitivity of the criterion: a deterministic bias

a^{2}A_{h}
, the risk increment of the shrunken signal, and a linear Gaussian term

2aG
, the signal seen through the noise direction of the criterion; both shifts
vanish as
a\downarrow 0
, recovering part (a), while for
\sqrt{m}\,\delta_{m}\to\infty

the bias diverges and part (b)’s divergence regime takes over. This proves Theorem
6
(c) for

a<\infty
.

Remark 12

(The
k
-fold criterion.)
For fixed
k\geq 2
, all parts of the criterion-process analysis hold with sums restricted to cross-fold pairs and training size
m_{r}=m(k-1)/k
. Throughout the remark the fold labels
(\mathsf{R}_{i})_{i\leq m}
are drawn independently of the data
(X_{i},\varepsilon_{i})_{i\leq m}
, by an approximately balanced random assignment, and all limits below are computed conditionally on the realized labels, under which they act as independent uniform draws on
\{1,\dots,k\}
; the infeasible-residual convention and the transfer of Lemma
15
apply verbatim at the folded sample sizes. The diagonal term scales as
m\,\mathbb{E}[Q_{m}^{\mathrm{kf}}\mid\mathbf{X}]\to\frac{k}{k-1}V(\eta)
, while the correlation drifts are unchanged,
mU_{m}^{\mathrm{kf}}\to\nu(\eta)
and
mR_{m}^{\mathrm{kf}}\to\rho(\eta)
, the cross-fold pair counts cancelling the normalization exactly, so
\Delta=\rho-2\nu
acquires no factor. For the diagonal term the same count is explicit: the folded statistic
Q^{\mathrm{kf}}_{m}(\eta):=\frac{1}{m\,m_{r}^{2}}\sum_{j}\sum_{i\colon\mathsf{R}_{i}\neq\mathsf{R}_{j}}\varepsilon_{\perp,j}^{2}\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}
has

m\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[Q^{\mathrm{kf}}_{m}(\eta)\bigm|\mathbf{X},\mathbf{R}\bigr]=\frac{1}{m_{r}^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i\colon\mathsf{R}_{i}\neq\mathsf{R}_{j}}\sigma^{2}(X_{j})\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}+o_{p}(1)=\frac{k}{k-1}\,V(\eta)+o_{p}(1):

under the balanced assignment each observation admits
m_{r}
partners, a
(k-1)/k
fraction of its
m-1
leave-one-out partners, so the cross-fold sum equals that fraction of the full off-diagonal sum up to the
o_{p}(m)
count imbalance, the summands being bounded; the prefactors combine through
\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{m_{r}^{2}}\to\frac{k^{2}}{(k-1)^{2}}
against the leave-one-out normalization
\frac{1}{(m-1)^{2}}\sum_{j}\sum_{i\neq j}\sigma^{2}(X_{j})\,g_{\eta}(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}\to V(\eta)
, the factor being
\frac{k^{2}}{(k-1)^{2}}\cdot\frac{k-1}{k}=\frac{k}{k-1}
; and the diagonal corrections cancel as in the leave-one-out diagonal,
c_{jj}=\sigma^{2}(X_{j})+O_{p}(m^{-1})
contributing
o_{p}(1)
. In the folded fluctuations, write
\mathsf{R}_{i}:=\mathcal{I}(i)\in\{1,\dots,k\}
for the fold label of observation
i
(the fold containing
i
, in the notation of the
k
-fold protocol of (
1
)), with the labels of the opening convention; the labels act in the limit as independent uniform draws on
\{1,\dots,k\}
, and
R,R^{\prime}
below denote the labels of a generic pair of observations: the chaos kernels become
\frac{k}{k-1}K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp}_{\eta}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{R\neq R^{\prime}\}
and
K^{D,\perp}_{\eta}(x,x^{\prime})\bigl[\tfrac{k}{k-1}\mathbf{1}\{R=R^{\prime}\}+\tfrac{k(k-2)}{(k-1)^{2}}\mathbf{1}\{R\neq R^{\prime}\}\bigr]
on
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{X,R})
; throughout this remark
\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{k}
denotes the chaos with the unnormalized kernel
K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp}_{\eta}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{R\neq R^{\prime}\}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{kf}}
the chaos of the displayed
D
-kernel, the prefactor
\frac{k}{k-1}
of the folded
U
-channel being carried once, by the coefficient in
\mathbb{H}_{k}
below; their eigenmodes are, per eigenvalue
\ell_{k}
, one symmetric mode with eigenvalue
\ell_{k}
and
k-1
antisymmetric modes with eigenvalues
-\ell_{k}/(k-1)
(resp.
\ell_{k}/(k-1)^{2}
), giving variances
2k\ell_{k}^{2}/(k-1)
and
2\ell_{k}^{2}[1+(k-1)^{-3}]
: the two-fold values at
k=2
, with leave-one-out limits as
k\to\infty
. The limit criterion is
\mathbb{H}_{k}(\eta)=\frac{k}{k-1}[-2\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{k}(\eta)+V(\eta)]+\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{kf}}(\eta)+\Delta(\eta)
, whose argmin is of the same structural type; the drifts
A
and
A_{h}
are unchanged, no downstream paragraph depends on the exact law of the limit, and all conclusions therefore hold for every fixed
k\geq 2
.

B.3.6
Proofs of the Selector Results and of Bootstrap Validity under Sample Splitting

Standing notation.

Throughout this subsection:

•

\varepsilon_{\perp,i}:=\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{\mathrm{sel}}
,
i=1,\ldots,m
, denote the Neyman-orthogonalized residuals on the selection sample;

•

S_{n}^{\perp}
,
g_{\eta}
,
V(\eta)
,
c^{*}
,
\nu(\eta)
,
\rho(\eta)
,
\Delta(\eta)
,
K^{\mathrm{sym},\perp}_{\eta}
,
\{\lambda^{\perp}_{k}(\eta)\}
,
K^{D,\perp}_{\eta}
,
\{\lambda^{D,\perp}_{k}(\eta)\}
,
A(\eta)
,
A_{h}(\eta)
, and
G
are as in Section
4.2
;

•

all statements indexed by
\eta
are uniform over
\eta\in\Upsilon
unless explicitly qualified.

We present the derivations for the leave-one-out criterion (
k=m
); the
k
-fold case is Remark
12
above.
The analytic inputs are Assumption
7
together with the uniform empirical-process machinery developed in the proof of Theorem
5
.
The kernel family
\{g_{\eta}\}
is uniformly bounded:
g_{\eta}
is a positive definite kernel with
g_{\eta}(x,x)=\sum_{i\geq 1}\mu_{i}(\gamma)\phi_{i}(x;\gamma)^{2}/(\mu_{i}(\gamma)+\lambda)\leq\lambda^{-1}\sum_{i\geq 1}\mu_{i}(\gamma)\phi_{i}(x;\gamma)^{2}=\lambda^{-1}k_{\gamma}(x,x)=\lambda^{-1}
, and
|g_{\eta}(x,y)|\leq\sqrt{g_{\eta}(x,x)g_{\eta}(y,y)}
, so
\lVert g_{\eta}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-1}
; it is continuous in
\gamma
by the deterministic modulus of part (iii)(a) of Lemma
16
and Lipschitz in
(\gamma,\lambda)
. For the non-degenerate (sample-mean type) processes of the fixed-alternative analysis, the Lipschitz property of
g_{\eta}
together with the covering argument yields asymptotic equicontinuity and hence weak convergence in
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
from the pointwise limits. For the degenerate quadratic statistics of the null analysis (
mU_{m}
,
mR_{m}
), stochastic equicontinuity is pathwise: Lemma
16
exhibits both as quadratic forms of the scaled mean embedding against deterministic operators with deterministic Lipschitz dependence on
\eta
, so no process-level entropy criterion is needed. All suprema over
\eta
that appear are measurable random variables,
every process involved having continuous sample paths on the compact rectangle
\Upsilon
: the empirical processes by the
C^{1}
-path arguments of the proofs of Theorems
5
and
6
, the limit fields by the functional calculus of Appendix
B.3.3
, which requires no eigenpair regularity. We apply these conventions throughout without further comment.

Lemma 22
(Argmin continuity)

Let
S\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{K}
be compact and let
C(S)
denote the space of continuous
real-valued functions on
S
with the uniform norm
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\infty}
.

(i)
Let
f\in C(S)
attain its minimum, write
\mathrm{Arg}(f)
for its nonempty
compact argmin set, and let
f_{m}
be random elements of
C(S)
and
x_{m}
random points of
S

satisfying
\lVert f_{m}-f\rVert_{\infty}\xrightarrow{p}0
and

f_{m}(x_{m})=\inf_{y\in S}f_{m}(y)
almost surely. Then

\operatorname{dist}\bigl(x_{m},\mathrm{Arg}(f)\bigr)\xrightarrow{p}0
; if
f
attains its minimum
uniquely at
x^{*}
, then
\lVert x_{m}-x^{*}\rVert\xrightarrow{p}0
.

(ii)
Let
F_{m}
be random elements of
C(S)
with
F_{m}\rightsquigarrow\mathbb{H}
weakly
in
C(S)
, where
\mathbb{H}
attains its minimum almost surely at a unique point, the

random argmin

\eta^{*}\in S
. Then every sequence of random minimizers of
F_{m}
, that is,
random points
\tilde{x}_{m}
of
S
with
F_{m}(\tilde{x}_{m})=\inf_{y\in S}F_{m}(y)
almost surely,
satisfies
\tilde{x}_{m}\xrightarrow{d}\eta^{*}
.

Proof.
(i) Fix
\varepsilon>0
. The set

A_{\varepsilon}:=\bigl\{y\in S:\operatorname{dist}(y,\mathrm{Arg}(f))\geq\varepsilon\bigr\}

is the complement of the open
\varepsilon
-collar around the argmin set. Since
f\in C(S)
,
the argmin set
\mathrm{Arg}(f)=f^{-1}(\{\min_{S}f\})
is nonempty and closed, and the distance
function
y\mapsto\operatorname{dist}(y,\mathrm{Arg}(f))
is continuous, so
A_{\varepsilon}
is
closed inside compact
S
and hence compact; Weierstrass’s theorem makes
f
attain its minimum
there. The minimizer lies outside
\mathrm{Arg}(f)
: the two sets are disjoint, a point of

\mathrm{Arg}(f)
being at distance
0
, short of the collar’s radius
\varepsilon
, and
f
exceeds

\inf_{S}f
at every point of
A_{\varepsilon}
, the infimum being attained exactly on

\mathrm{Arg}(f)
(if
A_{\varepsilon}
is empty, the event
\{\operatorname{dist}\geq\varepsilon\}
is empty
and the claim is vacuous). The margin

s_{\varepsilon}:=\min_{y\in A_{\varepsilon}}f(y)-\inf_{S}f
is therefore strictly positive: every
point at distance at least
\varepsilon
from the argmin set pays at least
s_{\varepsilon}
in
true objective value. The margin is deterministic, depending on
f
and
\varepsilon
only,
which is what allows the tail event below to be taken at a fixed threshold.

By hypothesis
x_{m}
is a minimizer of
f_{m}
: each realization of
f_{m}
is continuous on the
compact
S
and attains its infimum by Weierstrass’s theorem, so the class of random minimizers
is nonempty, and the defining identity
f_{m}(x_{m})=\inf_{S}f_{m}
holds almost surely; discard the
null event on which it fails, so that the identity is used below without comment. Split the
sample space into the single bad event

E:=\{\lVert f_{m}-f\rVert_{\infty}\geq s_{\varepsilon}/2\}
: the estimate is off by at least half
the margin. On
E^{c}
,

f(x_{m})\;<\;f_{m}(x_{m})+\frac{s_{\varepsilon}}{2}\;=\;\inf_{S}f_{m}+\frac{s_{\varepsilon}}{2}\;\leq\;\Bigl(\inf_{S}f+\frac{s_{\varepsilon}}{2}\Bigr)+\frac{s_{\varepsilon}}{2}\;=\;\inf_{S}f+s_{\varepsilon}:

the first step is the value bound at
y=x_{m}
,

\lvert f_{m}(x_{m})-f(x_{m})\rvert\leq\lVert f_{m}-f\rVert_{\infty}<s_{\varepsilon}/2
; the second is the
minimality of
x_{m}
for
f_{m}
; the third uses the infimum bound, whose proof is one line: let

y^{*}
be a minimizer of
f
over
S
(Weierstrass), then

\inf_{S}f_{m}\leq f_{m}(y^{*})\leq f(y^{*})+s_{\varepsilon}/2=\inf_{S}f+s_{\varepsilon}/2
, the infimum of a
pointwise-dominated family being bounded by the infimum of the dominating family; the fourth
collects. If
x_{m}\in A_{\varepsilon}
, then

f(x_{m})\geq\min_{y\in A_{\varepsilon}}f(y)=\inf_{S}f+s_{\varepsilon}
by the definition of the margin,
contradicting the strict display: on
E^{c}
the point satisfies

\operatorname{dist}(x_{m},\mathrm{Arg}(f))<\varepsilon
. Contrapositively, the event
E
must
occur whenever
\operatorname{dist}(x_{m},\mathrm{Arg}(f))\geq\varepsilon
, giving

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\operatorname{dist}(x_{m},\mathrm{Arg}(f))\geq\varepsilon\bigr)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}\bigl(\lVert f_{m}-f\rVert_{\infty}\geq s_{\varepsilon}/2\bigr)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}\bigl(\lVert f_{m}-f\rVert_{\infty}>s_{\varepsilon}/4\bigr)\;\longrightarrow\;0:

the first step is the event inclusion just established, the second is the inclusion

\{\lVert f_{m}-f\rVert_{\infty}\geq s_{\varepsilon}/2\}\subseteq\{\lVert f_{m}-f\rVert_{\infty}>s_{\varepsilon}/4\}
, and the rightmost tail vanishes by the
uniform-convergence hypothesis at the fixed deterministic positive threshold
s_{\varepsilon}/4
;

\varepsilon>0
was arbitrary, so

\operatorname{dist}\bigl(x_{m},\mathrm{Arg}(f)\bigr)\xrightarrow{p}0
.
Under uniqueness,
\mathrm{Arg}(f)=\{x^{*}\}
and
\operatorname{dist}(x_{m},\mathrm{Arg}(f))=\lVert x_{m}-x^{*}\rVert
, which is exactly the stated conclusion.

(ii) Write
m(h)
for the unique minimizer of
h
. The argmin map is well defined only on the
set of functions with a unique minimizer, and its measurability must be established before

\eta^{*}=m(\mathbb{H})
can be treated as a random element; a finite-net approximation does both.
Fix
\delta>0
and a finite
\delta
-net
\mathcal{N}_{\delta}=\{z_{1},\dots,z_{N}\}\subset S
, ordered
once and for all: every point of
S
lies within
\delta
of a net point, which compactness of

S
guarantees. Each evaluation
h\mapsto h(z_{j})
is continuous on
C(S)
, hence Borel
(
\lvert h(z_{j})-g(z_{j})\rvert\leq\lVert h-g\rVert_{\infty}
), so the net minimum

h\mapsto\min_{j}h(z_{j})
is a minimum of finitely many Borel functions; define
m_{\delta}(h)
as
the first net point attaining it, a lexicographic tie-breaking that makes
m_{\delta}
Borel, its
level sets being finite Boolean combinations of the events
\{h(z_{j})\leq h(z_{k})\}
. The maps

m_{\delta}
converge pointwise at every
h
with a unique minimizer: if
y^{*}=m(h)
and

\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
, the set
\{\lVert z-y^{*}\rVert\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}\}
is compact and avoids the
minimizer, so
\min_{\lVert z-y^{*}\rVert\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}}h(z)>h(y^{*})
strictly, while the net
point closest to
y^{*}
satisfies
h(z)\leq h(y^{*})+\omega_{h}(\delta)
; for
\delta
small no
net minimizer can lie outside the
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-ball, here

\omega_{h}(\delta):=\sup\{\lvert h(x)-h(y)\rvert:\lVert x-y\rVert\leq\delta\}
denoting the
modulus of continuity, which vanishes as
\delta\downarrow 0
since
h
is uniformly continuous
on the compact
S
. Pointwise limits of measurable maps are measurable on their convergence
set, the level sets of the limit being countable Boolean combinations of the level sets of the
approximants, and the convergence set is itself measurable by the Cauchy criterion applied to
the approximants;
m
is therefore Borel on its domain, and its extension by a fixed point of

S
outside the domain is Borel on all of
C(S)
, making

\eta^{*}=m(\mathbb{H})
a well-defined random element of
S
.

For the main argument, work with pairs.
F_{m}
is tight in
C(S)
as a weakly convergent
sequence, and the laws of
\tilde{x}_{m}
are tight because
S
is compact, the compact
K=S

serving every tolerance at once. Product tightness follows by the union bound: if

\mathbb{P}(F_{m}\notin K_{1})\leq\varepsilon/2
and
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{x}_{m}\notin K_{2})\leq\varepsilon/2

with
K_{1},K_{2}
compact, then
K_{1}\times K_{2}
is compact and covers the pairs to level

\varepsilon
, so the pairs are tight in
C(S)\times S
; Prohorov’s theorem then extracts, along
every subsequence, a further subsubsequence with
(F_{m},\tilde{x}_{m})\rightsquigarrow\nu
. The
first marginal of
\nu
is pinned by the projection
\pi(h,x):=h
, which is continuous because
convergence in the product is coordinate-wise convergence: the continuous mapping theorem gives

\pi(F_{m},\tilde{x}_{m})=F_{m}\Rightarrow\nu_{1}
, the first marginal of
\nu
, i.e. the law of the
first coordinate under
\nu
(
\nu_{1}(A)=\nu(A\times S)
for Borel
A\subset C(S)
); and since
the whole sequence satisfies
F_{m}\rightsquigarrow\mathbb{H}
, the subsubsequence converges to the
same limit, whence
\nu_{1}=\mu_{\mathbb{H}}
, the law of
\mathbb{H}

(
\mu_{\mathbb{H}}(A)=\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{H}\in A)
).

The functional

\mathrm{ev}(h,x):=h(x)-\inf_{S}h
, the suboptimality of
x
under
h

(
\mathrm{ev}(h,x)=0
exactly on
\mathrm{Arg}(h)
), is continuous on
C(S)\times S
, as

\bigl|\mathrm{ev}(h,x)-\mathrm{ev}(h^{\prime},x^{\prime})\bigr|\;\leq\;2\lVert h-h^{\prime}\rVert_{\infty}+\omega_{h^{\prime}}\bigl(\lVert x-x^{\prime}\rVert\bigr)

shows:
h(x)-h^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=[h(x)-h^{\prime}(x)]+[h^{\prime}(x)-h^{\prime}(x^{\prime})]
, so

\lvert h(x)-h^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\rvert\leq\lVert h-h^{\prime}\rVert_{\infty}+\omega_{h^{\prime}}(\lVert x-x^{\prime}\rVert)
, while

\lvert\inf_{S}h-\inf_{S}h^{\prime}\rvert\leq\lVert h-h^{\prime}\rVert_{\infty}
, infima being
1
-Lipschitz under the
sup norm. Joint continuity follows since

\omega_{h^{\prime}}(\delta)\leq\omega_{h}(2\delta)+2\lVert h-h^{\prime}\rVert_{\infty}
(triangle through
h
) and

\omega_{h}(2\delta)\to 0
.

Minimality gives
\mathrm{ev}(F_{m},\tilde{x}_{m})=0
almost surely, for every
m
; discarding the
null event on which the identity fails for some
m
, the random variable

\mathrm{ev}(F_{m},\tilde{x}_{m})
is identically
0
, so its law is the Dirac mass at
0
. Along
the chosen subsubsequence, the continuous mapping theorem sends it to
\mathrm{ev}
evaluated
under
\nu
; weak limits are unique, so the law of
\mathrm{ev}
under
\nu
is the Dirac mass
at
0
, that is,
\nu\{\mathrm{ev}=0\}=1
. With

U:=\{h\in C(S):h\text{ has a unique minimizer}\}
, also
\nu(U\times S)=1
: reading off the

h
-coordinate alone,
\nu(U\times S)=\nu_{1}(U)
by the definition of the first marginal,

\nu_{1}(U)=\mu_{\mathbb{H}}(U)
by its identification, and
\mu_{\mathbb{H}}(U)=1
by hypothesis;
and on

(U\times S)\cap\{\mathrm{ev}=0\}
the coordinate
x
coincides with
m(h)
:

\mathrm{ev}(h,x)=0
makes
x
a minimizer of
h
and uniqueness makes it the minimizer. The
intersection of two full-measure sets has full measure, so
\nu
is concentrated on the graph

\{(h,m(h)):h\in U\}
.

Consequently, for
every bounded continuous
\varphi:S\to\mathbb{R}
,

\int_{S}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{2}\;=\;\int_{C(S)\times S}\varphi(x)\,\mathbf{1}\bigl\{h(x)=\textstyle\min_{S}h\bigr\}\,\mathrm{d}\nu\;=\;\int_{C(S)}\varphi\bigl(m(h)\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{H}}\;=\;\mathbb{E}\bigl[\varphi(\eta^{*})\bigr],

with
\nu_{2}
the second marginal of
\nu
; the first equality drops an integrand supported on
the
\nu
-null set
\{\mathrm{ev}\neq 0\}
; the second holds
\nu
-almost everywhere because

x=m(h)
there, and then marginalizes a first-coordinate function,

\int\varphi(m(h))\,\mathrm{d}\nu=\int\varphi(m(h))\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{1}=\int\varphi(m(h))\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{H}}
;
the third uses that
\varphi\circ m
depends only on the first coordinate and is the definition
of
\eta^{*}=m(\mathbb{H})
. Since bounded continuous functions separate laws on a metric space,

\nu_{2}=\mathcal{L}(\eta^{*})
. Every subsequential weak limit of
\tilde{x}_{m}
is thus the law of

\eta^{*}
, so the subsequence
principle yields
\tilde{x}_{m}\xrightarrow{d}\eta^{*}
: were it not so, a bounded continuous

\varphi
and an
\varepsilon>0
would separate the corresponding integrals along a
subsequence, contradicting the convergence of its extracted subsubsequence. Unlike part (i), no pathwise comparison
between
\tilde{x}_{m}
and
\eta^{*}
on a common probability space is asserted: the applications
below consume only the distributional statement, reserving part (i) for concentration claims.
This is the classical argmin-continuity theorem
[
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, Ch. 3.2]
.

B.3.7
Proof of Theorem
7
(The Null Selector)

Proof of Theorem
7
.

The proof reads the weak convergence of Theorem
6
(a) in

C(\Upsilon)
, verifies the exact-minimizer hypothesis of Lemma
22
(ii)
through the argmin-set coincidence, and applies that lemma with
S=\Upsilon
; a
closing paragraph records the measurability and the independence of the limit. Fix
an arbitrary minimizer
\hat{\eta}_{m}
of
\mathrm{CV}_{m}
over
\Upsilon
; such
minimizers exist for every realization:
\mathrm{CV}_{m}
has continuous sample paths
on the compact rectangle
\Upsilon
by Assumption
7
(ii) and the
real-analyticity of the resolvent in
(\gamma,\lambda)
, so Weierstrass’s theorem
makes its argmin set nonempty.

Weak convergence in
C(\Upsilon)
.

By Theorem
6
(a),

F_{m}(\eta):=m\Bigl[\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta)-m^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{2}\Bigr]=-2mU_{m}(\eta)+mQ_{m}(\eta)+mR_{m}(\eta)+o_{p}(1)

converges weakly in
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
to

\mathbb{H}=-2\mathcal{C}^{\perp}+V+\mathcal{D}^{\perp}+\Delta
, whose sample paths are continuous almost
surely (Appendix
B.3.3
). Each
F_{m}
has continuous paths as well, being a finite
combination of the kernels
g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}})
, continuous in
\eta
by
Assumption
7
(ii). Both
F_{m}
and
\mathbb{H}
are therefore carried by
C(\Upsilon)\subset\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
, the
former surely, the latter almost surely. The subspace is closed for the uniform norm: given

f\in\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
and a sequence
f_{n}\in C(\Upsilon)
with

\lVert f-f_{n}\rVert_{\infty}\to 0
, fix
\varepsilon>0
, choose
n
with

\lVert f-f_{n}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon/3
, and pick
\delta>0
such that

\lVert x-y\rVert\leq\delta
implies
|f_{n}(x)-f_{n}(y)|\leq\varepsilon/3
, which is possible because

f_{n}
, being continuous on the compact set
\Upsilon
, is uniformly continuous; then for

\lVert x-y\rVert\leq\delta
the triangle inequality gives

|f(x)-f(y)|\leq|f(x)-f_{n}(x)|+|f_{n}(x)-f_{n}(y)|+|f_{n}(y)-f(y)|\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3}+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3}+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3}=\varepsilon,

so
f
is continuous (uniform limit theorem);
C(\Upsilon)
is a closed, hence Borel, subset of

\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
, so that
\{F_{m}\in C(\Upsilon)\}
and
\{\mathbb{H}\in C(\Upsilon)\}
are
events.

The portmanteau lemma, in its closed-set form, applied in the metric space
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)

to the closed set
C(\Upsilon)
gives

\limsup_{m\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigl(F_{m}\in C(\Upsilon)\bigr)\leq\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathbb{H}\in C(\Upsilon)\bigr),

and the left side equals
1
, every realization of every
F_{m}
having continuous paths; since a
probability is at most
1
,
\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{H}\in C(\Upsilon))=1
. The almost sure path continuity
of the limit is thus not only recorded a priori in Appendix
B.3.3
but is forced
by the convergence itself, and the values of
\mathbb{H}
on the null event

\{\mathbb{H}\notin C(\Upsilon)\}
are immaterial.

It remains to transfer the test functions. Let
\varphi:C(\Upsilon)\to\mathbb{R}
be bounded and
continuous,
|\varphi|\leq c_{\varphi}
. The Tietze extension theorem applies:
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)

is a metric space, hence normal;
C(\Upsilon)
is a closed subset; and
\varphi
is a continuous map
into the interval
[-c_{\varphi},c_{\varphi}]
. The theorem produces a continuous

\Phi:\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)\to[-c_{\varphi},c_{\varphi}]
with
\Phi(f)=\varphi(f)
for every

f\in C(\Upsilon)
; any one extension will do, and
\Phi
is in particular a bounded continuous test
function on
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
. Three comparisons chain the integrals:

\mathbb{E}[\varphi(F_{m})]=\mathbb{E}[\Phi(F_{m})]\to\mathbb{E}[\Phi(\mathbb{H})]=\mathbb{E}[\varphi(\mathbb{H})]:

the first equality holds for every realization, since
F_{m}\in C(\Upsilon)
surely and

\Phi=\varphi
on
C(\Upsilon)
; the convergence is the definition of weak convergence in

\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
,
\Phi
being bounded and continuous; the last equality holds

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, since
\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{H}\in C(\Upsilon))=1
. Every bounded continuous

\varphi:C(\Upsilon)\to\mathbb{R}
therefore satisfies

\mathbb{E}[\varphi(F_{m})]\to\mathbb{E}[\varphi(\mathbb{H})]
, which is weak convergence of
F_{m}
to

\mathbb{H}
in the metric space
C(\Upsilon)
, where weak convergence is defined through bounded
continuous test functions. The displayed convergence may accordingly be read in
C(\Upsilon)
, the
space in which Lemma
22
(ii) is phrased: the
F_{m}
are random elements of

C(\Upsilon)
, the Borel
\sigma
-field of the metric subspace
C(\Upsilon)
being the trace of the
Borel
\sigma
-field of
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
, and
\mathbb{H}
is a
C(\Upsilon)
-valued random
element after redefinition on the null event
\{\mathbb{H}\notin C(\Upsilon)\}
.

Argmin coincidence.

Write
c_{m}:=m^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varepsilon_{\perp,i}^{2}
for the discarded
summand, which does not depend on
\eta
. For any
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in\Upsilon
,
substituting the definition of
F_{m}
at the two points and expanding gives

\displaystyle F_{m}(\eta_{1})-F_{m}(\eta_{2})

\displaystyle=m\bigl[\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta_{1})-c_{m}\bigr]-m\bigl[\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta_{2})-c_{m}\bigr]

\displaystyle=m\,\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta_{1})-mc_{m}-m\,\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta_{2})+mc_{m}

\displaystyle=m\bigl[\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta_{1})-\mathrm{CV}_{m}(\eta_{2})\bigr],

each equality a single substitution, expansion, or collection; since
m\geq 1>0
,
the two criteria order
\Upsilon
identically, whence

\arg\min_{\Upsilon}\mathrm{CV}_{m}=\arg\min_{\Upsilon}F_{m}
as sets. Since the argmin
sets coincide exactly, any minimizer of
\mathrm{CV}_{m}
is a minimizer of
F_{m}
:

F_{m}(\hat{\eta}_{m})=\inf_{\eta\in\Upsilon}F_{m}(\eta)
almost surely, which is the
exact-minimizer hypothesis of Lemma
22
(ii).

Application of Lemma
22
(ii).

The lemma applies with
S=\Upsilon
, and each hypothesis is in place:

\Upsilon=[\gamma_{L},\gamma_{U}]\times[\lambda_{L},\lambda_{U}]\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is the
compact admissible rectangle of the splitting protocol of
Section
4.1
, part of the theorem’s hypotheses;
F_{m}
is a
sequence of random elements of
C(\Upsilon)
converging weakly to
\mathbb{H}
in

C(\Upsilon)
, by the first paragraph;
\mathbb{H}
attains its minimum over

\Upsilon
almost surely at a unique point, the random argmin

\eta^{*}\in\Upsilon
, which is the uniqueness hypothesis of the theorem; and

F_{m}(\hat{\eta}_{m})=\inf_{\eta\in\Upsilon}F_{m}(\eta)
almost surely, by the second
paragraph. The lemma yields

\hat{\eta}_{m}\xrightarrow{d}\eta^{*}=\arg\min_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\mathbb{H}(\eta)
, which
is the display of the theorem, and it holds for every minimizer since

\hat{\eta}_{m}
was arbitrary.

Measurability and independence.

The proof of Lemma
22
(ii) constructs the argmin as a Borel map

\mathfrak{m}:C(\Upsilon)\to\Upsilon
, by a finite
\delta
-net with lexicographic tie-breaking, and
shows
\eta^{*}=\mathfrak{m}(\mathbb{H})
, so
\eta^{*}
is a Borel function of

\mathbb{H}
. Every measurable selection from the argmin set

\arg\min_{\Upsilon}F_{m}
is a random minimizer in the sense of
Lemma
22
(ii), so the conclusions of the theorem cover any minimizer
the practitioner selects. The centered
process
F_{m}
depends on the data only through the selection sample, being assembled from the
Neyman-orthogonalized residuals
\varepsilon_{\perp,i}
and the kernels

g_{\eta}(X_{i}^{\mathrm{sel}},X_{j}^{\mathrm{sel}})
evaluated at selection-sample points, and the same
holds for its weak limit
\mathbb{H}
, realized in the coupling of Appendix
B.3.3

as a functional of white noise independent of the data. The selection and testing samples are
independent under the protocol of Section
4.1
, so

\eta^{*}=\mathfrak{m}(\mathbb{H})
is independent of the testing sample. The law of
\eta^{*}
is pinned by the inputs of

\mathbb{H}
, namely the kernel spectrum, the conditional variance function
\sigma^{2}(\cdot)
, and the
joint law of the chaoses
\mathcal{C}^{\perp}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\perp}
, which is the law statement of the
theorem.

B.3.8
Proof of Propositions
1
and
2
(Joint Convergence and Evaluation)

It suffices to spell out
H_{0}
; under
H_{1,n}
the identical argument applies with
\mathbb{H}^{a}_{h}
,
G
, and

S_{\infty}(\cdot;h_{c})
in place of
\mathbb{H}
and
S_{\infty}
, citing
Theorem
11
(i) for the selector marginal and the local-alternative conclusion of the
proof of Theorem
5
for the process marginal.

Marginals.
The selector
\hat{\eta}_{m}
is a measurable function of the selection sample alone,
and Theorem
7
gives
\hat{\eta}_{m}\xrightarrow{d}\eta^{*}
in
\Upsilon
. The statistic
process
S_{n}^{\perp}
is a measurable function of the testing sample alone, and
Theorem
5
gives
S_{n}^{\perp}\rightsquigarrow S_{\infty}
in
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)

with almost surely continuous limit paths; moreover
S_{n}^{\perp}
itself has continuous paths: it is a finite combination of

\langle k_{\gamma}(X_{i},\cdot),(C_{n,\gamma}+\lambda I)^{-1}k_{\gamma}(X_{j},\cdot)\rangle
terms, each
continuous in
\eta
by Assumption
7
(ii) and the resolvent’s continuity in
(C_{n,\gamma},\lambda)
. Weak convergence
therefore also holds in the separable space
C(\Upsilon)
: for
F\subset C(\Upsilon)
closed, its
closure
\bar{F}
in
\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
is closed and satisfies
F=\bar{F}\cap C(\Upsilon)

(uniform limits of continuous functions are continuous), so the Portmanteau lemma and

\mathbb{P}(S_{\infty}\in C(\Upsilon))=1
give

\limsup_{m}\mathbb{P}(S_{n}^{\perp}\in F)\leq\mathbb{P}(S_{\infty}\in F)
.

Independence and joint law.
The samples are independent,

\mathcal{D}_{m}^{\mathrm{sel}}\perp\!\!\!\perp\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{test}}
. Hence for all bounded
continuous
\varphi:\Upsilon\to\mathbb{R}
and
\psi:C(\Upsilon)\to\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\varphi(\hat{\eta}_{m})\,\psi(S_{n}^{\perp})\bigr]=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\varphi(\hat{\eta}_{m})\bigr]\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi(S_{n}^{\perp})\bigr]\;\longrightarrow\;\mathbb{E}[\varphi(\eta^{*})]\,\mathbb{E}[\psi(S_{\infty})],

which is integration against the product law
\mathcal{L}(\eta^{*})\otimes\mathcal{L}(S_{\infty})
on

\Upsilon\times C(\Upsilon)
. This product law has tight marginals (
\Upsilon
is compact;

S_{\infty}
is tight in
C(\Upsilon)
by the preceding paragraph), so the pair

(\hat{\eta}_{m},S_{n}^{\perp})
is tight in
\Upsilon\times C(\Upsilon)
; the displayed expectation
factorization, evaluated over bounded continuous
\varphi,\psi
(a determining class for the product
law), identifies
\mathcal{L}(\eta^{*})\otimes\mathcal{L}(S_{\infty})
as the unique weak limit of the
pair:

(\hat{\eta}_{m},S_{n}^{\perp})\rightsquigarrow(\eta^{*},S_{\infty})

in
\Upsilon\times C(\Upsilon)
, with independent components, and a fortiori in

\Upsilon\times\ell^{\infty}(\Upsilon)
.

Evaluation at the random selector.
The evaluation map
(\eta,f)\mapsto f(\eta)
is
continuous on
\Upsilon\times C(\Upsilon)
:

|f(\eta)-f^{\prime}(\eta^{\prime})|\leq\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+|f^{\prime}(\eta)-f^{\prime}(\eta^{\prime})|\leq\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\|\eta-\eta^{\prime}\|),

with
\omega_{f^{\prime}}
the modulus of continuity of the continuous path
f^{\prime}
; and

(\eta^{*},S_{\infty})\in\Upsilon\times C(\Upsilon)
almost surely. The continuous mapping theorem gives

S_{n}^{\perp}(\hat{\eta}_{m})\xrightarrow{d}S_{\infty}(\eta^{*})
. Under
H_{1,n}
, the same argument yields

S_{n}^{\perp}(\hat{\eta}_{m})\xrightarrow{d}S_{\infty}(\eta^{*}_{h};h_{c})
.

B.3.9
Proof of Theorem
8
(Bootstrap Validity under Sample Splitting)

Theorem
8
asserts that
\mathbb{P}\bigl(S_{n}^{\perp}(\hat{\eta}_{m})>c_{n,\alpha}(\hat{\eta}_{m})\bigr)\to\alpha
under
H_{0}
, for arbitrary relative rates
m,n\to\infty
, without any selector asymptotics.

By split independence,
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{S_{n}^{\perp}(\hat{\eta}_{m})>c_{n,\alpha}(\hat{\eta}_{m})\}]=\mathbb{E}[\theta_{n}(\hat{\eta}_{m})]
with

\theta_{n}(\eta):=\mathbb{P}(S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)>c_{n,\alpha}(\eta))
is the testing-sample rejection
probability at the fixed tuning
\eta
. The decomposition separates the two samples: the selector is a
function of the selection sample alone, while
S_{n}^{\perp}
and
c_{n,\alpha}(\cdot)
are functions of the
testing sample alone. For each fixed
\eta
, the pointwise null CLT gives

S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)\xrightarrow{d}S_{\infty}(\eta)
. Conditional convergence of

\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)
(Lemma
24
) together with continuity and strict
increase of
F_{\eta}
(Lemma
23
) gives

c_{n,\alpha}(\eta)\xrightarrow{p}c_{\infty}(\eta)
, and Slutsky’s theorem then yields

\theta_{n}(\eta)\to p_{\alpha}(\eta):=\mathbb{P}(S_{\infty}(\eta)>c_{\infty}(\eta))=\alpha

pointwise, where the equality to
\alpha
is the definition of the quantile.

The passage to the random argument uses the
uniform
version. Under
H_{0}
, we claim the
uniform conditional-CDF approximation

\sup_{\eta,t}\bigl|\mathbb{F}_{n}(t;\eta)-F_{\eta}(t)\bigr|=o_{p}(1),\qquad\mathbb{F}_{n}(t;\eta):=\mathbb{P}_{V}\bigl(\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)\leq t\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr).

The three ingredients are the pointwise conditional CLT, conditional equicontinuity from
Lemma
24
, and the continuity of
F_{\eta}
. First, the map
(\eta,t)\mapsto F_{\eta}(t)
is
jointly continuous, in two lines from Lemma
23
: its Pólya step gives

\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}|F_{\eta_{j}}(s)-F_{\eta}(s)|\to 0
for every
\eta_{j}\to\eta
, and each
F_{\eta}
is
continuous in
t
, so

|F_{\eta_{j}}(t_{j})-F_{\eta}(t)|\;\leq\;\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\,|F_{\eta_{j}}(s)-F_{\eta}(s)|\;+\;|F_{\eta}(t_{j})-F_{\eta}(t)|\;\longrightarrow\;0,

and sequential continuity is continuity on the metric space
\Upsilon\times\mathbb{R}
; by
compactness the restriction to any compact
K\subset\Upsilon\times\mathbb{R}
is uniformly continuous
(Heine–Cantor), with modulus

\omega_{F,K}(r):=\sup\bigl\{|F_{\eta}(s)-F_{\eta^{\prime}}(t)|:\;(\eta,s),(\eta^{\prime},t)\in K,\;\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert\vee|s-t|\leq r\bigr\}\;\downarrow\;0.

Second, the grid sandwich. Fix
M>0
and
K_{M}:=\Upsilon\times[-2\delta,M+2\delta]
; let

\mathcal{T}_{M}:=\{-2\delta,-\delta,0,\delta,\dots,M+2\delta\}
be a mesh-
\delta
grid and let

\{\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{N_{\delta}}\}
be a finite
\delta
-net of the compact
\Upsilon
; write

\Delta_{n}(M):=\max_{t_{j}\in\mathcal{T}_{M}}\,\max_{l\leq N_{\delta}}\bigl|\mathbb{P}_{V}(\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})\leq t_{j}\mid\mathbf{X})-F_{\eta_{l}}(t_{j})\bigr|,

a finite maximum. On the equicontinuity event

E_{n}(\delta):=\bigl\{\sup_{\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert\leq\delta}|\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)-\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta^{\prime})|\leq\delta\bigr\}
, take any
(\eta,t)
with

t\in[0,M]
, the net point
\eta_{l}
with
\lVert\eta-\eta_{l}\rVert\leq\delta
, and the grid point

t_{j}\in\mathcal{T}_{M}
with
t_{j}\leq t<t_{j}+\delta
. The process increments are bounded on

E_{n}(\delta)
, so the level-
t
events shift,

\{\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)\leq t\}\subseteq\{\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})\leq t+\delta\}\subseteq\{\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})\leq t_{j+2}\}
and

\{\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)>t\}\subseteq\{\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})>t-\delta\}\subseteq\{\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})>t_{j-1}\}
, because
t+\delta\leq t_{j+2}
and
t-\delta\geq t_{j-1}
; monotonicity of conditional distribution functions in
t
, the triangle inequality, and
the two defining displays above then give

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{V}(\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)\leq t\mid\mathbf{X})-F_{\eta}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\;\mathbb{P}_{V}(\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})\leq t_{j+2}\mid\mathbf{X})-F_{\eta}(t)

\displaystyle=\bigl[\mathbb{P}_{V}(\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})\leq t_{j+2}\mid\mathbf{X})-F_{\eta_{l}}(t_{j+2})\bigr]+\bigl[F_{\eta_{l}}(t_{j+2})-F_{\eta}(t)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\;\Delta_{n}(M)+\omega_{F,K_{M}}(2\delta),

\displaystyle F_{\eta}(t)-\mathbb{P}_{V}(\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)\leq t\mid\mathbf{X})

\displaystyle\leq\;F_{\eta}(t)-\mathbb{P}_{V}(\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})\leq t_{j-1}\mid\mathbf{X})

\displaystyle=\bigl[F_{\eta}(t)-F_{\eta_{l}}(t_{j-1})\bigr]+\bigl[F_{\eta_{l}}(t_{j-1})-\mathbb{P}_{V}(\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})\leq t_{j-1}\mid\mathbf{X})\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\;\omega_{F,K_{M}}(2\delta)+\Delta_{n}(M),

both pairs
\bigl(\eta_{l},t_{j+2}\bigr)
,
\bigl(\eta_{l},t_{j-1}\bigr)
being within

\lVert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\rVert\vee|s-t|\leq 2\delta
of
(\eta,t)
. Taking suprema over

(\eta,t)\in\Upsilon\times[0,M]
on
E_{n}(\delta)
,

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\sup_{t\in[0,M]}\bigl|\mathbb{P}_{V}(\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)\leq t\mid\mathbf{X})-F_{\eta}(t)\bigr|\;\leq\;\Delta_{n}(M)+\omega_{F,K_{M}}(2\delta)\qquad\text{on }E_{n}(\delta),

and
\mathbb{P}\bigl(E_{n}(\delta)\bigr)\to 1
for each fixed
\delta
by the conditional
equicontinuity display of Lemma
24
. Third, the complement of
[0,M]
: for

t<0
both distribution functions vanish,
\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}\geq 0
as a squared norm and

S_{\infty}(\eta)\geq 0
as a nonnegative quadratic form; for
t>M
the upper tails shrink
monotonically in
t
,
1-F_{\eta}(t)\leq\sup_{\eta^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}(S_{\infty}(\eta^{\prime})>M)
and

1-\mathbb{P}_{V}(\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)\leq t\mid\mathbf{X})\leq\mathbb{P}_{V}(\sup_{\Upsilon}\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}>M\mid\mathbf{X})
, so the triangle inequality gives

\bigl|\mathbb{P}_{V}(\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)\leq t\mid\mathbf{X})-F_{\eta}(t)\bigr|\;\leq\;\sup_{\eta^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}\bigl(S_{\infty}(\eta^{\prime})>M\bigr)\;+\;\mathbb{P}_{V}\bigl(\sup_{\Upsilon}\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}>M\mid\mathbf{X}\bigr),

and Markov’s inequality controls both tails: the conditional tail through the pathwise norm bound

\sup_{\Upsilon}\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}\,\overline{M}_{n}^{*2}
of the tightness paragraph of
Lemma
24
, giving
\lambda_{L}^{-2}\overline{M}_{n}^{*2}/M
with

\overline{M}_{n}^{*}=O_{p}(1)
(Lemma
11
), and the uniform null tail through

\mathbb{E}\,S_{\infty}(\eta)=\operatorname{tr}\boldsymbol{A}_{\gamma}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}

for every
\eta
(Appendix
B.3.3
), giving

\sigma_{\max}^{2}\lambda_{L}^{-2}/M
. Finally, every term of the finite maximum
\Delta_{n}(M)
vanishes
in probability: the pointwise conditional CLT gives

\tilde{S}_{n}^{\perp}(\eta_{l})\xrightarrow{d^{*}}S_{\infty}(\eta_{l})
, in probability over the data, and
conditional convergence in distribution, in probability, to a constant law forces the conditional
distribution functions to converge in probability at every continuity point of the limit (the
in-probability form of the CDF portmanteau), while
F_{\eta}
is continuous on
\mathbb{R}

(Lemma
23
), so every
t_{j}
is such a point. Choosing
M
large, then
\delta

small, then
n\to\infty
proves the claim.

The uniform quantile transfer gives

\sup_{\eta}|c_{n,\alpha}(\eta)-c_{\infty}(\eta)|=o_{p}(1)
: the straddle argument at the two points

c_{\infty}(\eta)\pm\varepsilon
, its margin being positive by continuity and strict increase of

F_{\eta}
at its quantile (Lemma
23
) and compactness of
\Upsilon
. The
rejection probability
\theta_{n}(\eta)
is a real-side object, so the real-side counterpart is needed alongside: the class
\{S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta):\eta\in\Upsilon\}
is asymptotically
equicontinuous with continuous Gaussian limit and pointwise marginals converging (the
asymptotic-equicontinuity and finite-dimensional-convergence paragraphs of the proof of
Theorem
5
), so

\sup_{\eta\in\Upsilon}\,\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl|\mathbb{P}\bigl(S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)\leq t\bigr)-F_{\eta}(t)\bigr|=o_{p}(1).

The two-sided bound is the triangle inequality

\bigl|\theta_{n}(\eta)-\alpha\bigr|\;\leq\;\bigl|\mathbb{P}\bigl(S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)>c_{n,\alpha}(\eta)\bigr)-\mathbb{P}\bigl(S_{\infty}(\eta)>c_{\infty}(\eta)\bigr)\bigr|+\bigl|\mathbb{P}\bigl(S_{\infty}(\eta)>c_{\infty}(\eta)\bigr)-\alpha\bigr|,

whose second term vanishes identically,

\mathbb{P}(S_{\infty}(\eta)>c_{\infty}(\eta))=1-F_{\eta}(c_{\infty}(\eta))=\alpha
by the quantile equation.
For the first term, complementation and the triangle inequality give

\displaystyle\bigl|\mathbb{P}\bigl(S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)>c_{n,\alpha}(\eta)\bigr)-\bigl(1-F_{\eta}(c_{\infty}(\eta))\bigr)\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\;\sup_{t}\bigl|\mathbb{P}\bigl(S_{n}^{\perp}(\eta)\leq t\bigr)-F_{\eta}(t)\bigr|

\displaystyle+\bigl|F_{\eta}\bigl(c_{n,\alpha}(\eta)\bigr)-F_{\eta}\bigl(c_{\infty}(\eta)\bigr)\bigr|:

the first summand is
o_{p}(1)
uniformly in
\eta
by the real-side display, and the second is at
most the modulus at scale
\varepsilon
of the jointly continuous
(\eta,t)\mapsto F_{\eta}(t)

(established above) on the event

\{\sup_{\eta}|c_{n,\alpha}(\eta)-c_{\infty}(\eta)|\leq\varepsilon\}
, whose probability tends to one;
both quantile arguments lie in
[0,\bar{c}+1]
on that event,
\bar{c}:=\sup_{\Upsilon}c_{\infty}
, so
uniform continuity on the compact
\Upsilon\times[0,\bar{c}+1]
makes the bound vanish as

\varepsilon\downarrow 0
. Hence
\sup_{\eta}|\theta_{n}(\eta)-\alpha|=o_{p}(1)
. Bounded
convergence against any law of
\hat{\eta}_{m}
on the compact

\Upsilon
gives
\mathbb{E}[\theta_{n}(\hat{\eta}_{m})]\to\alpha
, which proves the theorem; no convergence
of the index sequence inside an expectation is involved. The same argument covers the

\boldsymbol{\Pi}f=0
branch of Theorem
10
, where the projected null CLT holds
verbatim at
\hat{\theta}
(see the convention of Section
4.2
).

Lemma 23
(Continuity of the limiting quantile function)

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
be the fixed nominal level and let
c_{\infty}(\eta)
denote the
(1-\alpha)
-quantile of the null limit
S_{\infty}(\eta)
of Theorem
5
. Under Assumption
7
, the map
\eta\mapsto c_{\infty}(\eta)
is continuous on
\Upsilon
.

Proof.
The proof uses the sample-path continuity of
S_{\infty}
, established in Appendix
B.3.3
. We show that the marginal laws
\mathcal{L}(S_{\infty}(\eta))
are weakly continuous in
\eta
and that each distribution function
F_{\eta}
is continuous and strictly increasing; the quantile continuity then follows from Pólya’s theorem.

Weak continuity of the marginals.
By Appendix
B.3.3
,
S_{\infty}(\cdot)
has continuous sample paths on the compact set
\Upsilon
. Hence for any
\eta_{n}\to\eta
,
S_{\infty}(\eta_{n})\to S_{\infty}(\eta)
almost surely, and therefore
S_{\infty}(\eta_{n})\xrightarrow{d}S_{\infty}(\eta)
, i.e.
F_{\eta_{n}}\xrightarrow{w}F_{\eta}
weakly.

Continuity and strict monotonicity of
F_{\eta}
.
At each fixed
\eta
,
S_{\infty}(\eta)=\sum_{i\geq 1}w_{i}(\eta)(Z_{i}^{\perp})^{2}
with weights
w_{i}(\eta)=\mu_{i}(\gamma)(\mu_{i}(\gamma)+\lambda)^{-2}>0
(all
\mu_{i}(\gamma)>0
by Assumption
7
(iv)) and
\sum_{i}w_{i}(\eta)\leq\lambda_{L}^{-2}<\infty
. Whitening
Z^{\perp}=\Sigma^{\perp}(\gamma)^{1/2}U
with
U\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I)
on the support of the generically singular
\Sigma^{\perp}(\gamma)
(cf. Corollary
1
) represents the limit as
S_{\infty}(\eta)=\sum_{k\geq 1}\varsigma_{k}(\eta)U_{k}^{2}
, where
\varsigma_{k}(\eta)\geq 0
are the eigenvalues of
\Sigma^{\perp}(\gamma)^{1/2}W(\eta)\Sigma^{\perp}(\gamma)^{1/2}
and
W(\eta):=\mathrm{diag}(w_{i}(\eta))
: a weighted sum of independent
\chi^{2}_{1}
variables. At least one weight is strictly positive, namely
\varsigma_{1}(\eta)=\lVert W(\eta)^{1/2}\Sigma^{\perp}(\gamma)^{1/2}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}>0
. Positivity holds because
W(\eta)^{1/2}
has strictly positive diagonal and
